===Original===
To Norman Kenneth Stephen, late Senior Assistant Master of Harrow School, who was the first to show me that, though few can bend it, the long bow of English is on every man’s back, this book is respectfully and gratefully dedicated.

===Chinese===
谨以敬意与感激，将此书献给诺曼·肯尼思·斯蒂芬（Norman Kenneth Stephen）——曾任哈罗公学高级助教。是他最先让我领悟：英语的长弓虽悬于每个人的背上，能将其拉弯者却寥寥无几。

## IX Out of the Eater / IX 吃的从吃者出来

===Original===
Had our doom been set forth upon paper and submitted to Rose Noble himself, for his approval, I cannot believe that he would have altered one particular.

===Chinese===
倘若我们的厄运被写下呈文，送给罗斯·诺布尔（Rose Noble）本人过目，请他核准，我绝不相信他会改动其中任何一个细节。

===Original===
*We were entombed alive: this, by our own act, with the treasure under our hand, in the knowledge of an attack upon one man and a dog, who would count in vain upon our succour.*

*Had we had the tools, we had no longer the strength to hew our way back: indeed, to judge from the sound, it seemed likely that ten or more yards of our tunnel had fallen in. Yet, could we have performed this unthinkable task, it would only have been to fall into the enemy’s hands.*

*And the other way out was barred: and beyond the bars was the shaft, the mouth of which would be sealed in less than an hour: and beyond the shaft was the well, some ninety feet deep.*

===Chinese===
*我们被活活封在了里面：这是我们自己一手造成的——财宝就在手边，而我们明明知道，外面正有人袭击一男一犬，他们盼我们救援，只能是徒然盼望。*

*纵然有工具，我们也已没有力气凿出一条回路：而且听声音，坑道塌进去的恐怕不止十码。可就算我们能完成这桩不可思议的活计，也不过是自投罗网而已。*

*另一条出路被铁条封死：铁条之外是竖井，不到一个钟头，井口就要被封上；竖井之外是那口井，深约九十英尺。*

===Original===
Mansel was speaking.

“The first thing to do is to keep calm: the second is to break these bars. And now please don’t move for a moment, or we shall collide.”

We heard him make his way to the entrance: when he was there, he spoke again.

“Chandos, come here and lift Ellis. If I can find it, we may as well have his torch.”

===Chinese===
曼塞尔（Mansel）开口了。

「头一件事是保持镇定；第二件事是弄断这些铁条。这会儿请谁都别动，不然要撞到一起了。」

我们听见他摸索着走向入口；到了那里，他又开口了。

「钱多斯（Chandos），过来把埃利斯（Ellis）抬起来。要是我能摸到，最好把他那支火把也拿过来。」

===Original===
At once I moved to the entrance and, thrusting my hands between the bars, seized the corpse under its arms and endeavoured to lift it up. But it was too heavy for me: so Mansel and Hanbury raised me, whilst I held it against the bars.

The torch was alight in a pocket, into which the dead man must have thrust it, so soon as he heard our chisel enter the wall; and, when Mansel had taken it out and had felt in the other pockets, he told me to throw back the body away from the bars. This I was thankful to do.

The entrance was a frame of hewn stone, eight inches thick. In this the bars were set upright, four inches apart: and, close to its foot, one was filed almost through.

===Chinese===
我立刻挪到入口，双手从铁条间探进去，抓住尸首的腋下往上抬。可它太沉了，我抬不动；于是曼塞尔和汉伯里（Hanbury）把我托起来，我则把它抵在铁条上。

火把在一处衣袋里还亮着——那死人听见我们的凿子凿进墙里，必定马上就把它塞了进去；曼塞尔把它取出，又摸了摸另外几个衣袋，然后叫我离铁条远些，把尸首抛到后面去。这正是我求之不得的。

入口是一圈琢石砌成的框子，厚八英寸。铁条直立着嵌在框里，彼此相隔四英寸；贴近框脚的地方，有一根已被锉得快要断了。

===Original===
We cut this in two with the chisel without delay: but, if we then expected that our release was at hand we were grievously disappointed.

God knows what smith it was that wrought that bar: but he was a craftsman that knew his mystery: and I do not believe that such art is practised today. Severed though it was, we could neither break nor bend it: and, when we attempted to lever it up with the crowbar, we bent this into a hoop.

There lay the file within reach: but we could not bear the thought of a labour so lengthy and cudgelled our weary brains to find out some other way.

===Chinese===
我们用凿子毫不耽搁地把它锉成了两截；可要是我们本以为脱身近在眼前，那便大失所望了。

天晓得打造这根铁条的是哪一位铁匠：他是个深谙本行奥秘的匠人，我相信这样的手艺如今已经失传。铁条虽已锉断，我们却折不断也掰不弯它；想用撬棍把它撬起来，反倒把撬棍别成了一圈。

锉刀就在手边；可一想到那没完没了的工夫，我们就受不了，便绞尽疲惫的脑筋，想找出别的法子。

===Original===
Now the bars were all sunk, top and bottom, into holes cut in the stone, and, though our treatment had not displaced it, the upper portion of the bar we had cut could now be turned round in its socket and even moved up and down, and, but for the lower portion, which opposed it, it would have come down and away. Perceiving this, we determined to loosen the lower piece, in the hope that we could then raise this up, until it was clear of its bed, and thus remove the whole bar, by simply reversing the procedure by which it was undoubtedly set up.

It did not take long to loosen the lower portion, but, either because the stonework itself had settled or because, when the bars were planted, some trick of masonry was used, we could not make enough play to free this piece from its bed.

The knowledge that the water below was rising fast and that the mouth of the shaft would soon be sealed made it hard to give our minds to the problem as freely as we would have wished and easy to return to methods which we had already discarded as forlorn. And this, I suppose, was the beginning of panic. Be that as it may, I remember Mansel sitting down with his head in his hands, whilst I leaned against the wall, holding the torch and watching Rowley filing like a madman and Hanbury, with the hammer and chisel, trying to cut a channel out of the stone.

===Chinese===
那些铁条上下两端都嵌在石头上凿出的孔里；虽然我们的一番折腾没能把它弄松，我们锉断的那根的上半截已能在石臼里转动，甚至能上下活动——要不是下半截顶着，它早就整个脱落了。看明这一点，我们便决定把下半截弄松，指望随后把它抬起来，直到脱出石槽，再照着当初安设它的路数反其道而行，把整根铁条拆下来。

松动下半截没费多久；可不知是石工本身沉了下去，还是当初埋设铁条时用了什么砌石的巧劲，我们总腾挪不出足够的余地去，那截铁条就是脱不出石槽。

底下的水在飞快上涨，井口眼看就要封死，这两桩心事搅得我们无法专心琢磨难题，反倒动不动就退回到那些我们早已断定无望的办法上去。我想，这就是慌乱的开端。且不论如何，我记得曼塞尔双手抱头坐了下去，我靠着墙举着火把，看罗利（Rowley）发疯似的锉着铁条，看汉伯里抡锤使凿，想在石头上凿出一道槽来。

===Original===
Suddenly—

“Look here,” said Mansel rising. “Why don’t we try the wall? It let us in, and why shouldn’t it let us out? And, once we’re out, we can break our way into the shaft.”

We should, of course, have attempted this long before: but I suppose it was natural that the entrance, so nearly open, should have attracted and held the whole of our attention. But now we saw that, provided the void we had found ran right round the chamber, we should be without in a moment and very probably able to make our way past the entrance without having to displace any earth. For the entrance was two feet wide: but the shaft was three.

===Chinese===
突然——

「听我说，」曼塞尔站起来说，「咱们为什么不试试那堵墙？它放咱们进来的，为什么不能放咱们出去？再说，一出去，咱们就能凿穿井壁进到竖井里去。」

其实我们早该想到这一层：也难怪，那眼看就要洞开的入口吸住了我们全部的心神。可这会儿我们看清了，只要我们发现的那道空腔绕石室一周，我们顷刻便能脱身，而且多半能绕过入口出去，连一捧土都不必挖。因为入口只有两英尺宽，竖井却有三英尺。

===Original===
And so it fell out.

In less than five minutes, six more stones were out of the chamber’s wall, and, when I stepped through the new opening, there was the wall of the shaft twenty inches away. To breach this was very simple, yet took longer, I think, than even Mansel had expected; for we had ruined the crowbar and dared not work too fiercely for fear of spoiling the chisel or snapping the helve of the only hammer we had. But at last we had made an opening through which a man might pass.

While Mansel and I had been working, Hanbury and Rowley had put what remained of the treasure into the bags and had fastened their mouths. This by Mansel’s direction, “for,” said he, “I know that a man can take nothing out of this world, but, so long as he’s in it, he may as well keep what he’s got.”

===Chinese===
事情果然如此。

不到五分钟，石室的墙上又拆下了六块石头；我从新洞里跨出去，二十英寸外便是竖井的井壁。凿穿它再简单不过，可我觉得花的工夫比曼塞尔预计的还要长；因为撬棍已经毁了，我们又不敢下手太狠，生怕毁掉凿子，或是把仅有的一把锤子的柄弄断。但末了我们终于凿出了一个容一人通过的洞。

我和曼塞尔凿墙的时候，汉伯里和罗利已把剩下的财宝装进袋里，扎紧了袋口。这是奉了曼塞尔的吩咐，他说：「我知道人到头来什么也带不走，可只要一天还在人世，手里有的就还是攥住的好。」

===Original===
And there we were all with him, for, when you have staked your life on some adventure, it is a bitter business only to save your stake.

So we escaped from the chamber into the shaft, taking the bags with us.

There was a rope fastened to one of the bars. I suppose it had been bound there by the tanner, to enable Ellis to make the ascent of the shaft: but it stood us in very good stead, for the steps were very irregular and covered with slime.

===Chinese===
我们全都和他想到一处，因为当你把性命押在一场冒险上，末了却只保住了本钱，那滋味是苦的。

于是我们带着袋子，从石室逃进了竖井。

一根绳子拴在一根铁条上。想来是制革匠绑的，好让埃利斯攀上竖井；可它帮了我们大忙，因为石级高低不齐，又满布黏滑的污垢。

===Original===
It was at once arranged that we should descend one by one: for, rope or no, it was not a place to play tricks in, and, if two used the rope together, one, by slipping, might bring the other down.

I went first, dragging a bag behind me, and, when I had reached the water, Hanbury followed me down.

The water was roughly one foot from the top of the mouth of the shaft, and I could see a pale radiance which I knew for that of the moon. The air, too, was fresh, and, chill and dank as it was, because of the depth of the well, I can never describe the relief I found in breathing it, and I think it did more to refresh me body and soul, than anything that had happened since Mansel and I had found the water at the foot of the combe.

===Chinese===
当下商定一个一个地下：因为有绳子也罢没绳子也罢，这地方容不得半点花巧，两人同时抓绳，一个失手就会带下另一个。

我头一个下去，身后拖着一袋；我到了水面，汉伯里跟着下来。

水面离井口顶端约有一英尺，我望见一片惨白的光，我知道那是月光。空气也是新鲜的；虽说因为井深，又冷又潮，可呼吸到它，那份轻松我永远无法言传——自打曼塞尔和我在谷底找到水以来，没有一件事比它更让我身心一振。

===Original===
So it was, I think, with us all. And of such, I suppose, is the way of Providence; for, if ever four weary men had need of comfort, we needed it then.

*Except for a plank, floating upon the water, the well was as empty of gear as a blown egg.*

I do not think we had counted on anything else: we had, rather, deliberately refrained from leaping, so to speak, until we were come to the ditch. But we had hoped desperately to find something—some rope or scaffolding which would have given us a chance, if not of scaling the walls, at least of rising with the water and so avoiding the most miserable of deaths. But there was nothing at all: and, though I went out with the torch and, getting astride of the plank, recklessly raked the walls for as far as the torch would light them—and that was some thirty feet up—I might have spared my labour, for they were as bare as my hand.

===Chinese===
我想我们个个如此。天道如此，或许正因如此；因为若说有四个疲惫的人需要一点慰藉，我们那时正是。

*除了一块浮在水面的木板，井里空空荡荡，什么器具也没有，活似一枚掏空了的蛋。*

我想我们原也没指望别的：毋宁说，我们一直刻意不去奢望，好比不到沟边不跳。可我们死死盼着能找到点东西——一根绳子或一副脚手架，纵然爬不上井壁，至少也能随水上涨，躲开那最凄惨的死法。可那里什么都没有：我举着火把探出去，跨上木板，不管不顾地把火把照得到的井壁一路刮了一遍——能照到三十英尺高——也是白费力气，四壁光溜溜的，一如我的手掌。

===Original===
“Nothing?” said Mansel, as I came back to the shaft.

I shook my head: and, as they helped me ashore Hanbury and I, between us, let fall the torch, and there was an end of that.

For perhaps two minutes we stood, with our eyes on the water, already full four inches higher than when I had seen it first ten minutes before.

===Chinese===
「什么也没有？」我回到竖井时，曼塞尔问。

我摇摇头；他们把我拉上岸时，火把在汉伯里和我手里一滑，掉了下去，就此完了。

约莫两分钟，我们站着，眼睛盯着水面——比起十分钟前我头一回看见时，它已足足涨了四英寸。

===Original===
Then I heard Mansel catch his breath.

“By thunder!” he cried. “We must be losing our minds. What was the use of this plank without two beams? They’re not afloat, because they’re locked in the niches, but I’ll lay a monkey we find them two feet down.”

With that, he dived and, after what seemed a long time, came up with one of the rafters which Carson and I had cut from the outhouse roof. In a moment I had the other, and together we set them in the niches which were now two inches above the water line. Then we laid the plank across them, and, five minutes later, the four of us were standing upon the stage.

===Chinese===
随后我听见曼塞尔倒抽一口气。

「真见鬼！」他喊道，「咱们准是疯了。光有木板，没有两根梁，顶什么用？它们没漂起来，是因为卡在壁龛里。我敢押五百镑，它们就在水面下两英尺处。」

说着他一个猛子扎下去，过了仿佛许久，托着一根椽木冒出头来，那正是卡森（Carson）和我从披屋屋顶上砍下来的。转眼我便摸到了另一根；我们一起把它们架进壁龛——如今龛沿已高出水面两英寸。然后我们把木板搁上去，五分钟后，我们四人都站在平台上了。

===Original===
We had had a struggle to bring the bags out of the shaft, for they were immensely heavy: indeed, we had almost lost one: and this made us so uneasy that Hanbury went back to the shaft and cut a length from the rope and, with this, lashed them loosely together, so that they could hang from a beam, as a pair of saddlebags slung on the back of a horse.

We did not sit down, for the plank was nearly awash and very soon we should be able to raise the stage: but, as I have said before, all the way up the well the niches lay two feet apart, and, the beams being heavy and we having nothing to stand on, but only the water itself to buoy us up, we could not raise the rafters as much as two feet at a time, but must wait till the water had risen and made this distance less.

So we stood in a row on the plank: and, when the water was halfway to the calf of my leg, we raised the stage.

===Chinese===
把袋子弄出竖井可费了一场挣扎，它们实在沉得很；有一袋险些脱手。这让我们心里发慌，汉伯里便回到竖井，从绳子上割下一段，把袋子松松地捆作一处，好让它们像一对马褡子似的挂在梁下。

我们没有坐下，因为木板眼看要被水淹没，很快就能把平台抬高了；不过我前头说过，井里壁龛两两相隔两英尺，梁又沉，我们脚下除了托着自己的水再无立足之处，一次抬不上两英尺，只能等水涨上来，把这距离缩短。

于是我们在木板上一字排开站定；水涨到我小腿一半时，我们把平台抬了起来。

===Original===
Again we had a battle to save the bags, for to keep afloat ourselves during the transfer was almost as much as we could do, and swapping horses in midstream must be child’s play compared with the struggle we had to raise ourselves and our baggage twenty inches above the flood.

But at last it was over, and we were again on the plank, with nearly an hour before us before we must move again.

Enforced inaction, I suppose, never helped anyone. But, sitting there in the darkness, we had nothing to do but think. And, when I set down my thoughts, I think I am revealing more or less what was in each of our minds.

===Chinese===
抢救袋子又是一场恶战，因为单单让自己浮在水里换地方，已使我们竭尽全力；比起把自己连同行李抬出洪水二十英寸的这一仗，中流换马只能算小孩子的把戏。

但总算完了，我们重新站上木板，离下一次挪动还有将近一个钟头。

被迫闲着，我猜从没帮过谁的忙。我们坐在黑暗里，除了想，无事可做。我把我当时所想的记下来，多少也道出了我们每个人心里的话。

===Original===
Our state was melancholy.

We were as cold now as, a short time before, we had been hot. We had to chafe our fingers, to prevent them from growing numb. Mansel was stripped to the waist, and we were all drenched and dripping and utterly worn out.

Of succour from without there was no hope at all. Carson was miles away, and Bell, if indeed he was not dead or captive, was holding the postern gate.

===Chinese===
我们的处境是凄凉的。

方才我们热，如今又冷透了。我们得不停搓着手指，免得它们麻木。曼塞尔上身只剩一层皮肉，我们浑身透湿，滴着水，筋疲力尽。

外面的救援，一丝指望也没有。卡森远在数英里之外；贝尔（Bell）若还算活着、没被捉住，就守着边门。

===Original===
The water was rising at the rate of thirty inches an hour: but this pace would not last for long, and *twenty-seven hours must elapse before we could climb above the high-water mark*.

And, if by some miracle we could endure so long, if, before then, the thieves had not returned to the well, to find us waiting with the treasure, what then?

*Then we should still be thirty-four feet from the top … thirty-four feet …*

===Chinese===
水以每小时三十英寸的速度上涨；可这速度撑不了多久，而*要过二十七个钟头，我们才能升到洪水的水位线之上*。

何况，即便奇迹眷顾，我们真能撑那么久，即便在那之前盗贼没有回到井边，撞见我们守着财宝等他们，那又怎样？

*那我们离井顶仍差三十四英尺……三十四英尺……*

===Original===
We had been confronted with three apparently insuperable steps. First, we had been locked in the chamber: yet we had escaped. Then we had been trapped in the shaft: yet we had emerged. And now we were down in the well. That is to say, we had taken two of the steps, only to find that the third was insuperable indeed.

I will not say that there was no more spirit in us, but the figures with which we were faced would have daunted anyone. Twenty-seven hours of waiting: twenty-five several battles to raise the stage: and then—thirty-four feet which, unless some rope was dangling, we could not possibly scale. And when, after thirty minutes, Hanbury rose to his feet and said quietly, “I’m sorry but I can’t stand this,” I think we all understood.

“I’m going to try to climb up,” he added gravely, “by means of the niches.”

===Chinese===
我们面前横着三道看似无法逾越的台阶。第一道，我们被锁在石室里：可我们逃出来了。第二道，我们被困在竖井里：可我们出来了。如今我们落到了井底。也就是说，我们迈过了两道台阶，却发现第三道当真无法逾越。

我不敢说我们泄了气，可摆在眼前的数字足以吓退任何人。二十七个钟头的等待：二十五场抬升平台的苦战：然后——三十四英尺，除非恰好有根绳子垂着，否则我们无论如何爬不上去。三十分钟后，汉伯里立起身来，平静地说：「对不住，这个我受不住了。」我想我们都懂。

「我要试着往上爬，」他沉沉地补了一句，「踩着那些壁龛。」

===Original===
“Steady, George,” said Mansel, laying a hand on his arm. “The thing’s impossible. No one in our condition could bring it off.”

“I’m going to try,” said Hanbury. “It may be a chance in a million—”

“It isn’t that,” said I. “The niches come to end ten feet from the top.”

===Chinese===
「稳着点，乔治（George），」曼塞尔按住他的胳膊，「这事办不到。咱们这副样子，谁也上不去。」

「我要试试，」汉伯里说，「兴许是百万分之一的指望——」

「连这个也谈不上，」我说，「壁龛离井顶十英尺就断了。」

===Original===
There was a long silence: and presently Hanbury sat down.

And how long we sat still then I cannot remember, but I know that all of a sudden Rowley, who was sitting beside me, gave a great start and then began to laugh like a man at a play.

I laid hands upon him, for I thought he had lost his wits. But as soon as he spoke I knew there was nothing to fear.

===Chinese===
长久的沉默；过了一会儿，汉伯里坐下了。

之后我们静坐了多久，我记不得了；只记得坐在我身旁的罗利猛地一震，随即像戏院里的看客似的放声大笑起来。

我一把抓住他，还当他疯了。可他一开口，我就知道不必担心。

===Original===
“I’m sorry, sir, but it’s like a clown in the ring. We’d sell our souls for a ladder, and *we’re sitting on one all the time*.”

There was a moment’s silence.

Then—

===Chinese===
「对不住，先生，可这就像马戏场里的小丑。咱们肯拿灵魂去换一架梯子，可*咱们打头就坐在梯子上*。」

一时的静默。

然后——

===Original===
“Put it more clearly,” said Mansel. “I believe I see what you mean.”

And that, I confess, was more than I could have said.

“The beams, sir,” said Rowley. “We can set them above one another, and then, when we’re all on the top one, pull out the one below and lift up that. And so on. I know there’ll be ten feet to go, sir, when we get to the top: but it’s better than waiting until—until we can’t wait no more.”

===Chinese===
「说得更明白些，」曼塞尔说，「我想我明白你的意思了。」

而这，我承认，已非我所能夸口。

「是那两根梁，先生，」罗利说，「咱们可以把它们一根摞一根地架。等大伙儿都上了上面那根，再把底下那根抽出来提上去。就这么轮着来。我知道到顶的时候还差十英尺，先生，可这总比干等着强——等到等不动的时候。」

===Original===
“It is, indeed,” said Mansel heartily. “Rowley, I give you best. And when we get out, *as we shall*, I’ll thank you for saving my life.”

Then we all spoke at once and laughed and jested and clapped Rowley on the back, as if we were souls in a tavern and full of ale, instead of upon the brink of the most hazardous endeavour that ever four wretches made.

Then Hanbury slipped off the plank into the water, and the rest of us straddled the beam over which the kit-bags hung: and when he had freed the other, he gave it to us, and we fitted it into the niches two feet above.

===Chinese===
「那自然，」曼塞尔由衷地说，「罗利，我服了你。等咱们出去——*咱们准能出去*——我要谢你救了我的命。」

接着大家七嘴八舌，又说又笑，拍着罗利的背，仿佛一群酒馆里的汉子灌足了麦酒，而不是四个苦命人正站在平生最凶险的一搏的悬崖边上。

随后汉伯里从木板滑进水里，我们其余人跨坐到挂着装备袋的那根梁上；他解开另一根递给我们，我们把它架进两英尺上方的壁龛。

===Original===
Happily the beams were on edge, or they would not have borne our weight: but, before ten minutes were past, we would have given a fortune to have had a third. Whether the plank would have helped us, if we could have cut it down, I do not know: but the chisel we had left by the chamber, and there was a foot of water above the mouth of the shaft. And since it could not have carried us, though it might have served as a rail, we let it go.

When George had climbed out of the water, before we went any further, we determined exactly the system by which we must go. “For,” said Mansel, “if ever an exercise required a military precision, this is the stunt. We’ve only to make one mistake, and we shan’t be in a position to make any more.”

The first thing which we decided was to preserve the order in which we sat on the beam. Mansel was sitting at one end, and Hanbury at the other, each of them facing the wall: I was next to Mansel and facing the same way as he, and Rowley was next to George: and the bags hung in the middle, between Rowley and me.

===Chinese===
幸好那两根梁是侧立着的，不然吃不住我们的分量；可不出十分钟，我们宁可倾家荡产也想再有一根。那块木板若能砍短，能否帮上忙，我不知道；可凿子丢在了石室那边，井口以上已有一英尺深的水。它既然驮不动我们——虽或可当扶手用——我们也就由它去了。

乔治爬出水来，趁还没继续往上，我们把上攀的章程定了又定。曼塞尔说：「这桩事要是有一件非得拿出军旅般的精确不可，那就是它了。咱们只能错一步，之后就再没有出错的资格了。」

我们定下的头一件事，是保持各人在梁上的次序。曼塞尔坐一头，汉伯里坐另一头，两人都面对井壁；我挨着曼塞尔，与他同向；罗利挨着乔治；袋子吊在当中，在罗利和我之间。

===Original===
And, for the sake of convenience, I will do as Mansel did then and number us off; so that he became “Number One,” I became “Number Two,” Rowley became “Number Three,” and Hanbury “Number Four.”

Numbers One and Four were to move and take their seats on the beam we had just set up. They would then take hold of the niches two feet above, to gain what stability they could. Numbers Two and Three would then move and, between them, lift up the bags, before moving up themselves. When they were up and in place, Numbers Two and Three would lean down, keeping their balance by holding to One and Four, and, laying firm hold of the beam which was resting below, would draw it out of the niches and lift it up. They would bring it as far as their shoulders and there lay it down, when Numbers One and Four were to guide it home.

That was our method: and, though, looking back, I think we might have done better, the devil was driving, and so it had to serve.

===Chinese===
为了省事，我照曼塞尔当时的做法给我们编号：他成了「一号」，我成了「二号」，罗利成了「三号」，汉伯里成了「四号」。

一号和四号先挪到我们刚架好的那根梁上坐定，再抓住两英尺上方的壁龛，能稳住多少是多少。随后二号和三号挪动，两人合力把袋子提上去，再各自挪上去。等他们坐定归位，二号和三号便俯下身去，一手攀着一号和四号稳住身子，一手牢牢抓住还架在底下的那根梁，把它从壁龛里抽出来往上提。提到齐肩处放稳，一号和四号再引着它归入龛位。

这便是我们的章程；如今回想，我们原可以做得更好些，可那时鬼在后面赶，也就只好将就。

===Original===
Now at first all went like clockwork, and we must have climbed twenty-six feet, before the depth below us began to make itself felt.

I think we had all perceived that here was the serious drawback to Rowley’s plan, for the highest niches were nearly eighty feet up; but it was so important that we should not lose heart that no one had even hinted that a fear of falling might presently supervene.

Be that as it may, I know that all of a sudden the palms of my hands were dripping and I was afraid to move. Rowley and I, between us, had lifted the bags, and he had just taken his seat on the upper beam, but I dared not change my position, for I knew that, if for one instant I were to release my hold, I should not be able to regain it, but must inevitably fall.

===Chinese===
起初一切顺当如钟表，我们大约攀了二十六英尺，脚下的深渊才开始发作。

我想我们都看得出，这是罗利法子的致命短处，因为最高的壁龛将近八十英尺；可当时千万不能泄气，所以谁也没露一个字，说怕高这毛病随时会找上门来。

且不论如何，我知道自己的手心忽然沁出了汗，我不敢动了。罗利和我合力提起了袋子，他刚在上一根梁上坐定，我却不敢挪窝，因为我知道，只消一松手，我就再也抓不回来了，只有摔下去。

===Original===
By Mansel’s direction, I waited: and, after two or three minutes the attack seemed to pass away: but, a few minutes later came another, and everything had to wait until I recovered my nerve.

I suppose my head was the weakest, for I was the first to show fear: but presently Hanbury asked us to give him two minutes’ grace, and, when at length he moved and I turned to lift the bags, Rowley began to tremble, and five more minutes went by before he could play his part.

From then on, the ascent was a nightmare.

===Chinese===
奉曼塞尔的吩咐，我等着；两三分钟后，那阵发作似乎过去了；可几分钟后又来了一阵，一切都得停着，直到我缓过这口气。

想来我的脑子是最不济的，头一个露怯的是我；可过后汉伯里也请我们宽他两分钟；等他好容易动了，我转身去提袋子，罗利又抖了起来，又过了五分钟，他才顶上他的份。

从那时起，这一路攀援成了一场噩梦。

===Original===
If we had had a third beam, it would not have been so bad: but the rafters were very narrow and to bestride the upper, without any handhold above to help us to keep our seat, sometimes required such an effort as we could scarcely make.

Again, it would have been better, if we had not had to look down: but the dark motionless water was constantly catching the eye and reminding us of the doom which one false movement would evoke.

My hands ran so fast with sweat that I was continually fearful of letting fall the rafter which Rowley and I were to raise: and once, when my end was free, but his would not leave its niche, I had to let go of Mansel and put down my other hand, because, if I had not done so, the beam must have slipped from my grasp.

===Chinese===
若有第三根梁，情形还不至于太坏；可椽木很窄，跨坐上梁，头顶又无一物可攀扶稳住身子，有时费的力气大得我们几乎使不出来。

再说，若不必朝下看，也会好得多；可黑沉沉静伏的水总来攫人的眼目，提醒我们只要一步踏错，便是怎样的下场。

我的手汗出得飞快，我时时刻刻怕把该由罗利和我抬起的椽木失手掉下去；有一回，我这头已经脱了龛，他那头却卡着不出，我只得放开曼塞尔，腾出另一只手按住，因为不这样，那根梁必定要从我手里滑走。

===Original===
We dared not rest, if for no other reason, because the depth below us seemed to feed upon delay; for, so often as we waited for someone to recover his nerve, the bare idea of moving seemed to become monstrous and the renewal of the struggle a hazard we could not take.

With this increasing horror came other fears: and, though we spoke hardly at all, I think we imagined vain things. Now a beam seemed to be bending and now insecurely lodged: we supposed one beam to have fallen, and saw ourselves trapped in midair: we wondered if one of us fell, what the others would do: behind all, the ten nicheless feet to which we must come rose like some sinister cliff where the wave of endeavour should be stayed. But, while these apprehensions were transient, the height at which we were working was always there, and, what was worse, growing more obtrusive with every move we made.

Yet we went on somehow, making our way up the well, building our ladder as we went.

===Chinese===
我们不敢歇，单为一桩：脚下的深渊仿佛以拖延为食；我们每等一个人缓过神来，挪动这个念头本身便愈发狰狞，重新搏一场，便成了我们再不敢下的赌注。

随着这恐惧的滋长，别的幻惧也来了；我们虽不大开口，心里却在胡思乱想。一会儿这根梁仿佛弯了，一会儿仿佛没架稳：我们疑心一根梁已经坠落，想象自己悬在半空进退不得：我们盘算着，若有一人失足，其余的人该怎么办：而在这一切背后，那十英尺没有壁龛的高处，像一堵阴森的绝壁耸起，我们奋进的浪头注定要在那里折断。不过这些惊惶都只是一阵阵的；始终在场的，是我们攀援的高度，而且更糟的是，每挪一步，它便逼人一分。

然而我们还是撑了下来，一程一程往上挪，一路给自己搭着梯子。

===Original===
I do not remember feeling weary, but only most stiff and sore: and, indeed, I find it astonishing that we managed so well as we did; for, before we had located the chamber, we were almost deadbeat, and, when we were sitting on the plank, I was so much exhausted that, if I had fallen off, I am sure I should have sunk like a stone. Yet, though we were not sprightly, we moved with a will, and I think it must have been our nerves that kept us going, for I do not believe that we had any physical energy left.

After a long time we could make out the top of the well, for the light of the moon had vanished, and it was very dark. And, with this, a new dread came to plague us, namely, that the thieves would return before we were up. And at times we made sure we had seen the flash of a torch, and at others that Rose Noble was waiting and only letting us carry the treasure up.

We had moved, I think, thirty-six times—but I cannot be sure—and I had just taken my seat, when Mansel spoke.

===Chinese===
我不记得觉得累，只记得浑身僵痛；说来也怪，我们竟能撑得那样好；因为找到石室之前，我们已经半死，坐在木板上时，我疲乏到了极点，若那时跌下去，我确信自己会像块石头似的直沉到底。可我们虽谈不上矫健，却挪得极有决心；我想撑住我们的必是神经，因为我再不信我们身上还剩一丝力气。

好久之后，我们才辨出井顶的轮廓，因为月光早已没了，那里黑得很。随之，一桩新的恐惧又来纠缠：怕盗贼赶在我们上去之前回来。有时我们确信望见了火把的闪光，有时又疑心罗斯·诺布尔正守在上面，只等我们把财宝驮上去。

我们大约挪了三十六回——记不真了——我刚坐定，曼塞尔开了口。

===Original===
“This niche is my last,” said he. “Hanbury, do you say the same?”

After a moment—

“Yes,” said Hanbury.

“Very good,” said Mansel. And then to me “Carry on.”

===Chinese===
「这个壁龛是我最后一站了，」他说，「汉伯里，你呢？」

片刻之后——

「一样，」汉伯里说。

「很好，」曼塞尔说，随后对我说，「接着来。」

===Original===
So for the last time Rowley and I withdrew and lifted the beam: and Mansel and Hanbury guided it into place.

Then Mansel stood up and straddled it and ran his hands over the wall: and Hanbury did the same. But neither spoke, and presently both sat down.

===Chinese===
于是罗利和我最后一次抽出、抬起那根梁，曼塞尔和汉伯里引着它归了位。

曼塞尔立起身，跨坐上去，双手在井壁上摸索；汉伯里也照做了。可两人都没作声，过了一会儿，都坐下了。

===Original===
“William,” said Mansel, looking up, “are you sure we’re still ten feet down?”

“Certain,” said I. “I’ve marked it many a time.”

“Well,” said he, with a sigh, “I’d rather be ten feet down than thirty-four. Can anyone see the chain?”

===Chinese===
「威廉，」曼塞尔仰起脸问，「你准咱们还差十英尺？」

「千真万确，」我说，「我数过不知多少遍了。」

「唉，」他叹了口气，「差十英尺总比差三十四英尺强。谁看得见那根链子吗？」

===Original===
We could make out the windlass, but no one could see the chain. “Let’s hope it’s dangling,” said Mansel, “three or four feet away.”

With that, he and Hanbury descended and sat on the lower beam: then they withdrew the upper and gave it to me, and, while they and Rowley crouched down, I turned the timber crosswise, in the hope, if it was there, of striking the chain. But I encountered nothing, and after a moment or two I was thankful to give it up and to hear it slide back into place.

Then for a while we sat silent, continually gazing upward at the rim of the well.

===Chinese===
绞盘辨得出来，链子却谁也看不见。「但愿它正垂着，」曼塞尔说，「离咱们三四英尺远。」

说罢，他和汉伯里下到下面那根梁上坐了；再把上面那根抽出来交给我；他们和罗利蹲下身子，我把那根木头横过来，指望链子若真垂着，便好撞上。可什么也没碰到；一两分钟后我便庆幸地撒了手，听那根梁滑回了原位。

接着我们静坐了一会儿，眼睛不住地望着井沿。

===Original===
And then at last I perceived the only way. And, wet with sweat as I was, when I saw it, I broke out again. But it was the only way: and, after a little discussion, we made the attempt.

Mansel and Rowley bestrode the upper beam, this time facing each other, instead of towards the wall. I stood between them, in the middle of the lower beam. When they were ready, I put my hands on their shoulders and mounted the upper beam: and, when I was standing upright, I let myself fall forward against the wall. This was but three feet away. At once Hanbury mounted behind me and stepped up on to my shoulders. This brought his head to the level of the rim of the well, and an instant later he was up and within the redoubt.

In a flash he was at the windlass and had let go a length of chain. Then he locked the windlass and, pulling the chain in by hand, lowered it so that it hung between my face and the wall. I seized it easily enough, and one minute later I was out of the well.

===Chinese===
后来我终于看出了唯一的法子。我浑身汗透，一见之下又出了一身。可那是唯一的法子；略微商量之后，我们便试了。

曼塞尔和罗利跨上上面那根梁，这回面对面，不再面向井壁。我站在他们中间，踏着下面那根梁。他们坐稳后，我双手按上他们的肩，登上上面的梁；站直了，便向前扑倒在井壁上——井壁离我不过三英尺。汉伯里随即跟上来，踏着我的肩头站定。这一来他的头便与井沿齐平了，转眼间他已上去，进了堡楼。

一眨眼他已到了绞盘跟前，放出一段链子；随即锁住绞盘，用手一把一把把链子拉下来，让它垂在我脸和井壁之间。我毫不费力便抓住了它；一分钟之后，我出了井。

===Original===
Then we hauled up the bags and Rowley and, finally, Mansel himself. And, when he was up, with one consent we all lay down on the ground, and no one of us moved or spoke for five minutes or more. To tell the truth we were past speaking; and I cannot set down our emotions, because, even at this distance of Time, I can find no words which can tell our gratitude and relief.

So we all surmounted the third insuperable step, and, thanks to Mansel and Rowley, found ourselves risen from the dead. For Mansel brought us into the well, and Rowley taught us how to climb up, and, though they both made much of what Hanbury and I had done, I am afraid the depth of the well lent our performance a glamour which it did not deserve.

---

===Chinese===
接着我们把袋子和罗利吊上来，最后是曼塞尔本人。他一上来，我们不约而同全躺倒在地上，足足有五分钟，谁也不动，谁也不出声。说实话，我们已经说不出话；这份心绪我更写不出来，因为纵然隔着这么远的岁月，我也找不出字眼，写尽我们的感激与如释重负。

就这样，我们翻过了第三道无法逾越的台阶；多亏曼塞尔和罗利，我们死而复生。因为是曼塞尔把我们带进了井里，是罗利教会了我们怎么爬上来；虽然他们俩极力称道我和汉伯里的功劳，只怕井的深幽给我们那点作为平添了它当不起的光彩。

---

===Original===
The redoubt was deserted.

For this we were thankful: yet it quickened our concern for Tester and Bell: and, when I remembered Rose Noble’s way with a hostage, I felt uneasy indeed.

We were, all of us, streaming with sweat; so Mansel took a shirt and a jersey, which he found hanging up, and each of us took some clothing against the cool of the night.

===Chinese===
堡楼里空无一人。

这是我们求之不得的；可它又加重了我们对泰斯特（Tester）和贝尔的悬心；一想起罗斯·诺布尔处置人质的那一手，我心里实在七上八下。

我们个个汗流如注；曼塞尔便取了两件挂着的衬衫和一件毛线衣，我们每人也各拿了几件衣物，抵挡夜寒。

===Original===
Then we passed into the meadow and took to the woods.

We dared not go by the castle, but bore towards the shrine, presently turning left-handed across the road of approach. So we came down to the river, which we made at a point not very far from where Mansel had hidden the boat. To find this took us some minutes, for, although we could see the dawn coming, it was still very dark. But at length I fell into a gully; and there it was.

Then Mansel gave Hanbury Ellis’ pistol, which he had taken from the body before he descended the shaft.

===Chinese===
随后我们穿过草地，进了树林。

我们不敢打城堡跟前过，只朝小教堂方向去，不久便偏左斜越过门前的大道。这样我们下到了河边，离曼塞尔藏船的地方不远。找那条船花了几分钟，因为虽说已望见黎明，天仍很黑。末了我一脚踏进一条沟；船就在那里。

曼塞尔把埃利斯的手枪给了汉伯里——那是他下竖井之前从尸首上取下来的。

===Original===
“You and Rowley,” he said, “stay here and hang on to the bags, while Chandos and I take the boat and scull to the shoot. What we shall find I know no more than you: but what ever happens, I think you must both sit tight, for I know I’m too tired to swim, and we’ve been close enough to drowning for the last three hours. And, as soon as it’s light, I think I should dry that pistol as best you can.”

With that, we launched the boat, and at once I bent to the sculls.

When we came to the shoot, we listened: but, except for the lap of the water, we could hear no manner of sound.

===Chinese===
「你和罗利，」他说，「守在这儿，看住袋子。钱多斯和我划船到溜槽那边去。那边有什么，我跟你们一样不知道：可无论出了什么事，我想你们俩都得按住不动，因为我知道自己累得连游泳都不行了，这三个钟头里，我们已经跟淹死挨得太近。天一亮，我想你们最好把那支枪尽量弄干。」

说罢我们推船下水，我立刻俯身荡桨。

到了溜槽底下，我们侧耳细听：除了水声拍岸，什么也听不见。

===Original===
Then Mansel took his pistol and, withdrawing the magazine, took out the ammunition and wiped it dry. There was a round in the chamber, and he dried that as well. Then he reloaded the weapon, and, when it was quite to his liking, he put up his hand and rang the bell.

For a moment or two we sat waiting.

Then the flap was withdrawn.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔掏出手枪，退出弹匣，把子弹一一擦干。膛里还有一发，他也擦了。然后重新装好，直到他完全满意，才举手按了铃。

我们坐等了一两分钟。

活门拉开了。

===Original===
Now whether it was Bell who had withdrawn it or one of the thieves we could not possibly tell: if it was one of the thieves, now was the moment to enter, as Rose Noble had done: but whoever it was did not let down the ladder, and Mansel dared not demand it, because at once his speech would give him away.

Suddenly, to our great joy, we heard Tester let out the grunt which he kept for matters suspicious, whose claim to be passed in silence had yet to be proved.

“Bell,” said Mansel at once.

===Chinese===
拉开的是贝尔还是盗贼之一，我们无从判断：若是盗贼，此刻正是照罗斯·诺布尔的样闯进去的良机；可不管是谁都没放下梯子，曼塞尔又不敢开口讨，因为一开口，口音立时就会露馅。

突然，我们大喜过望，听见泰斯特放出了那声低吼——那是他专留给可疑之事的：那类事自诩不必声张，却还没证明配得上这份沉默。

「贝尔。」曼塞尔立刻说。

===Original===
“Sir,” said Bell.

“Where’s Rose Noble?”

“In the oubliette, sir. There’s four of them there, *an’ the innkeeper’s pulled up the rope, so they can’t get out*.”

===Chinese===
「先生。」贝尔应道。

「罗斯·诺布尔在哪儿？」

「在地牢里，先生。他们四个在那儿，*老板把绳子收上去了，他们出不来了*。」

===Original===
“But the postern?”

“Carson’s holding that, sir. When you didn’t come, I thought I’d best bring him here.”

“Well done, indeed,” said Mansel. “And now let down the ladder, and I’ll come up.”

===Chinese===
「那边门呢？」

「卡森守着呢，先生。你们没来，我想最好把他带到这儿来。」

「真是漂亮，」曼塞尔说，「好了，把梯子放下来，我上来。」

===Original===
Then he gave me his pistol and told me to pick up the others and row them across the river, treasure and all.

“And then come back here,” said he, “as soon as you can: for this is the last lap, and, between you and me, I’ve a fancy to win the race.”

With that, he went up the ladder, and I sculled back to the others as fast as I could.

===Chinese===
随后他把手枪交给我，叫我把其余的人接过来，连人带财宝摆渡过河。

「然后尽快回来，」他说，「这是最后一程了；不瞒你说，我还想赢下这场赛跑呢。」

说完他攀着梯子上了去，我拼命往回划。

===Original===
Hanbury and Rowley could hardly believe my news: and, indeed, to me it seemed almost too good to be true, for that Bell and Tester should be safe smacked of the supernatural, but that Rose Noble should have permitted himself to be snared was almost inconceivable.

I was back at the shoot in ten minutes, and at once Bell lowered the bucket full of our clothes. Then he let down food and wine, and, lastly, a medley of things—arms and papers and money and *Lockhart’s Life of Scott*. Finally, he descended and proposed to row me across and then himself to return for Mansel and Carson. But I had him across the river, before he had got on his clothes, and, when he had unloaded the boat, I sculled back for the last time.

I arrived to find Carson throwing down our last coil of rope, and, after a little, he descended, with Tester under his arm. Almost at once the rope-ladder fell into the water, and, an instant later, Mansel slid down the shoot.

===Chinese===
汉伯里和罗利简直不敢相信我的消息；说实话，连我也觉得好得近乎不真：贝尔和泰斯特安然无恙已近乎神迹，罗斯·诺布尔竟自投罗网，更是难以想象。

十分钟后我回到溜槽，贝尔立刻把装满我们衣物的吊桶放了下来。接着放下吃食和酒，最后是一堆杂七杂八的东西——枪械、文件、钱，还有洛克哈特的《司各特传》（Lockhart’s Life of Scott）。末了他自己也下来了，要摆我过河，再回来接曼塞尔和卡森。可他还没穿好衣服，我已经把他渡过了河；卸空了船，我最后一次荡桨回去。

我到时，卡森正把最后一盘绳索抛下来；过了一会儿，他夹着泰斯特下来了。几乎同时，绳梯落进水里，一眨眼，曼塞尔顺着溜槽滑了下来。

===Original===
He would not enter the boat, but laid hold of the painter and bade me bend to the oars.

So soon as we were ashore, we sank the boat; and, two minutes later, we all set out for the culvert, bearing our treasure with us, like men in a fairytale.

The dawn was up, but we held to the road, for to make our way across country was beyond our power. As it was, we lurched and staggered, and once I fell asleep walking and, taking no note of a bend, fell into the ditch. Yet Mansel drove us on, “for,” said he, “I’m not going to be caught at the post. There’s the postern and the shoot and the river between them and us: but four of us are no better than dead men, and I couldn’t hit Rose Noble at seven feet. And there you are. I’m sorry to spoil your outlook, but we’ve got to be in France before sunset, and there’s quite a long way to go.”

===Chinese===
他不肯上船，只抓住艇索，叫我奋力荡桨。

一上岸，我们就把小船沉了；两分钟后，我们全体动身去涵洞，携着财宝，活像童话里的人物。

天已放亮，我们仍旧沿大路走，因为横越野地已非我们力所能及。就这样，我们跌跌撞撞、东倒西歪，有一回我竟边走边睡着，没留意转弯，一头栽进了路沟。可曼塞尔驱赶着我们，「因为，」他说，「我可不想在岗哨上被逮住。他们和我们之间隔着边门、溜槽和大河：可我们四个跟死人差不多，我七英尺内都打不中罗斯·诺布尔。就是这么回事。对不住，扫了你们的兴，可我们赶在天黑前必须进法国，路还长着呢。」

===Original===
At last we came to the culvert.

There we left the rifles, bestowing them under the arch. And then, without more ado, we all climbed into the Rolls, and Carson drove us to Villach as fast as he could.

There we only waited to take up the second car: and Bell was set to drive this, because, after Carson, he was the least fatigued.

===Chinese===
末了我们到了涵洞。

在那里我们留下步枪，藏进桥拱底下。然后二话不说，全体爬进那辆劳斯莱斯（Rolls），卡森开着车，一路疾驶，直奔菲拉赫（Villach）。

我们在那里只耽搁着接上第二辆车：贝尔被派来开车，因为除了卡森，就数他最不累。

===Original===
Ten miles short of Salzburg we stopped; and, when we had done what we could to order our appearance, we emptied the Rolls’ toolbox and packed within it so much of the treasure as we could make it hold. It was a capacious coffer, but, when it was full, there still remained a good deal: most of this we hid in the tire of one of the two spare wheels, and what was still left we concealed about ourselves.

All this because of the Customs: for we knew very well that, if it was found at a frontier that we were laden with jewels, we should be certainly stopped and those in authority informed.

And here, for the first time, I perceived that, though we had lifted the treasure, we stood in imminent danger of losing every ounce; for, if once its existence came to the knowledge of the State, all the resources of the Law would be employed to prevent six foreigners from abducting so considerable a fortune.

===Chinese===
离萨尔茨堡（Salzburg）还有十英里，我们停了下来；稍稍整理了形容之后，我们把劳斯莱斯的工具箱腾空，把能塞进去的财宝尽量塞了进去。那口箱子容量不小，可装满之后，仍剩下许多：我们把大半藏进两只备用车轮之一的轮胎里，剩下的便藏在了各自身上。

这一切都是为了海关：我们心里透亮，一旦在边境被查出满载珠宝，必定被扣下，还会惊动当权的人。

也就在这时，我头一次醒悟：财宝虽到了手，我们却随时可能一两不剩；因为这事一旦让国家知晓，法律的全部机器都会开动，来阻止六个异邦人把这么一大笔财产劫走。

===Original===
This peril shocked me so much that I besought Mansel to wait and to let us dispose the treasure in some less conspicuous place. But he would not listen.

“I dare not wait,” said he, “because of Rose Noble. I’m not afraid of him, because now we’ve had some sleep: but I don’t want a brush with him in a public place. Whatever the outcome was, explanations would have to be made. *And we’re not in a position to explain.* Nobody is, when he’s carrying stuff like this. As for the Customs, we’ve as good a chance there today as we should have next week. I shan’t enjoy the passage, but it’s got to be made. As far as the toolbox is concerned, I’ll give you an excellent rule: if you’ve something to hide, always hide it in the most obvious place. And now don’t worry. If you can’t go to sleep, look inexpressibly bored. And please try not to perspire. Perspiration is the emblem of an uneasy mind.”

If that was a true saying, the officials who dealt with us were an inobservant lot, for, while they examined the Rolls, the sweat ran down my face. But Mansel paid the dues with an injured air, and, after a little delay, they let us pass.

===Chinese===
这一重危险叫我大为震动，我求曼塞尔停下来，让我们把财宝安顿在一处不那么惹眼的地方。可他不听。

「我不敢停，」他说，「是为了罗斯·诺布尔。我不怕他，因为如今我们睡过一觉了；可我不想在众目睽睽的地方跟他交手。无论结果如何，总得有个交代。*而我们这副样子，交代不得。*谁带着这样的货色，都交代不得。至于海关，今天过和下星期过，机会一样好。这一关我是不会好受的，可非过不可。说到工具箱，我送你一条极好的规矩：东西要藏，就藏在最显眼的地方。好了，别担心。睡不着的话，就摆出一副百无聊赖的脸。还有，请千万忍住别出汗。出汗，是不安之心的徽记。」

这话若灵验，那么查验我们的那几位官员可真是目力欠佳，因为他们搜检劳斯莱斯的时候，汗正顺着我往下淌。可曼塞尔一脸委屈地缴了税，稍一耽搁，他们便放行了。

===Original===
So we entered Germany: and at half past five that evening we came to France.

And here I thought all was over, for they turned us out of the car and took up the cushions and carpet and opened the petrol-tank. They did not open the toolbox, *because they saw Mansel do that*. I saw him do it, too, and thought he was out of his mind. He took out one of the rubbers with which we had covered the treasure and then put a foot on the toolbox and started to dust his shoes, talking politics all the time with the Frenchman in charge and becoming so engrossed in his discourse that the search had been done and we were back in the car, before he had finished his dusting and put his rubber away.

And, when later we spoke of the matter with bated breath, he merely observed that prevention was better than cure.

===Chinese===
就这样我们进了德国：当晚五点半，我们到了法国。

到了这里，我以为万事皆休，因为他们把我们赶下车，掀起了坐垫和地毯，打开了油箱。工具箱他们没开，*因为他们看见曼塞尔自己开了*。我也看见了，只当他疯了。他从我们盖财宝的胶皮布里抽出一张，一脚踩上工具箱，拍打起鞋上的灰来，嘴里跟那名押检的法国人谈着政治，谈得入了神，等他拍完鞋、把胶皮布收好，搜查早已完事，我们已经回到车上。

后来我们屏着呼吸说起这一节，他只淡淡来了一句：预防胜于治疗。

===Original===
“But you must have been worried,” cried Hanbury.

“Worried?” said Mansel. “I think it took a year off my life.”

And there you have Jonathan Mansel.

===Chinese===
「可你当时一定急坏了，」汉伯里嚷道。

「急坏了？」曼塞尔说，「我看折了我一年阳寿。」

这就是乔纳森·曼塞尔。

===Original===
Master of many things, he was especially master of himself. His self-control was so perfect that those who knew him best could no more read his heart than they could look through a plate of armour of proof. Add to this that he could think twice as swiftly as other men, and you will see the disadvantage at which his enemies stood. When we were in any trouble, because he was wiser, he saw more clearly than we the depth of the risk we ran: yet he was always the coolest, the most confident, the most matter-of-course. With it all, he was never secretive. All his movements were gentle: yet he had the strength of two men. He was most unassuming and generous: yet was most plainly revered wherever he went.

I never knew, till long after, that, when he became lame, as the result of a wound, he lost his balance and so his head for heights, and that, from that time on, it troubled him to so much as look down from a balcony on to a garden below. Yet on that awful night he came up the great well and gave no indication then or at any time of the agony he must have suffered from this terrible thorn in the flesh.

At Strasbourg we turned south, and, when we were deep in the country, we took a little byroad which led to a wood. There we spent the night. And at eight o’clock the next evening we reached Dieppe.

===Chinese===
万般事务，他无一不精，而最精的，是精于驾驭他自己。他的自制完美到这步田地：再知交的人也读不透他的心，如同看不穿一块千锤百炼的验甲。再加上他动念比旁人快一倍，你便明白他的敌人处在何等下风。我们每逢陷入险境，因为他更高明，他把我们所冒的风险看得比谁都透，可他永远是最镇定的、最有把握的、最若无其事的。尽管如此，他从不高深莫测。他一举一动轻柔温和，力气却抵得上两个人。他最谦冲，最慷慨；所到之处，却分明最受敬重。

我很久以后才知道，他受过枪伤，落下跛足，从此失了平衡，也就失了登高的胆量；打那时起，哪怕从阳台上俯瞰楼下的花园，也会叫他心里发紧。可是在那可怕的夜里，他攀上了那口大井，当时乃至事后，他对这块肉中刺必定带给他的煎熬，没露过一丝形迹。

到了斯特拉斯堡（Strasbourg）我们折向南，深入乡野之后，拐上一条小岔路，通向一座树林。我们在那里过了一夜。第二天傍晚八点，我们到了迪耶普（Dieppe）。

===Original===
So I came back to England the way I had come some seventy days before, with the dog-collar in pocket and my heart in my mouth.

---

And here, before I go any further, I will set down Bell’s tale.

===Chinese===
就这样，我循着七十天前来的路回到了英国，口袋里揣着狗项圈，心提到了嗓子眼。

---

写到这里，趁再往下说之前，我要把贝尔的故事摆一摆。

===Original===
He had heard the trap open, for he had not been asleep, and he and Tester had at once withdrawn to the ramp. No bomb had been thrown, but four men had at once descended into the oubliette. One of these was Rose Noble, and another the tanner, for he had passed close to the postern, and Bell had observed the odour which hung about him. Almost at once they had all gone into the shaft. They were a long time gone, and, before they came back, someone else had descended and followed them in.

Suddenly Bell had heard the unmistakable cry of a man in terror of death. This came from the shaft.

At once a spout of German had burst from the trap, and, since he could hear two voices, Bell knew that they must be those of the tanner’s allies. Their tones were plainly apprehensive, and again and again they repeated the name “Johann”: and at last Bell gathered that the cry must have come from the tanner and that *the two at the trap were suspecting foul play*.

===Chinese===
他听见活板门开了，因为他本没睡着，他立刻和泰斯特退到了壁垒。炸弹倒没扔，可有四个人当即下到了地牢。其中一个是罗斯·诺布尔，另一个是制革匠，因为他从边门近旁经过，贝尔闻见了他身上那股气味。他们几乎随即全下了竖井。这一去许久，回来之前，又有一个人下去，跟着他们进去了。

突然，贝尔听见一声惊心动魄的惨叫，那是一个人自知必死时的喊声。声音出自竖井。

活板门上顿时爆出一片德语；贝尔听得是两个人的声音，知道必是制革匠的同伙。听口气分明惶惶不安，他们一遍遍念着「约翰（Johann）」这个名字；末了贝尔听明白了：惨叫必是制革匠发出的，而*守在活板门上的两个人起了疑，以为出了变故*。

===Original===
Sure enough, when the thieves returned, as they presently did, and Rose Noble commanded the landlord to draw him up, the latter demanded “Johann”: and, when the thieves sought to bluff him, made it plain in pitiful English that *he and the tanner’s brother would take no one of them up until they heard “Johann’s” voice*.

Now what in the world we were doing Bell could not tell, but supposed we were holding the chamber, instead of the shaft: and, since, if they could not get up, the thieves were certain to try to force the postern, he decided to summon Carson to help him to hold the ramp.

He, therefore, whipped down to the gallery and, opening two of the windows, turned on our electric light, and then sped back to the postern to witness a turbulent scene, the thieves roaring orders and threats, and the innkeeper and his companion hurling down taunts and abuse. Then somebody fired at the trap, and at once, as though in answer, the rope came tumbling down on to the stile.

===Chinese===
果然，盗贼们回来——不久他们就回来了——罗斯·诺布尔喝令店主把他吊上去，店主却先讨「约翰」；盗贼们想蒙混，他便用破绽百出的英语把话挑明：*他和制革匠的兄弟听不见「约翰」的声音，就不给他们当中任何一个人吊。*

我们当时究竟在干什么，贝尔无从得知，只猜我们守着石室，没守竖井；而盗贼既然上不来，必定会来强攻边门，他决定召卡森来帮他守壁垒。

于是他飞身下了画廊，推开两扇窗，拧亮我们的电灯，又赶回边门，正撞见一场喧腾的对峙：盗贼们咆哮着下令、恐吓，店主和他的同伴把嘲骂劈头盖脸地砸下去。随后有人朝活板门开了一枪，仿佛应答一般，绳子应声砸在了梯级上。

===Original===
Then Rose Noble had turned upon Punter and rent him for leaving the trap, and Punter had cursed Rose Noble for taking the tanner’s life. But Rose Noble declared with an oath that the tanner had hoaxed them.

“He’s done in Ellis,” he said, “and the bags are down in the shaft. He was meaning to box us here and then go back with his fellows and pouch the lot. And, if we don’t get out of this hole, those other two black-blooded rats will have it yet.”

“But where’s the Willies?” cried Job.

===Chinese===
随后罗斯·诺布尔掉头冲着潘特（Punter）发作，骂他离开了活板门；潘特咒罗斯·诺布尔害了制革匠的命。可罗斯·诺布尔赌咒说，是制革匠耍了他们。

「他把埃利斯干掉了，」他说，「袋子就搁在竖井底下。他原打算把我们堵在这儿，然后回去同他的同伙把财宝一口吞下。咱们要是不出这个窟窿，那两只黑心烂肺的耗子早晚连这个也吞了。」

「可威利家的人上哪儿去了？」乔布（Job）嚷道。

===Original===
The question confounded Bell, but appeared to sober the thieves: for at once they lowered their voices, clearly believing that we were all in the ramp and presumably fearing that we should leave by the shoot and make for the well.

Presently Job had approached and endeavoured to force the postern, and, while he was so engaged, Bell shot him dead.

At once the other three had retired to the shaft, from which Bell fully expected that we should soon drive them out. But when presently Carson arrived, yet there was no sign of our coming, he could not think what had happened and began to fear very much that some accident had occurred.

===Chinese===
这一问问得贝尔发懵，盗贼们却像被浇醒了：他们立刻压低了嗓音，分明以为我们全在壁垒里，八成是怕我们从溜槽脱身、奔井边去。

过了片刻，乔布逼近边门，设法强攻；他正忙活，贝尔一枪把他打死了。

其余三人当即退回竖井；贝尔满心以为我们很快就会把他们从井里赶出来。可过了一阵，卡森来了，还是不见我们的踪影，他想不出出了什么事，开始非常害怕是出了变故。

===Original===
When Carson heard his story, he had at once decided that Bell must take some rest and that, if in three hours’ time, we had not appeared, they must force their way into the shaft, to see what the trouble might be. But, before, that time had expired, we had come to the shoot.

Four several times the thieves had approached the postern; but, I suppose, no one of them was minded to give his life for the other two; for a frontal attack alone could have been successful, and that they did not make.

In the hope of reducing their number, Carson had held his fire as long as he dared, but, though he had wounded Punter, they gave him no other chance.

===Chinese===
卡森听完他的讲述，当即断定：贝尔必须歇一歇，倘若三个钟头后我们还不露面，他们就强行凿进竖井，看看到底怎么回事。可没等时限到，我们已到了溜槽。

盗贼们先后四次逼近边门；可我猜，谁也不肯为另外两个卖命；因为正面强攻是唯一可行的路，而他们没有攻。

为了引他们露出破绽、减少他们的人数，卡森把枪口一直忍到最后；可除了打伤潘特，他们再没给他第二个机会。

===Original===
So, in the end, Rose Noble’s astonishing instinct overleaped itself: for, had he not slain the tanner, finding the fellow guilty of something he had not done, he must, I think, have had the treasure and four of us into the bargain.

---

There is little more to be told.

===Chinese===
到头来，罗斯·诺布尔那惊人的直觉终究跳过了头：倘若他没有错杀制革匠——那人是被安上了不曾犯下的罪名——我想，财宝连同我们四个，早都落进他手里了。

---

可讲的不多了。

===Original===
On the way from Newhaven to London we stopped in a lonely place and put the treasure back into the canvas bags. And, when we reached Cleveland Row, we carried it up and laid it in Mansel’s flat. And there, for the first time, we saw what it was we had won.

The spoil was that of a robber of high estate.

There was nothing common, and, except for a small bag of gold, no coinage at all.

===Chinese===
从纽黑文（Newhaven）回伦敦的路上，我们在一处僻静地方停了车，把财宝装回帆布袋。到了克利夫兰街（Cleveland Row），我们把它们抬上楼，安置在曼塞尔的公寓里。也是在那里，我们头一次看清了我们赢到手的究竟是什么。

这批掠获物，出自一位身份高贵的强盗。

里面没有一件俗物，除了一小袋金币，连一枚钱币都没有。

===Original===
There were jewels of all descriptions and many loose precious stones. There were brooches and clasps and circlets: there were cups and the hilts of poniards, studded with gems: there were two crucifixes and a monstrance and the crook of a pastoral staff, the presence of which, had he been charged with sacrilege, Axel the Red might have found it hard to explain: there was a golden chessboard, with ruby and emerald chessmen, as fine as you please: there was a scourge, the seven cords of which were loaded with seven diamonds, the size of full-grown grapes. There were jewelled dice and bracelets and eighteen or twenty rings: there were images and girdles and a golden hunting-horn: but most lovely of all was a triptych, whose three little, sacred pictures were done like stained-glass windows, only with precious stones.

===Chinese===
珠宝各式各样，还有许多散粒的宝石。有胸针、搭扣和环饰；有杯盏和镶满宝石的匕首柄；有两具十字架、一座圣体匣、一柄牧杖的弯头——倘若红发阿克塞尔（Axel the Red）被控渎圣，这些东西他怕是很难交代；有一面金棋盘，配着红宝石和绿宝石的棋子，精美绝伦；有一条鞭杖，七条鞭绳各坠一颗钻石，大如熟透的葡萄。有镶宝石的骰子、手镯和十八二十枚戒指；有神像、腰带和一支金猎号；而其中最可爱的，是一座三联圣像画，三幅小小的圣像，做得像彩绘玻璃窗，只是用的全是宝石。

===Original===
When we had examined it thoroughly, we packed it all into a plate-chest and lodged it at Mansel’s bank.

And there, I suppose, our adventure came to an end.

Most of the treasure we sold, but Mansel, Hanbury and I each kept some one of the gems. They made me take the triptych, because the secret of Wagensburg had been bequeathed to me. And I have lent it to a Museum, because, to be honest, I dare not house it myself.

===Chinese===
我们细细检视之后，把这一切装进一只银器箱，寄存到曼塞尔的银行里。

我想，我们的冒险至此终局。

财宝大半变卖了，但曼塞尔、汉伯里和我各留下了一件宝石。他们硬要我留下那座三联画，因为瓦根斯堡（Wagensburg）的秘密是传给了我一个人的。而我把它借给了一家博物馆，因为说实话，我自己不敢收在家里。

===Original===
For what we sold we received nine hundred thousand pounds, “which is very much less,” said Mansel, “than what it is worth: but that cannot be helped, for I never was any good in the countinghouse, and, from what I’ve seen of you two, you’re worse than I.” And, indeed, for my part, if I had been told it was worth but half a million, I should have been none the wiser and perfectly content.

Of this huge sum Mansel, Hanbury and I took each two ninths for himself and Carson, Rowley and Bell received one ninth apiece.

To my great surprise, when Bell had been given his cheque, he said that, if I was content to keep him, he had no wish to leave my service; when I pointed out that he was now a man of means, he said that, for his part, that did not alter the case; and since Hanbury and I were proposing to share an estate in the country, I told him to take a holiday and report to me in three weeks’ time.

===Chinese===
卖掉的这些，我们得了九十万镑。「比它实值可差远了，」曼塞尔说，「可那也没法子，我从来不是账房里的好手；再看你们两位的样子，比我还不如。」的确，就我而言，若有人告诉我它只值五十万，我也不会更明白，也不会有半分不满。

这笔巨款，曼塞尔、汉伯里和我各取九分之二归自己和卡森，罗利和贝尔各得九分之一。

大出我意料的是，贝尔领了支票，却说，只要我肯留他，他无意离开我家；我点破他如今已是有钱的人了，他说在他看来，这并不改变什么；汉伯里和我又打算合用一处乡下庄院，我便叫他休个假，三个星期后再来听差。

===Original===
Both George and I found it hard to part with Mansel: and, when the latter suggested that we should come down to Hampshire and spend a week with him there, we were only too glad to accept.

And there, in the midst of the New Forest, he brought us back to the world; for our doings of the last two months had thrown our focus out, and, when we looked upon the future, this seemed intolerably grey. But Mansel pointed the virtue of quiet enjoyment, maintaining that only those who knew the quality of peace could, when the moment came, taste the full flavour of battle: “for,” said he, “for the last two months we have been against the peace, and that is a condition which is all very well for a time but, if it is too much prolonged, will surely lose its sweetness and, what is infinitely worse, will sour the years that are left in the cellar of Life.”

So he did us an enduring service, for, but for that week in Hampshire, I do not think that either George Hanbury or I would ever have settled down, but, having the means to do so, would have gone out to rove the world in search of more excitement and so have dropped substance for a shadow and thrown our birthrights away.

===Chinese===
我和乔治都觉得与曼塞尔分别难舍；他提议我们去汉普郡（Hampshire）他那里住上一个星期，我们求之不得。

就在那里，在新森林（New Forest）的怀抱里，他把我们带回了人世；因为这两个月的经历把我们的焦距弄乱了，放眼将来，只觉一片灰暗，教人难耐。曼塞尔却为我们指点了安享清福的道理，说唯有懂得安宁真味的人，时机一到，才尝得出战斗的全副滋味。「因为，」他说，「这两个月来我们一直与安宁作对；这种境况短了无妨，拖得太久，必会失去甜味，更糟的是，会把人生酒窖里余下的岁月一并变酸。」

所以他帮了我们一个长久的忙；若没有汉普郡的那一个星期，我想乔治·汉伯里和我谁也定不下心来，多半会挟着钱财浪迹天涯，另寻刺激，舍了实利去追影子，把承继的家业弃如敝屣。

===Original===
As it is, I can now look back upon those seventy days as a man regards some picture, the contemplation of which never fails to bring him infinite delight: for they stand clean out of a quiet, orderly existence and, by the contrast, gain immeasurably.

Their burden is as vivid today as it was that sunshiny morning when we unloaded the toolbox, not far from the London road—the murder of the Englishman, and the quiet contempt of his prophecy that Ellis would come to grief: the level-crossing, and the fierce pounding of my heart as we sat awaiting the train: the courtyard of Wagensburg, and Mansel against the lime-tree with Rose Noble stretched at his feet: the earsplitting crash of the bomb, and Mansel’s steady voice calling the roll: the heat of the closed car’s engine scorching my back: the smell of tanning, and Tester’s menacing bark: Rose Noble’s weight upon me, and his heavy breathing as he set out to take my life: our last, stupendous effort to reach the chamber, and Ellis dead and staring against the bars: and then, our terrible battle with that most jealous of wardens, the great well.

The memory of these things I find as valuable as my share of the treasure itself, and I doubt if ever a man was so well paid for undertaking the care of a masterless dog.

===Chinese===
而如今，我可以回头去看那七十天，像看一幅画，端详它从来不会不叫我心醉：因为它们从一段宁静规整的岁月里截然拔出，相形之下，愈发生辉。

那七十天的分量，今天仍历历如新，一如那个晴朗的早晨，我们在离伦敦大道不远处卸下工具箱的时候——那个英国人的遇害，还有他断言埃利斯要遭殃时人们不置一顾的轻蔑：铁路道口，我们坐在车里等火车时狂擂的心跳：瓦根斯堡的庭院，曼塞尔倚着椴树，罗斯·诺布尔横陈脚下：炸弹震耳欲聋的轰响，曼塞尔沉稳的点名声：封闭车厢里发动机的灼热烤着我的后背：鞣皮的臭气，泰斯特威吓的吠声：罗斯·诺布尔压在我身上的分量，和他下手取我性命时粗重的呼吸：我们冲向石室的最后一搏，以及抵着铁条死不瞑目的埃利斯：然后，是与那位最嫉妒的守卫——大井——的可怕恶战。

这些记忆，我觉得同我分得的那份财宝一样值钱；我疑心，从来没有人因为应承照看一条无主之犬而得过这样的厚报。

===Original===
The latter is with me always, and I think her life is happy. I have called her “Rafter”; for, as the name of a dog, the word does well enough, while it will always mean a great deal to me.

And here I may say that Tester was more fortunate than she, for he never went into quarantine, but, instead, into the toolbox of the second car, to emerge upon Ashdown Forest with, to judge from his spirits, a new lease of life. But, then, he was a hardened smuggler, and had cheated the Customs this way a dozen times.

===Chinese===
它始终伴着我，我想它过得快活。我唤它「椽子」（Rafter）；作为狗名，这个词还算像样，而它对我永远意义深重。

写到这里不妨一提，泰斯特比它运气好，因为他从没进过检疫所，倒是进了第二辆车的工具箱，在阿什当森林（Ashdown Forest）冒出头来时，看那股欢实劲儿，像添了一条新命。不过话说回来，他本是个走私的老手，这条路数骗海关已骗过十几回了。

## VII Rose Noble Moves / VII 罗斯·诺布尔出招

===Original===
There was, naturally, much to be said: but, till Mansel returned to the dungeon, there was nothing to be done. And, since the commentary he made, so soon as he heard my news, was far more valuable than the swarm of conclusions which we had drawn out of the matter, I will set it down, using his words.

“A great deal is depending on how much the tanner saw. If he saw two come in the car, the presence of a third must have told him that we have some hiding-place hereabouts: if he saw the relief carrying timber, that should have made him think: but, if he saw the boat cross the river, he must know a damned sight too much. And it was a moonlit night.

“The point is﻿—what will he do?

===Chinese===
自然，要说的有很多：可在曼塞尔（Mansel）回到地牢之前，什么也做不了。何况他一听完我的消息便发的那番议论，比我们围着这件事掏出来的一大窝结论值钱得多，所以我打算用他的原话，把它记下来。

「制革匠看见了多少，干系极大。他若看见两个人乘车进来，第三个人的露面，必定叫他明白我们这左近有一处藏身的地方：他若看见换班的人搬木料，那也够他动一动脑筋：可他若看见小船渡河，那他知道得就未免太多了。何况那又是个有月亮的夜。

「要紧的是——他下一步会怎么办？

===Original===
“His instinct will be to put the innkeeper wise. But the innkeeper is in balk. He will, therefore, endeavour to communicate with him without the knowledge of the thieves. And the only way to do that is through the inn.

“Now, I think it more than likely that now and again the landlord returns to his inn﻿—*under escort*, of course. Otherwise, long before now, a hue and cry would have been raised. And that wouldn’t suit Rose Noble. About once a week, I imagine, they take him back to the inn: and they give him to understand that, if he gets out of the car, that’s the last living movement he’ll make. Again, he has, without doubt, been straitly advised that upon the first sign of any attempt at his rescue he will immediately die. If they’ve got these things into his head﻿—and, though he can’t speak German, from what I’ve seen of Rose Noble, I should say he was ‘above Babel’﻿—not fifty home-sweet-homes will drag him out of that car; and he probably tells his people that he’s having the time of his life. And that’s the way, I expect, they get their supplies.

“Very well. If the tanner tells the innkeeper, what will the latter do? *I think it more than likely that he will give us away.* I don’t say he’ll do so deliberately: but the tanner’s tidings will startle him, and, unless he’s alone to receive them﻿—and that is most improbable﻿—the thieves will perceive his emotion and demand to be told its cause.

“The first thing to do, therefore, is to visit the inn. If I had known this last night, I’d have gone there today. As it is, we must wait. I imagine that trouble is coming: but, unless the luck is against us, I don’t think it’s coming just yet.”

===Chinese===
「他的本能是给店主递个信。可店主眼下叫人扣住了。因此他会背着盗贼，设法跟他通个气。而这样做的唯一门路，就是客店。

「再者，我想店主隔些日子总要回客店一趟——*自然有人押送*。不然的话，追缉的风声早就大张旗鼓闹开了，那可不称罗斯·诺布尔（Rose Noble）的心意。我猜，他们大约每个礼拜押他回去一回：并且叫他心里有数，只消他敢下车，这就是他在世上最后一个动作。再者，毫无疑问，早有人严严实实告诫过他，图谋搭救的人一动念头，露出一丝风声，他立刻就没命。倘若这些念头都种进了他的脑子——虽说德语他一句不通，可就我见到的罗斯·诺布尔而言，我说他是『巴别塔也拦不住』的角色——那么，任凭唱上五十声『家呀，甜蜜的家』，也休想把他从那辆车里拖出来；他八成还捎话给家里，说他正快活赛神仙。我想，他们的给养就是这么办来的。

「很好。倘若制革匠告诉了店主，后者会怎么办？*我看十有八九，他要把我们卖出去。*我并不是说他存心如此：可制革匠的消息会吓他一跳，而除非他独自一人听信——这几乎不可能——盗贼们必定看得出他的慌乱，非要问个明白不可。

「因此，头一件事就是去客店走一遭。昨夜要是知道这一层，今天我就去了。如今只好等。我料想祸事是躲不掉的：不过只要运气不跟我们作对，我想它眼下还不会来。」

===Original===
And that, for the moment, was as much as Mansel said: but, as later that night I sailed through the sleeping country towards St. Martin, I could not help wondering whether I was not making this now familiar journey for the last time, and, though the next day was fair as a day can be, I had no pleasure in it, and the pastime of fishing seemed to have lost its savour. Yet, I might have spared my concern; for, when I returned to the dungeon, it was to find all well, and throughout the relief we seemed to have the world to ourselves.

That night Mansel and Hanbury visited the track below the combe, but found no sign of water drawn out of the well. When Bell and I were come, we began to take in more wood: by dint of working till cockcrow without a break, we carried all that was left, to our comfort more of mind than of body, for, when it was all underground, we could hardly move.

While we were thus engaged, some fifteen yards of our tunnel started to bulge and had to be shored up before we continued the shaft.

===Chinese===
这话，曼塞尔当下就说到这里：可是那天夜里，我的船驶过沉睡的乡野、往圣马丁（St. Martin）去的时候，我忍不住暗想，这条如今走熟了的路线，会不会是最后一遭；第二天虽然晴得不能再晴，我却兴味索然，连钓鱼这件消遣，也尝不出滋味来。不过这份悬心本是多余的；因为我回到地牢，一切安好，而整个换班期间，这天地仿佛只属于我们。

当夜，曼塞尔和汉伯里（Hanbury）去踏看谷下那条小道，不见井里汲过水的半点痕迹。贝尔（Bell）和我到后，又往里运木料：一口气干到鸡叫，中间不曾停手，把剩下的全部搬完——图的是心安，倒不是身子舒坦，因为都搬进了地下，我们几乎挪不动步了。

正忙着的时候，我们那条坑道有十五码上下的一段顶子鼓了起来，只好先支撑住，才好继续凿竖井。

===Original===
The next day Mansel visited Lerai and ate his lunch at the inn. The landlord’s wife proved only too willing to talk, and the first thing that Mansel learned was that her husband seemed to have thrown in his lot with the thieves, though what in the world they were doing and where they were she could not tell. With that, she had wrung her hands and presently thrown her apron over her head, declaring with tears that, if there was rogues’ company to be had, her lord would be sure to find it, though it lay a day’s journey off: for his mother had been a Roman, and, though she had died at his birth, the blood of lawlessness was in his veins. Then Mansel drew a bow at a venture and, observing that he would have thought that rogues in Carinthia were few, casually spoke of a tanner as the one rustic we had met that had worn the look of a knave. At once the hostess had let out a gush of abuse, avowing that the man and his brother were the black sheep of that part of the land, that her husband knew them well, and had harboured one for a fortnight when a warrant was out for his arrest. *On the last two days*, she added, *the tanner had come to the inn, and, despite her insistence that her husband was away on a journey, had each time stayed for some hours, as though in a hope of his return. But on neither of those two days had the closed car come, as it did from time to time, to take up all manner of food.* And here she fell to raving about the strangers’ score, by now amounting to some seventy English pounds, of which not one penny had been paid, declaring that here was the proof that her husband’s union with the thieves was something sinister, for that he was strict as a bailiff where debts were concerned. “However,” says she in the end, “no wind’s so ill that it blows no scruple of good: for the strangers’ ways may be evil, but the tanner’s are worse; and, at least, my husband is out of some villainy, for, now that the tanner has twice gone empty away, I do not think he will return.”

===Chinese===
次日，曼塞尔去了勒拉（Lerai），在客店吃了午饭。店主的妻子只怕话匣子一开就收不住，曼塞尔头一件打听明白的是：她丈夫像是跟那伙盗贼搅到了一处，可他们究竟在干什么、窝在哪里，她也说不上来。说着说着，她绞起双手，不多时又把围裙兜头一蒙，流着泪赌咒说，天底下只要有个贼窝，哪怕隔着一整天的路程，她那口子也必定寻得着：因为他母亲是吉卜赛女人，虽说生他时就没了，无法无天的血却流在他血管里。于是曼塞尔信手放了一箭：他先说，原以为克恩滕（Carinthia）地界上坏人不多，随后像不经意一般，提起一个制革匠，说那是我们一路遇上的乡下人里，唯一一副无赖嘴脸的。老板娘一听便破口大骂，指天画地，说那人和他兄弟是那一带的害群之马，她丈夫同他们烂熟，官府出票拿人那阵子，还窝藏过其中一个半个月。*她又补上一句：最近两天，制革匠来过店里，任凭她一口咬定丈夫出门在外，回回一坐几个钟头，像还巴望他回来。可这两天，那辆隔些日子来装走各色吃食的封闭汽车，一回也没露面。*说到这里，她又对着外乡人那笔账发作起来，积欠已攒到七十镑英金上下，一个便士也不曾付过；她一口咬定，这正证明她丈夫同盗贼的结交来路不善，因为讲起欠账，他严厉得像个执法吏。「不过，」她末了说，「再坏的风，也刮得来半星好处：外乡人的行径也许不干净，制革匠的更脏；至少，我丈夫总算脱开了某一桩恶事——制革匠两回都空手去了，我看他不会再来了。」

===Original===
Herein we hoped very much that the woman was right: for, if the tanner’s discovery came to the knowledge of the thieves, our valuable system of reliefs would certainly be imperilled, if not destroyed, to say nothing of the fact that they would instantly know that we were driving a shaft.

I have shown already that, since our brush with the tanner, we berthed the car each night at some different place: but Mansel was not satisfied with this precaution, and presently determined that the relief should always begin at eleven o’clock, and that, from ten o’clock onward, one of the four in the dungeon should watch as much as he could of the opposite shore. This may appear to have been an idle exercise, because the sentry was too distant to see or hear any movement which was made in the danger zone: and I must confess that I did not myself account it worth the expense of a workman﻿—for nowadays we laboured to all hours: but Mansel had given the order, and so it was done.

We had always kept in the dungeon a supply of tinned food, sufficient to last us some days: but this stock we now increased, until, if the worst came to pass, we could live for full three weeks without leaving the oubliette.

However, the days went by and no one troubled us: if the tanner watched us again, we never saw him: the soil at the foot of the gutter was sometimes drenched, but never the marsh we had made it: and, while the enemy’s labour was fitful, and they had, I think, no concerted plan of action, except to deny us the well, we continued to drive our shaft forward with all our might.

And here let me say that we were by now the owners of Wagensburg: for Ellis had let go his option, and Mansel had purchased the place. But I doubt if seigniory was ever so strangely enjoyed﻿—the freeholders having their being, like rats, in the bowels of the earth, and in constant dread of their presence being observed, and their enemies in possession and coming and going and doing just as they pleased.

---

===Chinese===
这一层上，我们极盼那女人的话是准的：因为制革匠的发现一旦传进盗贼的耳朵，我们这套宝贵的换班接济之制，纵使不毁，也必定岌岌可危，更不必说他们转眼就会知道，我们正在凿一口竖井。

前文已经交代，自同制革匠照过面之后，我们每晚把车停在不同的地方：可曼塞尔嫌这层防备不够，随即又定下章程：换班总在十一点钟起头；从十点起，地牢里四个人当中，须有一个尽力守望对岸。这看上去像是桩闲差，因为哨位离得远，险区里有什么动静，看也看不见，听也听不见：说实话，我自己也觉着这不值当搭上一个人手——如今我们昼夜不停地做工：可命令是曼塞尔下的，也就照办了。

地牢里向来存着些罐头，足够我们吃上几天：如今这份存粮添了又添，直添到万一最坏的事临头，我们不出囚井一步，也能足足过上三个星期。

然而日子一天天过去，没人来搅扰我们：制革匠若再窥探过，我们从未撞见他：水槽口下的泥土有时湿漉漉的，却从没淹成我们蓄意布下的那片沼泽；敌人那边做工三天打鱼两天晒网，而且除了扼住那口井不让我们沾边，我想他们并没有通盘的章法；我们便使出全力，照旧把竖井往前掘。

这里不妨一提：瓦根斯堡（Wagensburg）如今已归我们名下——埃利斯放弃了优先承购之权，曼塞尔把产业买了下来。只是我怀疑，自古以来可曾有人这样奇怪地享用领主权——持产的业主像耗子一样活在地的肚肠里，时时刻刻怕行藏被人识破；他们的仇敌倒占着宅子，进进出出，随心所欲。

---

===Original===
It was thirteen days to an hour since Tester had bayed the tanner, and Bell and I were engaged at the nose of the shaft, when I heard someone running towards us, and, the next moment, Rowley’s voice. Hanbury desired me, he said, to come to the gallery at once.

Leaving Bell to put out the searchlight and follow me down, I immediately left for the gallery, making what haste I could: but, after the glare of the lamp, I was as good as blind, and the light of the candles which were burning in the shaft and the oubliette, was too feeble to guide my steps.

However, at last I was down, to find Hanbury at the middle embrasure, peering into the night.

===Chinese===
距泰斯特（Tester）冲制革匠发出那阵狂吠，恰好十三天，一个钟头不差；贝尔和我在竖井的掘进面上忙着，忽听得有人朝我们跑来，紧接着是罗利（Rowley）的声音。他传话说，汉伯里先生请我立刻到画廊去。

我留下贝尔熄掉探照灯随后下来，自己立刻动身往画廊去，能多快就多快：可方才灯下强光晃眼，这会儿我跟瞎子差不多，竖井里和囚井里燃着的烛光又太弱，照不见我的脚步。

不过末了总算下来了，只见汉伯里在中间那处窗洞（embrasure）跟前，直往黑夜里窥望。

===Original===
His report was disquieting indeed.

“There’s someone across the river: I saw the flash of a torch.”

I do not think either of us doubted that the fat was now in the fire: for that anyone but one of the thieves would be using an electric torch about the quarry, or, indeed, for twenty miles round, was most improbable.

The time was half past ten; and Mansel was due to arrive at eleven o’clock.

===Chinese===
他的报告实在叫人心慌。

「河对岸有人：我望见火把闪了一下光。」

我想我们俩谁也不疑心大祸已然临头：因为盗贼一伙之外，谁会在采石场左近——说实在的，方圆二十英里——拿着一支电火把，那是断断想不到的。

这时是十点半；曼塞尔定的是十一点到。

===Original===
Now where he would berth the Rolls we could not tell, beyond that it would be at some place within five hundred yards of the spot we had used as a quay: but, since there were but two roads﻿—the one running south from Villach and into the other, which followed the river along﻿—for the thieves to mark her arrival would be most easy; and, once they had seen her stop and her occupants separate, the most maladroit attack could hardly fail.

There was, therefore, but one thing to do, namely, to try to reach Mansel before the Rolls had entered the danger zone; and, since he was always punctual yet never hurried, it was perfectly plain that, if we were to be in time, we had not one moment to lose.

In a twinkling, our plans were laid.

===Chinese===
他把劳斯莱斯（Rolls）停在哪儿，我们说不上来，只知总在我们权充码头的地点五百码之内；可路只有两条——一条打菲拉赫（Villach）往南，接上另一条沿河的路——盗贼要盯住车的到来，真是易如反掌；只消看见她停下、车里的人各奔东西，哪怕顶笨拙的袭击也断不会落空。

因此只有一件事可做：抢在劳斯莱斯闯进险区之前截住曼塞尔；他向来准时，却从不慌忙，若要来得及，显然一分钟也缓不得。

眨眼之间，章程就定了。

===Original===
Hanbury, who was a good runner, was to make the attempt: having swum the river and landed a little downstream, he was to strike across country and head for the Villach road. Bell and I were to follow, but, when we had crossed the river, we should approach the quarry and try to locate the thieves, so that, if Hanbury failed, at least we should be able to offer some other support. Rowley was to be left in charge of the oubliette.

Hanbury was gone in an instant, for he was lightly clad and waited for nothing: but for Bell and me to go unarmed would have been idle, and, since we must wrap up our pistols to save them from getting wet, we stripped and did the same with our clothes, before descending the shoot.

===Chinese===
汉伯里腿脚好，由他去试：泅过河，在下游一点的地方上岸，抄野地直奔菲拉赫大路。贝尔和我随后跟上，过了河，便逼向采石场，想法子摸清盗贼的方位，这样，汉伯里纵然失手，至少我们还能另想接应。罗利留守囚井。

汉伯里转眼就没了影，他衣衫单薄，什么也不等：可贝尔和我赤手空拳去，等于白去；我们把手枪裹好，免得打湿，又把衣裳也照这般裹了，这才下溜槽。

===Original===
We were soon across the water, and, having made the bank at a place where a gurgling brook ran into the river, were able to dress in its gully without much apprehension of being seen or heard.

Indeed, the night itself was an invisible cloak: I never remember darkness so impenetrable: and there was not a breath of air.

I then did my best to consider what manner of ambush the thieves would determine to lay, but could only decide that the track which led to the quarry and the junction of the two roads were, for the moment, the two most probable points: we, therefore, crossed the road and began to move towards the junction, in the hope of hearing some sound which would help us to a better understanding of what was afoot. The track which led to the quarry ran out of the Villach road.

===Chinese===
我们很快过了水，在一条汩汩作响的小溪汇入大河的地方上了岸，便在溪沟里穿衣服，不大担心被人瞧见听见。

说真的，黑夜本身就是一件隐身斗篷：这样密不透风怎么穿的黑，我平生未记：空气也一丝不动。

我随即竭力揣想盗贼会布下什么样的埋伏，却只能断定，通往采石场的小道和两条路的岔口，此刻是两处最可能的地界：于是我们横过大路，朝岔口走去，指望听见点什么声响，好把眼前的事看得更明白些。通往采石场的小道，正是打菲拉赫大路岔出去的。

===Original===
We had come, so far as I could judge, to within fifteen yards of the junction, when I heard the murmur of a voice a little way off. I at once stood still, the better to hear whence it came, but even as I did so, it ceased. After straining my ears in vain, I went cautiously on, to be instantly checked by a rope, stretched breast-high across the road, and stout enough to stop or disable a car that sought to pass by. This discovery shook me, for it showed that the thieves not only knew what to expect, but intended to take no chance of losing their prey. However, I was thankful to have made it: and, since Bell had with him his knife, we immediately severed the rope and let it lie.

It now seemed certain that, if we went on, past the junction, we should come to another rope, for to bar but one of two doors would have been out of reason: it was also equally clear that we had now come to the verge of the danger zone, and that this lay, roughly triangular, about the junction, with its base running directly from rope to rope, and its apex lying somewhere upon the Villach road.

===Chinese===
据我估计，我们走到离岔口十五码上下，忽听得不远处有人低语。我立时站定，好听清来处，可话音偏偏在这时断了。侧耳听了半天，一无所获，我便提着小心再走，登时被一根绳子拦住——绳子横绷在路上，齐胸高，粗得足以拦停或撞毁任何想过路的车。这一发现叫我心里一沉：它说明盗贼不但料得到会来车，而且打定主意，绝不肯放走口中食。不过我倒庆幸自己撞破了它：贝尔带着刀，我们当下把绳子割断，任它躺在地上。

眼下看来，再往前走，过了岔口，必还有一根绳子——两扇门只闩一扇，未免不通情理：同样明白的是，我们已到了险区的边上；这险区大致呈三角形，环着岔口，底边一头接一根绳、一头接另一根绳，笔直拉通，尖顶则落在菲拉赫大路上某处。

===Original===
Since I had no plan of action, to open the road, if we could, seemed plainly the first thing to do: and, with this intent, we picked our way to the river and started to hug its edge, in order to give the junction as wide a berth as we could.

We had reached a point opposite the junction, and, from where I was standing, I could see the pale smear which the Villach road made upon the black of the night, when I felt Bell’s fingers close upon my arm. I suppose his grip was telltale, for, though he breathed no word, in that instant I certainly knew that the worst had happened, and that *Hanbury had lost his race*.

So we stood, still as death, for five seconds: then I heard the brush of a tyre, as it rounded a hairpin bend.

===Chinese===
我原没有行动的章程，那么，能把路打开就先打开路，这再明白不过：于是我们朝河边择路而行，贴着水边走，好把岔口让得远远的。

我们走到正对岔口的地点，从我站的地方，望得见菲拉赫大路在夜色里抹下的那道惨白，就在这时，我觉出贝尔的手指扣住了我的胳膊。想必这一抓便泄了天机：他一言未发，可就在那一瞬，我确实知道，最坏的事出了——*汉伯里输了这一程*。

我们就这样站着，死一样静，足有五秒钟：随后我听见轮胎擦地的一声，正绕过一个发夹弯。

===Original===
I have heard it said that, though the approach of a crisis is apt to scatter the wits, its sudden descent will sometimes whip them back into a battle array. So it was with me at that moment: for, though for a quarter of an hour I had been groping feverishly, my disorder suddenly left me, and I saw as clear as daylight what I must do.

At once I drew my pistol and fired twice across the road.

I thought the consequent din would never die; but at last the echoes faded, and we were able to listen for lesser sounds.

===Chinese===
常听人说，危机将临未临，最能把人的神智搅散；可它一旦劈头砸下，有时倒会把散掉的理智重新整队。那一刻我正是如此：方才一刻钟我还在慌乱中乱摸，忽然间乱劲儿全消，自己该做什么，看得一清二楚。

我登时拔枪，隔着大路连放两响。

我只怕那阵轰响永世不散；好在回声终于淡了，我们才能细听更小的声息。

===Original===
The Rolls had stopped.

Now, though we had ruined the ambush, there was still the devil to pay, for I knew the way was too narrow to let the Rolls go about, and that to reverse in such darkness along so crooked a road was out of the question. But it suddenly came to my mind that if Mansel were to switch on his headlights and run for it towards Lerai, that is to say *turn to the left at the junction*, we should be out of the wood; for the thieves would almost certainly suffer the car to go by in the full expectation that she would be wrecked by the rope. I, therefore, decided to join Mansel as quickly as ever I could and, whispering to Bell to follow, hastened across the foreshore and on to the road.

===Chinese===
劳斯莱斯停了。

大祸虽已拆穿，麻烦却没有完：我知道那条路太窄，劳斯莱斯调不过头来；这样黑的天，在如此曲折的路上倒车，更是休想。可我忽然想起一着：倘若曼塞尔打开车头大灯，朝勒拉一路冲出去——也就是说，*在岔口向左转*——我们便算出了险；因为盗贼多半会放车过去，满心笃定它准叫绳子撞个粉碎。于是我打定主意，要尽快赶到曼塞尔跟前，一面低声叫贝尔跟上，一面抢过河滩，奔上大路。

===Original===
Pitch-dark though it was, I dared not go directly by way of the Villach road, and we had just started back towards Lerai, when I heard the deep breath of an engine, and then the Rolls coming, with the rush of a mighty wind.

Mansel had divined the best course, and was making his dash. And all would be well, *provided he turned to the left. If, at the junction, he turned to the right instead*﻿ ﻿…

I know that for one long moment my heart stood still.

Then, throwing caution to the winds, Bell and I turned and raced for the second rope.

===Chinese===
天黑得伸手不见五指，我也不敢径直走菲拉赫大路；我们刚折回勒拉方向，忽听得发动机深沉的吐息，随即劳斯莱斯到了，来势如一股狂风。

曼塞尔识破了最妙的走法，正在放胆飞驰。只消他向左转，一切便都太平。*可他若在岔口转向右边*……

我知道，有那么长长的一瞬，我的心停住了。

随后，贝尔和我不顾一切，转身朝第二根绳子狂奔。

===Original===
The junction was now bright as day, and the car was so close that, had there been nothing to gain, I should not have crossed its path, but have waited for it to go by. I found myself praying that Mansel would turn to the left. As he swooped at the corner I ran clean into the rope, but the Rolls was round and *coming* before I had opened Bell’s knife. I slashed at the rope like a madman, but before I could cut it right through the car swept by, and, ripping it out of my hand, mercifully snapped asunder such strands as were left.

As we ran in the wake of the Rolls, I heard an ejaculation and then a spurt of high words, but, though I was sure I heard Ellis, I could not distinguish Rose Noble’s masterful voice.

The Rolls had vanished, and I was beginning to wonder whether it would not be wiser to let Bell go on, while I returned to see what the thieves were doing and, if I could, meet Hanbury, when Mansel rose out of the shadows and spoke my name.

===Chinese===
岔口这时亮得如同白昼，车近得吓人，倘若无利可图，我断不肯横穿车道，宁可等它过去。曼塞尔扑向弯口时，我一头扎进了那根绳子；可还没等我打开贝尔的小刀，劳斯莱斯已经转过弯，*直冲过来*。我像个疯子似的朝绳子乱砍，可还没砍透，车已经扫了过去，把绳子从我手里生生夺走，大发慈悲，把余下的几股绷断了。

我们顺着劳斯莱斯的尾迹追跑，听见一声惊呼，随即是一阵高声对骂；埃利斯的声音我听得真切，罗斯·诺布尔那顾指气使的嗓门却分辨不出。

劳斯莱斯没了影。我正盘算是不是该放贝尔独追，自己折回去看盗贼们的动静，可能的话去迎汉伯里——就在这时，曼塞尔从暗影里立起身，叫出了我的名字。

===Original===
I told him my tale.

“I’m much obliged,” he said quietly. “You were up against time and the thieves, and you beat them both. When I am so placed, I hope I shall do as well. I think it likely that Hanbury missed his way: it’s very hard to go straight on a night like this. And that’s why we must get back. Your shots will bring him to the junction, and we don’t want an accident.”

With that, he showed us the boat and the sculls in a ditch, and proposed to take to the water and scull downstream. “For the way of a river,” said he, “is swift and safe and silent, and I was a fool not to have used it before.”

===Chinese===
我把这段经历讲给他听。

「多谢得很，」他不动声色地说，「你是同时跟时间和盗贼赛跑，两样都叫你赢了。哪天我也到了那步田地，但愿做得不输于你。我想多半是汉伯里迷了路：这样的夜，要认直路实在难。所以才必须回去。你的枪声会把他引到岔口来，我们不能出事故。」

说着，他把藏在沟里的船和桨指给我们，提议下水，荡桨顺流而下。「河的路子，」他说，「又快，又稳，又不声不响，早先不用它，我是傻子。」

===Original===
In this he was unfair to himself, for the relief nowadays took up the best part of an hour, four water-journeys having to be made each night: and, if these journeys had been considerably lengthened, we should have been worn out before we could get to bed.

Then he told us that the Rolls was gone, and would not come back that way; but that Carson had orders to be at the culvert at three, and, if no one of us came before four o’clock, to return to Salzburg. Before he went, he would leave a note wedged in the brickwork, to say he was safe, and, when twenty-four hours had gone by, he would come there again. So he would continue to do, until one of us came to meet him or he found a note under the arch.

“And now for Hanbury,” said Mansel: “and then for the oubliette.”

===Chinese===
这话他委屈了自己，因为如今换一回班要占去大半个钟头，一夜须来回渡水四趟：这些路程若再大大拉长，我们还没上床，人就先熬垮了。

随后他说，劳斯莱斯已经走了，不再打这条路回来；卡森（Carson）奉命三点到涵洞，四点前我们谁也没到，他就回萨尔茨堡（Salzburg）。动身之前，他留一张字条塞进砖缝，报个平安；过满二十四个钟头，他再去那里。他会一直这么办，直到我们有人去接他，或是在桥拱底下寻见字条。

「现在去找汉伯里，」曼塞尔说，「然后再回囚井。」

===Original===
We slipped down the river noiselessly, and, when we approached the bend, at a signal from Mansel, Bell, who was rowing, rested upon his oars. We could hear no sound at all, and, after drifting for a moment, Mansel whispered to Bell to head for the shoot.

So thick was the darkness that Bell, not unnaturally, sculled to the side of the stream and, when he could make out the bank, began to follow this down.

We had just passed the slope of the woods and come to the cliff, when a torch was flashed twice from the bank five paces away. Before we could think, came two flashes from the opposite side.

===Chinese===
我们悄没声地顺流而下；将近河湾，曼塞尔一示意，摇桨的贝尔便扣桨浮住。四下里什么也听不见；随水漂了片刻，曼塞尔低声叫贝尔朝溜槽划。

黑得这样浓，贝尔自然把船荡向溪流侧边，等辨出河岸，才顺着岸边往下划。

我们刚过林子的坡脚，来到崖前，五步开外的岸边，火把连闪了两下。我们还没来得及想，对岸又闪了两下。

===Original===
And that was all.

At once Bell lay on his oars: but, after listening intently, Mansel bade him scull for the shoot.

Almost at once we were there, and Mansel put up a hand and rang the bell.

---

===Chinese===
此外再无别的。

贝尔当即停桨；曼塞尔凝神细听片刻，还是叫他往溜槽划。

转眼船就到了，曼塞尔抬手按铃。

---

===Original===
So soon as the flap was lifted:

“Listen, Rowley,” says Mansel, putting his mouth to the shoot. “Is Mr. Hanbury with you?”

“No, sir.”

“Have you anything to report?”

“Two flashes a moment ago, sir: but nothing else.”

===Chinese===
活门刚一掀开：

「听着，罗利，」曼塞尔把嘴凑到溜槽口上说，「汉伯里先生在你那儿吗？」

「不在，先生。」

「有什么要报的吗？」

「刚才闪了两下光，先生：再没有别的。」

===Original===
Mansel turned to me.

“William,” he said, “I don’t think we’re out of the wood. To be perfectly honest, I’m altogether at sea. I know neither what to do nor what to think. But what bothers me most of all is﻿—*where is Rose Noble*? That being so, I’d rather you held the fort. So you go up, and send Rowley down to me. And then we’ll go and find George.”

I did not at all relish the prospect of garrison duty at such a time; for, if trouble was coming, to be mewed up within sound of the skirmish would be well-nigh unbearable.
