But, since I had nothing to plead but my own reluctance, I stripped with what haste I could, and, two minutes later, Rowley had taken my place.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔转向我。

「威廉（William），」他说，「我看咱们还没脱险。老实说，我心里全无主意，做什么、怎么想，都不知道。可最搅得人心神不安的是——*罗斯·诺布尔人呢*？既然这样，我宁愿由你守住营盘。这样吧，你上去，叫罗利下来找我。然后咱们去找乔治（George）。」

这种时候守营当差，我实在无意奉陪；倘若祸事将到，把人圈在听得见厮杀声的窝里，那滋味几乎受不了。可我除了自己不情愿，又拿不出旁的话讲，只好尽快脱去湿衣；两分钟之后，罗利便接了我的位子。

===Original===
The night was so dark and so still that, for a moment, I thought the windows were shuttered, and was stupidly astonished to find the timbers gone: and, when I looked out, I might have been gazing into a bottomless pit. However, I took my seat in the middle embrasure and, cupping my chin in my palm, stared resolutely into the darkness, with my ears pricked to gather the faintest sound. But all I could hear was the regular lap of the water against the face of the cliff.

It had all along been in my mind that Mansel and Hanbury would cross: and, when, but a short five minutes after the former had left, the bell from the shoot announced the latter’s return, I was less surprised than provoked by this well-worn trick of Fortune: for there was Mansel gone out on a sleeveless errand into the midst of some danger he could not read, and that at a time when the last thing we needed to do was to play the spy and everything was to be gained by lying close below ground.

===Chinese===
夜黑得古怪、静得古怪，有一瞬我竟以为窗户上了板，及至发现木板早没了，又呆呆地吃了一惊：朝外望去，简直像瞪着一口无底的深坑。不过我还是在中间那处窗洞里坐定，手托下巴，拿定主意直盯住黑暗，竖起耳朵捕捉最细微的声响。可听来听去，只有水波拍崖，一声一声，规规矩矩。

曼塞尔和汉伯里会碰头，这念头原本一直在我心里；所以，前者出门不过短短五分钟，溜槽的铃就报出后者回来了，命运这套演滥了的老把戏，与其说叫我吃惊，不如说惹我动气：曼塞尔偏偏挑了这时候出门，去办一趟白费腿脚的差事，一头扎进他看不透的什么危险里；而这正是我们最不该装探子、最该贴着地皮伏住的当口，伏得越低，进账越多。

===Original===
However, the milk was spilt: and, since to tread water is a laborious exercise, I lost no time in setting aside the flap and letting the ladder down.

It was immediately clear that George was very near spent, for, when I called to him he had not the breath to answer and when at last he was fairly upon the ladder, he hung there, panting, like a dog on a sultry day. I asked if I should come down, but he took no notice, and, after a little while, he began to ascend. I was alarmed by his demeanour, which was that of the survivor of some catastrophe, and, squatting down at the head of the ladder, reached out my hands to help him out of the shoot.

Now, I had intended to set my hands under his arms, but the moment I touched him he threw an arm round my neck. To save myself from falling I instinctively flung myself back and, since in that instant he heaved, the two of us fell down together on to the flags.

===Chinese===
可牛奶已经泼了；干耗着不动本是桩累人的营生，我马上掀开活门，放下了梯子。

一眼就看得出，乔治几乎耗尽了：我叫他，他连答话的气力都没有；末了好容易整个人扒上梯子，就挂在那儿直喘，像大伏天的狗。我问他要不要我下去，他不理；隔了一小会儿，开始往上爬。他那副神情叫我发慌，活像从哪场大难里捡回一条命的人；我在梯口蹲下，伸手去接他出溜槽。

我原打算把手垫到他腋下，可刚一碰着他，他一条胳膊就勾住了我的脖子。为了免于跌倒，我本能地往后一仰；恰在此刻他猛一挺，我们两个便一齐摔在石板上。

===Original===
“Thanks very much,” said Rose Noble.

Then I felt the mouth of a pistol pressed tight against my throat.

===Chinese===
「多谢抬举，」罗斯·诺布尔说。

接着，我觉得一支手枪的枪口抵紧了我的喉咙。

===Original===
I can never describe the disgust and the horror I felt: but the brain is a curious member, and, as I lay there on the pavement, with the bulk of his body upon me, I could not help thinking that Rose Noble’s ascent of the shoot was a remarkable feat for a man of his corpulent habit, and wondering how he had made it without the support of an oath.

He was lightly clad, but the clothes he was wearing were drenched; and the absurd conceit that I was in the clutches of some aquatic monster was most repugnant. Nevertheless, I had the sense to lie still: and, after a little, Rose Noble got to his knees.

For a moment he fumbled: then the bright eye of a torch illumined the chamber.

===Chinese===
那份恶心，那份惊骇，我永远写不出来：可脑子是件古怪的器官——我躺在石地上，压着他那一大坨身躯，居然还在想，以罗斯·诺布尔那身肥膘，攀上溜槽实在算得一项惊人的业绩，又纳闷他没赌一句咒，这一程是怎么上来的。

他衣衫单薄，穿在身上的却全湿透了；说我落在一头水怪爪牙里，这荒唐的念头实在叫我作呕。尽管如此，我还算明白，只有躺着不动：过了一小会儿，罗斯·诺布尔撑起了双膝。

他摸索了片刻：随后，一支火把亮眼般的光照亮了石室。

===Original===
At this my heart leaped up, for, had he desired to apprise Mansel of his entry, he could not have found a surer or swifter way; for all the three windows were open, and their sudden radiance was bound to publish my plight.

Rose Noble got to his feet and bade me do the same. I was glad to obey. Then he stepped back and looked about him.

“So,” he said, after a while. Then he leaned forward and spoke down the shoot. “Bunch.”

===Chinese===
这一下我的心猛地提起，因为倘若他想把自己进门的消息递给曼塞尔，再也找不出更稳更快的法子：三扇窗全敞着，骤然腾起的光亮，必定会把我的处境昭告出去。

罗斯·诺布尔立起身，叫我也站起来。我巴不得照办。随后他退后一步，环视四周。

「哦，」他隔了片刻说。随后他俯下身，朝溜槽底下喊。「邦奇（Bunch）。」

===Original===
“Hullo,” said Bunch from below.

“You saw me ring that bell?”

“Yes,” said Bunch.

“Fetch Ellis and Job over here. When you’re back, ring three times.”

“Right-o.”

===Chinese===
「喂，」邦奇在底下应道。

「我按铃你看见了？」

「看见了，」邦奇说。

「把埃利斯和乔布叫到这边来。你回来时，按三下铃。」

「得嘞。」

===Original===
Rose Noble straightened his back.

“Pull up that ladder,” he said.

“Not on your life,” said I. “You’ve come to the wrong house.”

I could hardly see him, for the light was full in my face: but I felt his eyes upon mine.

===Chinese===
罗斯·诺布尔挺直了背。

「把梯子收上去，」他说。

「休想，」我说，「你走错门了。」

我几乎看不见他，光正照满我的脸：可我觉出他的眼睛正盯着我的眼睛。

===Original===
At length he took a deep breath.

Then:

“Stand back,” he said, thickly.

I did so, folding my arms.

===Chinese===
末了，他深深吸了口气。

跟着：

「站开，」他瓮声瓮气地说。

我照办，抱起了胳膊。

===Original===
At once he straddled the shoot, and, putting the torch in his teeth, with his free hand felt for the ladder and pulled it up. Then he picked up the flap and sank this into its place.

This show of strength surprised me, for the flap was very heavy, and I would never have believed that a man so fat and flabby could have made light of such a task.

Rose Noble took the torch from his mouth and leaned his back against the wall.

===Chinese===
他随即叉开腿骑上溜槽，把火把衔在牙间，腾出的那只手摸着梯子，把它收了上来。接着他拿起活门，安回原处。

这份力气叫我吃惊：活门沉得很，这样肥胖虚软的一个人，竟能把这么重的活儿做得毫不费力，我断断想不到。

罗斯·诺布尔从牙间取下火把，脊背靠上墙根。

===Original===
“When Ellis comes,” said he, “I shall ask you to show us around. I like to think that you will grant that request.” I said nothing, and after a moment he continued slowly enough. “Nobody likes getting stuck: but, when he’s stuck good and proper, the wise guy swallows his dose. And now, listen to me, you young fool. I’ve taken possession here, *and here I stay*: so long as I stay, Mansel will remain outside: he’s very welcome to get up that drain﻿—if he can; but, short of the ladder, I don’t believe he’ll try. If he wants the well, he can have it: but I rather fancy he’ll be thinking more about you﻿—they say blood’s thicker than water﻿ ﻿… Well, he’s welcome to think. If he asks me, *I haven’t seen you: I found the ladder waiting and came right up*.”

===Chinese===
「等埃利斯回来，」他说，「我要请你带我们各处看看。我愿意相信，这个请求你会赏脸。」我没有作声，隔了片刻，他慢条斯理地接下去。「没有人喜欢受困：可要是被困得结结实实，明白人就把这剂药囫囵吞下。现在听我说，你这小傻瓜。这里我已占了，*我就守在这儿*：只要我守在这儿，曼塞尔就只能待在外头：那道排水沟他尽管去爬——只要爬得上来；不过我料定，少一把梯子，他不会试。井他要是想要，尽管拿去：不过我倒琢磨，他惦念的更是你——人们不是常说，血浓于水……好吧，由他去想。他若问起我，我就说*没见过你：梯子等在那里，我便直接上来了*。」

===Original===
“D’you think he’ll believe you?” said I.

“Why not?” said Rose Noble.

I had no answer to make, for, as I spoke, I saw the force of his words. Here truth was stranger than fiction: and Mansel would never believe that I had handed him in.

===Chinese===
「你以为他会信你？」我说。

「怎么不信？」罗斯·诺布尔说。

我答不上来，因为话一出口，我便掂出他这话的斤两。这里，真事比虚构还奇：曼塞尔决计不会相信，是我亲手把他拉上来的。

===Original===
“And so,” said Rose Noble quietly, “I guess we can count Mansel out.”

“Don’t you believe it,” said I. “He’s made rings round you in the past, and he’ll do it again.”

“Maybe he will,” said Rose Noble. “But unless and until he does, I reckon *it’s up to you*.”

“Indubitably,” said I.

===Chinese===
「这样看来，」罗斯·诺布尔慢悠悠地说，「我看曼塞尔可以不算数了。」

「这话你别信，」我说，「从前他绕着你们团团转，往后还会来一遍。」

「也许会，」罗斯·诺布尔说，「可在他做到之前——只要还没做到——我看*就全看你的了*。」

「毫无疑问，」我说。

===Original===
Rose Noble sighed.

“No fool like a young fool,” he said. Then he leaned forward. “Why put me up, when you haven’t a card in your hand?”

I shrugged my shoulders.

“Much cry, but little wool,” said I. “Come to the point. Are you trying to do a deal?”

===Chinese===
罗斯·诺布尔叹了口气。

「傻子没有比年轻的傻子更傻的，」他说。跟着他探身向前。「你手里连张牌都没有，何必跟我叫板？」

我耸了耸肩。

「雷声大，雨点小，」我说，「说正题吧。你是想做一笔交易？」

===Original===
“Yes,” said Rose Noble. “I am. Maybe it’s a shade one-sided; but you gave it that name.” He jerked his torch at the ramp which led to the oubliette. “I’ve yet to see these diggings; but I haven’t played ‘King o’ the Castle’ for a whole raft of years, and I guess it’ll save us all time if you give me the book of the rules. In return, I offer you a painless captivity, a hell of a lot of hard work, and a free pass-out three days after we touch.”

===Chinese===
「是，」罗斯·诺布尔说，「我是想做。也许偏了一星半点；可『买卖』这名字是你叫的。」他把火把朝通往囚井的坡道一扬。「这坑道我还一眼没看；『山大王』的把戏，我也整整好些年没玩了，我想你把规则手册交给我，咱们彼此都省工夫。作为回礼，我给你一场不遭罪的囚禁、一大堆要命的苦活，还有我们得手三天之后的一纸自由离场凭证。」

===Original===
I laughed.

“It is one-sided,” said I, as cheerfully as I could.

“These sort of deals often are,” was the grim reply.

I could not think what to do.

===Chinese===
我笑了。

「是够偏心的，」我说，装出尽量欢快的样子。

「这类买卖多半如此，」回答冷冰冰的。

我想不出该怎么办。

===Original===
I was unarmed, for I had left my pistol for Rowley when he had taken my place: the other firearms hung upon a wall of the chamber, all of them loaded, but all of them out of my reach. Yet, had they lain by my side, the fellow had me in check; and I knew that, were I to move, he would shoot me down. For all that, my case was nothing to what it must be when Ellis and Job were come. Till then, at least, we were but man to man: but, if Rose Noble was able to bring but one of them up, I was as good as lost: and so was our enterprise. That I must, therefore, take action before they could enter the shoot was very evident, and I saw at once that when he went to admit them was the moment to make my attempt. His torch, of course, was his blessing, and my unspeakable curse: but for this we should have been better matched, for, though he had his pistol, I knew my way about and was, indeed, accustomed to moving in the gallery without any light. I, therefore, determined, something desperately, that, when he stooped for the flap, by hook or by crook I must dash the torch from his mouth, and, if I could not there and then follow up this assault, at least to jump to one side and so out of his ken.

===Chinese===
我身上没有家伙——罗利接替我时，手枪留给了他：其余枪械挂在石室墙上，支支上着膛，却支支够不着。其实枪就搁在身边，这家伙也制得住我；我知道，我只要一动，他就会把我撂倒。尽管如此，我这处境比起埃利斯和乔布到来之后，还算不得什么。至少这会儿，我们还只是单打独斗：可罗斯·诺布尔哪怕只带上来一个，我多半就完了，我们的大业也随之一完。因此，必须赶在他们进溜槽之前动手，这再明白不过；而且我一眼看定：他去开门放他们进来的那一刻，正是我出手的时机。他那支火把，自然是他一人的护符，是我说不出的祸祟：若没这东西，我们本还可算棋逢对手——他固然有枪，可地形我烂熟，在画廊里摸黑走动本是我惯了的。于是我横下一条心，说拼也要拼：等他俯身去够活门，无论如何要把火把从他嘴里打掉；纵使不能当场乘势再扑，至少也要跳向一旁，跳出他的视线。

===Original===
And here it came to me that, so far as his torch was concerned, my luck was indeed dead out; for if ever any one of us went up or down the shoot without saving his torch from the water, whether because he forgot it or took the chance, so surely that torch was useless and would give no glimmer of light until it had been looked to and its battery changed.

So we stood, with the flap between us, I trying to watch Rose Noble behind the glare of the torch, he with his eyes upon mine, and both of us, I fancy, awaiting the throb of the bell.

At length:

“Well,” said Rose Noble.

===Chinese===
这时我才想起，单就那支火把而言，我的运气当真走到了头：我们中间不管谁上下溜槽，只要没把火把护得没沾水——不管是因为忘性发作，还是心存侥幸——那火把保管成了废物，非得侍弄过、换过电池，才肯再漏出半点光。

于是我们就这么站着，中间隔着活门：我竭力想隔着火把的强光盯住罗斯·诺布尔，他眼睛却锁定我的眼睛；两个人，我想，都在等铃响。

末了：

「怎么样，」罗斯·诺布尔说。

===Original===
I moistened my lips.

“There’s nothing doing,” said I. “Of course, you can do me in, but it won’t help you. For one thing, I’m not going to talk: for another, I’ve nothing to say, for﻿—believe me or not, as you please﻿—it’s Mansel and Hanbury together that do the sums. You talk about guys that get stuck: why, you’ve never been anything else. You’ve been stuck at the top of the well﻿—”

“See here,” said Rose Noble thickly, “when I want a —﻿—﻿— lecture, I’ll let you know. Meantime I’ll give you a hunch: and that is, *get right with God*.” The man’s hand was shaking: and I think this angered him, for he let out a frightful oath. “You say you’re not going to talk,” he continued presently. “Well, *there’s your right eye gone*. I don’t set up for a surgeon, but my hand don’t always shake, and an inch of red-hot wire makes a nice, clean job. And, maybe, when you’re short of a light, you’ll find your tongue: it’s not an operation you’ll find in the medical books, but, by —﻿—﻿—, *I’ve known it work*.”

===Chinese===
我润了润嘴唇。

「没得谈，」我说，「你自然可以结果我，可那帮不了你。一来我不打算开口：二来我也没有什么可讲，因为——信不信由你——打算盘的是曼塞尔和汉伯里两个人。你说什么被困住的明白人：嘿，你几时又不是困着的？你困在井口顶上——」

「你听着，」罗斯·诺布尔瓮声瓮气地说，「哪天我想听——的训话，自会通知你。眼下我先给你个提点：那就是，*趁早同上帝和好*。」这人的手在发抖：我想这叫他动了怒，因为他骂出一句可怕的咒。「你说你不打算开口，」他过了一会儿接着说，「好吧，*你的右眼这就没了*。我不自居外科郎中，可我的手也不是老这么抖，一寸烧红的铁丝，做起活来又利索又干净。再说，等你缺了那点亮，自然会找舌头说话：这台手术医书上查不到，可是，凭——起誓，*我见它灵验过*。」

===Original===
I suppose that the monster saw that his words had shocked me﻿—as, indeed, to be honest, they had; for, while they were dreadful enough, the meaning with which he spoke them was unmistakable: at any rate he laughed hideously﻿—the fat laugh of a satyr, that made my blood run cold.

And, as he laughed, the light of his torch went out.

We were both of us taken by surprise: but for this particular accident he, I imagine, was more unready than I, and, though he fired, he was an instant too late, for I had taken my chance and leaped to the right.

===Chinese===
想来这恶棍看出了他的话把我惊着了——老实说，也确实惊着了；因为话已够狠，他咬出这话时的那层意思更是明明白白：总之他狞笑起来——萨堤尔一般肥腻的笑声，听得我血都凉了。

他正笑着，火把的光灭了。

我们两个都吃了一惊：不过单就这场意外而言，我想他比我更没防备；他虽然开了枪，却迟了一瞬，因为我已抓住机会，向右一跃。

===Original===
The crash of the explosion covered what noise I made, and, before the racket was over, I was at the foot of the ramp, straining my ears.

I heard him grope for my body and curse when he found me gone. The next moment he fired again, and the bullet parted my hair. He must have fired at a venture; but so good was the shot that it seemed as though he could see, and I turned and fled up the ramp, like a startled hare.

Now, before I had taken ten steps, I saw the faint glow of the candles alight in the oubliette: these I had clean forgotten, but now in a flash I knew that I could not have made a more unfortunate move, for, having escaped from one light, here I was making my way towards another. To make matters worse, as I perceived my mistake, I missed my footing and fell, making the deuce of a noise: and in an instant I heard Rose Noble behind.

===Chinese===
爆炸的巨响盖过了我的声息，闹声未歇，我已到了坡道脚下，侧耳细听。

我听见他摸索着找我，摸了个空，便破口大骂。紧接着他又开一枪，子弹擦着我的头发过去。他必是瞎放的一枪；可这一枪打得那样准，倒像他看得见似的。我转身便顺坡道往上逃，像只受惊的野兔。

跑不上十步，我便望见囚井里蜡烛的一点微光：这本是我忘了个干净的东西，此刻才恍然明白，再没有比这更倒霉的一步棋——刚逃开一处光，却又朝另一处光走去。更糟的是，我刚看出自己的错处，脚下一滑跌了跤，闹出老大的动静：转瞬之间，罗斯·诺布尔已在身后。

===Original===
There was now nothing for it but to go on up the ramp, for this was far too narrow for me to let him go by: indeed, I fully expected that he would fire again, but I suppose he was minded to husband his shots. I, therefore, went rapidly on and had just decided to make a dash for the candles and put them out, if I could, before Rose Noble could reach the oubliette, when he let out a “Ha” of triumph, which showed me that he had viewed the glimmer ahead.

Now the slightest reflection would have told me that, moving from him to the glow, which was actually framed by the postern at the head of the ramp, I was offering such a target as no marksman could very well miss; and I think he must surely have been upon the edge of bringing me down, when the bell from the shoot was rung. Perhaps he held his hand to hear if it rang again: but, if so, he lost his chance, for I suddenly perceived my folly and fell on my face. As I did so, the bell rang again: and then a third time.

At once Rose Noble began to descend the ramp.

There was but one thing to do, and that was to follow him down. Once Ellis and Job were in, my race was as good as run. So down I went, moving beneath the shelter of the noise that he made﻿—for till you had the way of it the descent of the ramp was a difficult exercise﻿—and racking my brain for some fair opportunity of giving battle.

===Chinese===
这时除了继续顺坡道上，也别无他法，因为坡道太窄，容不得我让他过去：说实话，我满以为他还要开枪，想来他打算省着子弹。于是我一路疾走，刚拿定主意，要抢在罗斯·诺布尔赶到囚井之前扑上去吹灭蜡烛——若能办到——他就发出一声得意的「哈」，这说明前头的微光已入了他的眼。

其实略一动念便该想到，我背着他朝那团光走去，而那光恰好嵌在坡道顶头的边门框里，等于把自己摆成了神枪手也难打脱的靶子；我看他只差一线就要把我撂倒，恰在这当口，溜槽的铃响了。也许他停了手，想听是否还响：若是如此，他便失了机会，因为我蓦地省悟自己的蠢，扑面栽倒。我这一倒，铃又响了：接着第三下。

罗斯·诺布尔立时转身下坡。

只有一条路可走：跟他下坡。埃利斯和乔布一旦进来，我这局棋就算走完了。于是我跟着下坡，躲在他闹出的响动底下挪步——这坡道不摸熟门道，下去本是一桩难事——一面绞尽脑汁，想寻一个像样的交手机会。

===Original===
The rifles and, certainly, two pistols were hanging upon the west wall, that is to say, about three feet from the shoot: we kept them there on purpose that they might be always convenient to anyone leaving the chamber: but, for me, they could not have hung in a more unfortunate place, and I could not think how to reach them without Rose Noble’s knowledge that I was at hand.

Suddenly my heart leaped, and I thought of the wood.

At the eastern end of the gallery lay some timber, for we had not carried it all into the oubliette; and, though a joist was plainly a clumsy weapon to ply within four walls, I was cheered by the thought that it was also a formidable arm and possessed the clear advantage of a considerable range.

===Chinese===
步枪，无疑还有两支手枪，挂在西墙上，也就是说，离溜槽三英尺上下：枪特意挂在那里，图的是谁出石室都取用顺手：可对我而言，枪挂的地方再倒霉不过，我实在想不出，怎样才能在罗斯·诺布尔不知不觉——不知我在近旁——的前提下把它们弄到手。

猛然间我心头一亮，想起了那堆木料。

画廊东头堆着些木料，因为我们没有全搬进囚井；托梁这东西，在四壁之内使唤，分明笨重得很，可它同时是一柄吓人的家伙，又占着一条明摆着的便宜：抡开的半径不小。一想到这一层，我的胆气便壮了。

===Original===
I, therefore, made haste to come down to the foot of the ramp, but, as I gained the chamber, I heard Rose Noble setting aside the flap. How he had been so quick I cannot tell, but I saw in an instant that, if I was to dispute the entrance of Ellis and Job, I had not a second to lose. As I groped for the timber, I heard the ladder go down, and at that moment I touched a case of tinned food. This was open. Without waiting to think, I picked up a tin﻿—what it contained I know not; but I think it was fruit﻿—and hurled it with all my might at where Rose Noble must be. I know that it hit him, for he let out a cry of pain: but, before he could fire, I had flung another tin.

The case was full, and I had discharged five tins before I heard him coming with a volley of oaths. Instantly I whipped to the ramp and, as he went by, darted to the opposite wall. In a moment I had a pistol and, as he fired into the timber, I jerked up the ladder and, thrusting my arm into the shoot itself, fired directly into the water below. That this would discourage Ellis I felt very sure; and, since he had no instructions except to ring the bell, he could hardly be blamed, if, after such a reception, he beat a retreat. But there was still Rose Noble: and I knew very well that, though I had prevented his fellows, I had done so at the price of provoking most deeply the wrath of this terrible man. And, indeed, in a moment I heard a sound which told me that what had gone by was a skirmish and that the battle *à outrance* was just about to begin. I heard the long, heavy breathing of a man who is out to kill.

===Chinese===
于是我赶紧下到坡道脚下，可刚进石室，就听见罗斯·诺布尔掀开活门。他怎么这样快，我说不上来，但我立时明白，要拦住埃利斯和乔布进门，一秒钟也耽搁不得。摸索木料的工夫，我听见梯子放了下去，就在那一刻，我的手碰上了一箱罐头。箱子开着。来不及细想，我抓起一只罐头——里头装的什么，我不知道；我想是水果——朝罗斯·诺布尔必在之处，用尽力气掷了过去。我知道砸中了他，因为他痛叫了一声：可还没容他开枪，我又掷出一只。

箱子里满是罐头，我掷出五只，才听见他骂声连珠，冲了过来。我飞身抢到坡道，趁他从旁掠过，窜到对面墙根。转眼我手里已有一支手枪；他朝木料堆开枪的当口，我猛地拽起梯子，把胳膊探进溜槽口里，朝底下的水里直放枪。这一下准叫埃利斯灰心，我有十足的把握；再说他除按铃之外并没领受别的号令，受了这般招待便退了回去，也怪不得他。可还有罗斯·诺布尔：我心里明镜似的，拦住他的同伙固然是拦住了，代价却是把这位可怕人物的怒火撩到了十分。果然，不多时我听见一个声响，它告诉我：方才那不过是小规模的前哨接触，*决一死战*眼看开场。我听见一个存心取命的人那种悠长、粗重的喘息。

===Original===
I had never heard it before: but it is a sound as fearful as it is expressive; and I must honestly confess that, when I heard it, I was frightened to death. However, I tried to take heart by thinking that the odds were in my favour, for now we both were armed and I was not only the more agile, but knew our surroundings as the palm of my hand.

My first impulse was to fire in that direction from which the breathing came: but then I saw that the flash of my pistol would betray me and that, unless I hit him, before I could move, he would return my shot. And there, of course, he had the advantage of me: for, while I knew little of firearms, beyond that they must be handled with infinite care, Rose Noble gave the impression of being as ready with a pistol as are most men with a pen. Yet, strange as it seems, I found his bullets far less dreadful than his presence itself, and the thought that he must be approaching pricked me to make a move.

The first thing I did was to stumble upon the flap, and at once I heard him move forward towards the shoot. Instinctively, I shrank back, to find myself in the corner of the north and west walls. How I had missed the shoot I do not know, but there it was at my feet: I could feel its current of air.

===Chinese===
这声音我从前没有听见过：可它又吓人，又最能显出人的心思；老实说，听见它的时候，我怕得要死。不过我还是竭力壮胆，想着占便宜的总归是我：如今我们两个都有枪，我不但身手更敏捷，四周的地形更熟得像自己的手掌。

我的头一个冲动是朝喘息来处开枪：可随即看出，枪口的火光会出卖我，而且打不中他，不等我挪步，他的还枪就到。这一层他自然占着我上风：火器我懂得极少，只晓得须得加意小心地侍弄，罗斯·诺布尔却给人以这样的印象：使枪如同大多数人使笔，一样顺手。可是说来也怪，他的子弹在我看来，远不及他这个人本身可怕；一想到他必在步步逼近，便刺得我不能不动。

我头一件事，就是一头撞上活门，随即听见他朝溜槽挪近。我本能地后缩，一缩缩进了北墙与西墙交角的地方。怎么会放过了溜槽，我不知道，可它分明就在脚下：我觉得出它冒上来的一股风。

===Original===
If I did not move now, I was lost﻿—that is to say unless I could kill my man: for I was literally cornered, and, if Rose Noble came on, he would, any moment now, cut off my escape. I, therefore, stole to my left and had come to the first embrasure, some five feet away, when something swept past my chin and, then, lightly touching the wall on my left, sailed horizontally back, missing me this time by a hair’s breadth.

Again I recoiled, thus making my second mistake, for an instant later I knew he had picked up a helve and with this was sweeping the chamber to find me out. The moment the helve had sailed back I should have advanced: but I had let the chance go.

I was now fairly back in my corner, when again I heard the helve touch﻿—this time the wall on my right. This to my horror, for it showed that the cast which Rose Noble was making was almost done and that in four or five seconds the fellow’s terrible instinct would have its reward.

I, therefore, dropped on one knee and set my left hand on the flags, the better to steady my aim: but I knew that the odds were against me, because the moment he touched me he would know where to fire, whilst I could not possibly tell to within some two or three feet.

===Chinese===
此刻若再不动，我就完了——除非我能先撂倒他：因为我实实在在叫逼进了死角，罗斯·诺布尔只要再逼上来，随时会封死我的退路。于是我朝左边悄悄挪，离头一处窗洞还差五英尺光景，忽然有什么东西从我下巴前扫过，轻轻擦上左边的墙，又平平地荡了回去，这一回差之毫厘，没扫着我。

我又退，这就犯下第二个错，因为片刻之后我知道，他拾起了一根锤柄（helve），正抡着它满室横扫，要把我搜出来。锤柄荡回去的当口，我原该抢步上前：可我把机会错过了。

我刚老实退回角落，又听见锤柄触着了什么——这回是我右边的墙。这叫我心惊肉跳：它说明罗斯·诺布尔那一轮横扫行将收尾，再过四五秒，这家伙那骇人的直觉就要讨到彩头了。

于是我单膝跪地，左手按在石板上，好教枪口端得更稳：可我知道形势于我不利，因为他一碰着我，就知道往哪里开枪，我这边却断断估不到两三英尺以内。

===Original===
Rose Noble moved like a cat, giving no sound. Only the helve touched again, this time very close. I could hear the lap of the water and feel the air from the shoot flirting my face. And then, all of a sudden, I saw a bare chance of escape.

The flap when sunk into place, lay flush with the pavement, and so was supported by a ledge running around the shoot, cut out of the stone. This ledge was two inches in width﻿—wide enough to give handhold to a desperate man.

Hardly waiting to pocket my pistol, I entered the shoot, and a moment later I was altogether out of the gallery, yet able to reenter at will, hanging by my hands from the ledge, staring up into the chamber to mark what I could.

===Chinese===
罗斯·诺布尔走动像猫，不落一点声息。只有锤柄又触了一下，这回极近。水波的轻拍入耳，溜槽冒出的风拂在脸上。就在这时，陡然间，我看出了仅存的一线生机。

活门归了位，与地面齐平，四边由一圈凿在石头里的槽沿托住。这圈沿口两英寸宽——宽得足以给一个走投无路的人做抓手。

我几乎顾不上把枪收进兜里，便钻进溜槽；一眨眼，人已整个出了画廊，却又可随意再进——双手攀着槽沿吊着，仰面盯住石室，能看清多少是多少。

===Original===
I heard Rose Noble above me, tapping all around with the helve: I could hear his breathing quite close, and something wet fell from him on to my upturned face. This was blood: so I knew that I must have cut him with one of the tins. Suddenly I heard him stiffen and hold his breath. Then he turned round; and, as in a theatre, by some trick of production to pretend the coming of dawn, you may see the forms of men grow gradually out of the darkness, so *I saw the monster begin to take shape*.

He was standing crouched, with his knees well bent and his left leg a little advanced: his great head was up and his jaw jutted out like a peak: in his left hand he held the helve and in his right was his pistol, pointed and ready to fire: his pistol arm was crooked and steady as any rock.

He had but to drop his eyes for them to light upon me: but he did not, keeping them fixed, instead, upon what I judged must be the foot of the ramp.

===Chinese===
我听见罗斯·诺布尔在我头顶，拿锤柄四下里叩点：他的喘息近在咫尺，还有湿漉漉的什么东西从他身上滴下，正落在我仰起的脸上。那是血：由此可知，必是一只罐头割破了他。猛然间我听见他一僵，屏住了气。接着他转过身来；就像戏台上布景弄出假晨曦，人的身影从黑暗里一点一点显出来，*我看见那怪物渐渐现出了身形*。

他弓着身，两膝弯得很低，左腿稍稍探前：大脑袋昂起，下巴颏像山峰一样突出：左手拄着锤柄，右手握枪，枪口端平，随时要放：持枪的那条胳膊弯着，稳得像块岩石。

他只消一垂眼，就会看见我：可他没有，那双眼睛依旧钉住我揣想必是坡道脚下那处地方。

===Original===
All this I saw at first dimly, but gradually more and more clear, to my great astonishment; for the effect was magical, and I could think of no earthly explanation.

Then in a flash my brain cleared, and I knew it for the work of the searchlight, which Mansel or Hanbury was carrying down the ramp. They must have entered the dungeon by way of the trap and, hearing no sound from the gallery, were hastening thither in a concern for me which could wait upon no risks.

And here was I out of action: and there was Rose Noble, like a fowler, watching a bird come down into his snare.

There was only one thing to be done.

===Chinese===
这一切我起先看得影影绰绰，后来却越来越清楚，惊异不已；这光景像使了魔法，我实在想不出人间的道理。

随即我脑中一亮，明白那是探照灯的光——曼塞尔或汉伯里正举着它下坡道来。他们必是打活板门下了地牢，画廊那边听不见动静，便赶了过来；那份为我悬着的心，再等不得半点风险。

偏偏我在这个当口使不上力：罗斯·诺布尔却像个张网的捕鸟人，眼看鸟儿一头栽进他的网里。

只剩一桩事可做了。

===Original===
“Look out! Rose Noble!” I yelled, and let myself go.

It was a rough passage and cost me a lot of skin: but, of course, I fell down like a stone and was, I suppose, under water before Rose Noble had recovered from his surprise. For he never fired.

A moment later I was swimming downstream.

===Chinese===
「当心！罗斯·诺布尔！」我大喊一声，撒手放了下去。

这一程蹭得够呛，剥掉我不少皮：可我自然像块石头似的直坠下去，想必罗斯·诺布尔还没从惊愕里回过神，我已没进水中。因为他到底没开枪。

转眼我已顺流游开。

===Original===
And that was the end of my adventure; for I had hardly landed upon the castle side, when out of the forest came Rowley and asked me if I was unhurt.

At first I thought I was dreaming, but he said that he had come straight from the staircase-turret, after letting the others into the oubliette. His orders were to put back the slabs, destroy every trace of their removal and then lie close in the woods till break of day. He was then to cross the river, and, if he saw a towel in a window, to come to the shoot.

But I would not wait so long, because I feared that, finding me gone, when he had dispatched Rose Noble, Mansel would go out in my quest. So, since the boat was at hand, we at once sculled back to the shoot and there, sure enough, met Mansel about to go out after Rowley and then after me.

===Chinese===
我的冒险就此收场：因为刚在城堡那一侧上了岸，罗利便打林子里迎出来，问我可曾受伤。

起初我当自己在做梦，他却说，他把众人放进囚井之后，径直从楼梯角楼下来。他的命令是把石板照原样安回去，抹掉挪动过的一切痕迹，然后在林中伏到天亮，再过河去，若望见哪扇窗里挂着毛巾，就到溜槽来。

可我不肯等到那么久，因为我怕曼塞尔见我不见了，料理完罗斯·诺布尔，就会出门来寻我。好在船就在手边，我们当即荡回溜槽，果然在那儿迎见曼塞尔正要出门——先寻罗利，再寻我。

===Original===
“Are you hurt?” said he at once.

“Not a scratch,” said I. “Where’s Rose Noble?”

“Upstream, I think,” said Mansel, climbing into the boat.

Then he bade me go up to the gallery and expect him and Rowley again in ten minutes’ time. When I asked him where he was going, he said, “To bestow the boat.”

===Chinese===
「伤着没有？」他劈头就问。

「毫发无伤，」我说，「罗斯·诺布尔呢？」

「在上游，我想，」曼塞尔说着爬上船。

他叫我上画廊去，十分钟后再等他和罗利。我问他去哪儿，他说：「去把船藏好。」

===Original===
When I was up in the chamber, I was not surprised that he had asked me if I was hurt, for, though there was little disorder, blood was all over the place. We afterwards learned that this came from Rose Noble’s head, which I had cut open﻿—most likely with the first tin I threw. And this shows how tough the man was, for all his grossness: for the tin must have weighed three pounds, and I flung it with all my might.

Hanbury had little to tell, beyond that, as Mansel surmised, he had lost his way in the dark and, horrified at hearing my shots, had made at once for the junction to see what was there toward. There he had found Ellis and Job and the tanner and another he did not know. Ellis was raving about the escape of the Rolls and vowing most shocking vengeance against whosoever it was that had cut the ropes; and the four then appeared to be watching the road and the river, in observance of some design he could not comprehend. He had seen the two flashes from the woods, as also had they; and it was Job who had at once repeated the signal. Not long after that, the windows of the gallery had suddenly been illumined, to Hanbury’s horror and the great surprise of the thieves, who seemed too much dumbfounded to utter a word, though the tanner and his fellow let out at once a flood of excited talk. Then Hanbury had left them, to hasten in fear and trembling down to the water’s edge, where he had run into Mansel, himself hotfoot for the boat.

===Chinese===
回到石室，我明白他为什么问我伤着没有：屋里乱得不算厉害，血却洒得到处都是。后来我们才知道，血出自罗斯·诺布尔的脑袋——是我砸开的，多半就是头一只掷出的罐头。由此也见得这人何等结实，肥归肥：那只罐头足有三磅重，我是用尽力气掷出去的。

汉伯里没什么可说的，只除了正如曼塞尔所料，他在黑暗里迷了路，一听见我的枪声吓了一跳，立刻奔岔口，去看那边出了什么事。到了那儿，他发现埃利斯、乔布、制革匠，还有个他不认识的人。埃利斯正为劳斯莱斯的脱逃急得发狂，指着割断绳索的什么人，赌下骇人的誓要报复；那四个随后像是在监视大路和河面，奉行着某种他参不透的部署。林中那两下闪光他看见了，他们也都看见了；立时把信号重复一遍的，是乔布。过了不久，画廊的窗户突然亮起来，汉伯里吓得魂飞，盗贼们也大为错愕，仿佛惊得一个字也吐不出，制革匠和他那位同伴却立刻迸出一串兴奋的言语。随后汉伯里离开他们，提心吊胆赶到水边，正撞上曼塞尔——他自己也正脚不点地奔船而来。

===Original===
And there, when Mansel returned, he took up the tale.

“Rose Noble was in a boat, moored in midstream. I believe he was there all the time. In his eyes the Rolls was a plum, of which he would have been very glad: but our pithead was the very pie itself. So Ellis was given the job of taking the Rolls: but Rose Noble took on the business of locating and seizing the pithead.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔回来时，便在那里接过了话头。

「罗斯·诺布尔人在一条船上，拴泊在河心。我信他自始至终就在那里。在他眼里，劳斯莱斯是一颗梅子，得之自然欢喜：我们的井口才是馅饼本身。所以截车的事派给了埃利斯：寻访井口、一举拿下的买卖，罗斯·诺布尔留给了自己。

===Original===
“It was Punter, I imagine, that heard us, as we were sculling downstream, and gave the signal which Hanbury saw Job repeat.

“Rose Noble must have been beside us, whilst you, Chandos, went up and Rowley came down. But, being as shrewd as they make ’em, he held his hand.

“So much for speculation.

===Chinese===
「我猜是潘特（Punter）听见了我们顺流荡桨，打出了信号；汉伯里望见乔布把它重复了一遍。

「你上去、罗利下来那阵子，钱多斯，罗斯·诺布尔必是就伏在我们近旁。可他精明到顶，忍住没动手。

「猜测到此为止。

===Original===
“I’ve had some shocks in my life, but the sight of those windows lighted hit me between the eyes.

“We made enough noise embarking to be heard for a furlong or more, but Ellis and Co., I suppose, had no ears to hear. There was no room for Rowley, but he laid hold of the painter and swam behind.

“We missed friend Bunch by inches. I suppose he had lost his direction, for he was sculling downstream. So we came to the shoot. When I saw this was shut, I decided to go for the trap as fast as we could.

===Chinese===
「这辈子我也受过些惊吓，可望见那几扇窗亮着灯，真是结结实实打在我两眼中间。

「我们上船的响动，隔上二百来米开外也该听得见，可埃利斯一伙，我猜，全然充耳不闻。船上挤不下罗利，他便抓住缆索，在后头泅水跟着。

「我们同邦奇老兄擦肩而过，只差几寸。想来他迷了方向，正朝下游荡去。这样我们到了溜槽。见活门关着，我便决计以最快速度奔活板门去。

===Original===
“We dropped downstream and landed below the road of approach. I’m afraid we must have broken all Punter’s ‘marks,’ but that couldn’t be helped. Whilst I was forcing the door of the kitchen-hall, George and Rowley ran to the well for rope. They were back with a coil and a blanket before Bell and I had withdrawn the second slab.

“When we were down, we could hear no sound at all. The light we had seen was gone, and I don’t mind admitting that I was deeply concerned. Now a torch always makes you a target; yet I felt I must have light: so I sent Bell off for the searchlight without more ado.

“What then happened you know. But for your warning, Chandos, Rose Noble must have shot me dead.

===Chinese===
「我们顺流而下，在进庄的大路下方上了岸。只怕潘特布的『记号』叫我们踏破了不少，可这也是没法子的事。我撬厨房大厅那扇门的时候，乔治和罗利奔井边取绳子去了。贝尔和我还没抽开第二块石板，他们已带着一盘绳、一条毯子回来了。

「下到底下，一点声息也听不见。方才望见的光已经没有了，我也不怕招认，我心里着实悬着。火把一举，人就成了靶子；可我觉着非有亮不可：便不再耽搁，打发贝尔取探照灯去了。

「此后的事，你们都知道。钱多斯，若不是你那声示警，罗斯·诺布尔定把我打死了。

===Original===
“As it was, the tables were turned.

“Hanbury sat down in the ramp with the searchlight between his knees: and I took my stand behind him, ready to fire.

“I fancy Rose Noble knew that the game was up.

===Chinese===
「如此一来，情势整个翻了个个儿。

「汉伯里在坡道上坐下，探照灯夹在两膝之间：我立到他身后，持枪待发。

「我猜罗斯·诺布尔明白，这一局完了。

===Original===
“To bring us within his range, he was bound to enter the beam: and the moment he entered the beam: he would be certainly blinded and almost certainly shot.

“By way of rubbing this in, I audibly requested Hanbury to advance a couple of feet.

“A moment later I heard him go down the shoot.”

===Chinese===
「要把我们收进他的射程，他非得踏进那道光不可：而他一踏进光里，必定睁眼全盲，十有八九还得挨一枪。

「为把这层意思敲得更响，我出声请汉伯里朝前挪两英尺。

「片刻之后，我听见他顺着溜槽下去了。」

===Original===
Whilst we were eating some supper, I told my tale over again: but, when Hanbury and I would have discussed the future, Mansel checked us with a yawn.

Then he laughed.

“ ‘Sufficient unto the day is the evil thereof.’ Rose Noble has gone far enough towards murdering sleep. Don’t let us finish it off.”

===Chinese===
吃夜宵的时候，我又把经历讲了一遍：汉伯里和我正要商量前程，曼塞尔一个哈欠把我们止住了。

跟着他笑了。

「『一天的难处一天当就够了。』罗斯·诺布尔糟蹋我们的睡眠，已经作恶多端；可别再由我们自己补上一刀。」

===Original===
Then he told Bell to lay his bed, when he made it, across the flap of the shoot.

“I don’t see how they can lift it,” he added slowly: “but tonight has shown me that I don’t know a risk when I see one, so from now on I’m going to turn every stone.”

And then and there I happened to look at my watch. At first I made sure it had stopped, but, when I had checked it with Hanbury’s, I found it was telling the time. This was five minutes to twelve. And since we had been at supper for half an hour, my passage with Rose Noble cannot have taken up more than ten or twelve minutes of time. And that, but for its proof, I never would have believed.

===Chinese===
随后他吩咐贝尔，铺床时把床横搁在溜槽的活门上。

「我看他们怎么也抬不动它，」他慢慢补上一句，「可今晚教我明白，风险到了眼皮底下我都认不出来；所以打今夜起，每块石头我都要翻上一翻。」

恰好此时我看了看表。起初我断定它停了；跟汉伯里的表一对，才知道它好好走着，指到差五分十二点。晚饭既吃了半个钟头，那么我与罗斯·诺布尔这一场周旋，占去的时间至多十一二分钟。若无此为证，这话我断难相信。

===Original===
Soon the lights were put out, and we lay down to take our rest: but for an hour or more I could not slumber for thinking of those ten or twelve minutes and how close to death I had come.

And here let me say I was sorry for Rose Noble: for, unconscionable villain though he was, he deserved to have won that trick. To enter the gallery, as he did, was a great accomplishment, and, but for the failure of his torch, he must, I think, have brought us all to our knees. Yet, all he got for his valour was a broken head.

===Chinese===
不久灯灭了，我们躺下休息：可一个多钟头里，我只想着那十一二分钟，想着自己离死有多近，翻来覆去睡不着。

这里让我说一句：我为罗斯·诺布尔难过：他虽是丧尽天良的匪徒，那一墩牌本该归他赢。像他那样摸进画廊，是一桩了不起的本事；他的火把若不熄火，我想我们准得个个跪地求饶。然而这份勇烈换来的全部酬报，只是一颗砸破的脑袋。

## VI Tester Gives Tongue / VI 泰斯特报吠

===Original===
We established three bases—one at Salzburg, another at an inn at Villach, and the third at the village at which Mansel and Carson and I had lain on our way to Wagensburg. The name of this village was St. Martin, and it was distant from Lerai some thirty-five miles.

One base would have been enough: but three made our path smooth, for, by their use, curiosity regarding our movements was, so to speak, stillborn, and an ample supply of fresh food was continually assured.

Had we had but one base, the employment of a car *by night only*, the regular disappearance and return of the reliefs into which we were divided, and the constant demand for food to victual six men must have excited comment: but, as I shall show, the use of three bases altogether concealed our business, suggested that our party was consisting of but two men, and argued nothing more eccentric than a zealous interest in exploring the countryside.

===Chinese===
我们设立了三个基地——一个在萨尔茨堡（Salzburg），另一个在菲拉赫（Villach）的一家客栈，第三个设在曼塞尔（Mansel）、卡森（Carson）和我前往瓦根斯堡（Wagensburg）途中歇宿过的那座村子。村子名叫圣马丁（St. Martin），距勒拉（Lerai）约三十五英里。

一个基地原也尽够：可是三个基地让我们一路顺遂，因为借着它们，旁人对我们行踪的好奇可说胎死腹中，充足的新鲜食物也源源不断地有了保障。

倘若我们只有一个基地，一辆汽车*只在夜间*使用、分班轮值的人手定期消失又定期归来、加上要喂饱六张嘴的口粮源源不断，这些必定惹人议论：但正如我下面要说的，三个基地把我们的营务遮掩得滴水不漏，叫人以为我们一伙不过两人，除了一股热心探游乡野的雅兴，再看不出半点古怪。

===Original===
It was early decided that we should labour in pairs and that of every three days each pair should pass forty-eight hours in the dungeon and twenty-four hours abroad. Each pair was to have its own base, to which it alone would repair, and, the second car having been bestowed at Salzburg, only the Rolls would be employed.

Mansel and Carson were to use Salzburg, Hanbury and Rowley, Villach; Bell and I, St. Martin.

By way of illustration, Bell and I would leave St. Martin on a Monday evening, ostensibly to tour the country, taking sufficient food to last us for two full days. That night we would enter the dungeon, and Hanbury and Rowley would leave. Now, two days’ provision for two is one day’s provision for four, so that the food we had brought would feed Mansel, Carson, Bell and myself on Tuesday, and on Tuesday night Hanbury and Rowley would be back with a fresh supply. Upon their arrival, Mansel and Carson would leave, returning on Wednesday night to relieve myself and Bell.

This system worked very well: and I am sure that not one of our respective landlords so much as suspected the tale which, had it had a tongue, the Rolls could have told.

===Chinese===
早就定下，我们两人一组干活；每三天里，每组四十八小时待在地牢，二十四小时待在外头。每组有自己专用的基地，只由本组的人进出；第二辆车寄放在萨尔茨堡，只动用那辆劳斯莱斯（Rolls）。

曼塞尔和卡森用萨尔茨堡，汉伯里（Hanbury）和罗利（Rowley）用菲拉赫；贝尔（Bell）和我用圣马丁。

举例说，星期一傍晚，贝尔和我会离开圣马丁，表面上说是下乡游历，带足够吃两整天的食物。当夜我们进地牢，汉伯里和罗利则动身离开。须知两人两天的口粮，便是四人一天的口粮，所以我们带去的食物，够曼塞尔、卡森、贝尔和我星期二吃；到星期二夜里，汉伯里和罗利带着新的一批回来。他们一到，曼塞尔和卡森便动身，星期三夜里回来接替我和贝尔。

这套制度运转得极好：我确信，我们各自的房东，没有一个疑心到那辆劳斯莱斯倘若长着舌头，能讲出怎样一篇故事。

===Original===
The establishment of the bases, the bestowal of the second car, and the discovery and purchase of a collapsible boat took more than one day to accomplish; and forty-eight hours had gone by before Mansel, Hanbury, Carson and I alighted finally from the Rolls, two hundred yards from the river, and a little to the west of the bend above which the castle stood. The river was flowing from west to east, so we were upstream.

The night was fine, still, and very dark. For the last three miles we had travelled without any lights; but the inhabitants of Carinthia go early to bed, and we had met no one.

A hiding-place for the car was easy to find, and a little old quarry, long abandoned to Nature and approached by a grass-grown track, suited our purpose admirably. And here, in the thick of some bushes, we arranged to conceal the boat.

Then Mansel made much of Tester and set him to guard the car, telling him in so many words that Hanbury would soon be back to take him away, and that he himself would return in two days’ time. I am sure that the dog understood, for all his sprightliness left him, and he whimpered a little, when Mansel turned away. Carson told me later that that was his way; and I can testify to his indifference, when I brought him to meet Hanbury, and Hanbury to his excitement when he brought him to meet his lord.

===Chinese===
设立基地、安置第二辆车、寻购一条可折叠的小船，这些事花去不止一天；等曼塞尔、汉伯里、卡森和我终于从劳斯莱斯上下来，四十八小时已经过去。下车的地点离河两百码，略偏于河湾的西侧，城堡就矗立在河湾上方。河水自西向东流，所以我们是在上游。

那晚天色晴好，无风，也极黑。最后三英里我们熄了灯赶路；好在克恩滕（Carinthia）的居民睡得早，我们一个人也没碰上。

给汽车找藏身之处并不难：一座久已废弃、交还给了天地的老采石场，一条荒草丛生的小路通进去，正合我们的心意。就在这里，在一丛灌木深处，我们预备藏下小船。

随后曼塞尔把泰斯特（Tester）疼爱了一番，派他看守汽车，还明明白白地告诉他，汉伯里不久就回来接他走，他本人两天后也回来。我确信这条狗听懂了：曼塞尔一转身，他满身的欢势便一扫而空，还低低呜咽了几声。卡森后来告诉我，他一向如此；我也可以作证：我带他去见汉伯里，他爱理不理；卡森带他去见主人，他欢腾得不得了。

===Original===
Then we took up the boat and her sculls and the tools and the food we had brought, and went down the river.

This was smooth-flowing, and the current was slight. After a short search we found a convenient place, where a tree overhung deep water and a man could take hold of a branch and lower himself with ease into a boat below. Here it was simple, too, to launch the boat, which was astonishingly light: and Mansel, Carson and I were soon aboard. The boat would hold but three, and since Hanbury and Rowley were to lie that night at Villach, the former stayed where he was.

Three minutes later we were at the mouth of the shoot, and Mansel put up a hand and rang the bell. At once the flap was lifted, and I heard Bell asking if he should show a light.

===Chinese===
于是我们拿起小船和双桨，带上工具和带来的食物，沿河而下。

河水流得平缓，水势不大。没费多少工夫寻找，我们便觅得一处便利的地方：一棵树俯临深水，人攀住一根枝子，便能轻轻松松落到树下的小船里。在这里把船放下水也极省事，那船轻得惊人；转眼之间，曼塞尔、卡森和我已经上了船。船至多坐三个人，汉伯里和罗利当晚要宿在菲拉赫，汉伯里便留在原地。

三分钟后，我们到了溜槽口，曼塞尔抬手按了铃。活板随即掀开，我听见贝尔问要不要点灯。

===Original===
“On no account,” said Mansel. “But let the rope-ladder down.”

It must have been near half-an-hour before we stood in the gallery, for this was, of course, the first time we had used the shoot, and, since, as I have said, a third of its mouth was under water, its passage required some care, unless our clothes and victuals were to be immersed. Indeed, to go up or down we always stripped to the skin. The provisions we saved by the use of a large iron bucket, with a tight-fitting lid: when we had loaded this, we spread it with oiled silk before replacing the lid; so far as I can remember, no water ever circumvented this rude device. Our clothes were raised and lowered in a waterproof sheet. After a while we grew expert, and the relief then came to be done with a soldierly dispatch.

The first thing we did was to give Rowley his instructions and pack him off. Mansel was none too easy at letting him go alone, but he said that he was a swimmer, and knew how to pull an oar, and, since the sculls were so fastened that they could not leave the boat, it seemed certain that he would come safely to the other side.

===Chinese===
「千万别，」曼塞尔说，「把绳梯放下来。」

我们站进画廊时，怕不止过了半个钟头；因为这自然是我们头一回使用溜槽，而正如我说过的，槽口有三分之一没在水面之下，进出须得十分小心，不然衣服和吃食就要泡在水里。说实在的，凡上下溜槽，我们总是脱得一丝不挂。吃食用一只带严盖的大铁桶护着：装满之后，先铺上一层油绸，再合上盖子；就我所记，从来不曾有一滴水破过这粗陋的机关。衣服则用一块防水布吊上吊下。日子一久，我们手法纯熟，换班便办得像军人换岗一样干净利落。

我们办的头一件事，是向罗利交代清楚，打发他上路。让他独自去，曼塞尔实在不很放心；可他说罗利会水，又会划桨，而且双桨拴得牢牢的，断离开不了船，想必他总能安安稳稳到达对岸。

===Original===
“All the same,” said Mansel, “you must give us a sign, and, as I’m afraid to let you show a light, you must take this cord. Fasten it to the painter, and, when you’ve found Mr. Hanbury, cast it off. But, before you do that, tie two knots at its end. So soon as we find it slack, we shall pull it in: and, if the knots are there, we shall know that you’re safe.”

With that, we put out the light and opened the shoot, and, five minutes later, two knots at the end of the cord told us that Rowley and his master had joined forces.

Bell had nothing to report: but he and Rowley had ordered all our stuff so conveniently that, without more ado, we were able to go to bed.

===Chinese===
「话虽如此，」曼塞尔说，「你还是得给我们个信号。我不敢让你亮灯，你就带上这根细绳，拴在船首缆上；找到了汉伯里先生，就把它解开。不过解之前，先在绳头打两个结。绳子一松，我们就往里收：绳头上要是有那两个结，我们就知道你平安无事。」

说罢，我们熄了灯，打开溜槽；五分钟后，绳头的两个结告诉我们，罗利和他的主人已经会合。

贝尔没什么可汇报的：只是他和罗利把我们的东西归置得妥妥帖帖，我们不须多费手脚，便都躺下睡了。

===Original===
The next morning the new work was begun.

Mansel and Carson had bathed before I was awake, and, by the time I was ready, the measurements had been taken, and everything was in train.

Precisely at five o’clock I cut the first stone out of the dungeon wall: and, before half an hour had gone by, we had made a rectangular breach, three feet by nine feet high. To these dimensions we adhered, for, though such a height was uncalled for, it allowed the swing of a pickaxe and so paid for its maintenance many times over. To our relief, the soil behind proved sandy and so easy to work: but this condition convinced us that, as we drove our tunnel, so we must prop its roof and retain its walls with timber against a subsidence or bulge. To do this was simple, but how to procure the timber I could not think: yet Mansel had found out a way within the hour.

Some ten miles away stood a sawmill: and there was plenty of wood such as would suit us well. Of this Mansel proposed to purchase a stack and to drive as hard a bargain as ever he could. He would then require its free delivery to Wagensburg, “at which,” said he, “the miller will certainly kick, for to carry a load of timber up that road of approach, would make Hercules scratch his head. After an argument, we shall come to a compromise. I shall pay nothing for delivery, and he will dump the wood in the quarry where we stable the car. Thence, with a very ill grace, I shall consent to fetch it, as and when it is required.”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，新的工程开始了。

我还没醒，曼塞尔和卡森已经洗过澡；等我收拾停当，尺寸已经量好，一切都已就绪。

五点整，我从地牢的墙上凿下第一块石头；不到半个钟头，我们已经打开一个长方形的缺口，三英尺宽、九英尺高。这个尺寸我们一直恪守，因为九英尺的高度虽非必要，却容得下镐头抡圆，这份便宜往后百倍地偿清了维持它的工本。叫我们宽心的是，墙后的土质松沙，好挖得很：不过这情形也让我们认定，坑道往前推进，顶板必须用木料支撑，两壁也须用木料抵住，以防塌落或鼓胀。做起来不难，难的是木料从哪儿来，我一时想不出；可不出一个钟头，曼塞尔已经有了办法。

十英里开外有一座锯木厂，堆着大把正合我们用的木料。曼塞尔打算买下一垛，把价钱杀到最狠。随后他要求厂主免费送到瓦根斯堡，「到这一步，」他说，「磨坊主一定要跳脚，因为要把一车木料运上那条上堡的路，连赫拉克勒斯也要挠头。争上一番，我们折中解决：运费我一个子儿不付，他把木料卸在我们停车的那座采石场。往后要用的时节，我再老大不情愿地同意自己去取。」

===Original===
And so it fell out: so that in two days’ time we had ready to hand a great store of wood, of which few knew and none, I think, thought anything.

For the time being, however, we were hard put to it to find so much as a makeshift to stay the roof of the shaft: but, after a little, we sawed in pieces some benches, which we had found in the kitchen, and two days before had lowered into the oubliette: with their wood and that of some cases which had contained supplies, we contrived such temporary props as made it safe to proceed.

The soil we displaced we cast into the great dungeon, piling it up by the walls, for, though we must presently empty our winnings out of the shoot, to set ourselves this task before our time would have been unprofitable.

===Chinese===
事情果然如此：不出两天，大批木料已经唾手可得；知道的人寥寥，而据我看，知道了也没谁多想。

不过眼前，我们连一根撑住坑道顶的权宜之木都凑不出来；过了片刻，我们把厨房里寻见的几条长凳锯成几截——两天前已经把它们吊进了囚井（oubliette）——拿凳子的木料，再添上几口装过给养的板箱，凑合着支起些临时撑木，总算可以放心往前干了。

挖出来的土，我们全倒进大地牢，靠着墙堆积；掏出的土石固然迟早要从溜槽运出去，可过早给自己派这桩活计，并不合算。

===Original===
The work went apace, for we were strong men and determined, and laboured faithfully by shifts, so that the most was made of our endurance. There was plenty of air, and the conditions were, I imagine, much more pleasant than such as usually govern work below ground, for the dungeon was cool and spacious, and the second chamber made an admirable lounge.

Although we knew our direction, we were less sure of our relation to the chamber, so far as depth was concerned: but, after consultation, we decided to keep the floor of our shaft fifteen feet above that of the oubliette: for the meadow lay higher than the kitchen by at the least five feet, and, with another nine feet—that is to say, the height of our tunnel—to correct any vertical error, we surely could not go wrong. We, therefore, drove the shaft to this level, and kept it faithfully there up to the very end. By using the compass, we checked our direction as we went, taking the bearing at least twice in the day, to avoid waste of labour.

It had been arranged that Bell and I should leave, when Hanbury and Rowley returned, but our need of timber was so pressing that Carson and Mansel, who alone could deal with the miller, left in our stead. So Bell lay four nights in succession in the second chamber. This order, once taken, it seemed convenient to preserve: and, thereafter, Hanbury always relieved Mansel, Mansel me, and I Hanbury.

===Chinese===
工程进展神速：我们都是健壮的汉子，又下了决心，轮班苦干，恪尽职守，把耐力使到了十分。坑道里空气充足，我想，比起寻常井下作业的条件，这里不知要惬意多少：地牢凉爽宽敞，第二石室更是一间极好的休息室。

方向我们心中有数；只是就深浅而论，与那间石室的相对位置还没有十分把握：大家商议之后，决定让坑道的底面保持在囚井底面以上十五英尺：因为草地至少比厨房高出五英尺，再加上九英尺——也就是坑道自身的高度——容许上下误差，断不会出错。于是我们把坑道掘到这个高度，并且恪守不移，直到最后一刻。一路之上，我们用罗盘校正方向，每天至少测两回方位，免得白费气力。

原本说定，汉伯里和罗利一回来，贝尔和我便换出去；可木料要得急，能跟磨坊主打交道的又只有卡森和曼塞尔，便由他们替我们出去。这样贝尔一连四夜睡在第二石室。这个规矩一经定下，便觉着不妨沿用：此后总是汉伯里换曼塞尔，曼塞尔换我，我换汉伯里。

===Original===
Because his base was at Salzburg, Mansel had much less leisure than Hanbury or I: for not only was Salzburg more distant than either St. Martin or Villach, but such odd things as we needed were better to be found in that town, so that duties of one sort or another were constantly imposing upon his time of rest. But, if ever we pointed this out, he would not listen, maintaining that he did very well and enjoyed any sort of occupation better than idleness.

Of the first day’s labour there is no more to tell than of that of any other; and, since driving a shaft is a dull business, I shall not set down our progress, but only continue—as I have already begun, though something, I fear, at haphazard—to record such details as I think may illumine, as a picture a tale, the dry fact of our long labour; as well our odd doubts and difficulties, and the means we devised to lay them, as the happenings which stand clean out of the next six weeks and have little or nothing to do with our main endeavour: this was, in so many words, to reach the treasure-chamber before the thieves.

---

===Chinese===
曼塞尔的基地设在萨尔茨堡，闲暇便比汉伯里或我都少：萨尔茨堡不但比圣马丁、菲拉赫都远，而且我们需要的零碎物件，也多半要到那城里去寻，于是各式各样的差事接二连三，侵占他歇息的时辰。可我们一提起，他从不肯听，只说他挺好，无论什么差事，总比闲着强。

头一天的活计，同任何别的一天并无两样，没有什么可记的；掘坑道本是桩枯燥的营生，我们的进度我不打算记，只打算继续——我已经开了头，虽然我怕是有些杂乱无章——记下那些我以为能照亮漫长苦役这桩枯燥事实的细节，好比一幅画照亮一个故事；既记下我们古怪的疑虑和困难，以及我们排解它们的种种手段，也记下此后六周里那些全然跳出这桩营生、与我们的正业相干极少甚至毫不相干的事情：说穿了，我们的正业，就是赶在盗贼前头，摸进藏宝的石室。

---

===Original===
Our constant fear was that we should strike rock, for with that we knew very well we could not reckon, and to try to surmount such an obstacle would be work forlorn. Indeed, for a long time, whenever the pick struck a stone which was uncommonly obstinate, our hearts went into our mouths for fear that we had encountered this dreadful enemy: but, though more than once the size and rigidity of some boulder as good as realized our fears, we were spared this terrible blow, and never met anything worse than a long course of clay which later gave way to gravel as suddenly as it had taken its place.

We bathed morning and evening by way of the shoot: and, since we had no means of drying our towels, the relief always brought some fresh ones to carry it through its term. Our linen, of course, we could change when we went to the base. Indeed, all things considered, we were very well found and suffered next to no discomfort. The pavement of the gallery, certainly, made a hard bed: but, after a full day’s labour, I think any one of us could have slumbered upon the stile itself.

Examining this hideous deathbed, while they were yet alone, Rowley and Bell had found two naked poniards lying between the fences and covered with dust. The hilt of one was golden and very well done: the other’s was of silver and plainly made. I think there is little doubt that they had belonged to two victims, who were wearing them when they fell; and that the shock of the falls had shaken them out of their scabbards and down, clear of the bodies which they had lately adorned.

===Chinese===
我们时时刻刻担心撞上岩石，因为大家心里明白，真撞上了便再无计可施，想翻越这样的障碍，不啻一场绝望的挣扎。确实，有好长一段时间，镐头每磕上一块分外顽抗的石头，我们的心就跳到嗓子眼，只怕碰上了这个可怕的冤家：不过，虽说不止一回，某块巨石的块头和顽固几乎把我们的恐惧变成了现实，这当头一棒我们终究躲过了；顶坏也不过是长长一段黏土，而黏土又像它突然取代了先前的东西那样，突然让位给了砾石。

我们早晚都从溜槽下去洗澡：毛巾没有法子晾干，换班的人总带几条新毛巾来，供当值期间够用。换洗的衬衣，自然等回基地时再换。平心而论，我们的一切都齐备得很，几乎没受什么罪。画廊的石板地固然硬得很，可干了一整天活之后，我想我们随便哪一个，就是在梯级上也睡得着。

趁那里只有他们两个，罗利和贝尔查看过那张狰狞的死榻，在铁栏之间寻见两把光秃秃的短剑，蒙着灰尘。一柄的柄是金的，做工极精；另一柄是银的，做得朴素。我想，它们多半属于两名遇害者，遇害时正佩在身上；坠落的一震把它们从鞘里抖了出来，滚落开去，脱离了它们不久前还装点的尸身。

===Original===
Though I never found the work irksome, I enjoyed my nights at St. Martin and my days in the open air. Upon these I had seldom anything to do except to take my ease: for, as I have said, Salzburg could best supply such needs as we had, and Mansel and Carson always cared for the Rolls. By Mansel’s advice, I took to trout-fishing, for that was a quiet engagement and rested body and soul: and many a pleasant hour I passed beside some comfortable stream, gaining more refreshment than fish, of which I took very few, whilst Bell and Tester went rambling somewhere within call, like children let out of school, revelling in the mysteries of wood and meadow, and turning idleness into an enterprise.

The weather was wonderfully fine: though sometimes rain fell, the fall was always heavy and soon past, and I do not remember one day that was overcast or unseasonable.

A storm burst one night whilst the relief was taking place, or, to be more precise, when Mansel and Carson were sculling to the mouth of the shoot. Quick as we were, before they stood in the gallery their clothes were wet through; but, though the downpour was frightful, Bell and I dared not delay, lest the boat should be swamped. I never stripped more reluctantly, and even the river seemed snug beside the nakedness of the boat. The rain lashed my bare skin, whilst I sat waiting for Bell, but, when he came down and laid hold of the gunwale, as usual, to come aboard, of my endeavour to trim the craft, I slipped on the dripping thwart and fell clean into the river, capsizing the boat as I went. Meantime, unaware of this misadventure, those in the gallery let down our clothes with a run, and, believing that we had the bundle, lowered it into the water before they found out their mistake. However, we took no hurt, nor even cold in spite of our thirty-mile drive, which shows, I think, that we were in very good condition.

===Chinese===
活计虽说从不叫我觉得烦腻，我在圣马丁的夜晚和露天里的白天，却过得有滋有味。那些白天我多半无事可做，只管逍遥：因为我说过，我们需要的物件以萨尔茨堡最齐全，劳斯莱斯也总由曼塞尔和卡森照料。听曼塞尔的劝告，我钓起了鳟鱼：这是一桩安静的营生，养身又养心；多少个惬意的时辰，我在某条舒舒服服的溪流边度过，收获的神清气爽多过鱼——鱼我钓得极少；贝尔和泰斯特则在应唤得及的什么地方闲逛，像放学的孩子，在树林和草场的奥秘里乐而忘返，把游手好闲变成了一桩正经事业。

天气好得出奇：虽说偶尔落雨，雨总来得又急又猛，转眼便过；阴沉或反常的日子，我一天也不记得有过。

一天夜里，正逢换班——说得再确切些，曼塞尔和卡森正划着船去溜槽口——一场暴风雨当头炸开。我们动作再快，他们站进画廊之前，衣服已经湿透；可雨势虽吓人，贝尔和我却不敢耽搁，生怕小船灌满。我从不曾那样不情愿地脱过衣服；连河水，比起赤条条的小船来，都算得上安逸。我坐在船里等贝尔，雨水抽打着我赤裸的皮肤；等他下来，照例抓住船舷要上船，我正想把船调平，却在滴水的座板上滑了一跤，一头栽进河里，把船也带翻了。这当儿，画廊里的人不知出了岔子，把我们包衣服的包袱一放手放到了底，只当我们接住了，直到发觉弄错，包袱已经落了水。不过我们毫发无伤，连着三十英里的车程，居然连伤风也没得，足见我们的身子骨实在结实。

===Original===
The searchlight consumed much power; so, when Mansel left for Salzburg, he always took with him one battery and brought back another charged.

Our wireless set afforded us great pleasure. We received the English stations very well and so heard the news every evening, and music, whenever we pleased: but I fancy those that made it little dreamed that their notes were larding the shadows of so sinister a place.

That the dungeon and the chamber made up a grim suite cannot be denied: and, though no one of us said such a thing, I think we could all have spared the grisly memories with which the spot seemed charged. We were too tired to dream: what dreadful matters might else have ridden our slumbers! We laboured upon a scaffold and took our rest in a morgue: we came and went the way dead men had gone—surely, “such stuff as dreams are made on,” ill dreams. But, happily for us, we were too tired to dream. Still, if some tales may be believed, not every weary man has been so favoured. Indeed, if the pitiful dead walk, they must have picked their way between us as we lay in the gallery of nights: but, perhaps because we were so sorry for them, and found their murder so detestable, they had compassion on the strangers within their gates.

===Chinese===
探照灯耗电很凶；所以曼塞尔每次去萨尔茨堡，总带去一箱蓄电池，再带一箱充好电的回来。

那台无线电收音机给了我们极大的快乐。英国的电台收得很清楚，每天晚上听新闻，想听音乐随时可听：只是我猜，奏乐的人做梦也想不到，他们的乐声竟浸润着这么一个凶险地方的暗影。

地牢和石室凑成的一套居所阴森逼人，这无可否认：这话虽说谁也没有出口，我想我们个个都宁可省去这地方仿佛满载的种种可怖的记忆。我们累得不做梦：不然，还不知什么可怕的东西要来骑在梦头上！我们在刑架上做工，在停尸房里歇息：我们出入的路，正是死人走过的路——那当真是「梦所由织成的材料」，而且是恶梦。所幸我们累得不做梦。不过，倘若有些传闻可信，疲惫的人未必个个有这份福分。说真的，倘若可怜的亡魂真的行走，那么夜里我们躺在画廊的时候，他们必定在我们中间拣路而行：不过，也许因为我们十分怜惜他们，觉得他们的被害可憎之极，他们对门内的陌生客倒动了恻隐之心。

===Original===
So the days went by, and we drove the shaft forward, propping it with timber as we went.

---

===Chinese===
日子就这样过去，我们掘着坑道，一路用木料支撑。

---

===Original===
We had laboured for more than a week, before we went out to see how the thieves were faring. We were all impatient to know what things were happening in the enemy’s camp, but; as Mansel said at the time, we knew far better than they that for the moment the treasure was out of their reach, and to gratify pure curiosity at the expense of our work would have been the way of a schoolgirl. “All the same,” he continued, “we mustn’t behave as though they had thrown in their hand: and, when they’ve had time to find out that they are no match for the springs and that *without assistance* they never will lower the water as far as the shaft, that will be the moment at which to begin to watch. If you must know what another is going to do, when he will not tell you and your chances of observation are limited, the best of all ways to find out is to try to assume his outlook and to put yourself in his place. Very well. I may be wrong, but I think, when they’re sick of bailing, they’ll purchase a pump. And, when that proves useless, as it will, they’ll lift up their eyes to the fact that *help they must have*. They won’t like the look of that fact, and they’ll waste quite a lot of good time trying to find a way round: but at last *they’ll accept it*. And that is why it behoves us to drive our tunnel as fast as ever we can.”

On the evening of the tenth day, soon after the sun had set, Mansel and Carson and I went out to see what we could. Hanbury had been warned not to return before midnight, for we did not want our reconnaissance to delay or embarrass the relief.

We swam upstream, keeping close to the cliff, each with his clothes and pistol tied up in a wrap of oiled silk and strapped to the back of his head. Soon the cliff gave way to the steep slope of the woods; and at once Mansel, who was leading, took hold of an overhanging bough and swung himself on to dry land and out of sight. Carson and I followed. An immediate observation of the road beyond the river showed that, so far as we could see, there was no one in sight.

===Chinese===
我们苦干了不止一个星期，才出去探看盗贼们的动静。敌营里究竟在发生什么，我们都急不可耐地想知道；但正如曼塞尔当时说的，财宝眼下还够不着，这一点我们知道得远比他们清楚，而为一时好奇牺牲正业，那是小姑娘的做派。「话虽如此，」他接着说，「咱们可不能装出他们已经撒手认输的样子：等他们有时间弄明白，他们斗不过那些泉水，*没有帮手*，水永远也降不到竖井那么深——到那时，才是开始监视的时候。你非要知道一个人要干什么，他偏不告诉你，而你观察的机会又有限，那么顶好的法子，就是设身处地，拿他的眼光看事情。好。也许我猜错了，可我想，他们桶来桶去抽腻了，就会去买一台泵。等泵证明没用——它准没用——他们就会抬头看见一个事实：*非请帮手不可*。这个事实他们看着碍眼，会白白耗掉不少好光阴，想绕开它走：可到头来*他们只得认了*。正因为如此，咱们必须把坑道掘得越快越好。」

第十天傍晚，日头刚落，曼塞尔、卡森和我便出去探看。汉伯里已经打过招呼，午夜之前不要回来，因为我们不想让侦察耽搁或妨碍换班。

我们逆流而上，贴着崖壁游，各人的衣服和手枪用油绸包好，捆扎在脑后。不久，崖壁换成了树林的陡坡；领头的曼塞尔立刻抓住一根垂下的树枝，一荡上了岸，转眼没了踪影。卡森和我跟着上去。我们随即察看河那头的大路：就目力所及，路上没有人。

===Original===
We judged that, by going straight up, we should come to the sentinel peak: and, so soon as we had put on our clothes, we began to climb up through the forest in single file.

Either our judgment was at fault, or we bore too much to the left, for, after ten minutes’ walking, we saw the walls of the castle a bare thirty paces away. We, therefore, bent to the right and, using extreme caution, made our way under cover to the spot where I had met Bell on the night of the attack on the well.

We were now at the edge of the meadow, and nearly as close to the well as, short of entering the open, a man could come.

The light was fast failing, but as yet we could see well enough.

===Chinese===
我们估量，一直往上走，便能到哨峰（the sentinel peak）；于是穿好衣服，在林中鱼贯而上。

不是我们判断有误，就是偏左太多：走了十来分钟，城堡的墙已在三十步开外赫然在望。我们于是折向右边，十二分小心，遮掩着身子摸到那夜袭击水井时我和贝尔碰头的地方。

此刻我们已在草地边上；若不闯进空地，离那口井再不能更近了。

光线飞快地暗下去，不过眼下的能见度还尽够。

===Original===
The redoubt had been finished and now stood some six feet high about the well. I am sure it was loop-holed, but this did not appear: probably the loopholes were covered, whilst they were out of use. Of this fastness a third part was roofed, with what I could not distinguish, but rafters had plainly been laid from the wall to the cupola of the well. There was, therefore, some shelter from the heaven; but I could not help thinking that our quarters, grim as they were, were a hundred times more secure and comfortable than this wretched abode, which was, indeed, no better than the byre from which it had sprung.

There was a light burning behind the wall, and once or twice we heard voices; but we could see no movement without, and there was no sound of any labour.

Then Mansel bade Carson and me stay where we were, and himself stole forward along the edge of the wood. He was gone some time, and, when he returned, it was dark. There was, he reported, no sentry that he could locate, nor any sign of the car, which he thought was probably abroad. “If I am right,” he added, “there are but three of them here, and to judge from the voices, all those are within the redoubt. We have, therefore, a chance of eavesdropping which may not occur again. And now do exactly as I do: lift up your feet well and mind your step.”

With that, he began to step lightly over the grass, making straight for the covered portion of the redoubt: and, a moment later, we were standing beneath its wall, able distinctly to hear every word that was said.

===Chinese===
壁垒已经完工，围着水井立起约六英尺高。我确信它开了枪眼，只是看不见：多半枪眼不用时都遮掩着。这座堡垒的三分之一盖了顶，盖的什么我分辨不出，但椽木分明是从墙一直搭到井的圆顶上。所以头顶总算有点遮蔽；可我忍不住想，我们的住处虽然阴森，比这可怜的居所却安全舒服百倍——那地方说到底，还不就是生出它的那座牛栏。

墙后点着灯，有一两次我们听见人声；外面却看不见动静，也没有一点干活的声响。

随后曼塞尔吩咐我和卡森留在原地，自己沿着树林边悄悄摸上前去。他去了好一阵，回来时天已黑透。据他侦察，找不出哨兵，也不见汽车的影子；他猜想多半开出去了。「要是我猜得不错，」他又说，「他们这儿只有三个人，听声音，三个人都在壁垒里。所以咱们有机会偷听一回，这机会未必再有。现在，我怎么做，你们怎么做：腿抬高点，脚下留神。」

说着，他放轻脚步踏过草地，直奔壁垒盖顶的那一部分；片刻之后，我们已站在墙根底下，里面说的每一个字都听得清清楚楚。

===Original===
“Live an’ let live, Rose,” said Punter’s voice. “What’s the matter with the pump?”

“That,” said Rose Noble, “is just what I want to know. If the pump’s going to shift the water, why didn’t Big Willie have a pump?”

“ ’Cause he didn’t have time to go an’ get one before we blew in.”

===Chinese===
「井水不犯河水嘛，罗斯，」潘特（Punter）的声音说。「那泵有什么不对劲？」

「这正是我要问的，」罗斯·诺布尔（Rose Noble）说。「泵既然能把水弄走，大威利（Big Willie）当初怎么不弄一台泵？」

「因为咱们炸进来之前，他没工夫去弄一台。」

===Original===
“He’d a day and a half,” said Rose Noble. “And Big Willie’s not the ——— to dig a hole with his fingers, when he can have a spade.”

“A nerror of judgment,” said Job. “That’s wot it was. They ’ad to choose between goin’ an’ gettin’ a pump and ’avin’ a dart with their pails. Directly I see the water, I says, ‘ ’Ere’s room for a pump.’ I know. I ’ad to watch one once.”

“ ‘Watch one’?” said Rose Noble, contemptuously. “What do you mean—‘Watch one’?”

“ ‘Watch one,’ ” repeated Job. “They ’ave to be watched, of course. But they’ll deliver the goods. I tell you, we’ll empty this well in a couple of hours. Wait till you see the ——— a-buzzin’ away.”

===Chinese===
「他有一天半的工夫，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「有铁锹可用，大威利可不是那种———用手指头刨坑的人。」

「判断失着，」乔布（Job）说。「就是这么回事。他们得在去弄一台泵和拿桶碰碰运气之间挑一头。我一见那水，就说：『这儿搁得下一台泵。』我懂的。我从前看过一回泵。」

「『看过一回』？」罗斯·诺布尔轻蔑地说。「什么意思——『看过一回』？」

「『看过一回』，」乔布重复道。「泵嘛，当然得有人看着。可它们保管把活干好。我跟你说，两个钟头咱们就能把这口井弄干。等着瞧那———嗡嗡地转吧。」

===Original===
“My God,” said Rose Noble, brokenly. “He thinks it’s a motor-pump.” Punter let out a guffaw. “You ——— milkmaid, where would we get the power? Where’s the current to drive it? Where’s the plumbers and masons to set it up? I thought you seemed damned anxious to have a pump. Saw yourself ‘watching it,’ I suppose—with a bottle of Bass in your pocket and a fag in your face.”

“But—”

“It’s going to be worked by hand, Job,” continued his relentless comforter. “*Your* hand. You will have to work very hard, pushing a bar to and fro. I think it probable that you will sweat. Yes, I thought that’d faze you, you lazy skunk. There’s a million down in that well, but, rather than work for a week, you’ll let it lie.”

===Chinese===
「我的天，」罗斯·诺布尔有气无力地说。「他当那是台机动泵。」潘特放声大笑。「你这———的挤奶姑娘，咱们上哪儿弄动力去？驱它转的电流在哪儿？安泵的水管工和泥瓦匠又在哪儿？我就说你猴急猴急地非要一台泵。八成还盘算着自己『看着它』——兜里揣一瓶巴斯麦酒，嘴角叼一支烟。」

「可是——」

「那泵要用手摇的，乔布，」这位毫不留情的宽慰者接着说。「用*你的*手。你得拼命地干，把一根杆子推过来、拉过去。我看你八成要出汗。是喽，我就知道这一下能治住你，你这懒胚。那口井底下搁着一百万镑，可你宁可叫它躺着，也不肯干一个星期的活。」

===Original===
“ ‘Lazy’?” screeched Job. “ ‘*Lazy*’? Look at my ——— hands.”

“Oh, I reckon they’re dirty,” said Rose Noble. “You haven’t drawn enough water to rinse them clean.” The other’s protest he scorched with a terrible oath. “Oh, if I’d ‘Holy’ Gordon and two of his lads! They didn’t care where they slept, whiles the job was raw. They’d’ve shifted a ——— river, if it was keeping gold.” He expired violently. “Are you certain sure there’s no one back in the house?”

===Chinese===
「『懒』？」乔布尖声叫道。「『*懒*』？看看我这———双手。」

「哦，我看是够脏的，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「你打上来的水，还不够把它冲干净呢。」对方的分辩，被他一句可怕的咒骂烧了回去。「唉，要是『神圣』戈登（"Holy" Gordon）和他那两个小子在就好了！活儿刚开张那阵子，他们睡觉从来不挑地方。只要金子在里头存得住，他们能把一条———大河都给搬走。」他剧烈地喘着，像断了气似的。「你担保，房那边确实没有人？」

===Original===
“Certain sure,” said Punter. “And every mark’s as it was. There’s no one been through that courtyard for more than a week.”

“Then, what’s their game?” said Rose Noble, half to himself.

“You can search me,” said Punter. “I’ve dreamed about it o’ nights.”

“One thing’s plain,” said the other. “They’re banking upon our failure to lick those springs.”

===Chinese===
「担保没有，」潘特说。「所有的记号都原样没动。一个多星期了，那个院子里没一个人走过。」

「那，他们葫芦里卖的什么药？」罗斯·诺布尔半自言自语地说。

「这可难倒我了，」潘特说。「夜里做梦都惦记这事。」

「有一点是明白的，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「他们指望着咱们降不服那些泉水。」

===Original===
“An’ I don’t blame them,” muttered Job. “I’d back the ——— myself.”

Punter disregarded the gloss.

“An’, when we’ve chucked in our hand, they’re comin’ back.”

“That’s too easy,” said Rose Noble. “Besides, they’re a heap too careful to swallow a risk like that. Big Willie’s not got his feet up. But I’d give a bag of money to know his game.”

===Chinese===
「这也怪不得他们，」乔布咕哝道。「换我，我也押那———一手。」

潘特没理会这句插嘴。

「等咱们撒手认输，他们就回来。」

「这话说得太轻巧了，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「再说，他们精细得很，不会吞下这么大的风险。大威利可还没翘起脚来享福。可要能摸清他的路数，我情愿掏出一袋钱。」

===Original===
Rose, said Punter, “they’re waitin’. What else can they do? They didn’t half like that bomb. So they gave us the well. But they’re watchin’ an’ prayin’ all right, and, as soon as the pump starts suckin’, they’ll show a leg.”

“Maybe they will,” said Rose Noble. “But don’t tell me they’re sitting still. I’ve seen these Willies before, and they’re not that shape. I’d swear they were digging, but where’s their ——— shaft?”

“ ‘Diggin’,’ ” said Job. “My Gawd.”

===Chinese===
「罗斯，」潘特说，「他们等着呢。不等着又能干什么？那颗炸弹够他们受的。所以井就给了咱们。可他们盯着、祷着，这是一定的；泵一开吸，他们就要露头了。」

「也许会，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「可别跟我说他们干坐着。威利家的人（the Willies）我从前见过，他们不是那副德性。我敢起誓他们在挖，可他们的———竖井在哪儿？」

「『挖』，」乔布说。「我的老天。」

===Original===
“Where’s the use?” said Punter. “Say they’ve drove a tunnel as far as the well: you’d want half Lancashire to do it, but say they have. Well, who’s going to pull out a brick? There’s nothin’ the matter with the treasure, but there’s fifty tons of water waitin’ the other side. Talk about a dam burstin’. You don’t have to be an engineer to—”

“There’s a snag somewhere,” said Rose Noble. “I can feel it in my bones. They’re moving: I’ll swear they’re moving: and I think they’re underground. What did they want with the well that afternoon?”

“They wanted the buckets,” said Punter.

“No they didn’t,” said Rose Noble. “If that was all they were after, why wouldn’t young Willie speak? If you want my opinion, those buckets are down in the well. When Mansel had got what he wanted, he just looked around to see what mess he could make: and the buckets came first.”

===Chinese===
「挖了顶什么用？」潘特说。「就说他们挖了条坑道通到井边：那得要半个兰开夏（Lancashire）的人手，可就算他们挖成了。好，谁去拆一块砖？财宝本身没什么不妥，可另一边候着五十吨水呢。还说什么大坝决口。不用当工程师也……」

「其中必有蹊跷，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「我的骨头都觉出来了。他们在动：我敢起誓他们在动：而且我琢磨他们是在地底下。那天下午，他们要那口井干什么？」

「他们要的是那几只吊桶，」潘特说。

「才不是呢，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「要是他们图的就是那个，小威利（young Willie）干吗不肯开腔？依我看，那几只桶就沉在井底下。曼塞尔要的东西一到手，就四下里张望，看能搅出点什么乱子：头一件就是那几只桶。」

===Original===
“You don’t say?” said Punter. And then, “The dirty dog.”

I was aghast at the man’s perspicacity, but Mansel, beside me, began to shake with laughter.

“The point is,” said Rose Noble, “what did Big Willie want?”

No one vouchsafed any answer, and, after a pause, he continued, weighing his words.

“That pup was too blasted glib with his answers about the well. He wasn’t lying, but he talked like a ——— guide. Why? I’ll give you two answers, and you can take your choice. *Either he was giving us a line which they had found N.B.G.: or else he left out of his budget some one essential fact.*” He hesitated there for a moment. Then he went slowly on, speaking as though to himself. “ ‘A chamber,’ he called it: and then, ‘a recess in the wall.’ ”

===Chinese===
「有这话？」潘特说。跟着又道：「这条毒狗。」

这人的眼力叫我暗暗心惊，我身边的曼塞尔却抖着肩膀笑起来。

「要紧的是，」罗斯·诺布尔说，「大威利要的又是什么？」

没有人肯赏一个回答；停了片刻，他斟酌着字眼，接着说下去。

「那小崽子说起那口井，伶牙俐齿得可恶。他没撒谎，可他说得像个———导游。为什么？我给你两个答案，随你挑。*要么，他指给我们的是一条他们已发觉行不通的路子：要么，他那一番话里漏掉了一条顶要紧的事实。*」他迟疑了片刻，随后缓缓说下去，像是对自己说话。「『一间石室』，他是这么称呼的：后来又说，『墙上有个凹龛』。」

===Original===
“That’s right,” said Punter. “You bet it’s a sort of a cell, with a gratin’ to keep the bags from washin’ away.”

“Much more likely walled up,” the other replied. “No point in wet gold, if you can have it dry. And there’s where I’m bogged,” he added violently, “*There’s something below they know of that we can’t guess.* By ——— if I knew where they were.”

“Might help, might not,” said Punter.

“ ‘Might’?” sneered Rose Noble. “ ‘*Might*’?” I could hear him suck in his breath. “Any one of the six would do: Little Willie, for choice. I shouldn’t lose him twice. And I guess he’d see the point of putting us wise. A man mayn’t value his life, but he’s always devilish sticky about parting with one of his skins.”

===Chinese===
「一点不错，」潘特说。「保管是那么一间小牢房似的地方，装上格栅，免得袋子叫水冲走。」

「更可能是砌墙封死了，」对方答道。「能有干的金子，谁要湿的。我的难处就在这儿，」他猛地加上一句，「*底下有件什么东西，他们知道，咱们猜不着。*我要是知道他们在哪儿，———才怪。」

「也许有用，也许没用，」潘特说。

「『也许』？」罗斯·诺布尔冷笑道。「『*也许*』？」我听得见他倒抽一口气。「六个人里随便哪个都成：最好是小威利。我不会让他从我手里溜第二次。我琢磨他也明白，给我们透个信儿是划算的。一个人可以不把自己的命当回事，可要他舍出一层皮来，他总是万般地舍不得。」

===Original===
With his words came the chink of glass, and some liquid was poured.

“Seein’ ’s better than believin’,” said Punter. “Here’s to the ——— pump.”

I suppose the toast was honoured, and, after a moment, somebody rose to his feet.

===Chinese===
随着他的话音，响起玻璃杯的磕碰，有什么液体倒了出去。

「耳听是虚，眼见为实，」潘特说。「敬那———的泵。」

我想这杯酒是干了；过了一会儿，有人立起身来。

===Original===
“You two stay here,” said Rose Noble. “I’m going out for a stroll.”

Had I been alone, I should have run for cover, and, as like as not, had a bullet in my back for my pains. But Mansel stood fast. By his instant direction, Carson and I lay down while he set his back to the wall, sank his chin on his chest—to hide, as he afterwards told me, the white of his face—and folded his arms.

It seemed an age before Rose Noble passed by, for he went very slowly, as a man in his enemy’s camp, and every four or five paces he stood where he was, still as a graven image, using his eyes and ears. Indeed, I shall always believe that the fellow’s instinct had told him that we were at hand, for I never saw demeanour so suspicious, and I think that, but for the light of the lamp with which he had just been sitting, which had taken the edge from his vision, he must have perceived Mansel, for he certainly looked straight at him for two or three seconds at a time.

===Chinese===
「你们两个待在这儿，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「我出去遛遛。」

倘若只有我一个人，我准会拔腿就跑，找地方躲，而且多半还要为此在后背上挨一颗子弹。可曼塞尔纹丝不动。经他一声令下，我和卡森伏在地上，他自己则背靠墙壁，下巴沉到胸口——他后来告诉我，这是为了遮住脸上的白色——双臂交抱。

罗斯·诺布尔从我们身边走过，仿佛隔了一世那么久：他走得极慢，像置身敌营的人，每走四五步便站定不动，像一尊石像，眼观六路，耳听八方。说真的，我要求远相信，这家伙的直觉已告诉他我们就在近旁，因为我从没见过这般疑神疑鬼的行止；我想，若不是他方才一直坐在灯旁，灯光磨钝了他的眼力，他准会看出曼塞尔，因为他分明一连两三秒钟直直地盯着曼塞尔看。

===Original===
At last, however, the darkness swallowed him up, and Mansel lifted his head. For a moment he stood peering: then he signed to me to lie still and began to steal, like a shadow, the way the other had gone. I was taken aback at his movement, for, armed though he was, to go after the monster alone seemed out of reason. And, when I remembered the humour Rose Noble was in, I broke into a sweat.

It was not Mansel’s way to shoot a man in the back, and, since I made sure he was gone to kill Rose Noble, I lay awaiting his challenge with my heart in my mouth. But, after four or five minutes, to my surprise and relief, Mansel emerged from the shadows, as silently as he had gone, and whispered to Carson and me to get to our feet. Then he drew our heads together and spoke in our ears.

“Rose Noble has gone to the castle: while he’s out of the way, I’m going to satisfy Punter that we’re playing a waiting game. Carson will stay here and watch. If Rose Noble returns, shoot him. If he doesn’t, the moment I say ‘Good night,’ make straight for the sentinel peak. Chandos, you come with me, and do as I say.”

===Chinese===
然而黑暗终于把他吞了，曼塞尔抬起头来。他凝神张望了片刻，随即示意我趴着别动，自己像一道影子似的，朝那人去的方向悄然摸去。他这一动叫我吃了一惊：他虽然带着枪，独自去追那头怪物，似乎不合情理。一想到罗斯·诺布尔此刻的火气，我浑身是汗。

曼塞尔的规矩，是不从背后开枪打人；我认定他是去取罗斯·诺布尔的性命，便躺在原地，心提到嗓子眼，等他那声招呼。可过了四五分钟，叫我又惊又喜的是，曼塞尔从暗影里出来了，来时去时一般无声，低声叫我和卡森起来。随后他把我们两个的头拢在一起，贴着耳朵说话。

「罗斯·诺布尔到城堡去了：趁他不在，我要让潘特相信，咱们打的是一场坐等的算盘。卡森留在这儿望风。罗斯·诺布尔要是回来，就开枪打他。要是不回来，我一说『晚安』，你们就直奔哨峰。钱多斯（Chandos），你跟我来，我怎么说，你怎么做。」

===Original===
With that, he walked round the redoubt, with me at his heels. A gap in the wall to the south served as a narrow doorway and suffered the gutter to pass, and, after a careful survey, Mansel stepped lightly within.

“Good evening,” he said. “Put up your hands, please. And, above all, make no sound.”

The prohibition was needless, for Punter and Job were so much dumbfounded that neither of them, I am sure, could have cried out to save his life. Indeed, they sat like two waxworks, staring upon Mansel as though he were an apparition, with their jaws fallen and their eyes bulging out of their heads. Both were unshaven, and Punter was in his socks: a sorry pair of boots was standing on the rim of the well. The covered portion of the fastness was directly opposed to the doorway—above which hung a canvas blind, now hitched to one side—on the farther side of the well: and, since the two were there seated with their backs to the wall, Mansel had stepped to one side to have them in better view.

===Chinese===
说着，他绕壁垒而行，我跟在他脚后。南面墙上有个豁口，权充一道窄门，水槽也从那里通过；曼塞尔仔细察看之后，轻步跨了进去。

「晚上好，」他说。「请把双手举起来。还有，顶要紧的，别出声。」

这道禁令原属多余，潘特和乔布惊得目瞪口呆，我确信，就是喊一嗓子能救命，他们也喊不出来。真的，他们坐在那儿像两尊蜡人，直勾勾地瞪着曼塞尔，仿佛见了鬼，下巴垂着，眼珠子几乎瞪出眼眶。两人都没刮脸，潘特只穿着袜子：一双寒碜的靴子立在井沿上。堡垒盖顶的那部分正对豁口——豁口上方挂着一块帆布帘，此刻挽向一边——在井的那一面：那两人背靠墙坐在那儿，曼塞尔便侧身站开，好把他们看得更真切些。

===Original===
“Put up your hands,” he repeated.

The two obeyed.

Something between them and Mansel stirred in its sleep: and I saw that this was the innkeeper, stretched on the bare ground and tied, like a dog, to one of the pillars of the well.

===Chinese===
「把双手举起来，」他又说了一遍。

两人照办了。

他们和曼塞尔之间，有个什么东西睡梦里动了一下；我看清那是店主，直挺挺躺在光地上，像条狗似的拴在井的一根柱子上。

===Original===
“I’m sorry to intrude,” said Mansel, “but I’ve come all the way from Vichy, and to make such a journey for nothing is not my way. The really annoying thing is I’ve forgotten my measuring-line: but I dare say you can lend me some cord. William, there’s a coil on your left. Just make it fast to that crowbar, and take the depth.”

I instantly fell to work, and a moment later the crowbar was descending the well. While I was thus engaged, Mansel continued to talk.

“You see,” said he, “though I don’t suppose you know it, these springs are intermittent. It’s a well-known thing in Natural History, an intermittent spring. It ebbs and flows, you know, rather like the tides of the sea. Well, we had them on the ebb: and, if you’d got down to it that night you’d have had the treasure. I confess I was rather anxious, but when I took the depth the next day, while you were down in the combe, to my intense relief I found that the flood had begun. Now I shouldn’t tell you all this if the information would do you the slightest good. But it won’t: and I’ll tell you why. *Because you don’t know when to expect the ebb.* That’s where the well-digger’s statement is so valuable. Of course you can find out—by the process of—er—exhaustion.”

===Chinese===
「打扰了，抱歉，」曼塞尔说，「可我大老远从维希（Vichy）赶来，白跑一趟，不合我的规矩。真正叫人生气的是，我把测深绳忘了：不过想必你们能借我一段绳子。威廉，你左手边有一卷。把它拴牢在那根撬棍上，量一量深度。」

我立刻动起手来；转眼间撬棍已经下了井。我忙着量的时候，曼塞尔只管说他的话。

「你们瞧，」他说，「尽管我想你们并不知道，这些泉水是间歇泉。这是博物学上尽人皆知的事，间歇泉。它有涨有落，你们知道，很像海上的潮水。喏，那晚咱们赶上的正是落潮：你们要是那晚下了井，财宝就到手了。我承认我当时很捏一把汗；可第二天，趁你们下到谷底去，我量了量深度，如释重负地发现潮水已经涨起来了。这种事，倘或对你们有半点好处，我是断然不会告诉你们的。可它没有：为什么，我来告诉你们。*因为你们不知道落潮几时来。*这正是那掘井人的供述如此值钱的地方。当然，你们也能弄明白——用——呃——耗干的法子。」

===Original===
Here I touched bottom and began to pull up the bar.

“When it’s up,” said Mansel to me, “extend your arms and measure from tip to tip. You’re just six feet, aren’t you?”

I nodded, and he returned to Punter and Job.

“Of course, to get up against Nature is no end of a job. There’s a proverb you probably know, which is rather in point. ‘You can drive Nature out with a pitchfork, *but she’ll always come back*.’ I know you’re not using a pitchfork: you’re using a bucket instead. But, pitchfork or bucket, as you see, the result is the same. *She always comes back.*”

===Chinese===
说到这儿，我触到了底，开始往上提撬棍。

「提上来之后，」曼塞尔对我说，「把两臂张开，从指尖到指尖量一量。你正是六英尺，是不是？」

我点点头，他便回头去对付潘特和乔布。

「当然，要跟大自然顶牛，可是桩没完没了的差事。有句谚语，你们多半知道，正好用得上：『用草叉把自然赶出去，*她转眼还要回来*。』我知道你们使的不是草叉，使的是吊桶。可草叉也好，吊桶也好，你们看，结果一个样。*她总是要回来的。*」

===Original===
The cheerful tone in which Mansel delivered this dismal homily and the unpleasantly obvious pertinence of his remarks had their effect: for Punter looked ready to burst with mortification, and Job was regarding his second comforter with the fishy stare of one who perceives his fortune to be almost too repugnant to be true. Indeed, I had much ado to keep a straight face, and I dared not look at Mansel, for, if our eyes had met, I am sure the humour of the case would have been too much for us. I, therefore, busied myself with measuring roughly as much of the cord as was wet, and presently reported my finding of fifty-four feet.

“Dear, dear,” said Mansel. “That’s shocking. Why, the well will be full before dawn. You might just as well have rested today. Never mind. There’s nothing like exercise. And I expect you miss Ellis and Bunch.”

That shaft stung Job into speech.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔宣读这番凄惨的训诫，用的是那样欢天喜地的语调，他的话又那样扎耳朵地一语中的，功效立见：潘特满脸羞恼，眼看要胀破了；乔布瞪着这第二位安慰者，眼神死鱼一般，活像个看出自己的命苦得叫人不敢相信的人。说真的，我绷着脸绷得好苦，又不敢看曼塞尔：我们俩的眼睛一对上，这桩事的滑稽准叫我们绷不住。我于是专心致志，量了量那段打湿的绳子大约有多长，随即报出我的结果：五十四英尺。

「哎呀，哎呀，」曼塞尔说。「这可糟了。天不亮，井就要满了。你们今天歇着不干，反正也一样。别灰心。锻炼顶好。对了，你们一定想念埃利斯（Ellis）和邦奇（Bunch）。」

这一戳，把乔布刺得开了口。

===Original===
“ ‘Miss Ellis’?” he groaned. “Oh, good night, nurse!”

I was shaking with laughter, but Mansel only smiled.

“I passed them, coming from Salzburg. To be frank I don’t think they saw me: but they ought to have known the car. And now I must go. I’m sorry to miss Rose Noble, but you must make my excuses and say I was pressed for time. Good night.”

===Chinese===
「『想念埃利斯』？」他呻吟道。「噢，老天爷，饶了我吧！」

我笑得浑身直抖，曼塞尔却只是微微一笑。

「我从萨尔茨堡来，路上碰见了他们。老实说，我想他们没瞧见我：可他们该认得那辆车。好了，我该走了。见不着罗斯·诺布尔，很遗憾；请替我告个罪，就说我抽不开身。晚安。」

===Original===
With that he began to go backwards, and I stepped across the redoubt and out of the gap in its wall. The next second Mansel emerged, and we ran like hares for cover towards the sentinel peak.

“Behind a tree,” breathed Mansel. “They’re certain to fire.”

As I whipped round a trunk, somebody fired from the doorway, but the bullet passed over our heads.

“We must wait for Rose Noble,” said Mansel. “And, while they’re putting him wise, we can go on our way. You can’t fire straight and argue at one and the same time.”

===Chinese===
说着，他倒退着往外走，我则跨过壁垒，从墙上的豁口钻了出去。下一秒钟曼塞尔也出来了，我们像两只野兔，撒腿奔着哨峰的掩体跑去。

「躲到树后头，」曼塞尔低声说。「他们准要开枪。」

我刚闪到一根树干后面，就有人从门口开了一枪，可子弹从我们头顶飞了过去。

「咱们得等罗斯·诺布尔，」曼塞尔说。「趁他们向他报信的工夫，咱们只管赶路。人没法一边打得准，一边讲道理。」

===Original===
Rose Noble must have been returning, when the shot was fired, for we heard his voice almost at once demanding the truth.

Instead of telling him directly that we were at hand in the wood, both Punter and Job began to report what had passed with an incoherence which would have distracted anyone, and in an instant they were all three raving like men possessed. Under shelter of this exhibition, we beat our retreat, and, presently joining Carson, passed over the shoulder of the hill and back the way we had come.

We were well pleased with what we had found, but the head and front of our contentment was naturally furnished by Mansel’s brilliant stroke. Indeed, I cannot believe that the shrewdest of diplomats ever delivered a more dexterous thrust; and, when it is remembered that Mansel’s performance was improvised, that he conceived and executed it with his life, so to speak, in his hand, I think it will be clear that he was a man of lightning apprehension and notable detachment.

===Chinese===
开枪的时候，罗斯·诺布尔想必正在往回走，因为我们几乎立刻就听见他的声音，逼着问个明白。
