潘特和乔布谁也不肯直说我们就在树林里，反而语无伦次地汇报起方才的经过，那份乱七八糟，谁听了都要被搅昏头；转眼间三个人一齐狂喊乱叫，像着了魔。借着这场好戏的掩护，我们安然脱身，不久与卡森会合，翻过山肩，循原路回去。

此行所见，我们十分满意；而满意的头功，自然要归曼塞尔的妙手一着。说实话，我不信最精明的外交家能递出更漂亮的这一剑；想到曼塞尔这一手全是急中生智，是他一手一脚、提着性命——可以这么说——想出来又做出来的，我想谁都看得出，他是个眼光快如闪电、遇事超然出尘的人。

===Original===
By his admirable fable of the intermittent springs he had offered an answer, at once most credible and gloomy, to every one of the problems that troubled the thieves, and had hanged a very millstone about Rose Noble’s neck, for, by confirming the bulk of the suspicions which the latter had openly avowed, he had highly prejudiced such efforts to hearten his fellows as the monster might presently make.

Indeed, as we afterwards learned, for a fortnight after his exploit, next to no water was taken out of the well, but Rose Noble spent all of his time quelling mutinies, eating his own words and striving by hook or by crook to hold the gang together against some future attempt. That in this he succeeded does him, to my mind, great credit, for I have shown many times how ill he was served and what poor stomachs for work or for danger the other thieves had.

And here let me say that, to give the devil his due, Rose Noble possessed a commanding personality, swift, vigilant and unearthly strong: and, behind it, like some familiar, stood his astounding instinct, continually showing him things which no manner of wisdom or prudence could ever have perceived. It seems he never ceased to maintain that we were digging, and that with a conviction so infectious that, although he could produce not one jot or tittle of warrant for what he said, the others came to accept this surmise as an established fact, and, though they would draw no water, to search with more or less diligence for any sign of a shaft. And that was, I think, a remarkable achievement: and, as is sometimes the way, it had its reward.

---

===Chinese===
凭着间歇泉那则绝妙的寓言，他给了搅扰盗贼们的每一个难题一个又最可信又最阴郁的答案，并且把一副千斤磨盘挂上了罗斯·诺布尔的脖子；因为，他把后者公开说出的疑心大半坐实了，那怪物往后无论怎样给同伙打气，都要大打折扣。

说真的，据我们后来得知，他这一手之后的两个星期里，井里几乎没往外打过一桶水；罗斯·诺布尔把全部光阴都用在平息哗变、自食其言、千方百计把这一伙拢在一处，以图他日再举上。他居然办到了，在我看来，这着实是他的大能耐；因为我已经多次写过，他手下的人多么不济事，其余盗贼干活的胃口和临险的胆气又多么可怜。

写到这里，不妨说句公道话：罗斯·诺布尔确有一种摄人的气概，敏捷、警醒、强得近乎超凡；气概背后，仿佛立着一个护身的精灵，就是他那惊人的直觉，不断把无论怎样聪明审慎都看不出的东西指给他看。据说他从不肯放松「我们在挖」这个说法，而且说得那样有感染力：尽管他拿不出一丝一毫的凭据，其余的人却渐渐把这一猜测当成了定案，虽然他们不肯打水，却多少起劲地搜寻起竖井的踪迹来。这在我看来是一桩了不起的成就：而且，世事有时便是如此，它有了它的报偿。

---

===Original===
No one of us will ever forget the ninth night of June.

That day we had driven our shaft as far as the point from which Mansel had taken the bearing of the well, that is to say, we had covered just eighty-one yards: and there we had altered our course so as to head directly for where the chamber must lie. It seemed certain that eighty-nine yards, at the most, remained to be pierced; but we had fair reason to think that the distance would prove to be less, and that, when we had covered another eighty yards, we might expect any moment to strike the chamber. We were, therefore, come roughly halfway to the region we sought: and this elated us all, as did the knowledge that the thieves were making no serious endeavour to empty the well: for, though because of their vigilance, we had been unable again to approach the redoubt, we had twice visited the spot upon which the gutter discharged and found it comparatively dry.

It was Hanbury’s turn to relieve Mansel: the night was warm and moonlit, and there was no wind at all.

===Chinese===
我们中间谁也忘不了六月九日夜。

那一天，我们把坑道掘到了曼塞尔测井方位的那个点上，也就是说，整整八十一码：在那里我们改了方向，直对石室必然所在的地方掘去。剩下的顶多不过八十九码，这一点似乎确定无疑；但我们颇有理由相信，实际距离还要短些，等再掘上八十码，我们随时都可能凿穿石室。这么算来，我们大约已到了目的地的半途：这叫我们个个欢欣鼓舞；盗贼并没有认真抽水，这个消息同样叫人振奋：因为，虽然由于他们戒备森严，我们没能再近壁垒，我们却两次去看过水槽放水的地方，发现那里相当干燥。

这晚轮到汉伯里来换曼塞尔：夜暖，有月，一丝风也没有。

===Original===
The relief was more than half done: Carson, indeed, had already descended the shoot, and Mansel was stripped to the waist, *when we heard Tester give tongue*.

Sharp and clear, across the water came his deep, vigorous bark, bold and menacing.

For an instant we stood breathless, staring at the three long windows, through which the sound had come. Then, in a flash, Mansel had entered the shoot. I followed immediately, clad only in a zephyr and shorts, and was in the boat almost as soon as he. Without a word Carson bent to the sculls, and the craft leaped forward.

Our passage, usually so soon over, seemed that night as though it would never be done, and I well remember remarking how lovely the landscape looked, and what a queer contrast we offered—all of us dripping, and Carson, except for his shoes, mother-naked. All the time Tester was baying someone furiously.

===Chinese===
换班已过大半：卡森确实已经下了溜槽，曼塞尔上衣已褪到腰间，*就在这时，我们听见泰斯特报吠*。

低沉而雄健的吠声隔着水面传来，又尖又亮，勇猛而吓人。

有那么一瞬，我们屏息立定，瞪着三扇长窗，声音正是从那里来的。随即，说时迟那时快，曼塞尔已经跨进溜槽。我紧跟着下去，上身只穿一件薄绒衫，下身一条短裤，几乎与他同时落进船里。卡森一言不发，俯身荡桨，小船箭一般蹿了出去。

平常一会儿就完的水路，那晚像是永没有尽头；我至今记得自己还在心不在焉地想，夜色多美，而我们的模样多么古怪——人人滴着水，卡森除了脚上一双鞋，赤条条一丝不挂。一路之上，泰斯特冲着什么人狂吠不止。

===Original===
We were unarmed, and, as we came to the bank:

“Single file, please,” said Mansel. “We mustn’t bunch. And, when we come to the track, you will bear to the right and Carson to the left.”

I had the painter, and, before I had made fast the boat, Mansel and Carson were ashore and out of my sight.

===Chinese===
我们手无寸铁；快到岸时：

「一个跟着一个，」曼塞尔说。「千万别扎堆。到了那条小路，你往右，卡森往左。」

船首缆在我手里；我还没把船系牢，曼塞尔和卡森已经上了岸，出了我的视线。

===Original===
Now the track which led to the quarry lay seventy paces or so from the river’s bank, some half of which they had run before I was out of the boat. An impression was, therefore, given that we were but two, and I ran straight into some stranger at the mouth of the track. He had clearly let the others go by and was making good his escape. He was a giant of a man, but I am no featherweight, and the shock of our encounter sent us both to the ground. I was up in an instant, but he was quicker than I, and, what was worse, fleeter of foot, for, although I pursued him west for a mile or more, he gradually drew away and I had to give up his pursuit.

I then returned to the quarry, to Mansel’s evident relief; for all he had heard was our fall, and, before he had been able to reach the mouth of the track, the stranger and I were out of earshot. The car did not seem to have been touched, and no one of her locks had been tampered with: Tester was safe and sound: neither Mansel nor Carson had seen or heard anyone, and it seemed likely that the man I had chased had been the sole occasion of Tester’s wrath. Particularly to describe him, however, was beyond my power, for we had met in the shadows, and I had not seen his face. He was not one of the thieves nor yet the innkeeper; and, unless they had been reinforced, there was little to suggest that he had to do with the enemy, or, indeed, was anything more to be feared than an inquisitive peasant who had noticed George Hanbury’s arrival and found his behaviour strange. As such we found him unwelcome, but, if he was to see us no more, harmless. Yet, there was about the business one curious, disquieting fact, slight in its way as any ghost, yet one that we could not lay.

The fellow was a tanner by trade. That I could swear, for he reeked of the tannery. Now, I have always found an odour more reminiscent than even a melody, and I have but to become aware of some perfume to remember directly the circumstances in which I have smelt it before. Yet, though, the moment I scented the fellow, I knew I had encountered his like or his occupation quite recently, for the life of me I could not recall when or where it had been, and no artifice of Mansel’s could bring it back to my mind. In the ordinary way we should have dismissed the matter, but we could not help feeling that, if here was a clue to the interloper’s identity, it would be highly imprudent to cast it away; and, for myself, I was puzzled, for it was unlike my memory, having got so far, to be able to get no further. At last, however, we set aside the riddle, for there was work to be done, and, as Mansel said with reason, “sometimes the brain is mulish, and will do better if you put up your cudgel and let it be.”

===Chinese===
通往采石场的小路离河岸七十步上下；我还没下船，他们已经跑了一半。这样，敌人得下的印象是我们只有两人；果然，在小路口，我迎面撞上一个生人。他分明是放旁人过去，自己好乘机脱身。他是个大块头，可我也不是好相与的，两人一撞，双双倒地。我一跃而起，可他比我快，更糟的是，腿比我快：我朝西追了一英里多，他还是渐渐拉开，我只好放弃追赶。

随后我回到采石场，曼塞尔明显松了口气；原来他只听见我们扑倒的声响，等他赶到小路口，那生人早已带着我出了听觉之外。汽车似乎没动过，锁一把也没人碰过：泰斯特安然无恙：曼塞尔和卡森谁也没见着、也没听见什么人，看来我追的那个人就是惹泰斯特发怒的唯一缘由。可是，要描摹他的相貌，我就无能为力了：我们是在暗影里相撞的，我没看见他的脸。他不是盗贼之一，也不是店主；除非盗贼添了人手，几乎没有迹象表明他与敌人有干系，说到底，他至多不过是个好奇的乡下人，瞧见乔治·汉伯里来了，又觉得他行止古怪罢了。这样的人，我们自然嫌他不速；可只要他不再见到我们，也就无害。然而这桩事里有一节古怪、叫人心神不定的实情，轻飘得像一缕鬼气，我们却怎么也镇它不住。

那家伙是个制革匠，行当错不了：他浑身一股鞣皮坊的气味。我一向觉得，气味比曲调更勾起回忆，只要嗅到一点熟悉的香气，我立刻就会想起从前在什么境况下闻到过它。可怪就怪在这里：那家伙一近身，我就知道不久前遇见过同类或同行，可是拚了命也想不起是在什么时候、什么地方，曼塞尔百般设法，也勾不回我的记忆。照平常，我们早把这事撇下了；可我们总觉得，倘若这正是那闯入者身份的一条线索，随手丢弃未免太不明智；而我自己更是纳闷：我的记性从来不这样——明明追到了门口，偏偏寸步难行。不过末了，我们还是把这道哑谜搁下，因为活儿等着人干；曼塞尔说得有理：「脑子这东西，有时就是头骡子；你把棍子一撂，随它去，它反倒想得出来。」

===Original===
Our policy had been always to take no avoidable risk, and, since if the car had been discovered, our store of timber also had been remarked, we at once decided to get as much of this as the cover of night would allow into the oubliette. As a rule, the relief had brought enough wood to last us for twenty-four hours, and this much had been delivered by Hanbury and Rowley that night; but, since without timber to advance the shaft would be at best a very perilous business, to transfer every balk that we could seemed but a natural precaution.

We, therefore, set about this removal without more ado. Rowley was brought to help us, while Hanbury and Bell stayed to receive the wood. We laboured with all our might till an hour before dawn, by which time we had carried a great deal, though not, it appeared, so much as we had already employed.

We made no attempt to find a fresh hiding-place for the car; for, for one thing, we had no mind to leave her any more unattended, and, for another, it seemed better in future to berth her each night at a different point, so that, if our visit was expected, at least we should not be playing clean into some peeper’s hands. The boat and sculls we decided to bestow in a culvert some four miles away, for they were nothing to carry, and, to take them up, as we passed, would be but a moment’s work.

===Chinese===
我们的一贯方针，是不冒可免之险；既然汽车一旦暴露，我们存的木料也必定入了眼，我们当即决定，趁夜色掩护，能搬多少木料就搬多少，统统运进囚井。往常换班带来的木料，够我们用二十四小时；那晚汉伯里和罗利也送来了这一份；可没有木料，掘坑道顶多是一场极危险的买卖，能多搬一根方木是一根，算不得过分的谨慎。

于是我们立刻动手搬运。罗利被叫来帮我们，汉伯里和贝尔留在那头接收木料。我们拼尽全力，干到离天亮还剩一个钟头；到那时已搬了许许多多，只是看来还不及已经用掉的。

给汽车另找藏身之处的事，我们没做：一来我们不愿意让它再多落单，二来往后不如每晚换个泊处，这样，纵然真有人算准了我们的来头，至少也不至于把现成的把柄送进哪个窥视者的手里。小船和双桨，我们决定寄放在四英里外的一条涵洞里：那点分量不算什么，路过时顺手取走，不过一眨眼的工夫。

===Original===
The sky was pale when Rowley and I stood again in the gallery, and before Mansel could come to Salzburg, we knew that the sun would be high: but the smell of the timber all about us did our hearts good, and we lay down to sleep for an hour in the comfortable state of a garrison whose store of munitions is no longer without the fort.

Now, whether it was this reflection or the scent of the sawn wood that jogged my memory I cannot tell: but my mind whipped back to the tanner, and in a twinkling I remembered when and where I had noticed his particular odour before. And that put an end to my slumber before it was ever begun, and to that of the others as well: for, though I had clean forgotten it, I now recalled perfectly that *I had become aware of the smell of tan at one and the same instant that I was felled from behind on that night of alarms and excursions when Mansel was down in the well*.

===Chinese===
罗利和我重新站进画廊时，天已泛白；我们知道，不等曼塞尔从萨尔茨堡赶回来，日头就要升高了：可四下里木料的气味提着我们的精神，我们躺下睡了一个钟头，心里踏实，像一支弹药不再屯在堡外的守军。

至于究竟是这份盘算，还是锯开的木料气味触动了记忆，我说不上来：总之我的心一下子蹿回到那制革匠身上，一眨眼，我便想起了从前在什么时候、什么地方闻到过他那股特别的气味。这一来，觉还没睡就算完了，旁人的觉也叫惊散了：因为，先前我虽忘得一干二净，此刻却记得分毫不差——*曼塞尔下到井里的那个警报频传、奔走不休的夜晚，我从背后被击倒在地的那一刻，同一瞬间，我闻到了鞣皮的气味*。

## IV The Attack on the Well / IV 猛攻水井

===Original===
During the night the water rose twenty feet.

It went against the grain to post two sentries, when with twelve, or even ten arms we might have had the well empty by four o’clock. But to be surprised at such gruelling labour would have meant for us the end of everything; for, shaken and stripped and breathless, we could have put up no fight, and, except for the parapet of the well, there was no cover to hand.

Still, it seemed very likely that four of us, working hard, would be able to draw so much water before the sun went down that, with the help of the stage, we should find the mouth of the shaft. And, once we knew on which side of the well that lay, though the springs should deny us passage, at least we should have a second string to our bow.

With this object, we laboured till lunchtime, like men possessed—at least, Mansel and the servants laboured, whilst I sat above on the peak with a rifle across my knees. Hanbury was at the shrine.

===Chinese===
一夜之间，水涨了二十英尺。

本来只消十二个人手——哪怕十个人——卖力干到四点钟，兴许就能把井淘空；如今却要分出两个人放哨，这实在叫人于心不甘。可这样的苦役一旦遭人偷袭，我们便万事皆休；因为那时我们筋疲力尽、手无寸铁、气喘吁吁，根本无力还手，而且除了井口的护墙，近旁再无遮蔽可依。

不过，我们四个人若拼命地干，似乎很可能赶在日头落山之前汲出足够的水，再借助平台，找到竖井的井口。而一旦知道竖井开在井的哪一侧，纵然泉水不许我们通行，我们至少也多了一条后路。

怀着这个目的，我们像着了魔似的一直干到午饭时分——至少曼塞尔（Mansel）和仆人们是这样；我则端着步枪坐在上头的山巅。汉伯里（Hanbury）守在神龛那里。

===Original===
At one Rowley took my place, and Carson Hanbury’s.

This relief had not long been done, and we were at lunch in the meadow, when—as Hanbury had told us would happen, for he had seen him en route—the landlord of the inn arrived.

As he emerged from the wood, he looked around him, and, when he saw us at meat, he threw up his hands.

I made sure, as did Mansel, that Rose Noble was dead, but it soon appeared that the fellow was only distressed to find that our zeal to attempt to empty the well was unabated.

“Sirs,” he cried, bubbling, “it is dreadful to see you so bent upon so hopeless a task. You are killing yourselves in vain. I have sought everywhere for helpers, but, when I tell them for what their help is required, they laugh me to scorn. You will not get a man in all Carinthia. And the trout lie in the streams about you, thick as autumn leaves.”

===Chinese===
一点钟，罗利（Rowley）来换我下岗，卡森（Carson）换下汉伯里。

换岗完毕不久，我们正在草地上吃午饭，客栈的老板到了——汉伯里事先说过他会来，因为他半路上撞见过他。

他从树林里钻出来，四下张望，一见我们正用饭，便高高扬起了双手。

我和曼塞尔都认定罗斯·诺布尔（Rose Noble）已经死了；可很快便看出，这家伙不过是痛心地发现，我们淘空水井的热劲丝毫未减。

「诸位，」他嚷道，唾沫四溅，「看你们这般死心塌地做这桩没指望的事，真叫人心惊。你们这是白白送命哪。我四处找帮手，可一听说要帮什么忙，人人都笑话我。整个克恩滕（Carinthia）休想雇到一个人。而你们身边的溪流里，鳟鱼密得跟秋天的落叶一样。」

===Original===
Mansel laughed.

“The springs are the devil,” he said; “but we haven’t given up hope.”

“Ah, sir,” said the innkeeper, “but listen. You have not begun. You are still dealing with the first spring. When you are twenty feet down—”

“Make it forty,” said Mansel. “To be exact, forty-three.”

Lest he should think we were boasting, we took him to the well and showed him the measuring-line: his amazement was ludicrous, for he seemed unable to speak and gaped upon us as though we were demigods; and such in his sight, I suppose, we were, for in three days we had gone far to shatter a tradition which had endured for a century and a half.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔笑了。

「泉水真见鬼，」他说，「可我们还没死心。」

「唉，先生，」店主说，「可您听我说。你们压根还没开头呢。你们对付的还只是头一道泉。等你们下去二十英尺——」

「就说四十吧，」曼塞尔说，「说准确点，四十三。」

唯恐他当我们吹牛，我们把他领到井边，把测深绳拿给他看：他那副惊愕相简直滑稽，话都说不出来，只管张着嘴瞪我们，仿佛我们是什么半神；想来在他眼里，我们也确是半神，因为短短三天，我们已差不多把一个延续了一百五十年的老传统砸了个粉碎。

===Original===
Then Mansel asked him of his guests. Of these he spoke abstractedly, for his mind was full of what we had done with the well; but we learned that Rose Noble was recovered, and that Ellis had come back this morning and taken the three away—he knew not whither, but imagined to Salzburg. They had paid him nothing for their lodging, of which they had constantly complained, but had made him understand that they would come back. What more he said I cannot remember, save that the stores we had ordered were piled by the kitchen door, and after a little he left, still plainly bewildered by the progress which we had made.

I was for instantly withdrawing the two sentinels and making a mighty effort to get the treasure that night, but to this Mansel would not consent, for fear that Rose Noble and Ellis were not gone at all, but had pretended departure in the hope that the news would reach us and throw us off our guard. He suggested, instead, that Hanbury and I should only work for two hours, and should then relieve Carson and Rowley, “for in that way,” he said, “at sundown Carson and Rowley will be done and can go to bed, but you and Hanbury will be as fresh as paint: and, if Bell and I go gently this afternoon, we shall still have something to spare at nine o’clock.”

To Hanbury and me this arrangement seemed more than good, except that we both begged Mansel to give himself a rest. But of this he would not hear. For the next two hours, therefore, we worked as hard as we could, but the honours of that day went to Carson and Rowley, for, while I sat on my hilltop, I could hear them at work, and, knowing how severe was the labour, I would not have believed that two men could maintain the pace they did for nearly five hours.

===Chinese===
接着曼塞尔问起他的房客。他答得心不在焉，满脑子还是我们在井上的作为；不过我们得知，罗斯·诺布尔已经复原，埃利斯（Ellis）今早回来了，把那三个人接走了——去哪儿他不知道，猜想是去了萨尔茨堡（Salzburg）。他们住宿没付一个钱，还一直嫌这嫌那，只让他明白他们还会回来。他后来还说了些什么，我记不清了，只记得我们订的给养都堆在厨房门口；他待了一会儿便走了，仍是一脸茫然，被我们的进展弄得晕头转向。

我主张立刻撤回那两个哨兵，当天夜里就大干一场，把财宝弄到手；可曼塞尔不肯，他怕罗斯·诺布尔和埃利斯压根没走，只是佯装离去，指望消息传到我们耳朵里，好叫我们松懈大意。他转而提议，我和汉伯里只干两个钟头，然后去换卡森和罗利，「这样，」他说，「日落时卡森和罗利就能收工去睡觉，你和汉伯里却还精神十足；再说，今天下午我和贝尔省着点力气干，到九点钟还能有余力。」

在我和汉伯里看来，这安排再好不过，只是我们俩都求曼塞尔也歇一歇。可他根本不听。于是接下来两个钟头，我们拼尽全力地干；但那天的头功应归卡森和罗利，因为我坐在山头上，能听见他们干活的动静，深知那活计何等吃重，实在不敢相信两个人能照那样的速度一口气撑上将近五个钟头。

===Original===
When I came in at sundown, the two were ready to drop, but they looked very pleased, and Mansel told me with a smile that they had uncovered the shaft.

Half an hour back, he said, the suck and gurgle of air disputing with water had told its tale, and, though to ascend at once might be impossible, enough of the mouth was open for us to survey the shaft.

Then Hanbury and I made haste to fetch the stage, and Carson and Rowley were ordered back to the house. They were reluctant to go, but Mansel was determined that they should have food and rest, “and, if you stay here,” said he, “you will have neither, but will drift into helping us when you’re too tired to help yourselves. It’s possible that we may need you in two hours’ time; so go and eat and sleep, while you have the chance.”

Bell and Tester went with them, for Bell was to keep watch and prepare some food, and Tester was to be tied up, because he was sure to be distressed if Mansel went down the well and might even fall down in some effort to comfort his lord.

===Chinese===
日落时我下来，那两个已经快要累瘫，却满面喜色；曼塞尔含笑告诉我，他们已经把竖井的井口露出来了。

他说，半个钟头以前，空气与水相争的咕嘟声和吸气声已说明了一切；虽说还未必能立刻下去，井口已露出足够大的一块，够我们察看竖井了。

于是我和汉伯里赶忙去取平台，卡森和罗利则被吩咐回房里去。他们不情愿走，可曼塞尔执意要他们吃上饭、歇上觉：「你们要是留在这儿，」他说，「两样都捞不着，只会累到自身难保了还硬撑着帮我们。两个钟头之后，说不定正用得着你们；所以趁有机会，去吃去睡。」

贝尔（Bell）和泰斯特（Tester）随他们回去，贝尔要守卫并准备些吃食，泰斯特则要拴起来，因为曼塞尔若下井，它必定焦躁不安，说不定还会为了安抚主人而纵身跳下井去。

===Original===
Then I went down, with the light and the beams which Carson and I had cut the night before.

A foot or more of the mouth of the shaft was visible, and, by directing the light, I could see the steps within; but I never beheld a place which looked so black and uninviting, and there was now in the depths of the well an odour which I cannot exactly describe. It seemed to be a bad smell, grown faint with age.

I had my beams in place in a minute of time, and then gave the signal for Hanbury to lower the planks.

As might have been expected, the shaft was between the two beams, but so much had I twisted and turned during my descent that, though I knew from the niches that it must run north or south, I could tell no more. But, when the planks were in place, Mansel lowered the measuring-line, and under my direction, moved it until it hung plumb over the middle of the mouth. This showed, as I afterwards found, that the shaft ran north, towards the castle: and Hanbury marked the place by driving a peg out of sight into the ground.

===Chinese===
随后我带着探照灯下了井，还有我和卡森前一天夜里砍好的那几根梁。

竖井的井口已露出一英尺多，把灯光一引，我能望见里面的石阶；可我从没见过一处地方像这样黑黢黢、令人生畏，而且此刻井底深处有一种气味，我说不准该怎么形容，像是一股陈年的臭气，已经淡了。

一眨眼我便把梁架好了，随即发信号叫汉伯里把木板放下来。

不出所料，竖井正在两根梁之间；可我下井时转来转去，方向早乱了，虽然凭壁龛知道它必是南北走向，却再辨不出更多。木板放好后，曼塞尔放下测深绳，照我的指点慢慢挪，直到绳子端端正正悬在井口正中。这一来便验明——我后来才证实——竖井朝北，通向城堡：汉伯里在地上钉了一枚木销做了记号，外人看不出来。

===Original===
The stage, when I had built it, lay some six inches below the lintel of the mouth of the shaft. The water was rising fast, and I set the planks as close to the mouth as I could, so that by using the windlass, without disturbing the stage, we could do something to keep the water down.

Then I was pulled up, and Mansel made ready to descend.

Over his clothes he put on a waterproof suit, tight-fitting at the wrists and ankles as at the waist and throat. In his pocket he had a torch, and that was all.

Communication by shouting up and down the well had proved unsatisfactory, for the words arrived distorted and often unrecognizable: while I was below, therefore, Mansel and Hanbury had lashed a spring to the pulley-beam and fastened a cord to the spring. This rude apparatus worked very well, for, when the cord was pulled tight and then let go, the spring hit the beam with a smack which there was no mistaking. To the other end of the cord was fastened a biscuit-tin, which, in case of trouble, we were to dash against the wall. We also took the bell which rang from the kitchen hall and hung it inside the parapet so that, were it to ring while he was below, Mansel would receive so important a signal direct.

===Chinese===
平台搭好后，比竖井口的过梁低约六英寸。水涨得很快，我把木板尽量贴着井口安置，这样靠绞盘的力气、又不动平台，我们便能把水位压住一些。

随后我被拉了上去，曼塞尔准备下井。

他在衣裳外头罩上一套防水服，腕口、脚踝收得紧紧的，腰间和领口也是一样。衣袋里揣着一支火把，仅此而已。

在井里井外靠喊话联络，已被证明靠不住，话音传上去都变了形，常常听不出说什么；所以我下井的时候，曼塞尔和汉伯里已把一根弹簧绑在滑轮梁上，又把一根细绳系在弹簧上。这件粗陋的家什却灵验得很：把绳拉紧再一撒手，弹簧便啪的一声打在梁上，决计错不了。绳的另一头拴着一只饼干铁罐，一旦出事，我们就拿它砸墙。我们还把厨房大厅里响的那口铃取了来，挂在护墙内侧，这样，曼塞尔在下面时铃若响了，如此要紧的信号便能直达于他。

===Original===
So soon as Mansel was down, we were to start bailing, and the signal that he was clear and that we could pull up the seat was to be two strokes of the spring upon the pulley-beam.

We had hardly begun to lower him, when, to our dismay, the searchlight, which I had left burning upon the stage, went suddenly out: but Mansel cried to us to go on, and a moment later I saw the flash of his torch. Compared with the searchlight, this threw a miserable beam; and I was not at all happy to think he was going down thus embarrassed to a place which had seemed so dreadful when it was full of light. I think we all three had hopes that, when he was down, he would be able to put in order what had gone wrong: but, if we had, they were vain, for, after a little, the spring struck twice upon the beam, and, when we pulled up the seat by which we had let him down, the lamp was hanging upon the hook beneath.

Then we let down a bucket and started to bail, and we knew that Mansel was in the shaft, for the light which his torch had been giving had disappeared.

And, except that we laboured steadily for about five minutes, that is as much as I know of that day’s work, for then I was dealt such a blow on the back of my head that I fell down like an ox and lost consciousness.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔一下去，我们就开始戽水；他到了地方、我们可以往上拉座椅的信号，是弹簧在滑轮梁上敲两下。

我们刚把他往下放，就见留在平台上亮着的探照灯忽然灭了，我们好不丧气；可曼塞尔喊我们只管放，转眼间我望见了他火把的闪光。比起探照灯，这点光真可怜；想到他就这么狼狈地下去，去那个灯火通明时尚且狰狞可怖的地方，我心里老大不痛快。我想我们三个都巴望他下去之后能把毛病整治好：可这指望落了空，因为不多时，弹簧在梁上敲了两下；我们把送他下去的座椅拉上来时，那盏灯正挂在底下的钩子上。

随后我们放下吊桶，开始戽水；我们知道曼塞尔已进了竖井，因为他火把的光不见了。

而除了接着稳稳当当戽了约莫五分钟水，那天的事我所知尽于此了；因为就在这时，我后脑上重重挨了一记，像头牛似的栽倒，不省人事。

===Original===
Hanbury saw me fall, but, before he had time to cry out, he had been served as I had, and, since he was still senseless when I sat up, Mansel alone of us three can speak to what followed the assault.

This was the tale he told us so soon as he could.

“I made a bridge of a plank from the stage to a step in the shaft, and so spared myself immersion; and, though I got pretty wet, I was able to keep the torch out of the water. Then I drew the plank after me into the shaft, for, if I had left it in place, the bucket would have fouled it when you began to bail.

“The shaft is three feet wide by about five high. Its walls and steps are of stones from the riverbed, laid in cement. Its roof is curved and built of stones similar to those used for the well. I imagine it owes its style to the Count’s desire for secrecy, for all the stuff used to build it might well have gone into the well. The walls of the latter are certainly backed with pebbles as high as the first spring.

“The going was very unpleasant, not to say dangerous, for the steps are very rough and covered with slime. I had hoped, by counting them and measuring the rises and treads, to get figures from which Hanbury could tell pretty well where the chamber lies; but their flight is so irregular that without a rule I could do nothing valuable. So far as I could make out, the shaft runs dead straight. The air was abominable.

===Chinese===
汉伯里眼见我倒下，可还没来得及喊出声，他自己也挨了我同样的一下；等我坐起身时他仍昏迷未醒，所以袭击之后的事，我们三个里只有曼塞尔能说个分明。

他一缓过来，便给我们讲了下面这段经过。

「我拿一块木板在平台和竖井里一级石阶之间搭了座小桥，免得自己泡在水里；虽然湿得够呛，火把总算没沾水。随后我把木板也拖进了竖井，因为要是留在原处，你们一开始戽水，吊桶就会把它撞坏。

「竖井宽三英尺，高约五英尺。井壁和石阶都是河滩上的石头，用灰浆砌成。顶是拱的，砌顶的石子与砌井的相仿。我想这样式是出于伯爵保密的心思，因为修井的料子多半顺手就填进井里去了。井壁背面确实垫着鹅卵石，一直垫到头一道泉那么高。

「那路很不好走，说危险也不为过，石阶粗糙得很，又满是黏滑的淤泥。我原指望数一数级数、量一量每级的高低深浅，得出些数字，好让汉伯里大体推算出石室在哪儿；可石阶忽高忽低全无规律，没有尺子我量不出什么名堂。据我所能辨出的，竖井是笔直的。空气糟透了。

===Original===
“I had taken some twenty-five steps before I slipped.

“Now a fall in that shaft would be an ugly business, and I don’t think you could complain if you broke no more than a leg: and, as, all things considered, it’s not the place you’d choose for a first-class smash, I saved myself at the cost of dropping the torch. This, of course, was broken by the fall, and, although I recovered it, it would give me no light.

“To proceed in the dark seemed futile, and I had just begun to retrace my steps, when I became aware of a light which came from the well.

“I at once assumed that you had adjusted the searchlight and were letting it down, for I heard the windlass working: but a moment later I thought that I heard a whistle, and stopped in my tracks.

“Someone alighted heavily on the stage.

===Chinese===
「走了约莫二十五级，我一脚踩空了。

「在那么个竖井里摔一跤可够呛，你要是只摔断一条腿，也没什么可抱怨的：而且通盘想来，那可不是存心摔个头等好跤的地方，我便丢了火把保住自己。火把自然摔坏了，我虽把它捡了回来，它却再亮不起来。

「摸着黑再往上走，像是白费劲；我刚要转身往回走，忽然察觉井口那边有光下来了。

「我立刻认定是你们把探照灯弄好了正往下放，因为我听见绞盘在转：可过了一会儿，我像是听见一声口哨，便站住不动了。

「有个人重重地落在了平台上。

===Original===
“But for the whistle I had heard, that it was not you, Chandos, would never have entered my head. As it was, I made sure you would hail me almost at once: but, remembering that all things are possible, I waited for you to speak.

“That I did so was just as well, for I was still waiting when somebody snuffed and spat.

“Well, that eliminated any of us.

“I can’t pretend I wasn’t shaken.

“I dared not think what had happened to you and Hanbury: the servants, presumably, were obediently keeping the house: the enemy had the windlass: and I was trapped, good and proper, in a blind tunnel five by three, and eighty-odd feet below ground.

===Chinese===
「要不是听见那声口哨，我脑子里压根不会想到那不是你，钱多斯（Chandos）。当时我只当你马上就会招呼我：可转念一想世事无奇不有，便等着你开口。

「幸亏我这么等着，因为我还没等到，就听见有人抽了抽鼻子，啐了口唾沫。

「这一下，我们这边的人全排除了。

「我不能硬说我没慌。

「我不敢去想你和汉伯里出了什么事：仆人们多半规规矩矩守在房里：绞盘落到了敌人手里：而我呢，结结实实被困在一条五英尺乘三英尺的死胡同里，埋在地下八十多英尺深。

===Original===
“All of a sudden I wondered if the enemy knew I was there.

“I decided that the odds were that he did not. Unless he had seen me go down, there was no reason why he should know. The searchlight had failed: my torch had gone out: although the stage was there, I had withdrawn my bridge. The fact that he made no attempt to conceal his presence assured me that I was right.

“Instinctively I began to reascend the shaft.

“I had not thought that even Rose Noble would suspect our work at the well, and the reflection that I had been so heartily outwitted and outclassed was very bitter. The attack had been well done. One minute, you and Hanbury were bailing: the next, one of the five was descending the well. I assumed that he had come to reconnoitre. When he had made his report, the others would bail for a while, and then the actual attempt to lift the treasure would be made. Unless I could reach the servants, I did not see how this could fail.

“I had taken five or six steps, when a splash told me that my man was making the shaft. Though there was still one spare plank, the idea of a bridge had not, I suppose, occurred to him: but, after a struggle—in which he went under water—I heard him make the steps. For a moment he stood grunting and blowing, and trying to get his breath. Then he began to ascend. This surprised me, for I had made certain that he would first produce a torch; but what astonished me still more was the progress he made, for he climbed as well as I had when I had seen my way.

===Chinese===
「猛然间我想到，敌人知不知道我在那儿。

「我断定多半不知道。除非他看见我下去，否则他没来由知道。探照灯熄了：我的火把灭了：平台虽在，我那座小桥已经抽走。他毫不掩饰自己行踪这一点，叫我确信自己没猜错。

「我出于本能，开始重新往竖井上头爬。

「我原以为连罗斯·诺布尔也想不到我们在井上动手脚，如今被人家耍得团团转、高下立判，这滋味实在苦涩。这一手干得漂亮。前一分钟，你和汉伯里还在戽水：后一分钟，五人帮里的一个已经下井了。我猜想他是来侦察的。等他回报之后，其余的人会戽上一阵水，然后便要动手起宝了。除非我能赶到仆人那里，我看不出这事怎么会不成。

「我又爬了五六级，哗啦一声水响，告诉我那家伙正往竖井里来。平台上明明还有一块备用的木板，可我想他压根没想到搭桥这一层：扑腾了一阵——还沉下去一回——我听见他上了石阶。他站着呼哧呼哧喘了半天，好容易匀过气来，便开始往上爬。这叫我吃惊，我满以为他会先摸出火把；更叫我吃惊的是他爬得那么利索，比我看得见路时爬得还不在话下。

===Original===
“So we went up the shaft in single file, some thirty odd steps apart.

“My one idea, of course, was to get to the top of the well. The only way to do this was, unknown to the rest of the gang, to take his place and let them haul me up in his stead. What would happen when they saw their mistake no one could tell, but with luck I should have been landed before they saw what they had done.

“It was quite plain that, if I could escape the notice of the man in the shaft, I should stand a better chance of taking his place. Even if I had had room, I could not see to hit him under the jaw: unless I could knock him senseless, he would probably let out a yell: I was unarmed, and by a hand-to-hand fight in the dark in such a place I was as likely to come to harm as was he. But what weighed with me most of all was the natural reluctance to kill. Unless I laid him out, his shouts would give me away: but, if I put him out of action, in view of the pace at which the water was rising, he would either be drowned or trapped—probably trapped. And it seemed a shocking thing to sentence a fellow creature to such a terrible doom.

“To avoid him in the shaft was out of the question, but I thought if I could reach the chamber, I could let him go by to the treasure and start right back.

===Chinese===
「于是一前一后，我们顺着竖井往上爬，中间隔着三十来级。

「我自然只有一个念头：爬到井口去。唯一的法子，是在那伙人不知不觉间顶替他的位置，叫他们把他当成我，把我拉上去。等他们瞧出错了，会闹出什么来谁也说不好，可只要运气好，在他们明白过来之前，我已经上去了。

「很明显，只要井里那人察觉不了我，我顶替他的把握就大些。就算有地方施展，黑地里我也瞧不准，一拳打不中他的下颌：打不晕他，他多半要嚷出声：我又没带家伙，在那种地方摸黑肉搏，我和他是半斤对八两，谁也未必讨得了好。可压在我心头最重的，是人天生不肯杀人。不把他撂倒，他的喊声就会坏我的事：可一旦叫他失了行动能力，照水涨的速度，他不是淹死，就是被困——多半是被困。而把一条活生生的性命判给这样可怕的结局，似乎也太残忍了。

「在竖井里避让他是不用想的，可我想，只要我能赶到石室，尽可以让他过去直奔财宝，他一回头，我再动手不迟。

===Original===
“I was not afraid of his hearing me, for I was going quietly, but he made a lot of noise.

“The shaft seemed endless, but at last I felt a step which was clear of ooze. I took it, and two more, and then something I hadn’t expected told me that I was upon the threshold of the chamber itself. *Four iron bars, set up on end in the way.* From their shape, I should say they were crowbars, such as a well-digger used about his business. And I don’t suppose it took five minutes to bed them, but, once the cement had set, well, I don’t know if you’ve ever filed iron, but it’s tedious work. Top and bottom, they were bedded into hewn stone. There’s no doubt about it, Axel the Red was a very careful man.

“That it would come to a fight was now certain; so I set my back to the bars and awaited my man.

“Suddenly I heard the bell ring at the top of the well.

===Chinese===
「我不怕他听见我，我走得没声，倒是他自己吵得很。

「竖井像是没有尽头，可最后我终于踩到一级没有淤泥的石阶。踏上去，又上了两级，随后一件意料之外的事告诉我，我已到了石室的门槛跟前。*四根铁条，直挺挺竖在通道里。* 看那式样，该是撬棍一类，正是掘井人干活使的家什。把它们砌进去想必用不了五分钟，可灰浆一凝——嗯，不知你们锉没锉过铁，那是件磨人的活。铁条上下两头，都嵌进了琢石里。毫无疑问，红发阿克塞尔（Axel the Red）是个十分仔细的人。

「事情既已注定要动武，我便背靠铁条，等那人上来。

「忽然我听见井口那边的铃响了。

===Original===
“My man heard it, too, and stopped—about ten steps away.

“Of course, I knew what it meant, and praised God. But he was clearly alarmed, for he was holding his breath, and, I fancy, straining his ears. I know exactly how he felt, and, believe me, I don’t blame him.

“The bell rang again.

“A moment later I heard him begin to descend.

“Be sure I followed.

===Chinese===
「我那人也听见了，停住了——离我约莫十级。

「不用说，我知道那是什么意思，谢天谢地。可他分明惊慌起来，屏住了呼吸，我猜还在竖起耳朵听。他那份心情我体会得再透彻不过，说真的，我怪不得他。

「铃又响了。

「过了一会儿，我听见他掉头往下去了。

「你们放心，我紧跟着。

===Original===
“Before I did so, I tested every bar. They were all the same size, nearly an inch thick, not very rusty: and not one of them would budge.

“Such plan as I now had was to follow him into the well. When he had been reassured by those at the top, I thought it more than likely that he would reenter the shaft and try once again to reach the chamber: but, whether he did or no, once I was in the well, I should be immeasurably better placed to deal with whatever arose, if for no other reason, because to occupy that shaft, yet not dwell upon its undesirability as a retreat is almost impossible.

“And here let me say that, treasure or no, I was immensely surprised to find that any one of the five could withstand its terrors so well. Of the ugly side of Nature that kind of man usually fights very shy. Ellis, for instance, would never have gone down the well. I knew it wasn’t Rose Noble, and Punter would have cursed: but, whichever of the others it was, he was a brave man, for, if the devil had not been driving, I wouldn’t have gone up that shaft without a light for any money.

“The fellow descended steadily, and I came down after as fast as I dared.

===Chinese===
「跟下去之前，我把每根铁条都试了一遍。四根一般粗细，将近一英寸，锈得不算厉害：可没有一根挪得动。

「我眼下这点打算，就是跟他回井里去。等上头的人给他吃下定心丸，我想他多半会再进竖井，重试一次想到石室去：可他回不回，只要我到了井里，应付起任何变故来都强出百倍，不为别的，只因为守在那条竖井里，还硬不去想它作为退路有多不济，这几乎是办不到的。

「说到这里容我说一句：且不提财宝，那五个人里竟有谁顶得住这竖井的阴森，我实在惊诧之至。造化那阴森的一面，那种人向来避之唯恐不及。譬如埃利斯，就绝不会下这口井。我知道那不是罗斯·诺布尔，潘特（Punter）早该咒骂出声了：可无论是其余哪一位，他都是条好汉，因为若不是魔鬼逼着，打死我也不会没头没亮地往那条竖井里爬。

「那家伙一步步稳稳往下走，我跟在后面，仗着胆子尽量加快。

===Original===
“At last I heard him touch water. Then he took a deep breath and floundered out of the shaft. By the time he was on the stage I was still ten steps up. The water had risen, for they had not thought to bail, and the stage was submerged.

“I made what haste I could, but I heard no colloquy. I imagine that he whistled, but, as I slid into the water, the windlass began to work; and, when I took hold of the stage and could look about me, I saw my man in midair, lantern and all.

“I did not know whether to be sorry or glad, but, what was much worse, I did not know what to think. What mystified me was their silence. This seemed unnatural, and I could think of no reason why they should take him up without a word.

“I had pulled myself on to the stage and was listening to the click of the ratchet and watching the lantern rise, when, all of a sudden, I heard the windlass stop.

“Then a shot was fired, and, after a moment, I heard a flurry of voices at the top of the well.

===Chinese===
「末了我听见他踩到了水。他深吸一口气，扑腾着出了竖井。等他上了平台，我还在十级之上。水又涨了，因为他们没想到戽水，平台已经没在水下。

「我尽量加快，可没听见他们搭话。我猜他吹了声哨，我滑进水里时，绞盘已经开始转了；等我扒住平台、能四下张望时，我那位朋友已经悬在半空，连人带灯笼。

「我说不上该忧该喜，可更要紧的是，我不知该怎么想。叫我纳闷的是他们的沉默。这不合常理，他们一言不发就把人往上吊，我想不出个道理。

「我刚爬上平台，正听着棘轮咔哒咔哒响、望着灯笼往上升，绞盘声忽然停了。

「接着响了一枪，片刻之后，井口那边乱哄哄一片人声。

===Original===
“I assumed that the servants had come to dispute possession of the windlass, and, generally, counter the attack; and I would have given a lot to be above ground: but, all the same, it struck me that, ill placed as I was, I would very much sooner be standing upon the stage than dangling from the end of the chain some forty feet up.

“I had just come to this conclusion when the man who was in midair expressed the same view: at least, from the apprehension with which he invested an oath, I gathered that he felt his position. And, directly I heard his voice, *I knew who it was*. And the knowledge, as you may imagine, gave me plenty of food for thought.

“So I stood very still and waited, with my eyes on the lantern and my back against the wall.”

===Chinese===
「我以为是仆人们出来争夺绞盘、回击这场偷袭了；我情愿付出很大代价换自己在地面上：不过话虽如此，我还是觉得，纵然处境不妙，站在平台上，也远比吊在四十英尺上头的链子梢上强。

「我这念头刚落，悬在半空那位也表明了同样的看法：至少，从他咒骂时那股惶恐劲儿，我听得出他掂出了自己的处境。而他的声音一入耳，*我就知道他是谁了*。诸位不难想见，这个底细够我好好琢磨一阵的。

「于是我贴着井壁，一动不动地等着，眼睛盯着那盏灯笼。」

===Original===
That was as much as Mansel had to tell, and, since there was but one shot fired at that time, I can take up the story without a break, because I had just sat up and was trying to collect my wits, when I heard a cry and men running, and then the sound of a shot.

The moon had not yet risen, but I could make out the well and that I was sitting above it, half in and half out of the wood. My wrists were bound behind me, and my head was aching very much.

There were figures about the well, and I heard Rose Noble’s voice.

“Who fired?” he said. “We or they?”

“I did,” said Punter. “They saw me coming and ran.”

===Chinese===
曼塞尔要讲的就到这里。好在当时只响过一枪，这故事我可以接得天衣无缝，因为我刚坐起身，正在定神，就听见一声喊叫、一阵奔跑，接着是一声枪响。

月亮还没上来，可我认得出那口井，也认出自己正坐在井边，半个身子在林子里，半个身子在林子外。我的双手被反绑在背后，头疼得厉害。

井边有几个人影，我听见了罗斯·诺布尔的说话声。

「是谁开的枪？」他说，「是我们，还是他们？」

「是我，」潘特说，「他们瞧见我来，跑了。」

===Original===
“Pardon me,” said Rose Noble: “they heard you. You don’t know how to move. How many were there?”

“Two,” said Punter. “Servants, I think; but it may have been the two pups.”

“That’s right,” said another voice. “They was busy crankin’ the well.”

Here one of them must have looked down and seen the light, for there was a cry of surprise, and then a buzz of exclamation, of which I could make no sense.

Then Rose Noble spoke again.

===Chinese===
「恕我直言，」罗斯·诺布尔说，「他们没瞧见，是听见的。你走路的动静太大。一共几个人？」

「两个，」潘特说，「我看是仆人；不过兴许是那两只小崽子。」

「没错，」另一个声音说，「他们正忙着摇井上的绞盘呢。」

这当口，他们中间想必有一个低头看见了灯光，因为先是一声惊呼，接着是一片七嘴八舌的嚷嚷，我一句也听不真切。

随后罗斯·诺布尔又开了口。

===Original===
“Quite so,” he said. “Quite so. If there’s one down there, we’ve got him by the short hairs. And is that as far as you can see? Damn it,” he cried, “lift up your ——— eyes! **Think!** *What are they doing by night down in this well?* You may have a twist for sweet water, but——”

The rest of his sentence was drowned in a burst of appreciation of his discovery: and I never heard grown men so abandon themselves to their glee, for they shouted and stamped and laughed, like so many lunatics, and nothing that Rose Noble could do could bring them to order.

In the midst of the flurry another came running up, and then I heard Ellis’ voice.

As far as I could make out, they were now all five by the well, but, since they all continued to speak at once, I could hear nothing that was said.

Then Ellis was asked some question, and I heard his reply.

===Chinese===
「正是，」他说，「正是。只要下头有一个，我们就攥住了他的命根子。怎么，你就只能看到这么深？该死，」他喝道，「抬起你那——眼睛！**想想！** *他们深更半夜在这口井底下做什么？* 你们或许是为了取甜水才拐的弯，可是——」

他下半句被一阵赞叹声淹没了——众人都在捧他那发现的高明：我从不曾听成年的男人这样忘乎所以地欢腾，他们又是喊、又是跺脚、又是大笑，活像一群疯子，罗斯·诺布尔怎么弹压也压不住。

正闹得不可开交，又一个人跑了过来，接着我听见了埃利斯的声音。

据我所能辨出的，这时他们五个都到了井边，可人人都在抢着说话，我一个字也听不清。

后来有人向埃利斯问了一句什么，我听见他答道。

===Original===
“I think they’re out,” he said. “There’s no light or sound. When I tried the door, a dog barked: but that was locked, and the ground-floor windows are barred.”

“There’s three of them somewhere,” said Rose Noble. “Two of them ran from here, and there’s one below.”

“God give it’s Little Willie,” said Ellis, and sucked in his breath. “I’d like to meet him like this.”

“Me, too,” said Punter.

“Big Willie, you mean,” said Rose Noble. “Bag him, and we’ve got the lot. Besides,” he drawled, “if anyone’s thinking of scores, I reckon I’ve one to settle that takes precedence.”

===Chinese===
「我看他们出门了，」他说，「里头没灯光也没动静。我试门的时候，有条狗叫了两声：可门锁着，底层的窗户都钉了铁条。」

「他们有三个躲在什么地方，」罗斯·诺布尔说，「两个从这儿跑了，还有一个在底下。」

「上帝保佑，是『小威利』才好，」埃利斯倒吸一口气说，「我倒巴不得跟他在这种情形下会一会。」

「我也是，」潘特说。

「你该说『大威利』，」罗斯·诺布尔说，「把他装进袋子，我们就是囊中全收了。再说，」他拖长了调子，「谁要是记着账想清账，我算有一笔排在前头的。」

===Original===
The oath with which he enforced this dark saying was the most dreadful I have ever heard, and I began to strive, like a madman, to free my wrists, for the thought that Mansel was about to deliver himself into such cruel and bloody hands was insupportable.

“Job,” said Rose Noble, “back to the path and watch. If anything moves, let it have it: we don’t want to be disturbed.”

Here one of them found the searchlight, and they wasted a minute or two trying to make it light. Then they kept peering down the well and whispering and cursing one another for making a noise. Indeed, I never saw men so plainly out of their element, for they did not seem to remember that those they had put to flight had been using the windlass, or to notice what must have been manifest—that the lantern below them was nowhere near the water: and it was only after a lot of argument that two took hold of the windlass and felt the weight on the chain.

“It’s loaded,” I heard one say. “ ’Eavy as lead.”

===Chinese===
他给这句阴沉沉的话压上的那句咒，是我平生听过的最恶毒的；我一想到曼塞尔就要落进这样凶残嗜血的手里，便疯了似的挣扎起来，想把手腕挣脱。

「乔布（Job），」罗斯·诺布尔说，「回小路上去守着。有什么东西敢动，就给它一家伙：别叫人搅了我们。」

这时他们中有一个发现了探照灯，白费了一两分钟想把它弄亮。然后他们不住地朝井里张望，压着嗓子嘀咕，又互相咒骂对方弄出了声响。说实话，我从没见过男人像他们这样全然不在行，因为他们似乎不记得把他们打跑的人刚才还在用绞盘，也没注意到一桩再明白不过的事——底下那盏灯离水面还远得很：直争了半天，才有两个抓住绞盘，掂了掂链子上的分量。

「吊着东西呢，」我听见一个说，「沉得跟铅一样。」

===Original===
At that they all peered over the parapet again, and I did not know what to think, but was greatly afraid that the weight must be Mansel himself.

Here my attention was diverted to something which stirred by my side. To my relief, it proved to be Hanbury, bound as was I. I managed to move until I had my mouth to his ear, and, as soon as he could receive it, I told him as much as I knew. Except that his head was aching, he did not seem to be hurt, and, when I suggested that I should try to unfasten the cord which was binding his wrists, he turned on his side and put them up without a word. At once I turned my back on him and got to work upon the knots, but I made no progress, and, after a minute or so, his fingers brushed mine aside, and fell to work in their stead.

At that moment I heard Rose Noble say “Give way,” and at once two men at the windlass began to turn.

===Chinese===
这么一说，他们又都凑到护墙上往里看，我心里乱作一团，只怕那分量正是曼塞尔本人。

这时我留意到身边有什么东西在动。定睛一看，宽心了不少——是汉伯里，跟我一样被绑着。我一点点挪过去，把嘴凑到他耳边，他刚能听，我就把我知道的全告诉了他。他除了头疼，似乎没受别的伤；我一提议由我来解他腕上的绳子，他便一言不发侧过身，把手背了上来。我立刻背对着他解那些结，可解了半天没进展，约莫一分钟后，他的手指把我拨开，自己动起手来。

就在这时，我听见罗斯·诺布尔说「用力」，绞盘旁立刻有两个人转动起来。

===Original===
The moon was rising now, and I could see that the others were on their knees or crouching beside the parapet, ready, no doubt, to cover Mansel the moment he reached the top. That they thought this was necessary argues their respect for his arm, for even they must have realized that four men can never have had one at a greater disadvantage.

Hanbury worked feverishly, while I tried to think what to do.

Unless he were in danger of death, to attempt to assist Mansel until we had arms of some sort would be the act of a fool. I, therefore, decided that, when once we were free, we must try to reach the kitchen, join forces with the servants, and deliver a counterattack.

Hanbury had freed me, and I was wrestling with his bonds, when a faint light began to appear within the well.

This horrified us both, for we thought, of course, that it was that of Mansel’s torch; and that he should show a light which could assist none but his foes was not his way. Indeed, we now fully expected any moment to hear him address us, and ask why on earth we had kept him waiting so long.

===Chinese===
月亮这时升起来了，我看得见其余几个人正跪着或蹲在护墙旁，无疑准备曼塞尔一露头就瞄准他。他们以为这一手必不可少，足见他们对他的枪法有多忌惮，因为连他们也该明白，四个人对付一个，从来没占过比这更大的便宜。

汉伯里发疯似的解着绳结，我则在苦思对策。

除非他性命垂危，否则在我们弄到点什么家伙之前就去援救曼塞尔，是蠢人的行径。因此我拿定主意，一旦脱了绑，就要设法赶到厨房，与仆人们会合，发起反击。

汉伯里已经解开了我，我正跟他的绑绳较劲，忽见井里隐隐亮起了光。

这光把我们两个都吓坏了，因为我们自然以为那是曼塞尔的火把；可露一道光、只帮得了敌人的忙，这绝不是他的做派。说实话，我们这时满心以为随时会听见他招呼我们，问我们究竟为什么让他等了这么久。

===Original===
The glow in the well was very definite when at last I had Hanbury free, and at once we crept out of the wood and began to crawl by its edge towards the house.

I was in front, and going as fast as I dared, when I came face to face with Bell, who was crawling the opposite way. The first I knew of it was the barrel of a pistol pressed tight against my temple, for he had seen me coming, and had not known who it was.

Then, lying there, I told him as much as I knew, and he said that Carson and Rowley had taken up positions on either side of the path. He was to crawl to where he could see and hear what was going on at the well, and, at a flash from his torch, the three were to count two seconds and then open fire. The idea was to drive the thieves into the combe into which the gutter ran, but at any cost to keep them away from the house and out of the two woods, for that would give a chance of rescue to the occupant of the well.

I at once fell in with his plan, whereupon we decided that I should take his place. He, therefore, gave me his torch and one of the pistols he had, and, when I had arranged for him to give his other to Hanbury, and then return to Carson, I went about. As I passed Hanbury, I told him that Bell was there and, when he had got his pistol, to stay where he was.

===Chinese===
等我终于把汉伯里解出来，井里的光已经很分明；我们立刻爬出林子，贴着井沿朝房子那头爬去。

我在前头，仗着胆子尽量爬快，忽然与迎面爬来的贝尔打了个照面。我先觉出的是一截枪管紧紧抵住了我的太阳穴——他早看见有什么爬过来，却认不出是谁。

于是我们躺在原地，我把知道的都告诉了他；他说卡森和罗利已分别埋伏在小路两侧。他这边要爬到能看清、听见井边动静的地方，他的火把一闪，三个人数两秒钟便一齐开火。打算把盗贼赶进溜槽汇入的那条沟壑，但无论如何要拦住他们，不让他们近房子、也不让他们进那两片树林，因为只有那样，井里的人才有一线获救之机。

我立刻赞成他的安排，于是议定由我来顶替他的位置。他把火把和随身两支手枪里的一支交给我；我约好让他把另一支交给汉伯里、然后回卡森那里去，便掉头往回爬。经过汉伯里身边时，我告诉他贝尔就在这儿，叫他拿到枪后待在原地别动。

===Original===
I had hardly done so when a sudden clamour arose at the top of the well. The light was gone, but all four men were peering at something within.

At length:

“Haul him in,” said Rose Noble.

They were very inexpert and mortally afraid of falling, and hard words were exchanged and much swearing before their burden was landed roughly enough, with two on the top of him and the other two standing by.

Before a torch could be lighted, Punter let out a yell.

===Chinese===
我刚交代完，井口忽然喧声大作。灯光不见了，可四个人都在朝井里盯着什么。

只听得：

「把他拽上来，」罗斯·诺布尔说。

他们笨手笨脚，又怕自己摔下去怕得要死，你一言我一语地呛呛，赌了不少咒，才把那件「货」毛手毛脚拖上岸来——两个人压在他身上，另外两个在旁站着。

火把还没点着，潘特先嚎了一嗓子。

===Original===
“By ———,” he cried, “it’s that ——— that keeps the inn!”

To me his words came like a thunderbolt, and, between my relief and my astonishment, for a moment I felt quite dazed. Then it occurred to me that this was the moment to attack and that a sudden assault, coming upon them while they were so much engaged with the turn events had taken and were still uncertain what to think or do, would probably fare better than we could have hoped: so I took my pistol and torch, and, directing the face of the latter towards the house, gave the agreed signal, counted two seconds, and fired.

This was as Carson had arranged, and nothing could have been better, for the five of us fired almost at once, and so unexpected a volley would, I should think, have disconcerted a Napoleon himself.

No one fell, and, without so much as a cry, the four thieves scattered and ran straight for the combe—*with the innkeeper pelting behind*. Two of them fouled the gutter and fell to glory, but, perhaps because they had run into the moonlight, they were not content to lie, and, picking themselves up, rushed violently after their fellows down the slope, like the Gadarene swine. This much I saw, for I had run along by the edge of the wood, and I sent a shot after them, before hastening to the well.

===Chinese===
「凭——起誓，」他嚷道，「是开客栈的那个——狗东西！」

这话对我无异晴天霹雳，我又是释然又是惊愕，一时竟有些发懵。随即我想到，动手就在此刻：事态急转直下，他们还围着这变故转磨磨，不知该想什么、做什么，冷不防一记突袭，多半比我们指望的还要顺手：于是我端起手枪和火把，把火把的光面转向房子那头，发出约好的信号，数了两秒，开枪了。

一切正如卡森的安排，再好不过：我们五个几乎同时开火，这样一通出其不意的排枪，我想连拿破仑本人也要乱了方寸。

没有人倒下；四个盗贼一声没吭，四散奔逃，直奔那条沟壑——*客栈老板在后头紧追不舍*。有两个绊在溜槽上，摔了个结结实实，可也许因为跌进了月光里，他们不甘心就这么躺着，爬起来又发疯似的追同伙下坡而去，活像格拉森的猪群。这些是我亲眼所见，因为我一直贴着林边跑；我朝他们背后放了一枪，便赶忙奔向井边。

===Original===
The seat was not on the hook, but only a bight of rope, so that, had I not seen him run, I should have thought that the innkeeper was ready to drop with fatigue: but we had the seat slung in a twinkling, and Carson and Rowley lowered it into the well.

I must confess that I waited in fear and trembling for I knew that the water must have risen a foot or more, and the thought that the landlord had emerged alive from the well filled me with the gravest misgivings for Mansel’s safety. I was also quite sure that the thieves would presently return, when, if we had not raised Mansel, we should present to them as fair a target as they had offered us, and the likelihood that they would bungle a second and better chance seemed small indeed.

When the seat was nearly down, Hanbury took hold of the signal cord and swung the biscuit-tin, and a moment later, to our indescribable relief, the spring struck once upon the beam. At once we locked the windlass: and, in an instant, another two blows gave us the signal to hoist.

Then we all five fell upon the windlass, and brought Mansel up with a run, and, only waiting to take up the searchlight, the seat, the measuring-line and the bell, we left the meadow in good order, with Carson, rifle in hand, bringing up the rear.

===Chinese===
钩子上没有座椅，只搭着一股绳圈；若不是亲眼见他跑了，我真要以为客栈老板是累得瘫软才掉下去的：转眼间我们便把座椅挂好，卡森和罗利把它放了下去。

我得承认，我提心吊胆地等着，因为我知道水必定又涨了一英尺还多；一想到老板能活着出得井来，我对曼塞尔安危的疑惧便沉到了底。我还有十分把握，盗贼转眼就会回来；到那时，我们若还没把曼塞尔吊上来，就会摆给他们一个跟我们方才给他们的同样好的靶子，而指望他们把第二次、也是更好的一次机会再搞砸，希望实在渺茫。

座椅快到底时，汉伯里抓住信号绳，摇了摇饼干铁罐；片刻之后，弹簧在梁上敲了一下，我们如释重负，简直无法形容。我们立刻锁住绞盘：一转眼，又是两下，示意我们可以起吊了。

于是我们五个人一齐扑上绞盘，一阵疾转，把曼塞尔拉了上来；收起探照灯、座椅、测深绳和那口铃之后，我们便秩序井然地撤离了草地，卡森端着步枪殿后。

===Original===
Two minutes later we were within the house, where Tester greeted Mansel as though in fact he knew that he was risen from the dead.

Here I will say what I should have set down before, namely, that the walls of the kitchen-quarters were immensely thick, and must have formed part of the castle which was burned down. The windows were high above ground and heavily barred, and, since we had hung up curtains some distance away from the frames, neither by day nor night could anyone from without see into the rooms.

Then supper was served, and Mansel and Bell and I told each his tale.

Bell had little to add to what I now knew: but one thing that he said we all found interesting, and that was that the second attack had undoubtedly come from the south, that is to say, from the combe into which we had packed the thieves, “for,” said Bell, “although the alarm bell went, it went but once, and I think it was rung by one man coming up from the west: but, when the innkeeper’s party took to their heels, they ran north by the path through the woods and past the house: and, since I am sure that was not how they had come, they must have done so because they were driven that way.”

===Chinese===
两分钟后我们进了屋，泰斯特迎着曼塞尔，那亲热劲儿，倒像它真个知道主人是从死里回来的。

这里补记一件本该早就写下的事：厨房一排的墙厚得出奇，必定是被焚毁的那座城堡的遗构。窗户高离地面，钉着粗重的铁条；我们又把帘幕挂在离窗框有一段距离的地方，所以无论白天黑夜，外头谁都休想窥见屋里。

随后开晚饭，曼塞尔、贝尔和我各讲了各的经过。

贝尔能补充的已不多：不过他说了一件事，我们都觉得有意思——第二次进攻无疑来自南面，也就是说，来自我们把他们赶进去的那条沟壑，「因为，」贝尔说，「警铃虽然响了，却只响了一声，我想是有人从西边上来的一个人摇的；可客栈老板那一伙人逃命的时候，是朝北顺着穿过树林、经过房前的那条小路跑的：我敢肯定他们来时不是走的这条路，所以他们那样跑，必定是被赶过去的。」

===Original===
Only when all had been said, did I remember Job.

When I spoke of him, Carson smiled.

“I heard what Rose Noble said, sir, so it was hardly fair. He said ‘Back to the path, and watch,’ so *I did as he said*.”

“I hope you didn’t kill him,” said Mansel.

“Oh, no, sir,” said Carson, Then he hesitated. “I was going to ask you, sir—supposing it had been Rose Noble…?”

===Chinese===
直到大伙儿都说完，我才想起乔布。

我一提起他，卡森就笑了。

「罗斯·诺布尔的话我听见了，先生，所以这么办不算太亏待他。他说『回小路上去，守着』，我*就照他说的办了*。」

「你没要他的命吧？」曼塞尔问。

「哦，没有，先生，」卡森说。随后他迟疑了一下。「我正想请教您呢，先生——假如那是罗斯·诺布尔……？」

===Original===
Mansel shook his head.

“Certainly not,” he said. “And here let me say you can all of you thank your stars that you’re such bad shots. I don’t wish to sound ungrateful, but, unless I’m being tortured, you must leave these volleys alone. That’s the way people get hurt. *Except in the last resort, you are never to fire to hit.* That is the handicap or disadvantage of——”

As Tester growled, some object parted the curtains, and fell clean on to a loose cushion which belonged to the Rolls.

The terrier leapt at it, but, before I could think, Mansel had sent him flying and, with a great cry of “**Down!**” had hurled the thing into the kitchen and fallen upon his face.

The next instant a most frightful explosion shook the house, which I verily thought was coming about our ears; for the lights went out and the whole of the plaster of the ceiling in the servants’ hall fell down on our heads; and, what with the concussion and the dust and the tinkle of falling glass and the sudden return of the pain in the back of my head, my wits very nearly left me, and, when I heard Mansel speaking, he seemed to be a great way off.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔摇了摇头。

「断然不可，」他说，「说到这里我倒要说，你们枪法这么糟，各位真该谢天谢地。我并不想显得不领情，可除非是我正受着拷打，这种排枪你们千万收起来。伤人就是这么伤的。*除非万不得已，你们绝不准开枪求中。* 这正是——的弊害或不便——」

泰斯特正低吼着，一件什么东西拨开帘幕飞了进来，不偏不倚落在劳斯莱斯（Rolls）的一只松软坐垫上。

那小猎犬扑向那东西，可我还没来得及想，曼塞尔已把它踢飞，大喝一声「**趴下！**」，将那物件掷进厨房，随即整个人扑倒在地。

下一瞬，一声骇人的爆炸震得整座房子直摇，我真以为屋子要塌在我们头上：灯全灭了，仆人大厅顶棚上的灰泥劈头盖脸砸下来；震荡加烟尘，加碎玻璃的叮当乱响，加我后脑猛地回转来的剧痛，我的神志几乎散了，听见曼塞尔说话时，那声音像隔着老远。

===Original===
“Is anyone hurt?” he said, and called the roll.

Mercifully, no one was touched, for we had been all six in the servants’ hall: and, when he called Tester, I heard the dog rush in answer to lick his face.

“That was a bomb,” said Hanbury dazedly.

“That was a bomb,” said Mansel. “And now I withdraw what I said a moment ago. *You can shoot Rose Noble and Ellis as soon as you get the chance.*”

===Chinese===
「有人受伤没有？」他说着便挨个点名。

谢天谢地，谁也没碰着，因为我们六个当时都在仆人大厅里：他点到泰斯特时，我听见那狗飞奔应声，去舔他的脸。

「那是一颗炸弹，」汉伯里昏头昏脑地说。

「那是一颗炸弹，」曼塞尔说，「那么，我收回刚才的话。*一有机会，罗斯·诺布尔和埃利斯，你们尽管开枪。*」

===Original===
I have tried to analyse the feelings of us all at that time, with poor success.

Whether the lust for gold had mastered us, whether a hatred of the thieves suffused our outlook, whether their attempts to thwart it had but toughened our resolve I cannot say: but I know that after the bomb had been thrown into our midst, we would, one and all, have died in agony rather than let the treasure fall into the enemy’s hands.

That we found the act an outrage was, I think, reasonable: we had certainly fired upon them, as Mansel had warned them we should do: but that we had done in the open, where they had a good chance of escape and every opportunity of defending themselves. In return, they had taken a weapon of a barbarous kind, and had used it in circumstances so favourable to its energy that, had it not fallen where it did, and had the room door been shut, we must all six have perished miserably.

In view of what had happened since sundown, we knew that one phase of our struggle had come to an untimely end. Our cake, so nearly baked, had fallen back into dough, and every plan we had made was now impracticable. But, in spite of all there was to be decided, we were too much obsessed and confounded by the attempt which had so nearly made an end of us all to bring our minds to bear at all profitably upon other matters. Add to this that Mansel was physically worn out, and that Hanbury and I were suffering from the blows we had severally received.

We, therefore, lay down to sleep in the harness-room, to which, directly after the explosion, we had withdrawn. Tester was put in the Rolls, to watch the stables, and the servants were to take turns of guarding the passage which led to the kitchen hall. Yet, weary as we were, for a long time we could not sleep: and, ridiculously enough, Hanbury and I were greatly troubled by thirst: but, since our store of water stood in the kitchen, there was nothing to be done, for, supposing it was still available, no one could have reached it in silence or without showing a light.

===Chinese===
我曾试着剖析我们当时每个人的心绪，却难得要领。

是黄金的欲望攫住了我们，还是对盗贼的仇恨弥漫了我们的心境，抑或他们阻挠我们的种种伎俩反倒把我们的决心磨得更硬，我说不上来：但我知道，炸弹掷进我们中间之后，我们人人宁可痛苦万状地死去，也不肯让财宝落进敌人手里。

我们觉得这一手卑劣无耻，我想是合乎情理的：我们确曾向他们开枪，曼塞尔也预先警告过会如此：可那是在光天化日之下干的，他们有很好的逃脱机会，也尽可以自卫。而他们回敬我们的，是一件野蛮的凶器，又用在最助长其威力的场合，倘若那东西落的不是那个地方，倘若房门是关着的，我们六个人必定死得凄惨。

眼看日落以来接连发生的事，我们知道这场搏斗的一个阶段已中途夭折。我们的蛋糕，眼看就要烤熟，却又塌回了生面，拟好的每一步棋如今都行不通了。可尽管有千头万绪待定，那桩几乎断送我们全部性命的袭击盘踞在我们心头，搅得我们六神无主，对别的事再也提不起心思。何况曼塞尔体力已到强弩之末，我和汉伯里又各自带着挨打的伤痛。

于是我们到马具房里去睡——爆炸一过我们就退到了那里。泰斯特被安置进劳斯莱斯，看守马厩；仆人们则轮流守卫通往厨房大厅的那条过道。然而我们虽然疲惫，却久久不能入睡；说来可笑，我和汉伯里渴得厉害：可我们的存水全在厨房里，一点法子也没有，因为就算水还在，谁也不能不出声、不亮灯地摸到那儿去。

## III The Battle with the Springs / III 与泉水的搏斗

===Original===
When, ten minutes later, Mansel sat down on a bench and told us that we were forestalled, Hanbury and I stared at each other in dismay.

Before we could speak, Mansel proceeded to take the whole of the blame.

“I chose the wrong evil,” he said. “We had six hours’ start of Ellis, and I threw it away. I thought I was fighting a battle when I was running a race. And that was a bad mistake. And now we’ve all three got to think. Ellis has got the wheel, with the Law behind him: we’ve got the chart. The very least he expects is a compromise.”

===Chinese===
十分钟之后，曼塞尔在一张长凳上坐下，告诉我们说，我们叫人抢了先；汉伯里和我面面相觑，一时大为沮丧。

还没容我们开口，曼塞尔先把这桩不是，整个儿揽到了自己头上。

「两害相权，我挑错了要防的那一个，」他说。「我们本比埃利斯（Ellis）先走六个钟头，却叫我白白扔掉了。我以为自己在打一场仗，其实是在跑一场竞赛。这是个糟糕的错误。如今咱们三个都得动脑筋了。埃利斯握着方向盘，背后有法律撑腰；咱们握着的只有那张图。他最低的指望，也是要跟咱们讲个折中。」

===Original===
“Never,” said I.

“I agree,” said Mansel, “for every reason. And that’s why we’ve got to think how to get the wheel.”

He rose then, and, promising to return in half an hour, sauntered away with Tester at his heels.

Hanbury and I sat still in the bright sunshine, saying little, but racking our brains for some way out of the pass.

===Chinese===
「万万不能，」我说。

「我同意，」曼塞尔说，「理由有一千条。也正因如此，咱们才得动脑筋，想法子把方向盘弄到手。」

说罢他起身，答应半个钟点后回来，泰斯特（Tester）跟在脚后，他便信步走开了。

汉伯里（Hanbury）和我呆坐在明亮的日光里，不大说话，只是绞尽脑汁，想找出一条走出这步困境的路。

===Original===
To me it seemed to be surely a case of stalemate, a position which could only be relieved by our withdrawal from the field. If we had plainly retired, Ellis would hesitate to purchase, for to expend two or three thousand pounds on preventing another from taking what you would like to enjoy is the investment of a Croesus, and, though there was a chance that his purchase would suggest to us the wisdom of coming to terms, to stake so much money so blindly would be unwarrantable. And I think that Hanbury thought the same, for beyond insisting that Mansel had made no mistake, to which I heartily agreed, he only remarked that the race was seldom to the swift, and that he was glad to have seen Salzburg.

The half-hour had not expired before Mansel came back, and, after glancing at his watch, sat down on the bench between us and asked me once again to describe Ellis.

I did so carefully.

“That was the fellow I saw,” said Hanbury.

“Very well,” said Mansel. “Now, listen. A week ago in London we dined at the Carlton Grill. There and then we agreed to buy Wagensburg, if the place was for sale. At the table next to us was seated a coarse-looking man, who seemed more than once to be listening to what we said. All of us noticed this, but, after we left the grillroom, we thought no more of the matter and went our ways. Yesterday we saw him again in the streets of Salzburg, and it occurs to us that he is the man who holds an option to purchase Wagensburg. If we are right, it is clear that he is no genuine purchaser, but an unprincipled villain, who is merely seeking to enrich himself *at the vendor’s expense*.” With that he rose to his feet and knocked out his pipe. “And now, we’ll go back to the house-agent. We’re all very angry, you know: but if I seem too much annoyed, you can try to calm me down.”

===Chinese===
在我看来，这分明是一局僵棋，唯一的解法，是我们从棋盘上撤身。倘若我们公然退走，埃利斯在收买上便会踌躇：因为花上两三千镑，去阻拦旁人取走你自己也乐意享用的东西，那是克罗伊斯（Croesus）式的豪举；虽说他的收购也可能提醒我们，讲条件方为聪明，可这样盲目押上偌大一笔钱，于理说不通。我想汉伯里心里也是一般想法：他除了力说曼塞尔并没有失算——这一点我打心底赞成——只补了一句，说捷足者未必先登，又说，他很高兴总算看过萨尔茨堡（Salzburg）了。

半个钟点还没到，曼塞尔便回来了；他瞥了一眼表，在我们中间的长凳上坐下，又叫我讲一遍埃利斯的模样。

我仔仔细细讲了一遍。

「就是我在街上见的那个家伙，」汉伯里说。

「好，」曼塞尔说。「听着。一个星期前，我们在伦敦的卡尔顿烤肉馆（Carlton Grill）吃饭。就在那里、当时，我们议定：这地方若肯出卖，便买下瓦根斯堡（Wagensburg）。邻桌坐着一个粗相的男人，似乎不止一次侧耳听我们说话。我们人人留意到了，可一出烤肉间，便把这事丢开，各自走散。昨天我们在萨尔茨堡街上又见着他，我们便想到：他正是那个握有优先购买瓦根斯堡之权的人。若是我们没猜错，那么分明可见，他并不是真心买主，而是一个毫无信义的恶棍，一心只想*损卖家而自肥*。」说到这里，他站起身，磕了磕烟斗。「现在，我们回房产经纪人那里去。我们个个火冒三丈，这你是知道的：要是我显得太动火，你便设法劝我息怒。」

===Original===
What followed I shall never forget.

By the time we arrived at the office, Mansel was seemingly beside himself with rage, and, when we were presently admitted to the agent’s room, he began to storm and rave like any madman. At first, such was his incoherence, that the agent was frightened to death, but, so soon as he gathered that Mansel was not angry with him, but with some common enemy, he became greatly excited, and, apparently catching the frenzy which possessed Mansel, demanded with howls of fury to be informed of the truth. This he was so long denied that I thought he would have lost his reason, for Mansel, while withholding the facts, never ceased to recite the most horrid and galling conclusions, and the unfortunate agent was actually squinting with emotion when Mansel had mercy upon him and told him his tale.

To judge from its effect, he told it very well, for, long before he had finished, the agent’s eyes were burning with wrath and indignation, and, when Mansel said he would wager that Ellis had never set foot in Carinthia, much less laid eyes upon Wagensburg, the other screeched that it was true, and that, when he had opened and shown him his book of photographs of properties for sale, Ellis had actually studied the opposite page. That Mansel would not have committed such an error was very obvious, for he had spoken throughout as though he knew Carinthia as well as the palm of his hand, and had constantly referred to castles and villages by name and to Wagensburg itself with a skilful familiarity which would have deceived a Judge. Indeed, all things considered, it was not at all surprising that the agent was swept off his feet and, snatching up a copy of the letter he had yesterday sent to Ellis, thrust it into Mansel’s hands and demanded brokenly to be told what he should do.

The letter acknowledged the receipt of five pounds, and stated that, by virtue of having paid that sum, Ellis had secured the sole right to purchase Wagensburg for two thousand five hundred pounds, and that this right would endure for one calendar month.

===Chinese===
接下去那一幕，我永远不会忘记。

等我们到了事务所，曼塞尔看上去已是怒气攻心、不能自持；我们一被让进经纪人的房间，他便大吵大闹，活脱脱一个疯子。起初他语无伦次，经纪人吓得魂飞魄散；可他一待听明白，曼塞尔恼的并非他，而是共同的仇家，便也大为激愤，像是染上了缠住曼塞尔的那股疯劲，嚎叫着非要把实情打听出来不可。这实情他讨了半天也没讨到，我以为他都要急疯了：曼塞尔把事实按住不说，只管把最骇人、最气人的结论一遍遍数落，那可怜的经纪人竟激动得眼睛发斜，曼塞尔这才发了善心，把原委讲给他听。

看它收效如何，便知这段故事讲得极好：还没讲完，经纪人的两眼已经烧起怒火与愤慨；等曼塞尔说他敢打赌，埃利斯从没踏进克恩滕（Carinthia）一步，更不用说见过瓦根斯堡了，对方尖声叫道这话不假，还说，他翻开他那本待售产业的照相簿给埃利斯看时，埃利斯竟对着对页看了个仔细。曼塞尔绝不会出这样的纰漏，这一点再明显不过：因为他自始至终的谈吐，就像对克恩滕了如指掌，时时点出城堡村庄的名字，说起瓦根斯堡更是那份圆熟老到的熟稔，连法官也要被他瞒过。总而言之，经纪人被掀了个跟头，实在不足为奇；他一把抓起昨天写给埃利斯的那封信的底子，塞进曼塞尔手里，语不成声地求他指点，该怎么办才好。

那封信确认收到五镑，声明埃利斯既已付清此数，便取得了以两千五百镑购买瓦根斯堡的专有权利，此项权利有效期为一个月历月。

===Original===
“Lease me the property today,” said Mansel, laying the letter down, “for fifty years at a rent of five pounds a year: in return, I’ll undertake, if Ellis doesn’t exercise his option﻿—and when he hears of the lease I don’t believe he will﻿—to purchase Wagensburg forthwith for three thousand pounds.”

For a moment the agent stared, then he began to laugh like a maniac; and Mansel with him.

The two laughed till they cried, as did Hanbury and I, for, though we did not understand what was the joke, unmoved to witness such paroxysms of mirth was beyond our power.

===Chinese===
「今天就把这产业租给我，」曼塞尔放下那封信说，「租期五十年，年租五镑；作为回报，我承担一笔买卖：埃利斯若不行使他的优先购买权——一听到这份租约，我相信他就不会行使——我便立时出三千镑，买下瓦根斯堡。」

经纪人愣了片刻，随即发狂似的笑起来；曼塞尔也跟着笑。

两人笑出了眼泪，我和汉伯里也一样，因为虽说我们不明白笑点在哪里，眼见这样一阵狂笑的发作，要无动于衷，是万万办不到的。

===Original===
At last:

“Prepare the papers,” said Mansel, sinking into a chair and taking out banknotes. “I’ll give you three hundred pounds as an earnest of the purchase price, to be returned to me if Ellis goes on.”

With that, he turned to Hanbury and me and told us what he had arranged, while the agent ran into an adjoining room and began to give instructions to one of his clerks. Very soon we heard a typewriter in action and within half an hour the two Agreements had been signed. Then Mansel wrote the agent a cheque for twenty-five pounds and said that that was compensation for the trouble and annoyance he had caused by discussing his private affairs in a public place: and so we parted, full of goodwill and understanding, which were immensely enhanced by the knowledge that we had undone a common enemy.

At six o’clock the next morning we left for Lerai, servants and baggage and all, in the two cars. We spent the night at the inn, and the next day, in compliance with instructions from Salzburg, the postmaster brought us the keys of Wagensburg.

Be sure we had taken possession within the hour.

===Chinese===
末了：

「把文书备好，」曼塞尔说着陷进一把椅子，掏出钞票。「我先给你三百镑，作购价的定金；埃利斯若照样买下去，这笔钱退还给我。」

说着他转向我和汉伯里，把他安排停当的事体讲了一遍；那厢经纪人跑进隔壁房间，吩咐起手下一名文书。不多时便听得打字机响动，半个钟点之内，两份协约都签了字。随后曼塞尔给经纪人开了一张二十五镑的支票，说这是赔补他在公共场所谈论私事、给人家添的麻烦与烦恼：于是我们尽欢而散，满怀好意与体谅；而一想到我们已经扳倒了一个共同的仇敌，这份好意与体谅愈发深厚。

第二天清早六点，我们带着仆人、行李，连人带物，坐上那两辆汽车，往勒拉（Lerai）进发。当夜宿在客栈；第二天，邮政局长奉萨尔茨堡方面的指示，把瓦根斯堡的钥匙送来交给我们。

不出一个钟头，我们便已接管了堡子，这还用说么。

---

===Original===
To pick our quarters was plainly the first thing to do, and, after a short consideration, we decided to use the kitchen and servants’ hall. These were both spacious, and looked not into the courtyard, but on to the woods and meadows towards the great well. They were served by a decent hall, with a house door at either end, and a passage led into the stables by way of a harness-room. All this was very convenient. As well as possessing a certain privacy, the rooms were easy of access and could be approached directly from either side of the mansion: once within the stables, the cars would be under our hand; and, whether they were in the kitchen or in the harness-room, where it was arranged they should sleep, our servants would never be more than a few steps away.

So soon as the decision was taken, that part of the house was opened, the stable doors were set wide and the servants fell to cleaning our quarters as hard as they could “for,” said Mansel, “once we’re dug in, we’ve little or nothing to fear: but come on a man whose house is out of order, and you’ve an ally in his camp who is worth as much as yourself.”

Then he gave me a map and binocular, and asked me to stay on the terrace as sentinel, desiring me to locate what roads I could see and, when I had done that, to take Tester and prove the ground towards Lerai and see if there was a spot conveniently near from which the village and the bridge could be observed.

Then he and Hanbury began to unload the cars, unpacking the stores which we had bought at Salzburg and disclosing a quantity of stuff which we had brought from England, prominent among which were some electrical apparatus and a great deal of wire.

===Chinese===
头一件事，分明是选定我们的住处。略一商量，我们便决定用厨房和仆人厅。这两间都宽敞，不朝院子，却对着树林和草地，朝着大井那面。它们由一间还算体面的大厅进出，厅的两头各有一扇正门，又有一条过道，经过马具房通进马厩。这一切都很便利。这几间屋子既有一点私密，进出又方便，从宅子的哪一头都能径直走进去：一进马厩，汽车便在我们手边；而我们的仆人，不论是在厨房，还是在马具房——那是说定了他们睡觉的地方——离我们都不过几步。

决定一定，那部分房子便开了，马厩大门敞开，仆人们赶紧动手，打扫我们的住处。「因为，」曼塞尔说，「一旦安营扎寨，我们便没什么可怕的了；可要是碰上一个家里没安顿利索的人，那他营中便有了你一个内应，抵得上你自己。」

随后他交给我一张地图和一副望远镜，叫我留在露台上放哨，要我把望得见的道路一一辨明；办完之后，再带上泰斯特，朝勒拉那面踏勘一遍，看看近处有没有合适的地方，可以瞭望村子和那座桥。

然后他同汉伯里开始卸车，把我们在萨尔茨堡置办的给养一一取出，又亮出我们从英国带来的一大批物件，其中显要的是几件电气器材和一大卷电线。

===Original===
I saw no more, for I had my work to do, but, when I came back with my report﻿—which was negative, for I could discover no point at all reasonably near from which the village could be viewed﻿—two of the servants were washing the empty cars, the kitchen fire was burning, the hall was full of gear, orderly arranged, a table was set for luncheon under a tree, and the band of a well-known London restaurant was making us free of a selection from *La Bohême*.

When our meal was over, we sat and smoked on the terrace, while the servants were eating theirs, and then, for the first time, I began to appreciate the full charm of our surroundings.

The grandeur of the landscape which the terrace was commanding, as no royal box ever commanded a stage, the dignity of the pleasance upon which we sat, and the high woods all about us, made our present life seem like a handsome dream: and the silence, the sunlight and the sweet air showed me a side of Nature such as I never expect to see anywhere else.

===Chinese===
别的我没有再看见，因为我也各有职守；等我带着回话回来——回话是否定的，望得见村子的近处，一个点也寻不出——两个仆人正在擦洗空车，厨房的火烧着，大厅里摆满器材，安置得井井有条，树下已经摆好午饭的桌子，伦敦一家名馆子的乐队，正奏起*《波希米亚人》*的选段给我们受用。

饭吃完，我们在露台上坐着吸烟，仆人们则吃他们的；这时我才头一回领略到，我们这方风物的魅力究竟有多大。

露台统摄着的那片山水是何等壮丽——没有哪座皇家包厢这样统摄过戏台——我们坐处这片园地是何等庄严，四围又是何等参天的林木，都叫我们眼前这日子像一场华美的梦：那份静穆、那片日光、那缕甜香的空气，让我看见了大自然的一面；这样的一面，我在别处再不敢指望见到。

===Original===
My musing, however, was soon ended, for Mansel asked me to point out the roads I had managed to identify, and, though these were few, before he and Hanbury had imbibed what information I had, the servants had finished eating and Carson and Bell had returned to their work on the cars.

We then went to work with the wire which Mansel had brought, and, after two hours, had laid an invisible trap across the road of approach, and clean around the castle as far as the garden door which we were to use. This we connected to a battery, and then to a bell which hung in the kitchen hall, and, after a little adjustment, to our great content the arrangement worked very well, for the slightest pressure at any point was instantly reported. Then we laid a wire between the stables and the kitchen, and another from the kitchen to the great well, and so established a means of communication which might at any moment prove of great value. When this had been done, we turned our attention to installing electric light, and before sundown our quarters were adequately illumined, and the searchlight which belonged to the Rolls was able, at our will, to reveal the depths of the well, as I will warrant they had never been revealed before. Then supper was served, and Mansel drew up some orders for the following day. These will sufficiently appear, but he wrote out some general orders which stayed pinned up in the hall, and, so far as I can remember, this was how they ran.

===Chinese===
可我这番神游很快便断了：曼塞尔要我把我辨明的道路一一指给他看；虽则寥寥无几，可他同汉伯里还没把我这点见闻听进去，仆人们已经吃罢，卡森（Carson）和贝尔（Bell）也回去照旧侍弄汽车了。

于是我们动手用曼塞尔带来的电线布置，两个钟头之后，一道看不见的绊线已在进路上布好，绕着堡子整整一圈，直到我们要用的那道园门。绊线接上电池组，再接到厨房大厅里挂着的一口铃；略一调试，这布置竟运转得称心如意：随便哪一点略一受压，铃上立时便知。随后我们在马厩和厨房之间牵一条线，又从厨房牵一条到大井，从此有了一种随时可能大显身手的传信法子。这桩事做完，我们便转去装电灯；日头未落，住处已经照得通亮，劳斯莱斯（Rolls）上那盏探照灯，听凭我们的意思，便能照彻井底——这样的照法，我敢担保它此前从未领教。接着晚饭摆上来，曼塞尔又起草了次日的几道命令。这些下文自见；他还写下几条通则，一直钉在大厅里；就我记忆所及，条文是这样：

===Original===
1. Reveille at 4 a.m.
 Breakfast at 7 a.m.
 Supper one hour after sundown.
2. No light of any kind which can be seen from the courtyard will be shown even for an instant.
3. Accumulators will be charged, if necessary, from 4:15 a.m. and, in any event, each engine will be started and run for five minutes from that hour.
4. So long as any sign of occupation can be seen from the courtyard, the road of approach will be watched.
5. The alarm tape, wires and bells will be tested at reveille, dinnertime and sundown.
6. In addition to their other duties: Carson will take sole charge of the cars and the electrical apparatus. Rowley will act as quartermaster and cook. Bell will clean the quarters and maintain the water supply.
7. No one will leave the castle without acquainting me.
8. The first sign of any approach will be immediately signalled.
9. There will always be someone in the kitchen if there is no one on guard.

===Chinese===
1. 凌晨 4 点起床。
 7 点用早饭。
 日落后一小时用晚饭。
2. 凡从院子里望得见的任何光亮，即便一瞬，也不得外露。
3. 必要时，自凌晨 4:15 起给蓄电池充电；无论如何，自那一刻起，每台发动机都须发动并空转五分钟。
4. 只要从院子里望得出一丝有人居住的迹象，进路便须有人瞭望。
5. 报警绊带、电线和警铃，须在起床、午饭和日落时试验。
6. 除各自其他职分外：卡森专管汽车与电气器材。罗利任军需兼司厨。贝尔负责打扫住处、维持供水。
7. 不经我知晓，任何人不得离开堡子。
8. 一有来人逼近的头一个迹象，立即发出信号。
9. 若无人放哨，厨房里须始终有人。

===Original===
I can remember no more, but these rules show that Mansel did all that he could to guard against surprise and to insure that our work should go forward with the least embarrassment.

For all that, we were none too well placed. To gain the curtilage of the castle, without being observed, was singularly easy, and the well was two hundred yards from the garden door, so that anyone at all curious and prudent could watch our labour by the hour, and any sudden, determined aggression by three or four armed men would be difficult to counter.

We sat on the terrace for a while before going to bed, and heard some music from London, and at length the news, and, but for Mansel, Hanbury and I, I think, would have sat there most of the night, for a full moon was shining out of an empty sky and the prospect which the terrace commanded seemed more lovely than ever.

===Chinese===
能记得的只有这些；但这些条文可见，曼塞尔凡能做到的都做了，为的是防备突袭，也让我们的活计顺顺当当、少受掣肘。

尽管如此，我们的处境也说不上安稳。不叫人瞧见便摸进堡子的院墙之内，再容易不过；井离园门足有两百码，谁只要稍有一点好奇心、又稍会拣地方，尽可以一连几个钟头瞧着我们的劳作；三四个带枪的家伙要是突然发难、志在必得，我们也很难招架。

睡觉前，我们在露台上又坐了一会儿，听了些伦敦来的音乐，末了还听了新闻；要不是曼塞尔，我想我和汉伯里会一直坐到大半夜，因为一轮满月挂在空荡荡的夜空里，露台上望出去的景致，似乎比什么时候都更可爱。

===Original===
We were up and abroad the next morning before it was light, and, as soon as the alarums had been tested, repaired to the well.

Mansel had all ready a measuring-line. This was a very fine cord knotted at every inch and tagged at each foot, and at each end was fastened a lump of lead. The use of this proved that what the well-digger had said of its capacity was substantially correct, for the well was two-thirds full of water, and its depth was ninety-six feet. Allowing for the facts that the man would have spoken of metres, and not of feet, and that in so many years a deposit had surely been formed at the bottom of the well, we had no reason to suppose that his actual figures were not as good as ours. Sixty-two feet of water lay in the well.

I have already said that the well was twelve feet across, and, after a moment’s calculation, Hanbury drew in his breath.

===Chinese===
第二天清早，天没亮我们就起身出门；警报装置一试过，便奔井边去了。

曼塞尔早已备下一条测深绳：一根极细的细绳，每隔一英寸打一个结，每一英尺处系一个小牌，两端各坠一块铅。用它一量，便证明那打井人说的容量大体不差：井里存着三分之二的水，井深九十六英尺。考虑到那人报数用的想必是米，不是英尺，又考虑到这么多年，井底必有淤积，我们没有理由不信，他报的实数同我们量的一样准。井里的水，足有六十二英尺。

我已说过，这井口宽十二英尺；汉伯里心算了一下，倒抽了一口气。

===Original===
“That chamber is sealed,” he said, “faster, I fancy, than even the well-digger dreamed. If we had a pump﻿—”

“We should be no better off,” said Mansel. “A pump can push water, but to pull it up from a depth is beyond its power. If we installed a plant to supply the house, to make sure of water we should have to sink the pump; and no pump that ever was made could empty this well. Whether we can do it by bucket I can’t possibly tell, but I’m told that the last six months have been unusually dry, and so, if we go all out, I think we may be able to beat the springs.”

Hanbury examined the cupola which sheltered the well.

“We have two pulleys,” he said. “If we put a beam across and above the windlass, we can use two buckets together and, with the windlass, three.”

===Chinese===
「那间石室是封死了，」他说，「依我看，封得比打井人梦里想的还要严实。我们要是有一台水泵——」

「也讨不到多大便宜，」曼塞尔说。「水泵能压水，可要把水从深处提上来，它无能为力。倘若装一套供宅子用的设备，想保住水，就得把泵沉下去；可天底下造出来的泵，谁也抽不干这口井。靠桶能不能成，我不敢说；不过听说过去六个月雨水出奇地少，所以只要我们豁出去干，我看未必斗不过这些泉水。」

汉伯里把遮着井口的圆顶亭察看了一番。

「我们有两只滑轮，」他说。「在绞盘上方横架一根梁，便能同时吊两只桶；再加上绞盘吊的那只，便是三只。」

===Original===
“That’s right,” said Mansel. “And now here’s another point. We must make a gutter to take the water away. If we don’t do that it’ll find its way back to the well.”

Then he picked up a stirrup and, looping the leather on to the hook which was dangling over the well, asked me if I would go down and see what I could.

I was only too ready to comply, but before I descended he made me put on a warm coat against the chill, and fastened about me a length of fine wire rope. I then set my foot in the stirrup and gripped the chain, and, Hanbury working the windlass and Mansel paying out the rope, I passed down into the well.

===Chinese===
「对，」曼塞尔说。「还有一层。必须做一道水槽把水引开。不这样，水会自个儿流回井里去。」

说着他拿起一副马镫，把皮带圈挂到悬在井上的钩子上，问我肯不肯下去，看看能有何作为。

我求之不得；可临下井前，他硬叫我穿上一件暖和的外套御寒，又给我周身系好一截细钢丝绳。我把脚踩进镫里，抓紧链条；汉伯里摇着绞盘，曼塞尔松着绳，我便缓缓沉进井里去了。

===Original===
When I was close to the water, I bade them stop, and, when they had made all fast, they let down the searchlight.

The well was beautifully built, and I do not believe such masonry is often rendered today. The stones had been finely cut, and, though no doubt they were cemented together, they were fitted so closely that the cement did not appear. There were no bars at all, nor any foot or handhold that I could see, but presently I discovered a series of niches regularly cut in the wall, one above the other and about two feet apart. They were scarce an inch deep, and I could not believe that there was any man, living or dead, daring or skilful enough to rise or descend by this means. Happening, however, to look round, I perceived another series, exactly corresponding, a quarter of the way round the well. These were more deeply cut; and it immediately occurred to me that these niches had supported one beam of a wooden stage on which the men had stood whilst building the wall. Sure enough, on the opposite side were the niches for the other beam, one twice as deep as the other: so that, given four clean-cut beams, the men who were working could have raised their stage as they pleased, without any help, two feet at a time. One or two pieces of wood were rotting on the surface of the water, and, here and there, little ferns were growing apparently out of the wall, but, otherwise, except that the sides were weather-stained, the well, so far as I could see, was as sound as a bell, and might have only been finished the week before. I could not see the spring working, although I peered very hard, and, since the surface of the water seemed to be motionless, I came to the conclusion that such water as we had drawn had been replaced in the night, and that, having filled this pool to the brim, the little underground stream had resumed its course. And here I soon saw I was right, for there was no watermark above the surface, and the well was plainly full.

All this information I cried to Mansel and Hanbury, and both were greatly delighted at the fine condition of the well; but, as I hung there, seemingly so far beneath them, the magnitude of our task appalled me, and I felt that the silent guardian of the leather bags was to prove a more formidable enemy than Ellis himself.

Then they pulled me up, and the atmosphere seemed sultry after the chill of the well.

===Chinese===
快到水面时，我叫他们停住；待他们一一拴牢，便把探照灯放了下去。

这井砌得极精工，这样的石工，如今怕是不多见了。石头琢得齐整；虽然无疑是用灰浆粘合的，却拼合得天衣无缝，看不见灰浆的踪影。井壁没有铁条，也望不见一处落脚或攀手的地方；不过不多时，我发现壁上凿着一排壁龛，上下成列，彼此相隔约两英尺。龛深还不到一英寸，我不信古今哪一个人，胆子再大、本事再高，能凭这些爬上爬下。可是碰巧一转身，我又望见同样的一排，在绕井四分之一圈的地方。这一排凿得深些；我立刻想到，这些壁龛原是承一根木梁的，砌井的人便站在搭在梁上的木架上干活。果然，正对面又有承另一根梁的壁龛，一深一浅，浅的恰是深的一半：只要备下四根削得方正的木梁，干活的人便能随自己的意思，不用旁人帮手，一次两英尺，把木架升上去。水面上漂着一两块朽木，井壁各处又钻出些小小的蕨草；除此之外，井壁只带着风渍，这口井据我所能望见的，结实完好，仿佛上个星期才完工。我瞪大眼睛使劲看，也看不见泉眼出水；水面似乎纹丝不动，于是我断定，我们提走的水已在夜里补足，那条小小的地下水道灌满这潭水之后，便又照旧流它的去了。这一点，我很快便知道自己没有猜错，因为水面以上不见水渍，井分明是满的。

这些见闻，我大声报给曼塞尔和汉伯里；井这样完好，两人都喜不自胜。可当我悬在那里、仿佛在他们脚下极深极深的地方，我们这项工程的分量把我压住了；我觉得，这守着皮袋的沉默卫兵，怕要证明是个比埃利斯更难对付的仇敌。

随后他们把我拉了上来；井里的阴寒一过，外面的空气倒像有些闷热。

===Original===
Then we went back to the stables, where was some sawn wood, and before it was time for breakfast we had fixed a beam upon the free pillars immediately under the dome, and had lashed a pulley into place upon either side. Whilst we were doing this, Carson and Bell were bringing up planks, of which we had found quite a store, for with these we meant to make a gutter, in the shape of an endless trough, to conduct the water away. By the time we had settled upon the line this should take, it was seven o’clock, so we left the business there and returned to the house.

At breakfast we came to a decision of some importance. This was, plainly, that we should not attempt to conceal our endeavours to empty the well. It was, therefore, arranged that Mansel should drive to Lerai, as soon as he had bathed, and acquaint the landlord of the inn with our intention.
