The stone below must have been loose, for, the moment we levered, it yielded, and a second later I pulled it out with my hands. The two below came away, and, as Hanbury gave me the searchlight, I heard a strangled cry.

===Chinese===

得给撬杠腾出地方：于是我又拿起镐，其余的人把家什排开，预备最后的冲击。

这当儿锉声没停；听那金属的震响，有一根铁条已快断成两截。果然，我一撂下镐，那声音又停了，跟着传来几下捶击，仿佛那干活的人相信，剩下那一丝，捶也能捶断。

我们不再等了。

我把凿子的刃口嵌进石缝；我扶住它，曼塞尔抡锤把它砸到底。

底下那块石头想必本是松的；一撬便动，一秒钟后我已用手把它抽出来。底下那两块也跟着脱出来；汉伯里把探照灯递给我时，我听见一声闷哑的喊叫。

===Original===

On the tiny floor was a bag, thick covered with dust, of the shape of a sack of corn. Its mouth was shut, but one of its sides was gaping and spilling the stuff it held. By its side was another: but this was all gone to ruin, and its contents lay in a heap. The dust lay so deep over all that it might have been trash, but I saw the shape of a crucifix standing up out of the ruck.

Immediately opposite was the entrance, barred by the four iron bars. Behind these I saw two faces, unshaven, like those of beasts. The one I had never seen, but the other was that of Ellis: and that I believe I shall see so long as I live, for if ever the devil possessed the soul of a man he possessed it then.

The other seemed blinded by the searchlight: but Ellis glared full at the lamp, as though it were no more than a taper, with his face working with passion and his eyes starting out of his head.

===Chinese===

小小的地面上有一口袋子，蒙着厚厚的尘，样子像一口袋谷子。袋口是扎着的，一侧却豁着口，正往外淌它盛的东西。旁边还有一口：可那一口已经烂尽，袋里的东西摊作一堆。灰积得那么深，瞧着满可以当废物；可我看见破烂堆里立着一只十字架的轮廓。

正对面便是入口，四根铁条拦着。铁条后头，我看见两张脸，须髯乱蓬蓬，像野兽的脸。一张我从未见过；另一张是埃利斯（Ellis）：我相信这一张，只要我活着，便会一直看见；因为若说魔鬼曾占据过人的魂灵，那一刻占据的便是他。

那另一个仿佛被探照灯照瞎了眼；埃利斯却直瞪着灯，好像那不过是一支细烛：满脸狂怒地抽搐，眼珠几乎爆出眶外。

===Original===

Suddenly he laid hold of the bars and wrenched them this way and that, screaming, like some animal with rage; and, when they would not yield to his frenzy, he clapped his face up against them and spat like any demoniac, lending the whole force of his body to this disgusting act. Then he started, as one who recovers his presence of mind, and I saw a hand fly to his hip.

At that moment Mansel fired, and the fellow fell suddenly forward against the bars. As he did so, the other man turned, and Mansel fired again. But, when the noise had subsided, we could hear him descending the shaft.

Then Ellis’ body slipped sideways, till the head was against the wall, which held it up at an angle which was different to that of the trunk.

“God forgive me,” said Mansel, “but I’d do it again.”

===Chinese===

突然他抓住铁条，疯子似的左右猛拽，尖声嘶叫，像一头盛怒的兽；铁条抵死不动，他便把脸贴上去，恶鬼一般地啐起来，全身的力气都使在这桩叫人恶心的举动上。随后他猛地一怔，像是回过神来；我看见一只手飞向他的腰间。

就在那一刻，曼塞尔开了枪；那家伙猛地朝前一栽，抵在铁条上。他这么一栽，另一个转身要走；曼塞尔又是一枪。响声沉下去之后，我们还听得见他顺着竖井往下爬。

随后埃利斯的身子歪向一边，头抵住了墙；墙把它支着，支起的角度同躯干的并不一样。

「上帝饶恕我，」曼塞尔说，「可再来一回，我还是要干。」

===Original===

With that, I climbed into the chamber, and Mansel followed me in.

With the hammer and crowbar, we soon had three more stones out, and Hanbury made his way in.

Then Mansel told Rowley to give him the canvas bags.

“And ‘Quick’ ’s the motto,” said he. “I missed the tanner, and now he’ll give the alarm. But, once we’re beyond the postern, they can have the oubliette.”

===Chinese===

说罢，我爬进石室；曼塞尔跟着进来。

拿锤子和撬棍，不多时我们又起出三块石头；汉伯里也进来了。

随后曼塞尔叫罗利把帆布袋递给他。

「如今这局就得讲一个『快』字，」他说，「制革匠没打中，他这就去报信了。可只要咱们过了边门，囚井便随他们拿去。」

===Original===

Rowley had brought the bags and was standing without the chamber, looking in, with his hands on the wall, when I heard a rustle behind him and saw him drop suddenly forward across the breach we had made.

A mass of soil had broken away from the “window” and had fallen on the back of his legs.

He was not hurt, but was pinned; and, whilst I supported his body, Mansel and Hanbury climbed back into the tunnel and shifted the fallen earth.

They worked feverishly; but two or three minutes went by before he was free and I was able to help him into the chamber.

===Chinese===

罗利已把袋子拿来，正站在石室外面，双手扶着墙朝里望；这时我听见他背后一阵簌簌，只见他猛地朝前一扑，横在我们凿开的豁口上。

好大一块土从「窗子」上塌下来，砸在他两条腿的后头。

他没受伤，却被压住了；我架住他的身子，曼塞尔和汉伯里爬回坑道，把塌下来的土挪开。

他们狂乱地干着；可两三分钟过去，他才脱了身，我才把他扶进石室。

===Original===

“And that,” breathed Mansel, brushing the dirt from his hands, “is about as clear a time-signal as ever there was.”

I held one canvas bag open and Rowley the other, while Mansel and Hanbury shovelled the stuff within. There were gold and stones and jewels and all manner of lovely things, but I think we were thinking of safety and the way to the oubliette.

Then Mansel lifted his head and touched Hanbury on the arm.

For a moment we knelt there, listening.

Then, something faint, but clear, came Tester’s vigorous bark.

===Chinese===

「这个么，」曼塞尔掸着手上的土，低声说，「算是顶顶分明不过的一声报时信号了。」

我撑开一口帆布袋，罗利撑开另一口，曼塞尔和汉伯里把东西往里铲。有金子，有宝石，有珠玉，有各色可爱的物事；可我想，我们心里想的只有脱身，同通往囚井的路。

随后曼塞尔抬起头，碰了碰汉伯里的胳膊。

有那么一会儿，我们跪在那里，侧耳听着。

接着，微弱却分明的，传来泰斯特起劲的一声吠。

===Original===

And that was the only time we heard him give tongue that night, for the next instant came a shuffling and then a rumbling sound; and, when we had brought the searchlight up to the breach in the wall, had we not known its angle, we could not have told where the shaft we had driven had been.

“Where’s the pickaxe?” said Mansel quietly.

I told him it had lain in the shaft.

Then came another rumble and the searchlight went out.

===Chinese===

那一夜，我们只听见他开了这一回口；因为下一刻起了一阵窸窣，跟着是一阵轰隆；等我们把探照灯举到墙上的豁口，若不是记得方位，简直认不出我们掘的那条坑道原来打哪里过。

「镐呢？」曼塞尔平静地说。

我告诉他，镐原撂在坑道里。

跟着又是一阵轰隆，探照灯灭了。

## II The Way to Wagensburg / 二 通往瓦根斯堡之路

===Original===
It was the next morning, when I was walking down St. James’s Street on my way to the Bank, that I stopped to glance at the maps which were spread in a shopwindow. I had done so many times before, for I often went by that way and, though I am no geographer, a map or a plan has for me some attraction to which I invariably yield. I had taken my look and was just about to pass on when I suddenly observed before me a map of Southern Austria, drawn to a large scale.

I was naturally most interested and at once began to look for Wagensburg, for so large was the scale that the property might well have been marked: but, though I soon saw Villach, most of the names were not at all easy to read, for the country was plainly very mountainous and the lettering was often lost against the heavy shading of the heights. For all that, if I could have gone closer I think I might soon have found the name I was seeking, but the map was some way from the glass, and I could not even stand fairly in front of it, because of another idler, who was standing before the window, regarding its wares. I waited a moment or two, expecting that he would pass on, but he did not, so I approached my face as close to the pane as I could without flattening my nose, in one last endeavour to locate the castle before I gave up the attempt. At this, the other seemed to notice my presence and turned to look at me, and, when instinctively I glanced at him, I saw that it was Ellis himself.

I do not know which of us was the more taken aback, but Ellis was the first to recover and turn away. For myself, I stood gaping and staring after him, as he walked rather jerkily away towards Piccadilly.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，我沿圣詹姆斯街（St. James's Street）走去银行，半途在一处橱窗前停下脚步，看了看陈列的地图。这样的事我做过多回，因为那条路我常走；我虽算不得地理家，一张地图或一幅平面图对我却总有种说不出的吸引力，叫我回回都招架不住。我照例望了一眼，正要走开，忽见眼前挂着一幅奥地利南部的地图，比例尺画得很大。

我自然大感兴趣，立刻找起瓦根斯堡（Wagensburg）来，比例尺那么大，那处庄园很可能标得上去：虽然不久便望见了菲拉赫（Villach），多数地名却很难认，因为那片国土显然山峦重重，注字常常湮没在群峰浓重的阴影里。话虽如此，若能凑近一点，我想很快便能寻到我要找的名字；可那地图离橱窗玻璃颇有一段距离，我连正对着它站都站不成——另有一个闲人先在窗前站着，端详橱窗里的货色。我等了一两分钟，指望他走开，他却不走；我便把脸尽可能凑向玻璃，只差没把鼻尖压扁，作最后一搏，想在放弃之前寻到那座城堡。就在这时，那人似乎留意到我在场，转过脸来望我；我出于本能回看了他一眼，一看之下，认出那正是埃利斯（Ellis）本人。

我说不上我们两人谁更吃惊，但埃利斯先回过神来，转身走了。至于我，只是张口结舌，直愣愣望着他的背影，看他步子发僵地朝皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）走去。

===Original===
My first impulse was to follow him, though what good that would have done I do not know: and indeed I started uncertainly to hasten up the street in his wake; but what design I had was soon frustrated, for he entered a cab which was crawling close to the kerb and was instantly driven away.

I have often wondered what would have been his feelings if he had known of the statement which lay in my breast-pocket, while we were shoulder to shoulder before the window, and whether he would not have made some desperate attempt to possess himself of the document there and then: and, all things considered, I verily believe he would have tried, for, because of that paper, he had already put his neck in a noose, and, as the saying is, a man may “as well be hanged for a sheep as a lamb.”

The disappearance of Ellis must have restored my wits, for I realized in a flash that the first thing which I must do was to inform Mansel. I, therefore, ran down the street to Cleveland Row and happily found him in the act of leaving his flat.

Directly he saw me, he turned, and I followed him through his hall and into the dining-room.

===Chinese===
我头一个念头便是跟上去，虽然跟上又能怎样，我也说不出；而且我确实起步踉跄，沿着街快步追去。可这点算盘顷刻落空，因为他钻进了一辆正贴着路边缓行的出租车，车子立时开走了。

我们肩并肩站在橱窗前的时候，假如他知道我胸口袋里放着那份供状，心中会作何感想，会不会当场孤注一掷，把那份文件夺到手——这一点我时常纳闷；而通盘想来，我实在相信他会动手，因为为了那张纸，他已经把脖子伸进了绞索；俗话说得好，反正「偷大羊是绞刑，偷羊羔也是绞刑」。

埃利斯一走，我方才回过神来，因为我恍然省得，头一件要办的事便是通知曼塞尔（Mansel）。于是我沿街奔到克利夫兰罗街（Cleveland Row），运气不坏，正赶上他要出门。

他一见我，便转过身去，我跟着他穿过门厅，进了餐室。

===Original===
“Yes?” he said shortly.

I told my tale, and he frowned.

“I don’t know whether this is good luck or bad luck,” he said. “But I think it’s bad luck. Anyway we must take no chances.” He thought for a moment there. Then he went on: “Ellis now knows for certain that Wagensburg interests you. If your excitement was apparent, he may even suspect that you hold the secret itself. That you made no attempt to detain him wouldn’t weigh much with me, because a good many people would hesitate to seize a man in the West End for a murder which nobody knows has been committed in France; but he would probably ascribe your failure to reluctance to court inquiry. So I’m glad you followed him.”

===Chinese===
「什么事？」他简短地问。

我把事情讲了一遍，他皱起眉头。

「我说不好这是好运气还是坏运气，」他说。「可我看是坏运气。无论如何，侥幸半点也冒不得。」他在那里略一沉吟，接着说道：「埃利斯这下千真万确地知道，你对瓦根斯堡有意思。要是你的兴奋劲儿露了形迹，他甚至会疑心秘密本身就在你手里。你没去拦他，这在我这儿说明不了什么——为着一桩无人知晓发生在法国的凶案，要人在西区当街拿人，换谁都要踌躇；他多半只会把你的按兵不动，归到不敢招惹盘查上去。所以我高兴你跟了他一阵。」

===Original===
“He didn’t see me,” I said.

“I expect the cab had a back window,” said Mansel. “Anyway, Ellis will act. He may even try to watch.” He took out his case and lighted a cigarette. “I’d like you and Hanbury to take the boat-train tomorrow. And I’ll go over tonight.”

Then he gave me careful directions, told me on no account to return to Cleveland Row and not to walk alone after dark.

“You see,” he said, “you’ve lost Ellis, but you mustn’t make sure that Ellis has lost you. I rather expect he’s thinking of other things, but you never can tell. And those cabs can turn on sixpence.”

===Chinese===
「他没瞧见我。」我说。

「我猜是那车后头有窗，」曼塞尔说。「反正埃利斯会有动作。他甚至可能想法来盯梢。」他掏出烟盒，点了一支烟。「我要你明天和汉伯里（Hanbury）搭渡轮联运列车走。今晚我自己过海。」

随后他细细叮嘱了一番，要我无论如何别再回克利夫兰罗街，天黑之后不要独自走路。

「要明白，」他说，「你甩掉了埃利斯，可千万别认定埃利斯也甩掉了你。我倒想他这会儿满脑子是别的事，可谁也说不准。再说那些出租车，一枚六便士硬币大的地方就能掉头。」

===Original===
As I walked down Pall Mall, I felt as though every step I took was marked by a hundred eyes.

I lodged the envelope at the Bank, and then drove off to find Hanbury and tell him of the change in our plans. Then we went out together and bought two tickets to Paris for the following day.

I do not suppose two men ever used their eyes as we did from then until we saw Mansel again; and that, I imagine, was just what Mansel wanted, for, although I do not think he thought it likely that I should be followed, he would have been very glad to know what Ellis was doing and whether the man was alone or going to work with a gang. But Hanbury and I proved broken reeds, and, when we rejoined him, we had nothing at all to report: and this shows of how much use we were, for it afterwards appeared that Ellis had turned at the top of St. James’s Street and had driven back to see me turn into Cleveland Row, that I had been followed to the Bank, to Hanbury’s father’s house and to Cook’s office, and that it was only at Boulogne that touch with me had been lost. And for that relief we had Mansel’s prevision to thank.

===Chinese===
我沿帕尔马尔街（Pall Mall）走去，只觉得每迈一步，都有一百双眼睛盯着。

我把信封寄存进银行，随即坐车去寻汉伯里，把我们计划的变动告诉他。然后我们一道出门，买了两张次日去巴黎的车票。

依我看，打那时起直到我们再见到曼塞尔，天下没有两个人像我们这样使足了眼睛四处察看；而我想，这正是曼塞尔求之不得的：因为尽管他未必认为我会被人跟踪，他却很乐意知道埃利斯在干什么，这人是单枪匹马，还是要纠集一伙。可惜汉伯里和我是两根靠不住的芦苇，同他会合时一无所报；这也足见我们顶什么用——因为事后才知道：埃利斯在圣詹姆斯街口就掉转车头，折回来看我拐进克利夫兰罗街；此后我被一路跟到银行、跟到汉伯里父亲家、又跟到库克旅行社（Cook's），直到布洛涅（Boulogne）才把我的行踪跟丢。能有这份解脱，全亏曼塞尔料事在先。

===Original===
At four that afternoon the commissionaire attached to my Club brought me my passport and took away all my luggage, both light and heavy; and, when I left Town the next morning, the suitcase I took with me held nothing but worn-out clothes, for which I had no use. Of Hanbury the same can be said. We left by the morning train, and came to Boulogne about noon of a beautiful day. We were soon off the boat and, since we had reserved no seats in the Paris train, we made a fuss of securing the ones we wanted as well as a table for the first luncheon to be served. When our luggage was up on the rack and the porters had been dismissed, we strolled up and down the platform, like everyone else, but, after a little, we wandered on to the quay and presently out of sight.

===Chinese===
当天下午四点，我那家俱乐部的门房把护照送来给我，又收走了我全部行李，轻的重的都在内；次日早晨我离城时，随身那只手提箱里，除了几件破旧衣裳——反正我也用不上——什么也没有。汉伯里的情形可以说一样。我们搭早班车动身，在一个晴美的日子约莫正午到了布洛涅。我们很快下了船；因为没在去巴黎的火车上订座，便大张旗鼓地占下合意的座位，又给头一顿午餐占好一张桌子。行李搁上行李架、打发走脚夫之后，我们像旁人一样在月台上溜达；过了一会儿，我们踱到码头上，转眼便没了踪影。

===Original===
We made our way to a tavern in the heart of the town, and there found Mansel’s Rolls-Royce, and, within, Mansel himself, smoking and drinking beer, and arguing with the host about the Battle of the Somme. He had come that day from Dieppe.

He seemed very pleased to see us, and ordered luncheon at once, “for,” said he, “I want to be in Strasbourg by dawn, and lie up there for the day. I was watched out of London last night, and, though they’ve lost me now, they’ll probably make a fresh cast.”

===Chinese===
我们进了城中心一家酒馆，在那儿找到了曼塞尔的劳斯莱斯（Rolls-Royce）；车里坐着曼塞尔本人，又是抽烟又是喝啤酒，正同店主争辩索姆河战役。他是当天从迪耶普（Dieppe）过来的。

他见我们来到，似乎很高兴，立刻吩咐开午饭，「因为，」他说，「我要赶在天亮前到斯特拉斯堡（Strasbourg），在那儿蛰伏一天。昨晚我出伦敦时就被人盯上了；如今虽说跟丢了，他们多半会重新搜寻一遍。」

===Original===
“But what could they do,” said Hanbury, “if they did pick you up on the road?”

“Why, what they have done,” said Mansel, “not very far from Chartres. Don’t forget,” he added, “that we three hold the secret which that man held. And I think Ellis thinks we do.”

And there, I think, for the first time, it came to me how great was the power of those two leather bags which lay in the chamber of the great well, and I seemed to see them as impassive, relentless twin gods, bringing this man to death and holding that man for the gallows and sending another four or five pelting across a continent, like so many thieves in the night, to God knows what fortune, while, as for the hatred and malice and uncharitableness which they were inspiring, even the compilers of the Litany cannot have contemplated so poisonous a flow of soul. But, though it seemed very shocking, I felt very cheerful to think I was one of the “thieves,” and the thought of stealing a march upon men so bold and determined as Ellis and his friends, was like a glass of champagne.

===Chinese===
「可他们就算在半道上咬住你，」汉伯里说，「又能怎么样？」

「嗬，照他们已经干过的那样，」曼塞尔说，「就在离沙特尔（Chartres）不远的地方。别忘了，」他补了一句，「咱们仨握着那人从前握着的秘密。而且我看，埃利斯就认定咱们握着。」

到这时，我想我才头一回省悟，躺在大井井室里的那两只皮袋，握着何等的威力；我仿佛看见它们化作冷漠无情、铁面无私的一对孪生神祇，送这个人去死，把那个人押上绞架，又撵得另外四五个家伙像一群深夜里的窃贼，横穿大陆狂奔，奔向天晓得的命运；至于它们激发出来的仇恨、恶意与不仁，只怕连《连祷文》（the Litany）的编纂者也不曾料到世间会有这样恶毒的心魂奔涌。然而这景象虽叫人心惊，一想到自己也是「窃贼」中的一个，我倒兴致勃勃；想到能抢在埃利斯和他那伙胆大而果决的人前头，恰似饮下一杯香槟。

===Original===
Whilst we were lunching, a basket was stocked with provisions against our journey and then tied up in two cloths because of the dust. Indeed, great attention was paid us by the people of the inn, who manifestly knew Mansel and thought the world of him.

===Chinese===
我们吃午饭的当口，有人往一只提篮里装满路上的干粮，又因怕尘土，用两块布裹扎起来。客栈里的人对我们殷勤备至，他们分明熟识曼塞尔，且把他佩服得五体投地。

===Original===
We all ate very well, while Mansel spoke of the car. Of this he was plainly proud, and I was surprised that he thought it wise to leave her alone in the street. I said so presently, when he laughed and asked me to fetch him a map which he had left on its seat. I went to do so and found the car guarded better than I had dreamed. As I leaned over the side, a small, white dog rose from the driver’s place, and, had I been the devil himself, I could not have been accorded a more hostile reception. The whole street rang with a storm of barking, and, if I had taken the map, I should certainly have paid in blood for my capture. At this, Mansel appeared and, after making much of the terrier, a purebred Sealyham, picked him up out of the car and put him into my arms. I stroked and spoke to him, and presently he licked my face.

“And now,” said Mansel, “he knows that you are my friend and will let you come and go and do as you please.”

Then he called Hanbury and made him free of the dog’s confidence in the same way. After that, he asked me, if I had done eating, to stay with the car, because he wanted to give the Sealyham his lunch; for he set great store by the terrier, as was very proper, for I never saw a more attractive or intelligent dog. His name was Tester, and I believe, so fine was his instinct, that he understood every word that was said to him and many that were not: and I know that from that time on I was his most obedient servant, and he my very good friend: but more than that he never was, for Mansel was his master, and he knew no other.

===Chinese===
大家都吃得很好，曼塞尔则说起他的车。这车他显然得意得很，我倒奇怪他竟放心让它独自停在街上。我正要说这话，他却笑着叫我去取一册他忘在车座上的地图。我依言走去，才发现这车守卫得比我想的严密得多。我往车边一探身，驾驶座上立起一条白色小狗；哪怕我是魔鬼本人，也受不到更不客气的接待。满街顿时响起狂吠的暴风雨，我若真把地图取走，只怕要拿血来赎。这时曼塞尔走了过来，把那只小狗好一顿抚弄——那是纯种的西里汉姆梗（Sealyham）——随后把它从车里抱出来，放进我怀里。我抚着它、同它说话，不多会儿，它便舔起我的脸来。

「这一来，」曼塞尔说，「它知道你是我的朋友，随你来去走动，随你怎么着它都不管了。」

随后他唤过汉伯里，如法炮制，也叫它把信任交给了汉伯里。这之后，他请我若吃完了饭，就守在车旁，因为他要给西里汉姆梗开午饭；他对这只小狗器重得很，这原也合情理，因为我从没见过比它更讨人喜欢、更有灵性的狗。它名叫泰斯特（Tester）；我深信它天资颖悟，别人对它说的话，它字字都懂，没说出口的，它多半也懂；而且我知道，从那时起，我便做了它最听话的仆役，它则做了我的至交；但仅止于此，再无进一层，因为曼塞尔是它的主人，它心里再没有第二个人。

===Original===
The car was a new model, and the coachwork had been carefully done. It was, as Mansel said, a true touring body, for, though it was slim to look at, it had a great capacity and was so constructed that two could sleep in it with ease and comfort, and, when it appeared to be empty, its hidden lockers concealed all manner of stuff not usually carried in cars, but invaluable to a pioneer. There were brandy and “first field-dressings,” a medicine-chest and bandages, lint and splints: but most important in my eyes was a little armoury of weapons and ammunition and handcuffs, “of which,” said Mansel, “I hope we shall have no need; but I like to think that they’re there.” Then he showed us that in the driver’s pocket he carried a heavy pistol, ready for use; and that, I think, completed my conviction that Ellis was trying to play a losing game and would very soon curse Wagensburg and the day he first heard its name.

===Chinese===
这辆车是新款，车身做工考究。正如曼塞尔所说，是不折不扣的旅行车身：看上去苗条，容量却大，构造上容得两个人舒舒服服安睡；表面看似空空如也，暗藏的柜屉里却收着种种寻常汽车不带、对开路先锋却无价可估的物件。有白兰地，有「战地急救包」，有药箱和绷带，绒布和夹板；但在我眼里顶要紧的，是一处小小的武库——枪械、弹药，还有手铐。这些东西，曼塞尔说：「但愿咱们用不上；可一想到它们就在那儿，我心里踏实。」随后他指给我们看，驾驶座的衣袋里还揣着一支沉甸甸的手枪，随时可用。看到这里，我想我是彻底信了：埃利斯在打一场必输的牌，用不了多久，他就要咒骂瓦根斯堡，咒骂自己头一回听说这名字的那一天。

===Original===
It was half past two when we left Boulogne for Strasbourg, and a wonderful journey it was. I sat with Mansel and Tester, and Hanbury sat behind. It had been raining a little, but was now very fine; the country through which we passed was agreeably fresh and blowing; and the car had the way of a swallow in the air. Mansel drove very fast, without seeming to do so, and maintained an average of forty-five miles to the hour with an astonishing precision. He neither hurried through towns nor showed any inconsideration to men or beasts upon the road, but such time as he lost on this account he won when the road was open, without any fuss, for the car was willing, and he was a remarkable judge of pace and distance, and, while he was yet a great way from some predicament, could tell to a hair what was within his power: and that is more than most men can do.

When evening came and we began to see cows being driven home, we turned down a lane and stopped. Then Mansel and Hanbury alighted and took Tester for a stroll, whilst I stayed with the car and prepared our supper. This we ate easily, for we were to stay there an hour. Except to take in petrol, we did not stop again till we came to Strasbourg: and that was at one o’clock. There was a fine moon, and I remember looking up sleepily to see how the old cathedral was lacking one of its spires. We chose a small hotel in an unimportant street, and only two rooms were taken, for Mansel slept in the car in a garden at the back of the inn.

===Chinese===
两点半，我们离开布洛涅，向斯特拉斯堡进发，这一程走得妙极了。我同曼塞尔、泰斯特坐前排，汉伯里坐后排。先前下过一阵小雨，这时天已放晴；我们一路经过的田野清新鲜爽，和风习习；那车行驶起来，有如空中的一只燕子。曼塞尔开得飞快，看着却不像在赶，平均每小时四十五英里，快慢拿捏得惊人地精准。过市镇他不赶路，对路上的人畜他也从不失礼；因此上耽搁的工夫，一到空旷的路面便不声不响补了回来——车是肯卖力的车，他又是估速测距的绝顶好手，离什么险情还远着呢，已能毫厘不差地算出自己还有几分余力：这份本事，寻常人万难企及。

傍晚时分，路上开始见到有人赶牛回圈，我们便拐进一条小路停下。曼塞尔和汉伯里下车，牵了泰斯特去遛弯，我留在车边做晚饭。饭吃得从容，因为我们要在那里歇一个钟头。此后除了加油，一路再没停过，直到斯特拉斯堡，到时已是一点钟。月色正好，我记得自己睡意惺忪地抬起头来，看那座老教堂怎样缺了一座尖塔。我们在一条不起眼的街上挑了家小旅馆，只开了两间房，因为曼塞尔睡在旅馆后园的车里。

===Original===
We had reached Strasbourg sooner than Mansel had expected, and, though he had intended to stay in that city till dusk, the thought of wasting the whole of a valuable day was more than he could endure, and by nine o’clock of that morning we were again upon the road.

Our way now lay through The Black Forest and was at times most solitary. Mansel drove with his ears pricked, and if ever I spoke, begged me to hold my tongue; but I could not share his vigilance and, when we stopped for a moment by the side of the way, asked him how Ellis could have had time to contrive our pursuit or attack.

His reply was unanswerable.

===Chinese===
我们到斯特拉斯堡比曼塞尔预计的早；他原打算在这座城里蛰伏到黄昏，可一想到白白糟蹋一整天的宝贵光阴，他就受不了，于是那天上午九点，我们重又上了路。

这一段路要穿行黑森林（the Black Forest），有时荒僻得很。曼塞尔竖着耳朵开车，我一开口，他便求我住嘴；我却体察不到他那份警觉，趁我们在路边略停的工夫，问他埃利斯哪来的时间布置追踪或袭击。

他的回答无懈可击。

===Original===
“I could have done it,” he said, “with luck and money and friends, and, though the first can’t be bought, the other two can. Ellis had a secret to sell. Be sure he’s sold it, for he couldn’t fight us alone. Therefore, he has money and friends. And, since the Castle of Wagensburg is plainly everyone’s way, not to look out for us would be the act of a fool. They know that we’re going direct, because we’ve no time to spare. That suggests Strasbourg. So somebody flies to Paris and gets to Strasbourg some hours ahead of us. They can’t watch a city, but they can watch the frontier posts. And, don’t forget, *buried treasure* is the very deuce of a goad.”

===Chinese===
「这事换了我，」他说，「凭运气、金钱和交情，照样办得成；头一样买不来，后两样买得到。埃利斯手里有桩秘密要出手。十拿九稳他已经出手了，因为单枪匹马他斗不过咱们。这么着，他手里就有了钱，也有了人。既然瓦根斯堡城堡分明是谁都得走的地方，不加防备咱们，那才是傻子的行径。他们知道咱们是直奔而去，因为咱们时间紧。这就指向斯特拉斯堡。于是有人飞赴巴黎，抢在咱们前头几个钟头赶到斯特拉斯堡。一座城他们看不住，边境关卡却看得住。还有，别忘了，*埋藏的财宝*是天底下顶厉害的一根刺棒。」

===Original===
As he spoke, a very faint sound came to our ears.

It was the high-pitched note of a powerful electric horn, as yet some distance away and between us and Strasbourg.

Mansel had the Rolls moving before I was well in my seat, and we were very soon doing some sixty odd miles to the hour, but the next time I heard the horn it sounded much closer, and, after a moment, Mansel slackened his speed and let a closed car go by. He was very careful, however, to keep this in sight, and when, two or three miles on, we saw it slow down and stop in the midst of the road, he asked me to fasten Tester to a short chain which was attached to the coachwork close to my feet.

As we approached, a man who had alighted from the car spread out his arms as a signal to us to stop, not in a peremptory manner, but rather as does a man who is in need of assistance. Mansel waved in reply and applied his brakes, but he overran the closed car by nearly a hundred yards, before bringing the Rolls to rest on the crown of the road.

===Chinese===
他话音未落，一缕极细的声音传进我们耳朵。

那是一支大功率电喇叭的尖啸，离得还远，正在我们与斯特拉斯堡之间。

我还没坐稳，曼塞尔已让劳斯莱斯动了起来，转眼间时速便到了六十多英里；可那喇叭声再传进耳朵时，已近了许多。过了一会儿，曼塞尔放慢车速，放一辆封闭汽车超了过去。不过他处处留神，始终把那车瞭在眼里；行出两三英里，见它慢下来停在了路当腰，他便叫我用一条短链子把泰斯特拴住——链子就装在我脚边的车身上。

驶近时，从那车上下来一个人，朝我们张开两臂，示意停车——那架势并不专横，倒像个求援的人。曼塞尔扬手作答，踩下刹车，却又滑出将近一百码，才让劳斯莱斯停在路面中央隆起的地方。

===Original===
Then we turned round in our seats and waited for the stranger to move.

For a while he seemed to be expecting that we should come back, but, when it was quite evident that we were not going to move, he spoke for a moment with someone within the car and then began to walk in our direction.

“Hanbury,” said Mansel quietly, “watch that car. The moment it moves, ask Chandos to give you a match.”

The stranger was wearing dark glasses, which he did not remove; his hair was fair and his complexion ruddy. He was not very tall, and his hands were coarse and rough. He walked jauntily and wore his hat on one side.

===Chinese===
我们在座位上转过身去，等那陌生人动身。

一时之间，他似乎还指望我们倒回去；等到看清我们绝无动静，他同车里的人低语了片刻，便朝我们这边走来。

「汉伯里，」曼塞尔低声说，「盯住那辆车。它一动，就叫钱多斯（Chandos）给你一根火柴。」

那陌生人戴一副墨镜，并不摘下；头发淡黄，面色红润。个子不高，一双手粗大糙劣。走路神气活现，帽子歪戴着。

===Original===
As he came up, he gave us “Good day” in French, and then very haltingly inquired if any one of us could speak English.

“We are English,” said Mansel.

“Why, that’s fine,” said the other. Then very calmly he asked us to come back and look at his car, “for,” said he, “she seems to be nearly red-hot, and none of us knows enough to change a wheel.”

“If she’s so hot,” said Mansel, “no one can help you at all for half an hour. I should open both sides of the bonnet and push her into the shade. I’ll send you help from the very next garage I pass.”

“Now be a sport,” said the stranger, laying a hand on the Rolls and casually lifting his hat. “Come an’ ’ave a look at the swine.”

“Bill,” said Hanbury to me, “give me a match.”

===Chinese===
他走上前来，先用法语道了声「日安」，随后磕磕绊绊地问，我们中间可有人会说英语。

「我们是英国人。」曼塞尔说。

「哦，那可太好了。」那人说。跟着他十分平静地请我们回去看看他的车，「因为，」他说，「那车快烧红了，我们谁也不懂换轮子。」

「既是这样烫，」曼塞尔说，「半个钟头之内谁都帮不了你。依我说，把引擎盖两边都支起来，把车推到阴凉里去。等过了下一个修车行，我就打发人来帮你。」

「行行好嘛，」陌生人说着，一手搭上劳斯莱斯，随手把帽子撩了撩。「过来看看这猪猡。」

「比尔，」汉伯里对我说，「给我根火柴。」

===Original===
“Shall I send you help?” said Mansel, as the Rolls began to move.

The stranger’s answer was to try to apply the hand-brake, but, as he was feeling for the lever, I hit him under the jaw, and he fell back into the road.

Then the Rolls shot forward, and, as I was unready, I fell myself upon Mansel, who was laughing like a child at a circus, while Tester was barking uproariously and trying to burst his chain, and Hanbury was kneeling on the back seat, shouting “Gone away” and making derisive gestures with both hands.

Before I had got my balance, the closed car was out of sight, but Hanbury told us, with tears, that its occupants’ haste to alight, before it had stopped, had done as much damage as I, for that, as a man was descending from the front of the car, someone behind him flung open the second door and that this hit the one in the back and knocked him down and then returned upon the other, who was himself halfway out. So it seemed that we had had very much the best of the brush and that Ellis and his friends had gained nothing but a couple of heavy falls: but Mansel, when he had done laughing, began to frown, “because,” said he, “though I’d sooner be before than behind them, I’d very much sooner not be on their road at all. Too many ‘circumstances over which one has no control’ on the road today.”

===Chinese===
「要我打发人来帮你吗？」曼塞尔说着，劳斯莱斯动了起来。

陌生人的回答是伸手去扳手刹；可他的手还在摸那根杆，我一拳已捣在他下颌底下，他仰面跌倒在路上。

劳斯莱斯猛地蹿了出去；我没有提防，一头跌在曼塞尔身上。他正笑得像马戏场里的孩子；泰斯特吠得天翻地覆，拚命要把链子挣断；汉伯里跪在后排座位上，高喊「跑啦——」，两只手比着嘲弄的手势。

还没等我站稳，那辆封闭汽车已经没了踪影。不过汉伯里笑得眼泪直流地告诉我们，车上那伙人不等车停稳便抢着下车，摔得跟我一样惨：前头一个人正往下爬，后面有人猛地推开另一扇车门，那门撞着后头那位，把他撞倒，又荡回来砸在另外那位身上，而后者半个身子已经探出了车外。这样看来，这场交手我们占足了便宜，埃利斯一伙什么也没捞着，只结结实实摔了两跤。可曼塞尔笑够了，却皱起眉头，「因为，」他说，「我宁可在他们前头，不愿在他们后头，更巴不得压根儿不同他们走一条路。今天路上，『人力无法左右的意外』未免太多了。」

===Original===
The words were hardly out of his mouth, when we rounded a bend to see a level-crossing ahead. And the barriers were down.

When we were very near, Mansel stopped and Hanbury and I leaped out. At once, as there was no keeper, we endeavoured to raise the poles, but these had been lowered by machinery, controlled, I suppose, by some distant signalman, and were fast locked into place.

“Never mind,” said Mansel quietly, stepping out of the driver’s seat. “Chandos, you take my place, and, Hanbury, sit by his side.”

With that, he climbed into the back and lighted a cigarette.

===Chinese===
话音未落，我们拐过一道弯，只见前方一座铁路道口。栏木已经放下。

驶到近前，曼塞尔停车，我和汉伯里跳下车去。道口无人看守，我们立刻想法去抬那两根栏木，可栏木是机器放下来的——想必由远处某个信号员操控——牢牢锁死在位置上。

「不要紧，」曼塞尔平静地说着，走下驾驶座。「钱多斯，你来坐我的位子。汉伯里，你坐他旁边。」

说罢，他爬进后排，点上一支烟。

===Original===
We did as he said, and, the engine running, I sat with my hand on the lever and my foot on the clutch wondering what was to happen and reflecting rather dismally that we had laughed too soon.

It was a quiet place, and the sunshine was very hot. Except for the murmur of the engine, there was no sound at all.

So we sat, waiting for the train or the closed car.

Two minutes must have gone by before the latter appeared, rounding the bend like fury and raising a storm of dust.

===Chinese===
我们依言行事；发动机响着，我手握变速杆、脚踩离合器，猜不透接下去会怎么样，一面相当凄凉地想着：咱们笑得太早了。

那地方静得很，日头又毒。除了发动机的低吟，四下里再无一点声息。

我们就这样坐着，等火车，或者等那辆封闭汽车。

大约过了两分钟，后者才出现，凶神也似拐过弯来，卷起漫天沙尘。

===Original===
“Don’t start till I say so,” said Mansel, and slewed himself round on the seat.

The road was none too wide, and we were full on the crown.

With my eyes on the driving mirror, I saw the car approach.

It was in the driver’s mind to thrust alongside, but, if he had done so, he could not have crossed the metals, for the gate was less wide than the road; so he brought his car to rest behind us and a little to the right.

===Chinese===
「我不发话，谁也别动。」曼塞尔说，在座位上侧转身来。

路面不算宽，我们又正停在路当心。

我盯着后视镜，看那车驶近。

那司机有心贴上来与我们并排，可他若真这么做，便过不去铁轨——道口的栏门比路面窄；于是他把车停在我们身后，稍稍偏右。

===Original===
“If anyone moves,” said Mansel, “I’m going to fire.” The windows of the car were open, so they heard what he said. “You’ve tried to stop me by force and you’ve pursued me; and at the first town I come to I’m going to prefer a charge. My papers are all in order; I’ve a licence to carry a pistol; and my luggage is in the car. Perhaps you can say the same. If you can’t, you’ll be detained﻿—pending inquiries.”

“Bluff,” said someone.

“Then call it,” said Mansel.

===Chinese===
「只要谁动一动，」曼塞尔说，「我就开枪。」那车的窗子开着，他们听见了他的话。「你们想凭武力拦我，还一路追赶我；到了头一个镇子，我就去控告你们。我的证件样样齐全；我有携枪执照；行李也在车上。这话，你们未必说得上。说不出来，你们就要被拘留——听候查究。」

「唬人。」有人说。

「那就叫破它。」曼塞尔说。

===Original===
“You’ve assaulted us,” said another. “I asked you a civil question, an’ you slogged me under the jaw. An’ you talk about the police.”

“Yes,” said Mansel, “I do. Because my record’s clean. Then again, I speak German quite well; and that’s a great help.”

“Bluff,” said the first speaker. “You know as well as I do that you won’t go to the police. You can’t afford to.”

“If you mean,” said Mansel, “that I don’t want to waste my time, that’s perfectly true.”

“I don’t,” said the other. “I mean that, much as you like ’em, the last thing you want just now is to catch the eye of the police.”

Mansel raised his eyebrows.

===Chinese===
「你打了我们，」另一个说。「我客客气气问了一句，你一拳捣在我下巴上。亏你还有脸提警察。」

「不错，」曼塞尔说，「我是提。因为我履历清白。再说，我德语讲得相当好；这可帮了大忙。」

「唬人。」头一个说话的说。「你跟我一样清楚，你不会去找警察。你担不起。」

「你若是说，」曼塞尔说，「我不愿意浪费时间，那一点不假。」

「我不是这个意思，」那人说。「我是说，警察再招你喜欢，你眼下顶不愿意的，就是叫他们看上你。」

曼塞尔扬了扬眉毛。

===Original===
“I’m not going to argue,” he said, “but I can’t help thinking that you’re mistaking me for somebody else. Excuse me,” and, with that, he fired.

The silence which succeeded the explosion was that of the grave. I had, of course, jumped violently and now sat still in my seat, as if under a spell, though my heart was pounding like a labouring pump and I was expecting every instant the shock of battle. But this did not come. So far as I could see by the mirror, those in the closed car were sitting as still as I, and, after a moment or two, Mansel spoke again.

“The next time anyone moves, I shall try to hit him,” he said. “And I think perhaps it would be better if you all four folded your arms. Thank you.”

“Today to you,” said the man who had spoken last.

“Indubitably,” said Mansel.

===Chinese===
「我无意争辩，」他说，「可我总忍不住想，你们是把我错认成什么人了。失陪。」话音未落，他开了枪。

枪声过后，静得像坟墓。我自然狠狠一震，这会儿直挺挺坐在座位上，仿佛被施了定身法，心里却像一只吃力的抽水机咚咚擂着，时时刻刻等着厮杀打响。可这一仗没有来。借着后视镜望去，封闭汽车里的人同我一样纹丝不动；过了一两分钟，曼塞尔又开口了。

「下回谁再动一动，我就照着他打，」他说。「还有，我看你们四位最好都把胳膊抱起来。多谢。」

「祝今日行猎尽兴。」最后开口的那位说。

「那是一定的。」曼塞尔说。

===Original===
Then we were left to our thoughts, and to wonder if ever the train was coming by.

I was disquieted.

Mansel had spoken boldly, but you cannot make bricks without straw, and the man who had taken him up was not even shaken, very much less deceived. For the moment we had them in check, but the changes and chances of the road were manifold, and, unless we could run right away, as like as not we should be cornered again. And the next time they would be more careful.

It was while I was thinking of these things that I happened to lower my eyes to the mat at my feet, and there, beneath me, I saw an adjustable spanner. Mansel had used it that morning to tighten a bolt, and, in his haste, had omitted to put it away.

===Chinese===
剩下的便只有各想各的心事，纳闷那趟火车究竟还来不来。

我心里不安。

曼塞尔话说得硬气，可是巧妇难为无米之炊，接他话茬的那人连晃都没晃一下，更谈不上受骗。这一刻我们把他们镇住了，可路上的变数多得很，除非一口气跑脱，多半还要被逼进死角。而且下一回，他们会更小心。

我正想着这些，眼睛无意间垂到脚边的脚垫上，就在我身子底下，躺着一把活动扳手。那天早上曼塞尔用它紧过一颗螺栓，匆忙之间忘了收起。

===Original===
Shakespeare has said somewhere that “the sight of means to do ill deeds, Makes deeds ill done.” So it was with the spanner. For directly I saw it, I thought that here was the means to spoil the petrol-tank of the closed car and so put the enemy out of action.

I picked up the spanner, slid this into my pocket and turned to Hanbury.

“Take my place,” I said quietly. “I’m going to disable their car.”

===Chinese===
莎士比亚在什么地方说过：「一见到行恶的工具，人便做出恶事来。」这把扳手于我正是如此。我一看见它，立刻想到：叫那辆封闭汽车油箱报废的家什，现成就在这儿，如此便可教敌人动弹不得。

我拾起扳手，塞进衣袋，转向汉伯里。

「来换我的位子，」我低声说。「我要去弄坏他们的车。」

===Original===
Hanbury blinked once or twice and gulped as though he would protest, so soon as he found his tongue; but I opened my door and stepped out without more ado.

I sauntered up to the barrier and glanced up and down the rails. Then I turned round and looked at the two cars.

Hanbury was in my place and Mansel did not seem to have moved. He was sitting easily sideways, covering the car with his pistol and supporting his right wrist with his left arm. The four men, of whom Ellis was not one, were wearing blue glasses and sitting like images, with folded arms.

===Chinese===
汉伯里眨了两三下眼睛，喉咙里咕地咽了一声，仿佛话头一到舌上就要抗议；我已推开车门，不多耽搁，下了车。

我慢悠悠走到栏木跟前，朝铁轨两头望了望，然后回过身来，打量那两辆车。

汉伯里坐进了我的位子，曼塞尔似乎纹丝未动。他侧身坐着，姿态闲适，手枪罩定那辆车，左手托着右腕。那四个人——埃利斯并不在内——戴着蓝镜片眼镜，抱着双臂，坐得像四尊石像。

===Original===
I stepped to the radiator of the Rolls, unscrewed the cap and peered within. Then I frowned and, spanner plainly in hand, stooped as though to tighten the plug. After appearing and disappearing once or twice, I replaced the cap, still frowning, and disappeared again. A moment later I was beneath the car. It was a tight fit, but the pulse of the closed car’s engine covered the noise I made. I worked along on my back as best I could, until my head was level with the Rolls’ hind wheels. To pass from beneath the Rolls to beneath the closed car meant crossing about six feet of open road, but there was not more than a yard between the two cars’ wings, and, though, from where I lay, I could see the hat of the man beside the driver, I judged that, unless he moved, the strip of road I must pass was just out of his sight. So, since to see where I was going was now essential, I turned very gently upon my face and took a deep breath. Inch by inch I covered those six feet of open road, and I must admit that I did it with my heart in my mouth, for I could by no means be sure that I was not in some view, and, though I hoped for the best, once one of the enemy knew that I was beneath their car, pistol or no, it was most unlikely that they would make no attempt to learn my business.

===Chinese===
我走到劳斯莱斯的水箱跟前，拧开盖子朝里看了看，随即皱起眉头，手里明晃晃拿着扳手，弯下腰去，作势要紧那颗螺栓。我露了两回身、又隐了两回身，末了把盖子拧回去，仍旧皱着眉，再次隐没了。片刻之后，我已钻到那车底下。地方紧得很，好在封闭汽车发动机的搏动盖住了我弄出的声响。我仰面躺着，尽力向前挪，直到脑袋齐着劳斯莱斯的后轮。要从劳斯莱斯底下挪到封闭汽车底下，就得横穿约莫六英尺的露天路面；好在两车的挡泥板之间相距不过一码，而且从我躺着的地方能望见司机旁边那人的帽顶，我估计，只要他不动身，我必经的那一条路面恰好在他的视线之外。既然看清去路此刻成了要紧事，我便极轻地翻过身去趴下，深深吸了一口气。那一寸一寸爬过六英尺露天路面的工夫，我不瞒人说，是提着心、吊着胆干的，因为我断难担保自己不在什么人的眼里；而且，纵然我往好处想，敌人一旦晓得我钻到了他们车底下，有没有手枪且不论，他们多半断不会不想法子弄清我的来意。

===Original===
At last, however, it was over, and I was well out of sight and under the closed car.

I turned again upon my back, and there I lay for a moment to get my breath, for the strain of moving so flat upon my face had been exhausting, and the heat and noise of the engine at such close quarters had been unpleasant. I was, too, half choked and blinded with the dust, which hereabouts lay very thick.

As I was taking my rest, I became suddenly aware of a great noise, which was not that of the engine, but seemed to be coming upon me at a terrible speed. For an instant I lay paralysed, unable to think what it was. Then, in a flash, I knew it was the sound of the train for which we had waited so long.

===Chinese===
好在这桩事总算完了，我妥妥地隐住身形，到了封闭汽车底下。

我又翻回仰面，在那里躺了片刻，好缓一口气；那样贴地伏行实在耗人，同发动机靠得这样近，热气和声响也叫人难受。而且尘土在这里积得极厚，呛得我半透不过气，迷得我睁不开眼。

我正在歇息，忽然惊觉一阵巨响，不是发动机的声音，倒像以一种可怕的速度朝我扑来。刹那间我僵躺着，想不出那是什么。随即一个激灵，我知道了：那就是我们苦等已久的火车。

===Original===
What would happen when it had gone by and the barriers rose, I did not stay to think. Indeed, I do not remember how I got to the tank, but I know that I was fumbling with the spanner and that the dust was falling thick into my mouth and eyes as the train roared on its way.

The bottom of the tank was all encrusted with dirt, but I had this off in a twinkling and fitted the spanner to the plug. Twice I tried to move it, and twice it refused to budge, for I do not think it had ever been undone since the car was built, but had been painted over, so that its shape was half gone and it might not have been a plug at all, but only a knob.

I had just reset the spanner, licked my hands and taken another hold, when a sudden, unmistakable clatter announced that the barriers were up.

===Chinese===
火车驰过、栏木升起之后会怎么样，我来不及想。说实在的，我怎么摸到油箱底下的，自己也不记得；只知道我当时正摆弄着扳手，火车呼啸着奔远，尘土簌簌地直往我嘴里眼睛里灌。

油箱底满结着泥垢，我一眨眼便抠了下来，把扳手套上放油塞。试了两回，两回都纹丝不动；我想打从造车那天起，它就不曾拧开过，上面漆了一层，棱角都糊掉了，倒不像个塞子，倒像个圆疙瘩。

我刚重新卡好扳手，舔了舔手，再攥上一把，一阵突兀、再明白不过的咔哒声，宣告栏木升起来了。

===Original===
At once I heard Mansel’s voice.

“Stand by, William,” he said, using my Christian name. And then: “I’m going now,” he continued, “to prefer my charge. From what you say I gather you’re going to follow, so we shall meet again.”

He said more, but that was as much as I heard, for his words showed me something I had not dreamed of, namely, that *he did not know that I was not still in the Rolls*, but thought he was free to proceed, and﻿—what was perhaps more serious﻿—that upon this important point the four in the closed car were better informed than he.

===Chinese===
立时，我听见曼塞尔的声音。

「待命，威廉，」他说，叫的是我的教名。跟着他又说：「我现在去控告你们。听你们的口气，我料定你们是要跟上来的，那咱们回头再见。」

他还有话说，可我听到的就这么多，因为他的话叫我看明白了一件我做梦也想不到的事，那就是，*他并不知道我已经不在劳斯莱斯里*，还只道他可以放心前行；而——这一点也许更要紧——在这个紧要关节上，封闭汽车里那四个人比他知道得多。

===Original===
With these thoughts in my mind, I put forth all my strength, and I think any screw must have yielded to the frantic effort I made. The drain-plug gave way with a crack, and, after one or two turns, I felt the petrol running over my hands. I continued to work desperately, and, a moment later the plug fell out of its hole, and, with a soft gush, the spirit began to pour out into the dust.

I thrust the drain-plug into my pocket, because, without that, a hogshead of petrol would not avail the closed car, but she would have to be towed until she came to some place which boasted a lathe and a man sufficiently skilled to fashion a substitute. Then, regardless of the downpour of petrol, I scrambled clear of the car. As I did so, I heard Mansel raise his voice.

“William,” he cried, “don’t look for it any more: we must have dropped it farther back.”

I knew at once that Hanbury must have told him my errand, and that now he was giving me my cue: so, wondering how the inmates of the closed car would take my entrance, I stepped to the middle of the road, and then, waving the spanner, jogged cheerfully into view.

===Chinese===
我怀着这些念头，使出全身的力气；我想，任凭什么螺钉，也熬不过我这股狂劲。放油塞咔的一声松动了，拧过一两转，我便觉出汽油淌过了我的手。我还在拚死地拧；片刻之后，塞子脱出孔眼，汽油轻轻一涌，开始朝尘土里倾泻。

我把放油塞塞进衣袋，因为没有它，任凭一大桶汽油也是白搭，那车只能叫人拖着走，一直拖到某处有车床、又有好手艺人的地方，配出一个替身来。随后，不管汽油劈头浇下，我连滚带爬，脱出车底。就在这时，我听见曼塞尔提高了嗓音。

「威廉，」他喊道，「别再找了：准是掉在更后头了。」

我立刻明白，汉伯里准把我的差事告诉了他，此刻他正在给我递话头；于是我一面纳闷封闭汽车里那些人见了我的登场会作何感想，一面走到路当中，挥着扳手，欢欢喜喜地小跑着露面。

===Original===
As I came alongside:

“Let her go,” said Mansel.

Hanbury had set the door open, and the car was moving when I flung myself in. As the Rolls swept over the metals, I heard a shout of surprise, and this was immediately followed by a veritable bellow of rage, which I like to think showed that the occupants of the closed car accredited me with some malicious attempt upon its efficiency. And I think that in that I am right, for, instead of pursuing, they all flung out of the car, and, when we sailed round a bend, they were behind their vehicle, which was standing apparently deserted, with all four doors open, to the side of the dusty road.

Then Mansel took over the wheel, and I showed him and Hanbury the drain-plug and told my tale. Presently we ran through a village, where there was not so much as a forge, and a few miles further on we came to a smooth-flowing stream. Here Mansel gave me five minutes to strip and bathe, and, while I did so, Hanbury unpacked clean clothes for me to put on. When I came back to the car, they had opened three bottles of beer and drank my health in the most handsome fashion. But I think it is clear that, though anyone, who was not too fat, could have crawled underneath the two cars, only a great personality could have held four such villains at bay for nearly ten minutes and so brought us safe and sound out of so perilous a pass.

We came to Salzburg that night, to find the three servants arrived and our rooms waiting: and here Mansel slept in a bed for the first time since he had left London, while Carson, his servant, lay in the car in his stead.

===Chinese===
待我赶到车边：

「开车。」曼塞尔说。

汉伯里早把车门推开，我扑进车里时，车已动了。劳斯莱斯碾过铁轨时，我听见一声惊呼，跟着便是一声十足咆哮的怒吼——我愿意相信，那吼声表明封闭汽车里的人认定我对那车的机能下了什么毒手。我想这一点我没有想错，因为他们并不追赶，全都扑出了车厢；待我们兜过一个弯，他们已经落在车后，站在自家车子旁边——那车停在尘土飞扬的路边，四扇车门全敞着，分明被撇下了。

随后曼塞尔接过方向盘，我把放油塞拿给他和汉伯里看，讲了我的经过。不多时我们穿过一座村庄，村里连个铁匠炉都没有；再行几英里，来到一条平缓清澈的溪流边。在这里，曼塞尔给了我五分钟，让我脱衣下水；我沐浴的工夫，汉伯里打开干净的衣裳，备我换上。回到车上时，他们已经开了三瓶啤酒，十分漂亮地为我的健康干杯。不过我想有一点是明白的：只要不太胖，谁都能从那两辆车底下爬过去；可要把四个那样的恶棍钳住近十分钟、教我们平平安安闯过这样一处险境的，只有一位顶天立地的大人物。

当天夜里我们到了萨尔茨堡（Salzburg），见三个仆人已然赶到，房间也为我们备好；在这里，曼塞尔自离伦敦以来头一回睡上了床，他的仆人卡森（Carson）便代他躺在车里。

---

===Original===
The next day we breakfasted together in Hanbury’s room, and there, after we had eaten, a council was held.

It was most probable that, if Wagensburg was in the market, some one of the agents in Salzburg had the castle upon his books: and, since Ellis had taken the field, at once to set about the purchase seemed plainly the best thing to do. Yet, to seek to buy a property of such consequence without having so much as seen it, was out of the question, for not only should we be unable to judge the price we were asked, but such an astonishing action would be certain to arouse comment: and that was the last thing we desired.

We, therefore, determined to devote the next day to a reconnaissance, in the course of which we should explore the country round Wagensburg, and, if it was vacant, the property itself. We should then, at least, be qualified to play the part of people so much attracted by a domain as to desire to own it, and, though an agent might think that we had more money than brains, it was unlikely that he would look for any deeper explanation of our eagerness.

===Chinese===
第二天，我们在汉伯里的房里一同用早饭；饭后，就在那里开了一场议事会。

十有八九，倘若瓦根斯堡确在待售，萨尔茨堡总有一位代理人的册子上登记着这座城堡；而埃利斯既已出场，立刻着手购置，分明是上策。可是，连看都没看过一眼，就想买下这样一处要紧的产业，那是断然不行的：不但人家开的价我们无从估量，这样惊人的举动也必定招人议论——而议论，正是我们最避讳的东西。

于是我们议定，第二天专作一次勘察：探一探瓦根斯堡四乡的地界，若堡中无人，再看产业本身。这样，至少我们便够资格扮演那种给一处庄园迷住了心窍、一心想把它据为己有的人；代理人也许会觉得我们有钱没脑子，却不大会追问我们的热切背后还有什么更深的名堂。

===Original===
Another thing we decided was to purchase a second car. Sooner or later we must have one, for the Rolls would not hold six as well as baggage, and it might well prove worse than inconvenient to break our party in two. And, since to carry out the reconnaissance in some car less distinctive than the Rolls might be to our advantage, we determined to make our purchase before we did anything else.

Then Mansel requested Hanbury to stay in Salzburg, while he and I and Carson went out alone. It was a big thing to ask, but Hanbury immediately agreed in the most handsome way, insisting that he would be better employed in caring for the Rolls after her long run than in making a fourth in what was “a three-men job.” But Mansel made him promise not to go out alone, but always to take with him one of the other servants, when he left the hotel.

By midday we had a car. It was not new, but had been carefully used, and had only come to be sold the day before. It bore a well-known name, was swift and very well found, and, after putting it to several tests, Mansel drove to a Bank and paid the price we were asked without more ado.

===Chinese===
我们还议定再买一辆车。这车迟早要备下，因为劳斯莱斯装不下六个人加行李；把我们一伙拆作两半，只怕比不方便还要糟。再说，用一辆不像劳斯莱斯那样惹眼的车去作勘察，或许于我们有利，于是我们决意先买车，再办别的事。

随后曼塞尔请汉伯里留在萨尔茨堡，让他同我和卡森单独出去。这请求分量不轻，汉伯里却立刻极其漂亮地应承下来，一口咬定，与其在那样一桩「三个人的差事」里凑第四个数，不如把跑了长途的劳斯莱斯照料好更有用。不过曼塞尔要他答应，出门不许独个儿走，凡离开旅馆，必带上另外几个仆人中的一个。

到正午，车已到手。这车不算新，却用得仔细，头天刚送来出卖。它挂着一个尽人皆知的牌号，跑得快，行头又齐全；试了几试之后，曼塞尔驶到一家银行，二话不说，照价付了钱。

===Original===
Then we ate a short luncheon, bade Hanbury goodbye, and, taking Carson and Tester, left for Carinthia soon after one o’clock.

It was a notable run. The country was mountainous, and the scenery superb: the further south we went the more picturesque became the way, and the villages grew less frequent and more and more unspoiled.

Everywhere there were woods and forests, high and low, and, among them, streams and pastures and an occasional farm, with a saucer on every ridgepole to keep the witches away.

About sundown we came to a village that Mansel knew.

===Chinese===
随后我们匆匆吃了午饭，同汉伯里道了别，带上卡森和泰斯特，一点钟过后不久，便动身往克恩滕（Carinthia）去。

这是一程出色的旅程。山国重重，风景绝佳：越往南走，路越入画，村子越稀，也越来越不带人手改削的痕迹。

到处是树林和森林，高高低低；林间有溪流、草场，偶尔一座农庄，每根屋脊上都摆着一只碟子，好叫女巫不敢近前。

日头快落时，我们到了一座曼塞尔熟识的村子。

===Original===
There was but one inn, and that did not look as if it had much to offer, although, to judge by its size and style, it must once have been a house of some importance and have served people of quality. But, as it turned out, I was never better lodged in my life; for I slept in a great four-poster amid furniture which must have been of great value; my huge room was spotless: the linen was unusually fine and smelt very sweet of some herbs, with which it had been laid away; and the attendance was such as, I imagine, travellers used to hope for a hundred years ago. The fare, too, was excellent﻿—trout, and an omelet and most delicious bread, with plenty of fresh fruit and cream: and, since there was no garage or coach-house, they opened the great doors that belonged to the house itself, and Mansel drove the car into a vast, flagged hall, to the great entertainment of the regular customers of the inn, who were there gathered drinking, before they went to their beds.

We were on the road the next morning by six o’clock, and before it was seven we had sighted Wagensburg.

We had stopped for a moment by the way to study the map, and I was trying to determine exactly which road we were using, when Mansel gave a light laugh and touched me upon the arm.

I followed his gaze.

===Chinese===
镇上只有一家客栈，看上去拿不出多少好处；不过论格局气象，它从前想必是一户颇有身份的人家，接待过有身份的人物。可结果呢，我一辈子没有住得比这更好过：我睡在一张大四柱床上，四周的家具想必都很值钱；我那间大屋纤尘不染；被单被褥异样地细洁，散发着一股香草的甜味——想必收存时便垫着这些；伺候的情形，我想，正是百年前的旅人所指望的那一种。饭菜也是上等的——鳟鱼，一只煎蛋卷，香极了的面包，还有多多的鲜果和奶油：而且这里既没有车库，也没有车房，他们便把宅子自己的大门敞开，曼塞尔把车径直开进一座铺石的大厅，让客栈的老主顾们看得乐不可支——那些人正聚在那里喝酒，喝完了才去睡觉。

次日早晨六点前我们上了路；不到七点，已经望见了瓦根斯堡。

我们半路停了一停，对着地图琢磨；我正要断定我们走的究竟是哪一条路，曼塞尔轻轻一笑，碰了碰我的手臂。

我顺着他凝望的方向望去。

===Original===
He was looking up over his shoulder at a range of high woods which fell sharply to a river. At one point a bend of the river bit into the line of the woods, and above the bend rose a cliff, some hundred and fifty feet high. And on the edge of the cliff stood a castle wall.

Neither of us said anything; but, after a little, Mansel started the engine and I put away the map.

Three miles farther on we came to the village of Lerai, where was a bridge. We stopped at the inn there and ordered breakfast, and, while this was being prepared, Mansel talked with the host.

All that we wanted to know, the latter told him. Wagensburg was to be sold: the village postmaster held the keys of the house: no one had viewed the property since it had been for sale. And, when Mansel said he should like to see the castle, the innkeeper called for his hat and set out to find the postmaster and ask for the keys.

===Chinese===
他回头仰望着一片高高的林带，那林带陡直地落到一条河边。有一处，河湾咬进了林线，河湾上方耸起一堵崖壁，约莫一百五十英尺高。而崖沿上，立着一道城堡的墙。

我们谁也没说话；过了一小会儿，曼塞尔发动了引擎，我收起地图。

再行三英里，到了勒拉（Lerai）村，村上有座桥。我们在那里的客栈停下，要了早饭；趁着预备的工夫，曼塞尔同店主攀谈。

我们想知道的一切，店主都告诉了他。瓦根斯堡就要出售：村里的邮政局长掌着房子的钥匙：产业挂牌以来，还不曾有人去看过。曼塞尔说想看看城堡，店主便叫人取来帽子，动身去找邮政局长要钥匙。

===Original===
When Mansel told me all this﻿—for I cannot speak German, and had understood next to nothing of what had been said﻿—I could have thrown up my hat; and even he was very plainly elated to think that, except for Ellis, our way was so easy and clear. The breakfast was served, but I could hardly swallow for sheer excitement, so that Mansel advised me to think upon fishing and trout-streams, “because,” said he, gravely, “such contemplation is not only very restful, but very much in point, for, remember, it is trout that has brought us to these parts, and, if we like Wagensburg, we do so because it will make an agreeable fishing-lodge.”

Since Mansel never spoke without reason, I strove to do as he said, but I fear my reflections upon angling were incoherent and of no value, for fish are not found in wells, nor do ropes and lanterns form part of an angler’s kit. Before, however, the landlord was back with the keys, Mansel had me in hand, and when the former reappeared, I was listening to such a discourse upon flies as must, I think, have interested the most unlikely fisherman that ever was born. To round the picture, the host was brought into the session and made free of Mansel’s words, to which, since he was something of an angler, he responded so warmly that I began to think that we should never start, or that, at any rate, Ellis or one of his confederates would first appear.

However, at last we left, with the landlord sitting with Carson in the back of the car.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔把这些讲给我听——我德语不会说，方才的话简直一句也没听懂——我恨不得把帽子抛上天；连他也分明欢欣鼓舞，觉得除了埃利斯，我们这条路走得又平顺又干净。早饭端上来了，我却兴奋得几乎咽不下去；曼塞尔便劝我去想钓鱼，想鳟鱼溪，「因为，」他一本正经地说，「这样的遐想不但十分安神，而且十分切题——记住，是鳟鱼把我们带到这地方来的；我们若相中瓦根斯堡，也是因为它会做一处上好的渔舍。」

曼塞尔开口从来没有无缘无故的话，我便照他的话努力去想；只怕我对垂钓的冥想杂乱无章、一无是处，因为井里找不着鱼，绳子和灯笼也不是渔具箱里的物件。不过，店主还没拿着钥匙回来，曼塞尔已收住了我的心神；等他重新露面时，我正听着一篇论飞蝇钓饵的高论——我想，哪怕天底下最没有渔翁相的人听了，也必定入迷。为成全这幅图画，店主也被拉进这席谈话，尽听曼塞尔的言语；他也是个懂钓鱼的，应答得那样热络，我几乎以为我们永远动不了身了；要不然，也是埃利斯或者他的哪个同伙先出场。

好歹末了总算动身，店主同卡森坐在车后。

===Original===
We crossed the bridge, and, almost immediately leaving the main road, turned to the right up a narrower, rougher way, which, it presently appeared, led only to Wagensburg. For a while the road ran by the river: then it climbed up gradually into the woods and finally lay like a shelf cut out of the side of the forest above the water and tilted up like a ramp to the castle itself. To this there was no gateway, but the road ran right on to the terrace, the wall of which we had seen from the other side. The house was long and low, and stood upon two sides of a pleasant courtyard, to which, upon its third side, the terrace made an apron, with a row of sweet-smelling limes standing between the two. Upon the fourth side stood the stables and the chapel, with a mighty gateway between: through this gateway the road we had used went on, leading, so far as I could see, to woods and hanging pastures, and made, undoubtedly, to serve the estate. There were trees in the courtyard, and, a little to one side, by the house, an old parapeted well.

The sun was shining full on the terrace, and Mansel drove into the courtyard and stopped in the shade.

Then we alighted, and the landlord took us over the house.

This was agreeable, and full of fine, big rooms; but there was no running water, and a lot would have had to be done to turn the mansion into a comfortable home.

===Chinese===
我们过了桥，不多时便离开大路，向右拐上一条更窄、更糙的路；这条路眼见得只通往瓦根斯堡。开头一段，路贴着河走；随后渐渐爬进树林；末了像一层搁板，从水面上方的林坡里凿出来，又像一道斜坡，直翘到城堡跟前。城堡没有门楼，路一直通到露台——就是我们从对岸望见墙沿的那一座。房子又长又低，坐落在宜人庭院的两面；第三面，露台铺成一片前坪，两下之间立着一排香椴。第四面是马厩和小教堂，当中隔着一座雄伟的门楼：我们来的那条路便穿过这道门楼继续延伸，照我看，通向树林和悬在坡上的草场，无疑是为庄园产业修的。庭院里有树；稍偏一侧、靠着房子的地方，有一口老井，围着石栏。

日头正照满露台，曼塞尔把车开进庭院，停在荫凉里。

于是我们下了车，店主领我们看房子。

房子招人喜欢，宽大讲究的房间很多；却没有活水，要把它改成一处舒服的住家，还得大大动一番手脚。

===Original===
Mansel, however, seemed well pleased with all that he saw and took good stock of everything, counting up the bedrooms, stepping the salons, and snuffing the air for damp, as though he seriously contemplated taking up residence there for good and all.

At last he turned to the landlord.

“And now about water,” he said. “That’s so often the stumbling-block with these castles up in the hills.”

At once the landlord insisted that the supply was superb. No castle in all Carinthia, he declared, was better furnished with water.

===Chinese===
不过曼塞尔见什么都中意，样样都仔细打量：数卧室，步量客厅，嗅空气里有没有潮气，倒像当真打算在此长住到底。

末了他转向店主。

「再说说水的事，」他说。「山里这些城堡，十有八九就栽在这上头。」

店主立刻一口咬定，水源好得很。他夸口说，全克恩滕没有一座城堡的水比这更足。

===Original===
“Good,” said Mansel, “I’m glad to hear it, because, as a rule, with a well so close to a cliff﻿—”

Here the host interrupted to say that the well in the courtyard was little worth. That, he explained, was the original well: but, as Mansel had surmised, it was dug too close to the cliff, and its gift was meagre and uncertain. *Years ago another well had been dug in the meadows beyond the gateway, a well of great size and depth, the springs of which had never been known to fail, which was so much of a wonder that it was more famous than the castle itself, and was still known thereabouts as The Great Well of Wagensburg.*

===Chinese===
「好，」曼塞尔说，「听你这么说我很高兴，因为，一般说来，一口井离崖这么近——」

说到这里，店主插进来，说庭院里那口井没多大用处。他解释道，那是原先的老井：不过，正像曼塞尔猜到的那样，凿得离崖太近，出的水又少又不定。*多年以前，人们在门楼那边的草场上又凿了一口井，又大又深，泉源从未干涸过，奇得赛过堡子本身，比城堡还有名，那一带至今还叫它瓦根斯堡大井（The Great Well of Wagensburg）。*

===Original===
“Well, that’s all right,” said Mansel. “I suppose the water’s good.”

“The water is excellent, sir, and clear as crystal.”

“When was it last cleaned out?”

The landlord threw up his hands.

“Clean out The Great Well of Wagensburg! Why, sir, it is bottomless. I do not suppose it could be done. But the water is perfect: that I will guarantee.”

===Chinese===
「那好，」曼塞尔说。「水想来不错。」

「水好极了，先生，清得像水晶。」

「上一回清掏，是什么时候？」

店主把两手一摊。

「清掏瓦根斯堡大井！哎哟，先生，那是口无底井。我想谁也办不到。可这水是十全十美的：这个我敢担保。」

===Original===
Mansel frowned and put his head on one side.

“All wells should be cleaned from time to time. Never mind. Where does it lie?”

We followed the landlord out of the courtyard, past the chapel and stables and into a wood. Two minutes later we turned into a fair meadow that sloped gently to another wood upon the opposite side. And in the midst of the meadow lay the great well.

When we came near, it was clear that it merited its name.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔皱起眉头，把头一歪。

「井都该不时清掏。不碍事。它在哪儿？」

我们跟着店主出了庭院，经过小教堂和马厩，走进一片林子。两分钟后，我们转进一块好好的草场，缓缓斜向对面另一片林子。大井就卧在草场中央。

走近一看，它果然名不虚传。

===Original===
Twelve feet across it was, with a broad stone parapet about it and a turret-shaped roof above.

Four pillars were supporting the roof, and two of these held the windlass, which was a massive business, laden with a quantity of chain. Bucket there was none, but an empty hook was dangling over the depths.

When he saw there was no bucket, the landlord’s face fell; but, after a moment, he said that no doubt it had been put in the stables against being carried off, and, begging us to await his return, started back the way we had come.

While he was gone we walked to the farther wood and gradually round the meadow, which we found was something of a plateau, for the ground fell away on three sides and only rose on one, that is to say on the side of the farther wood: here it soon rose very sharply into a peak, which commanded a view of the castle and some of the path we had come, as well as for some distance the two approaches to the meadow on which there was no wood.

Whilst we were looking about us, we perceived the landlord returning, bucket in hand, and, when we got back to the well, he and Carson were lowering it into the depths.

===Chinese===
井口宽十二英尺，四周围着宽宽的石栏，顶上一座塔楼似的罩顶。

四根柱子撑着罩顶，其中两根架着辘轳——那是笨重的大件家什，缠着好些铁链。水桶是没有的，只一只空钩悬在深井上头晃荡。

店主一见没有水桶，脸色便沉了下去；过了一会儿，他说那桶准是怕人偷走，收进马厩去了，随即央我们等他回来，顺着来路回去了。

趁他不在，我们走到那头的林子，又慢慢绕着草场走了一遭；发觉这草场颇像一块台地，因为三面的地势都往下落，只有一面升高——就是说，靠着那头林子的那一面：在这里，地势很快陡升为一座峰头，望得见城堡、望得见我们来路的一段，还有相当距离之内通往草场的两条路——那两条路上没有树子遮挡。

我们正在四下打量，看见店主提着桶回来了；等我们回到井边，他和卡森正把桶往深处放。

===Original===
The water came up clear and clean and cold, to the great glee of the landlord, who seemed by that circumstance to consider his protests proved, although there was nothing to show how much there was in the well, or whether the water itself was fit to drink. Mansel, however, appeared satisfied, and, after some further discussion, we made our way back to the castle by the path through the wood.

Thence we drove back to Lerai; and presently, having rewarded the innkeeper and declared that, if we bought Wagensburg, he should be our agent for obtaining supplies, left, as was only to be expected, amid a perfect flurry of “nods and becks and wreathed smiles.”

As we drove out of the village:

“The art of life,” said Mansel, “is to make valuable friends.”

===Chinese===
水提上来，又清又净又凉；店主喜不自胜，仿佛这一下便证明他方才的话句句是真；虽然井里有多少水、水本身够不够喝，却无从知道。不过曼塞尔似乎满意了；又商议了几句，我们便顺着穿林的路径，回到城堡。

从那里我们驶回勒拉；不多时，犒赏了店主，声明若买下瓦根斯堡，就请他做我们采办给养的代理人，随后动身——那场面，果然不出所料，是满堂「点头致意、躬身招呼、笑靥相迎」的热闹。

车子驶出村子的时候：

「人生的艺术，」曼塞尔说，「就是结交有用的朋友。」

===Original===
For the next three hours we proved the country round about, identifying castles and villages and, thanks to the power of the car, covering a great deal of ground. Then at last we turned north and ran into Salzburg that night at eleven o’clock.

Hanbury was glad to see us, and was naturally agog to hear our tale, but he had no news beyond that he had found the offices of the principal house-agents and thought he had seen Ellis at the door of our hotel.

Herein he was right.

When Mansel visited a house-agent on the following day and, after inquiries, announced that he was disposed to purchase Wagensburg, the agent opened his eyes.

“Sir,” he said, “you are a few hours too late. Wagensburg is not sold, but it is not for sale. By a curious coincidence I granted an option to purchase this very property yesterday afternoon.”

===Chinese===
接下来三个钟头，我们把四乡踏看了一遍，指认城堡和村庄，仗着车的马力，跑了好大一片地面。末了我们折向北，当天夜里十一点进了萨尔茨堡。

汉伯里见我们回来很高兴，自然急不可待要听我们讲；可他并没有什么消息，只知道他寻着了那几家顶大的房产代理行的门面，还仿佛在旅馆门口瞧见了埃利斯。

在这一点上，他没有看错。

次日，曼塞尔去见一位房产代理人，问询之后，表示有意购下瓦根斯堡；那代理人瞪大了眼睛。

「先生，」他说，「您晚到了几个钟头。瓦根斯堡没有卖掉，可是也不再出卖。说来奇巧，昨天下午，我刚把购买这处产业的选择权许给了一个人。」

## I The Well-Digger’s Statement / I 掘井人的供述

===Original===
When the first of these things happened, that is to say upon the twentieth day of April, 192‒, I was twenty-two years old, a little stronger than most men of my age, and very ready for anything that bade fair to prove more exciting than entering the office of my uncle, who was a merchant of consequence in the City of London.

I had lately been sent down from Oxford for using some avowed communists as many thought they deserved, and, though George Hanbury﻿—for he had been with me in the affair﻿—and I received much sympathy and more complimentary letters from complete strangers than we could conveniently answer, I think we were both more distressed than we would have cared to admit to take our leave of Christchurch before our time. For my part, I had been glad to get out of England and to put the matter as far away from my mind as ever I knew.

I had, then, spent five weeks at Biarritz, the guest of some people called Pomeroy, with whom, such was their benevolence, I believe I might have stayed indefinitely; but a letter from Hanbury, with whom I was to share a flat, threatening to forego the agreement if I did not return to Town, at length precipitated my departure.

===Chinese===
这些事里的头一桩发生时——就是说，在 192——年四月二十日——我年方二十二，比大多数同龄人壮实一些，而且凡是看来会比踏进我叔叔的写字间更来劲的事，我都一概兴致勃勃，乐于趋赴；我叔叔是伦敦金融城里一位颇有名望的商人。

不久之前，我叫牛津勒令退了学，只因我把几个公开的共产党人收拾了一顿——收拾之狠，许多人认为他们正活该如此；虽然我和乔治·汉伯里（George Hanbury）——他同我一起卷在这桩事里——收到了许多同情，还有更多素不相识的人寄来的恭维信，多到我们无暇一一作答，可我想，我们两个心里都觉得，没到期限便辞别基督堂学院（Christchurch），比我们肯于承认的更难受。就我而言，我倒高兴离开英国，把这桩事从心里推得愈远愈好。

于是，我在比亚里茨（Biarritz）住了五个星期，是几位姓波默罗伊（Pomeroy）的人家的座上客；他们为人厚道，我相信要在那里住多久都随我。可是汉伯里来了信——我们原说定要合住一套公寓——威胁说，我若不回城，他便要作废前约；这封信终于催我动了身。

===Original===
I returned as I had come, alone in my car, making for Dieppe and spending the first night at Angoulême and the second at Tours.

From Tours to Dieppe is a comfortable day’s run, and I rose that April morning, intending to pass my third night on the packet which should take me to England.

I left Tours about ten in the morning and came to Chartres at one. There I purchased my luncheon and, after taking in petrol, reentered the car, for the weather was very fair, and I meant to eat by the way.

Accordingly, a few miles farther, I stopped by the side of the road, and, leaving the car, sat down on a grassy bank to eat my meal.

It was a fine, smooth day, and the sunshine seemed almost as hot as it had been at Biarritz. The world, so far as I could see, I had to myself. The road stretched white and empty and straight for miles upon either hand.

I was never much of a trencherman when I had to eat alone, and my meal﻿—a pâté de Chartres and some fruit, and a bottle of beer﻿—was soon done; but, since I had plenty of time and the beer had made me heavy, I lay down in the warm grass and went to sleep.

===Chinese===
我归途一如来路：独自驾着汽车，取道迪耶普（Dieppe）；头一夜宿在昂古莱姆（Angoulême），第二夜宿在图尔（Tours）。

从图尔到迪耶普，是一天舒舒服服的路程；那个四月的清晨我起身时，心里打算着第三夜就在载我回英国的邮船上度过。

我约莫上午十点离开图尔，一点钟到了沙特尔（Chartres）。在那里买了午饭，添了汽油，重新上车；天气极好，我打算边走边吃。

于是又行了几英里，我把车停在路边，下了车，在一片生草的土坎上坐下用餐。

那是个晴朗宁帖的日子，阳光炙人，几乎同比亚里茨时相仿。目力所及，天地仿佛尽归我一人。公路白白的、空空的、直直的，前后左右伸出去好几英里。

独自吃饭时，我从来算不得大肚汉；这顿饭——一块沙特尔肉酱饼（pâté de Chartres）、几个水果、一瓶啤酒——转眼便完了；可我时辰宽裕，啤酒又催得人身上发沉，我便躺进暖烘烘的草地，睡着了。

===Original===
I now know that I must have slept for near fifty minutes before I was awakened by the voices of two men, who were somewhere quite close to me. They were speaking English, and from the speech and the tone of one of them, it was clear that his temper was out of hand.

“*You*, by hell,” he was crying, and I think it was the bitterness and the enmity with which he kept investing the pronoun that brought me so wide awake. “*You.* And who are *you*? *You* choose, do you? And what about us? Seven years I’ve done﻿—seven years out of my life. And the others﻿—”

“Your confinement,” said the other coolly, “seems to have affected your brain. The secret’s mine, and you know it. Why, because you’ve been in prison, should I make it over to you?”

“Because we’re partners,” blurted the first. I could hear him swallow. “That’s why.”

“Partners?” said the other. He laughed lightly. “Let me refresh your memory. For five years I led you, Ellis﻿—you and the other four. I gave you two-thirds of every cent we took. Then, one day, you struck. You demanded five-sixths. When I refused, you swore you’d work on your own﻿—with what result we know.” He laughed again. “So much for partnership. Add, then, two points,” he continued: “first﻿—that I had the secret before ever I saw your face, and, second﻿—that at your trial *you tried to save yourself by letting me in*.”

===Chinese===
如今我知道，我必定睡了将近五十分钟，才叫两个月人的说话声吵醒；他们就在离我很近的地方。他们说英语；从其中一人的言语和腔调听来，他分明是怒气再也约束不住了。

「*你*，见鬼，」他喊道；我想，正是他往这个代词上一处处灌注的怨毒与仇意，把我惊得彻底醒了。「*你*。你又是*谁*？*你*来挑，是吧？那我们呢？我干了七年——七年，从我这条命里挖走的七年。还有那几位——」

「你这阵子坐牢，」另一个冷冷地说，「怕是把脑子坐坏了。秘密是我的，你知道。就因为你蹲过一回监狱，我就该把它移交给你么？」

「因为我们是合伙人，」头一个冲口而出。我听得见他咽唾沫。「就为这个。」

「合伙人？」另一个说。他轻轻笑了。「我来替你温一温记性。整整五年，埃利斯（Ellis），我带着你们——你和另外四位。我们每弄到一分钱，我都分你三分之二。后来有一天，你倒戈了。你要六分之五。我拒绝了，你就赌咒要单干——结果如何，我们都知道。」他又笑了。「合伙二字，不过如此。那么，再添两点，」他接着说，「第一——早在见到你的脸之前，秘密已经在我手里；第二——开庭的时候，*你为了自保，把我供了出来*。」

===Original===
I cannot describe the contempt with which these last words were uttered, and Ellis was plainly stung, for he let out a volley of protest, declaring that it was not he that had done it, and that the papers had reported the matter wrong.

“I was in Court,” said the other, and laughed again. Then I heard him yawn. “And so, you see,” he continued, “you can’t be surprised that I don’t jump at the chance of making you free of a fortune at my expense.”

I had at first been astonished that I could hear so perfectly, for I was sure that the speakers were upon the opposite side of the bank. Then I perceived that I had my ear to a drain which must give directly into the wood beyond, and that, if I was minded to listen, I was ideally placed. But I could, of course, see nothing, and to hear, yet not see, these two fellows was more than I could endure. I therefore rose from my gully and made my way by inches to the grass which was growing long upon the top of the bank. Into this I passed, like a snake, with the utmost caution, for I could now hear the voices almost as loud as before, and in a moment I was looking down upon two men, who were standing in a miniature glade, with the wood thick about them, and the bank upon which I was lying blocking the hither end.

===Chinese===
这末一句出口时含着怎样的轻蔑，我无法形容；埃利斯分明被刺痛了，因为他喷出一连串抗议，声辩那不是他干的，是报馆把事情登错了。

「我当时就在法庭上，」另一个说，又笑了。随后我听见他打了个哈欠。「所以你瞧，」他接着说，「我不肯抓住这个机会，让你白享一份由我破费的家财，你总不至于诧异吧。」

起初我惊异自己竟能听得这样真切，因为我断定说话的人就在土坎的那一面。随后我看明白了：原来我一只耳朵正贴着一个涵洞，涵洞必定直通后面那片树林；我若存心偷听，天底下再没有比这更称心的位置了。可我自然什么也看不见；这两个家伙，听得见却看不见，叫我实在熬不住。于是我爬出自己那条沟，一寸一寸挪向坎顶上长长了的野草。我像一条蛇，万分小心地钻了进去，因为说话声如今听起来几乎同先前一样响；转眼之间，我已俯瞰着两个月人了：他们立在一小块林间空地上，四围树林密密的，而我方才躺卧的那道土坎，正挡住空地的这一头。

===Original===
The one was dressed in old tweeds, that had been well cut: he was a slight, handsome man, and wore a fair, close-cut beard: his eyes were grey and steady: he looked a gentleman. His arms were folded, and he was leaning against a tree, lazily regarding the other as though he were unclean.

The latter was a big, coarse man, soon to be fat. He was flashily dressed, with a slip to his waistcoat, and cloth-topped, patent-leather boots; and all his clothes argued an elegant taste like that of a blackamoor. His mouth was brutal, and his small, black eyes were set close in his head, and I remember wondering how two so different men could ever have agreed together for so long as five years.

Ellis was trembling with rage.

“You see,” said the other, “there’s really no more to be said. For the moment, so far as I am concerned, the treasure of Wagensburg will stay where it is. Whether later on I shall lift it, I really don’t know; but, if I do, that I shall seek your assistance, Ellis, is most improbable. Of course, you’re at liberty to go and look for yourself. You know where it is﻿—to within some four or five miles,” and, with that, he took out tobacco and started to fill a pipe.

I had never thought of such blasphemy as that which his words provoked. Ellis spouted imprecations, at once so dreadful and couched in such filthy terms that, had he then and there fallen dead, it would have seemed to me the natural consequence of such iniquity.

===Chinese===
一个穿旧花呢服，剪裁却极好：人清瘦，眉目俊，蓄一部淡色的短须，眼睛灰而沉定：望上去是位绅士。他双臂交叠，倚着一棵树，懒懒地打量另一个，仿佛那人身上带着什么腌臜。

另一个是个粗大的汉子，眼看就要发胖。他穿得花哨，马甲里衬着一块招摇的衬胸，脚上蹬一双呢面漆皮靴；他这一身衣着，处处透出一派讲究的趣味，像摩尔人（blackamoor）的做派。他一张嘴相凶横，一双眼睛又小又黑，深嵌在脑袋里，挨得极近；我还记得自己暗自纳罕：这样两个判若云泥的人，怎么会相安共事，一竟五年之久。

埃利斯气得浑身发抖。

「你明白，」另一个说，「实在没有什么可说的了。就眼下而论，在我这一头，瓦根斯堡（Wagensburg）的财宝就让它待在原地。往后我要不要把它起出来，我真的不知道；不过，就算我起，要我再求埃利斯你帮忙，那是顶顶不可能的了。当然，你尽可以自己去寻。你知道它在哪儿——误差不出四五英里。」说着，他掏出烟丝，装起烟斗来。

他这句话招来的那等渎神之咒，我从来连想也不曾想过。埃利斯泼出一阵咒骂，既骇人听闻，字字又污秽不堪；倘若他当时当地一头栽死过去，我看也不过是这等恶行自然的下场。

===Original===
The other heard him out, busy with his pipe.

Then﻿—

“Eloquent as ever,” he said. “Can you find your own way back? Oh, and by the way,” he went on, not waiting for any reply, “don’t come here again, or anywhere that I am. I have no use for you, and I dislike your company.”

He whistled as though for some dog and started to stroll down the glade, pausing for a moment to bring a match to his pipe, and commanding my great admiration by his insolent scorn of the other’s violent and menacing demeanour.

===Chinese===
另一个忙着装点他的烟斗，任他骂了个够。

然后——

「还是老样子，能言善辩，」他说。「回程的路，你认得么？哦，还有一句话，」他不等人回答，接着说，「别再到这儿来，也别到我去的任何地方来。我用不着你，我也不喜欢有你在场。」

他打了个呼哨，像唤狗一般，随即顺着空地信步走去，中途停了一停，划火柴点上烟斗；他对另一个那副凶神恶煞的架势所摆出的倨傲的蔑视，叫我不胜叹服。

===Original===
I was, indeed, in the act of admiration when the murder was done.

As the other hunched his shoulders above his pipe, Ellis struck him high up to the right of the spine, and, either from the force of the blow or from the wound, the other fell down on his face with a knife in his back.

The murderer staggered across him and nearly fell over the body, bringing himself up against a tree on the far side, panting with stress. So he stayed for a second, with his knees loose and his back flat against the trunk, staring at what he had done. Then he raised his head, and his eyes met mine.

===Chinese===
说真的，凶案就在我叹服未了的时候干出来了。

那一个正耸着肩俯就烟斗，埃利斯一记狠的，打在他脊柱右侧偏上的地方；不知是那一击的腕力还是刀伤所致，他脸朝下扑倒了，背后插着一把刀。

凶手踉跄着跨过他，险些绊倒在尸身上，撞住空地那头的一棵树才站定，喘得厉害。他这样僵了一息，膝盖发软，脊背平平地贴着树干，直瞪瞪看着自己干下的事。随后他抬起头来，目光同我的撞上了。

===Original===
I suppose it was natural that I did not seem able to move. I seemed to be in a trance.

I watched him draw out a pistol and take deliberate aim. I know his hand was unsteady, and I think the bullet went high; but the shot broke the spell that held me, and I heaved myself back down the bank before he could aim again.

I was on my feet in an instant, but, though I did not feel faint, I was shaking like a leaf. After a moment, however, I flung myself again at the bank, rather dazedly, but taking care to make the top at a different place.

Ellis was gone.

The body lay as it had fallen, and a big Alsatian was nosing and licking the face. Already there was a great stain upon the back of the light, tweed coat.

I leaped down lightly and, setting the dog aside, turned over the body as gently as I could. I remembered having read somewhere that you should not withdraw a knife. The man was breathing, so I carried him over and propped him against the bank. Then I ran for my flask, which was in the car. His eyes were half-open when I returned, and his hand was on the dog’s collar, and the dog’s head on his chest. I gave him what brandy I could, but most of it ran over his chin.

===Chinese===
想来是自然的：我竟像动弹不得似的。我仿佛被摄去了魂。

我眼看他拔出一把手枪，从容瞄准。我知道他的手在抖，我想子弹打高了；但这一枪震破了我的迷障，趁他还来不及再瞄，我已翻身滚下了土坎。

一转眼我已站起身来；虽说不觉眩晕，我却抖得像一片风里的树叶。然而片刻之后，我又懵懵懂懂扑向土坎，只是留了神，从另一处地方翻上坎顶。

埃利斯不见了。

尸身保持倒下的原样，一条高大的阿尔萨斯犬（Alsatian）正嗅着、舔着他的脸。浅色花呢上衣的背部，已经洇开好大一片血渍。

我轻轻跳下坎去，把狗拨到一边，尽量小心地把尸身翻过来。我记得在什么地方读到过：刀是不可以拔的。那人还在呼吸，我便把他抱过去，靠着土坎支起。随后我跑去取随身的小酒壶——它搁在车里。我回来时，他双眼半睁，一只手搭在狗的项圈上，狗的头伏在他胸前。我把白兰地能喂的都喂了，大半却顺着他的下巴淌掉了。

===Original===
“I saw the whole thing,” I said. “I’m sorry I couldn’t warn you, but Ellis was too quick.”

The other smiled faintly.

“But I’ll get him,” I added fiercely. “Tell me as much as you can.”

The other shook his head.

“Let him go,” he murmured. “Let him work out his own damnation. How much did you hear?”

===Chinese===
「起头到末了，我全看见了，」我说。「只恨来不及给你报信；埃利斯下手太快了。」

另一个淡淡一笑。

「可我一定要逮住他，」我狠狠补上一句。「你能说多少，就说多少吧。」

另一个摇了摇头。

「由他去吧，」他低声说。「让他自己把自己作践到该去的地方。你听到了多少？」

===Original===
“He wanted your secret,” I said. “The Wagensburg treasure, you called it. And you didn’t see the point.”

He smiled again.

“Are you fond of dogs?” he breathed.

“Yes.”

“Will you take care of mine?”

“I will.”

===Chinese===
「他想要你的秘密，」我说。「你管它叫瓦根斯堡的财宝。可你并不觉得这有什么了不得。」

他又笑了。

「你喜欢狗么？」他气声说。

「喜欢。」

「肯替我照管我的狗么？」

「肯。」

===Original===
He nodded.

“Good man,” he whispered. Then, “Look in her collar,” he murmured, “and you’ll find she can pay for her keep.”

His eyes closed then, and he lay so still for a while that I thought he was dead.

Suddenly﻿—

“Raise me,” he said. I did so. “What’s England like?” he said. “I haven’t been able to go there for seven years.”

I tried to tell him.

===Chinese===
他点了点头。

「好样的，」他低语。跟着又说：「瞧瞧她项圈里，」他喃喃道，「你会知道，她养得起自己。」

随后他阖上眼睛，静静地躺了好一会儿，我只当他已经死了。

突然——

「扶起我来，」他说。我照办了。「英格兰怎么样？」他说。「我有七年没能回去了。」

我竭力讲给他听。

===Original===
“But the country’s the same,” he said thickly. “The woods, and the meadows at sundown and﻿—”

That was his last word, for a terrible rush of blood came from his mouth, and he died as did Falstaff, speaking of green fields.

His blood was all over my hands and the dog’s coat, but I presently found a stream and cleansed the two of us.

I had rather a business to keep the dog with me, for, though she was timid, she would have stayed with the corpse: but I turned a strap, which I had, into a leash and, speaking her kindly, tried to show that I was her friend. And what with the excitement and horror of the whole business, my efforts to keep out of sight of passing vehicles, my constant outlook for Ellis and my anxiety to avoid association with the murder that had been done, I forgot to examine her collar for several hours. And this was as well, for my mind was full enough. Indeed, to this day, try as I will, I cannot tell how I came to Rouen nor yet to Dieppe. But I know that the car had been shipped and that I was aboard, arguing about quarantine, when I remembered the words of the dead Englishman.

In the same instant it came to me that, for such as had eyes to see, the collar was directly connecting me with the crime. As soon as convenient, therefore, I went up on deck, cut the leash down to a collar and, making the change in fear and trembling, stuffed the stout original into my coat pocket, out of which, do what I would, it bulged terribly.

Indeed more than once I came within an ace of dropping it overboard.

===Chinese===
「可乡下总还是老样子，」他含混地说，「那些树林，还有日落时分的草场，还有——」

这是他末一句话了；因为一大口血猛然从嘴里涌出，他就此死了——同福斯塔夫（Falstaff）一样，口里说的还是青翠的田野。

他溅了我两手血，狗身上也是；可不多时我寻见一条溪水，把我们两个都洗净了。

要把这条狗带在身边，倒费了我一番手脚：她虽胆小，却执意要守着尸首；我便把随身的一条皮带改成引绳，好言好语哄她，想让她明白我是她的朋友。加之这一整桩事的惊骇可怖、躲避开往车辆的种种苦处、时刻提防埃利斯的警觉、唯恐同这桩凶案沾上干系的忧心，我竟一连几个钟头忘了查她的项圈。这倒也好，因为我心里已经满满当当。说实话，直到今天，任凭我怎样用力想，也想不起自己是怎么到的鲁昂（Rouen），又是怎么到的迪耶普；只知道汽车已经装了船，人也上了船，正在为检疫的事同人争执，忽然想起那死去的英国人的话来。

同一瞬间我又省悟到：在长着眼睛看得见的人看来，那只项圈正直接把我同这案子拴在一处。于是一得便，我便走上甲板，把引绳铰得只剩项圈，战战兢兢改换停当，把那根粗厚的原件塞进上衣口袋；可随我怎么摆布，它总在口袋里鼓得怕人。

说真的，有一两回，我险些儿就把它丢进海里去了。

===Original===
It was in my mind to say that I had found the dog collarless on the highway, and that was the tale I told at Newhaven as carelessly as I could. But, while I told it, I sweated, and the collar in my pocket felt like a packing-case.

===Chinese===
我心里打定的话是：狗是我在公路上拾的，拾来时项圈也没有；到了纽黑文（Newhaven），我便把这番话装出满不在乎的样子说了。只是说的时候，我汗出如浆，口袋里那只项圈沉得像一只货箱。

---

===Original===
It was late when I reached London, for there was no one at Newhaven who was licensed to receive the dog, and, though I might have left her in her hutch to await the coming of the carrier for whom I had sent, I had not the heart to do so. I have never seen a dumb animal, that was not bodily sick, in such evident distress. She would neither eat nor lie down, but sat for the most part with her head drooped, staring upon the ground. If ever I made to leave her, she would look at me so miserably that I spent the whole of the morning seated on a box by her side, and, when at length the carrier took her in charge, I could not meet her gaze, but, muttering some words of comfort, patted her hanging head and hurried away.

I drove straight to —﻿—﻿—’s Hotel, there to find a letter from Hanbury asking me to dine that night at his father’s house. I accepted immediately. Indeed, the invitation was just what I wanted, for I had already determined to tell Hanbury all that had happened to me the day before and to share with him whatever a scrutiny of the dog-collar might disclose.

And here I may say that I looked at the collar in my bedroom at —﻿—﻿—’s Hotel, but could see nothing at all unusual within or without. The plate was engraved with a date, 17‒10‒16, which, I supposed, meant something to the dead man, but, except that it was un-English, there was nothing about it which called for any remark. I was sure, however, that when the leather was opened, we should find something within, and I hoped very much that this would prove of more interest than a hundred-pound note.

By the time I had bestowed the car and had bathed, it was six o’clock; so I put on evening clothes and, slipping the collar, which I had tied up with string so that it lay pretty flat, into my pocket, walked to the Club of which I had quite recently been elected a member.

It was unlikely that news of the murder would yet have reached England: for all that, I scanned every evening paper carefully; but there was nothing in any of them about the crime.

===Chinese===
我到伦敦时天色已晚，因为纽黑文没有领有执照可以接收狗的人；虽说本可把她留在犬舍里，等我着人去请的运送人上门来接，我却狠不下这个心。不曾生病的牲畜里，我从没见过哪一只露出这样明显的凄惶。她不吃，也不卧，多半时候垂着头坐在那里，直盯着地面。我只要一作势要走，她便用那样凄苦的眼光望我，害得我整整一上午坐在她身旁的木箱上；末了运送人把她接了过去，我却不敢同她对望，只喃喃说了几句安抚的话，拍拍她垂着的头，便匆匆走开了。

我径直驱车去了某某旅馆，在那里见到汉伯里一封信，约我当晚到他父亲家里吃饭。我立刻应允。说实在，这份邀请正是我求之不得的，因为我早已打定主意，要把前一天遭遇的一切原原本本告诉汉伯里，并且不论细看狗项圈能看出什么名堂，都同他一道分担。

这里不妨说一句：我在某某旅馆的卧房里也细看过那只项圈，里里外外却看不出一丝异样。牌子上刻着一个日期，17-10-16，想必对死者自有它的意义；但除了不像英式做法，也没有什么可说之处。可我确信，只要割开皮革，里面必有东西；我还极希望，那东西会比一张百镑的钞票更有些看头。

等我安顿好汽车、沐浴完毕，已是六点钟；我便换上夜礼服，把那只项圈——已经用细绳捆好，可以平平地躺着——塞进衣袋，步行去了我新近才当选会员的那家俱乐部。

凶案的消息此刻还不至于传到英国；话虽如此，我还是把每一份晚报仔细搜检了一遍；但哪一份里也寻不见这桩罪案的踪影。

===Original===
I was to dine at eight, but so soon as I had done with the papers, such was my impatience to see Hanbury that I felt I could wait no longer, and, very soon after seven, I went to the members’ lobby where I had left my coat.

My coat was gone.

For a moment I stared blankly at the peg on which it had hung: then I began to go feverishly about the cloakroom, plucking at coat after coat which at all resembled mine and hoping desperately to come upon it.

I could only think that some member had made a mistake, for the Club was above suspicion and I could not believe that a stranger would have been so bold or so successful. Yet I was worried to death, because whoever had taken the coat was bound to find the collar and certain to remark the inscription upon the plate. Indeed, I saw myself going down to a very sea of troubles, for, you will remember, I had sworn I found the dog collarless, and thereby put myself on the wrong side of a matter the truth of which Ellis and I alone knew.

I had made my vain tour of the lobby and was standing there hot and helpless, wondering what I should do, when a tall, nice-looking man limped into the room.

===Chinese===
我八点钟赴宴；可报纸一放下，急着见汉伯里的那股焦躁便再按捺不住，觉得再也等不得了；七点刚过不久，我便往寄存外套的会员门厅去了。

外套不见了。

我对着那根衣帽钩直直发了半晌的怔；随后便发疯似的在衣帽间里转来转去，凡同我那件略有几分相似的衣裳，一件件扯扯拉拉，死心塌地巴望把它撞个正着。

我只能猜想是哪位会员拿错了，因为这家俱乐部无可指摘，我也不信哪个外人竟敢这样放肆，还能这样得手。可我急得要死：拿走外套的人必定会发现项圈，也必定会留意牌子上刻的字。我简直看见自己一头栽进无边的苦海；因为你们记得，我赌过咒说狗是项圈也没有的——这就把自己摆到了事情的错的一边，而事情的真相，天底下只有埃利斯和我两个人心里有数。

门厅里白转了一圈，我又急又乏，束手无策地站在那里正拿不定主意，一个高身材、眉目清朗的男人一瘸一拐地走进了房间。

===Original===
I suppose my face told my story, for he looked straight at me and smiled.

“I’m awfully sorry,” he said.

Then he slid out of his coat and held it for me to put on.

I stared like a fool.

“It’s yours, isn’t it?” he said. “*Dog-collar in the right-hand pocket?*”

“That’s right,” I blurted somehow.

===Chinese===
想必我的脸色把原委全招了；因为他直望着我，微微一笑。

「实在对不住，」他说。

说着他脱下自己的外套，抖开来替我撑住，让我穿上。

我傻子似的直瞪着他。

「这是你的，不是么？」他说。「*右手口袋里，有条狗项圈？*」

「不错，」我不知怎么冲口答道。

===Original===
Then I turned round and he helped me into the coat.
