“And a good thing too,” he said. “But for that collar, I very much doubt if you’d ever have seen it again. It’s exactly like mine. I didn’t know there were two such good garments about. And this doesn’t mean I’m not sorry, because I am. It was most careless of me.”

I assured him that it did not matter and would have gone, but he detained me by talking, whilst he was finding his coat, and, when we went into the hall, he laid a hand on my shoulder and called a page.

“My name is Mansel,” he said gravely. “I beg that you’ll drink with me.”

I found it difficult to refuse, so I said I would take a cocktail, and we went and stood by the fire and I told him my name.

===Chinese===
我转过身去，他扶着我把外套穿上。

「这也是桩好事，」他说。「要不是那只项圈，我十分怀疑你还能不能再见到它。它同我那件一模一样。我原不知道世上竟有两件这样好的衣裳。这可不是说我不觉得抱歉；我是抱歉的。是我太大意了。」

我向他担保说这算不了什么，转身便要走，他却一面翻找自己的外套，一面谈笑把我绊住了；到了门厅，他把手搭上我的肩头，唤来一个小听差。

「我叫曼塞尔（Mansel），」他庄重地说，「请你陪我喝一杯。」

我实在难以推辞，便说要一杯鸡尾酒；我们走到炉火边站定，我也把名字告诉了他。

===Original===
When we had drunk, he turned.

“I must make a confession,” he said. “I’m very interested in the date upon your dog-collar. Why did you put it there?”

There were a thousand answers: but I had not one upon my tongue. Yet, if I had been ready, I do not think I should have lied again. Honestly, I was rather grateful that the blow had fallen so soon, for, at least, in this way I had the chance of telling my tale before the papers told theirs, and Mansel had the look of a capable friend.

“I didn’t put it there,” said I.

“Ah,” said he, and waited.

===Chinese===
酒一喝完，他便转过身来。

「我得招认一件事，」他说。「你那条狗项圈上的日期，我很有几分兴趣。为什么要刻上它？」

回答有一千个：可我一个也说不出。不过，即使答得上来，我想我也不会再撒谎了。老实说，这一击来得这样快，我倒有几分感激；因为至少这样一来，我抢在报纸之前把故事讲出来的机会还在，而曼塞尔，一副干练朋友的模样。

「那不是我刻的，」我说。

「哦，」他说，随即等着。

===Original===
“I can’t tell you now,” I went on, “because it’s too long a story, but if you’ll make an appointment﻿ ﻿…”

“Any time after ten tonight,” he said, and, with that, he gave me his card.

This bore the address of a flat in Cleveland Row.

“Can I bring a friend?” I said suddenly.

“Why certainly,” said he.

===Chinese===
「这会儿没法讲给你听，」我接着说，「因为说来话长；可您要是肯约个时间……」

「今晚十点以后，几点都行，」他说；说着递给我一张名片。

名片上印着克利夫兰街（Cleveland Row）一处公寓的地址。

「我能带一位朋友来么？」我突然问。

「那自然可以，」他说。

===Original===
We parted then, and I went to my dinner with George.

To him I said nothing, except that I had an engagement that night for both of us. He looked at me rather hard, but asked no questions, and at a quarter to ten we set out for Cleveland Row.

Looking back, it seems more than strange to me that upon such a little matter as a couple of similar overcoats, hung up upon neighbouring pegs, should have depended life and death and fortune. But so it fell out. For Jonathan Mansel was, I think, the only man in the world who could have captained our enterprise and brought it through such vicissitudes to a triumphant end.

===Chinese===
我们随即分手，我便去赴同乔治的晚饭。

对他我一个字没提，只说那晚有个约，须我们两个都去。他把我盯得相当紧，却什么也没问；十点差一刻，我们动身往克利夫兰街去了。

如今回想，像两件相似的大衣挂在相邻的衣帽钩上这样一桩小事，竟系着生死、系着一生的财运——这在我看来真是奇上加奇。可事情就这样发生了。因为乔纳森·曼塞尔（Jonathan Mansel），我想，是普天之下唯一能把我们这番事业带在手上、历尽万般变故而终成大功的人。

---

===Original===
Mansel and George Hanbury listened to my tale without a word.

When I had finished, Mansel sat back in his chair.

“I can’t tell you much,” he said. “But the inscription on that collar is not a date. It’s a number. The man you saw murdered was in the secret service during the War. I knew him﻿—as ‘Number 171016.’ He was known to be a crook but he was a very good man. He’d a big future. Then Ellis cooked his goose﻿—saddled him with four big robberies in open Court. They let him get out of the country, but of course he couldn’t come back. He was broken up, I heard, for his heart was right in the game. I suppose that’s why﻿ ﻿…”

He broke off and nodded at the collar.

For a while none of us spoke.

Then I took out a knife and passed it to Mansel.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔和乔治·汉伯里听我讲完这段事，一言未发。

我讲完，曼塞尔往椅背上一靠。

「能告诉你们的不多，」他说。「可那只项圈上刻的不是日期，是号码。你看见被杀的那个人，大战期间在情报机关里做事。我认识他——大家都叫他『171016 号』。人人都晓得他是个骗子，可他是个非常出色的人，前程远大。后来埃利斯断送了他——当着满法庭，把四桩大劫案栽到他头上。他们放他出了国，可他自然回不来了。听说他垮了，因为他那一颗心原是在这行当里的。我想，就为这个……」

他把话头一收，朝那只项圈点了点头。

一时我们谁也没作声。

随后我掏出一把小刀，递给曼塞尔。

===Original===
“Will you open the collar?” I said.

We were sitting about a table, with the collar before us and a light hung above.

Mansel cut some stitches and, little by little, ripped the lining away. Almost at once some yellow material appeared, very stained and wrinkled and lying as flat against the collar as the lining itself. I made sure this was padding, but, when he had made the opening a little larger, Mansel got hold of the stuff and pulled it out.

It was a piece of oiled silk and seemed to have been part of a tobacco pouch, for, when it was unfolded, it had the form of an envelope without its flap. Within this again was a piece of thin notepaper, of which when it was opened, we could see three sides had been covered with a clear, close-written hand.

Mansel read it aloud, while Hanbury and I peered, one over either arm.

===Chinese===
「你把项圈拆开，好不好？」我说。

我们围着一张桌子坐定，项圈摆在面前，头顶吊着一盏灯。

曼塞尔剪断几针线脚，一点一点把衬里撕开。几乎立刻便露出一些黄色的东西，污渍斑斑，皱缩不堪，平平地贴着项圈，同衬里一样服帖。我只当那不过是衬垫；可他把豁口再撑大一点，便一把捏住那东西，拽了出来。

那是一块油绸，看样子原是烟丝袋上的一部分，因为摊开来便是一只去了封盖的信封的形状。里面还有一张薄薄的信纸；展开来看，三面都写满了清晰而紧行的字迹。

曼塞尔高声念着；汉伯里和我一边一个，各从他一条臂膀上望过去。

===Original===
> “My great-grandfather dug the great well of Wagensburg. He and his brother dug it with their father, the three working together in the great drought of 17﻿—. The well is ninety feet deep. The first spring rises thirty feet down, so that normally there is sixty feet of water. There is no well like it hereabouts. They could not have got so deep but for the great drought. All the work in the well was done by my great-grandfather and his brother and their father alone. The masons cut the stones as they were told and brought them and the wood and the mortar to the top, but no one went down except the father and his two sons. That was by order of the Count. They used to sleep at the Castle, whilst they were doing this work. Out of the well there runs a shaft. The shaft leaves the well about eighty feet down. It runs up at an angle into a chamber. The chamber is just above the level of the first spring. No one knew of the shaft except my great-grandfather and his father and his brother and the Count. The shaft was very difficult to dig. The Count had an evil name and was very much feared. He would go down into the well to see the work. The rain came before the work was done, and the Count was beside himself for fear that they would not be able to finish it before the water came in. At the end they were working day and night. This was because the Count would let no one else go down the well. When the shaft and the chamber were done, the four of them went down one night when everyone else was asleep. The Count had two leather bags. These were very heavy. They got them down and up the shaft and into the chamber. It was raining hard, and the water was up to the shaft, and the next day it was above it. The three finished the masonry of the well, but the Count now allowed them helpers to keep the water down. On their last night at the Castle the Count killed two of the three with his own hands. My great-grandfather escaped, and though a long search was made for him he was never found. He escaped into Italy and returned two years later, when the Count was dead. He always meant to go down and get the bags, but there was never another drought severe enough to empty the well. When he was dying he told my grandfather this, and my grandfather told my father and he told me. I don’t know which way the shaft runs. There are steps in the shaft. The Count cannot have recovered the bags because the great drought was followed by three very rainy years, and, as the springs are normally abundant, he could not have emptied the well without employing a lot of labour. I am unmarried and I have never told this to anyone else.
> “**Note.** The Count was clearly the notorious Axel the Red, who was nothing more or less than a common robber, and was reputed to have amassed a vast fortune. In 1760 Wagensburg was burnt and he perished in the flames. The castle passed to the Crown, and was sold twenty years later. It was then restored.
> “Wagensburg lies in Carinthia twenty-nine miles from Villach and four from Lerai.
> “September, 1904.
> “**Note.** W. came into the market in 1910.
> “Failed. Ellis knows.
> “June, 1910.”

===Chinese===
> 「我的曾祖父挖过瓦根斯堡那口大井。他和他的兄弟，是同他们的父亲一道挖的，三个人在17——年的大旱里合力干成。井深九十英尺。第一道泉在三十英尺深处涌出，所以寻常总有六十英尺的水。这一带没有第二口井比得上它。若不是那场大旱，他们决不能挖得这样深。井里的活计，全是我曾祖父和他兄弟、他们的父亲三个人做的。石匠照吩咐把石头凿好，把石头连同木料、灰浆运上井口；下井的却只有父亲和他的两个儿子。这是奉了伯爵的令。干这活的时候，他们吃睡都在堡里。井里通出一条竖井。竖井在井深约八十英尺处岔出去，斜着往上，通进一间石室。石室恰在第一道泉的水位之上。竖井的事，除了我曾祖父、他的父亲、他的兄弟和伯爵，谁也不知道。竖井开凿极难。伯爵名声很坏，人人怕他。他要下到井里去察看活计。活计没完，雨先来了；伯爵急得发狂，生怕水进来之前干不完。到了末了，他们日夜赶工。这是因为伯爵不许别人下井。竖井和石室完工之后，一天夜里，趁着旁人都在熟睡，他们四个下了井。伯爵带着两只皮袋。皮袋很沉。他们把它们顺着竖井搬上搬下，弄进了石室。那时大雨滂沱，水已经涨到竖井口，第二天便漫过了。井的砌石是那三个人完工的，只是伯爵如今许他们雇人帮着压水了。他们在堡里住的末一夜，伯爵亲手杀了三个中的两个。我曾祖父逃脱了；虽然搜捕了很久，却始终没有寻见。他逃进意大利，两年之后回来，那时伯爵已经死了。他一心想下井去取出那些皮袋，可再没有一场大旱厉害得能把井淘空。他临终把这事告诉了我祖父；祖父告诉了我父亲；父亲又告诉了我。竖井朝哪边去，我不知道。竖井里有石阶。伯爵决不能已经起出了那些皮袋，因为大旱之后接连着三个多雨的年头，泉水本来又旺，他若不雇一大批人手，决不能把井掏干。我没有娶亲，这件事我从未告诉过任何人。
> **附记。** 这位伯爵显然就是恶名昭著的红发阿克塞尔（Axel the Red），说到底不过是个寻常的劫盗，据说积攒下一宗庞大的家财。1760 年瓦根斯堡焚毁，他葬身火窟。城堡收归王室，二十年后变卖，其后经过修缮。
> 瓦根斯堡在克恩滕（Carinthia），距菲拉赫（Villach）二十九英里，距勒拉（Lerai）四英里。
> 1904 年 9 月。
> **附记。** W 于 1910 年上市。
> 未成。埃利斯知情。
> 1910 年 6 月。」

===Original===
I stood up and looked at George.

“Well,” I said, “when do we start?”

George Hanbury shrugged his shoulders.

“Your time’s my time,” he said.

“And my time’s Mansel’s,” said I.

I have often wondered how I came to say such a thing to a man upon whom, three hours before, I had never set eyes. Yet I meant what I said: and I think the truth is that the royalty of Mansel’s nature had already subjected me, for I know that, if he had said at once that he could not join us, I should have been unreasonably dismayed.

===Chinese===
我站起来，望着乔治。

「怎么样，」我说，「咱们几时动身？」

乔治·汉伯里耸了耸肩。

「你的时间便是我的时间，」他说。

「我的时间便是曼塞尔的时间，」我说。

我常常纳罕，对这样一个人——三个钟头之前，我从未见过他一面——我怎么会说出这样的话来。可我说的是真心话：我想，实情是，曼塞尔天性里的王者气度已经把我收服了；因为我知道，他倘若立刻说他不能入伙，我准会无端地沮丧。

===Original===
Mansel rose from his chair and knocked out his pipe at the grate. Then he stood up quite straight and folded his hands.

“This may be a school-treat,” he said, “and it may not. Treasure and trouble frequently go together. I haven’t been Villach way since the year before the War, but unless things are very different to what they were﻿—well, if you fought a duel with a couple of Lewis guns, nobody’d take the trouble to come and see what it was. As likely as not they wouldn’t hear you. It’s not at all crowded. Well, that’s all right for a school-treat.” He stopped there for a moment. Then he proceeded thoughtfully. “Life’s full of twists and turns, but to take on a job like this is to tackle Blind Corner itself: and that’s a turning where danger signs and warnings are little worth, for all the care in the world won’t help you to see ahead, and it’s never policed. Whether you two are free to go is your affair. I will go﻿—upon one condition, which I see slight reason why you should accept. The condition is that from first to last in this venture you two will do what I say. You see, I’m older.”

I thought the condition a very mild one and said as much: so did Hanbury. But it has occurred to me since that I should have found it presumptuous in anyone else.

Be that as it may, we gave our word to obey him in every particular, and then we sat down at his feet while he rough-hewed our campaign.

Of this, he showed that there would be three phases. First, the acquisition of Wagensburg: then, the lifting of the treasure: and, lastly, the disposal of it. And, since all he said was very much to the point, I will use, so far as I can recall them, his very words.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔从椅上立起身，在炉栅上磕了磕烟斗。随后他站得笔直，双手交叠。

「这也许不过是学堂游园会那一类的事，」他说，「也许不是。财宝和麻烦常常结伴同行。大战前一年以后，我没再去过菲拉赫那一带；可要是那儿同当年没什么两样——那么，你们就是用两挺路易斯机枪决斗，也没有谁肯费神跑来瞧瞧是怎么回事。多半他们连响声也听不见。那儿一点也不挤。好，这对学堂游园会倒正合适。」他停了片刻，随后沉吟着说下去。「人生尽是曲折；可接下这样一桩差事，就是去闯『盲角』本身：那是一处弯道，危险路标和警告在那里全不中用，因为你再怎么当心，也看不见弯那边，而那里从来无人巡守。你们两位走得开走不开，是你们自己的事。我是要去的——只是依一个条件；我看你们没有多少道理要依它。条件是：这桩买卖从头到尾，你们两位都照我说的做。你们明白，我年长些。」

我觉得这条件十分温和，便照实说了：汉伯里也一样。可我事后想起，这条件若是出自身外任何人，我准会觉得是僭妄。

话虽如此，我们还是立誓，事无巨细都听他调度；随后我们便坐在他脚下，由他把我们这番征略削凿出一个毛坯来。

照他所见，这一番事该分三个阶段。第一，取得瓦根斯堡；其次，起出财宝；末了，处置财宝。他说的句句中的；所以我便就记忆所及，把他的原话照录如下。

===Original===
“The first thing to do is to buy the property. Until we’re the landlords, the treasure is not ours to lift. To rent the place may be simpler and, so, tempting: but I don’t see the force of finding a King’s ransom for somebody else. If Wagensburg’s in the market, well and good. I’ll find the money: I don’t think the price will be high. If it’s not in the market, we shall have to pay more than it’s worth: but in these days you can usually buy a place even if it isn’t for sale.

“To get to the treasure shouldn’t be difficult, but, unless the well’s dry, I’m sure we can’t do it alone. To keep pace with springs that give freely is very hard work: it’s a very deep well: all wells are dangerous. We may even find that we can’t use the well at all, but must drive a new shaft from above. Above all, it’s most important that we should not be disturbed. We should only have to be seen at work to be suspected. To my way of thinking, it’s at least a six-men job. If we each take a servant who’s honest and fit and game, we shall get there quicker and better and much more comfortably.

“How we shall dispose of the treasure is a matter we can only decide when we see what shape it takes.

“So much for the plan of action.

“What turns the whole thing from an exercise into a venture is the fact that *Ellis knows*. Whether we shall clash with him or not, we can’t possibly tell. I hardly think it’s likely myself. Sooner or later, of course, Wagensburg will draw Ellis as a magnet draws steel. ‘Where your treasure is,’ you know. But, when a man’s just done a murder, he usually lies pretty low: and, when he knows that somebody saw him do it, he lies lower still. For all that, what Ellis will not understand is why you haven’t gone to the police. That will make him think very hard. And, knowing that you overheard what the dead man said, he may attribute your silence to a wish to hide something which reference to the police would disclose. In which case we should clash﻿ ﻿… Because of this possibility we’ve got to move on our toes, always go the long way round and watch with both eyes. Remember this. Whether he knows it or not, we’re going to get in Ellis’s way. He may never know it. But if he does know it, well, you’ve seen what he does with people who get in his way﻿—provided they give him the chance.”

===Chinese===
「头一件事是把这份产业买下来。我们一天不是地主，那财宝便一天不归我们去起。租下来也许更省事，所以也有诱惑力；可替别人凑出一笔国王的赎金来，我看不出有什么道理。瓦根斯堡若在市面上，那再好不过。钱我来想法子：价钱我想不会高。它若不在市面上，我们就得出高于实价的价钱；不过如今这年头，一处产业纵然不肯出卖，你也多半买得到手。

「要够到财宝，不该有多大难处；可除非井是干的，我断定单凭我们自己是办不到的。要赶得上畅涌的泉水，是极费力的活：井非常深：而井总是危险的。我们甚至会发现井根本用不得，必须从上面打一条新竖井。最要紧的是，我们决不能受搅扰。只要叫人看见我们在干活，便会被起疑心。依我看，这至少是六个人的活。我们若每人带一个诚实、能干、有胆色的仆人，便会到得更快、做得更好，人也舒服得多。

「财宝到手之后如何处置，那要等看清它是什么形状，才拿得了主意。

「行动方略，就说到这里。

「把整件事从一场演习变成一桩冒险的，是这样一个事实：*埃利斯知情*。我们会不会同他撞上，谁也说不定。我自己倒觉得未必。不过，瓦根斯堡迟早会像磁石吸铁一样把埃利斯吸来。『你的财宝在哪里』，这话你们是知道的。可一个人刚杀过人，往往蛰伏得很深；他要是知道有人看见了这一幕，便蛰伏得更深。话虽如此，埃利斯有一层想不透：你们为什么不去报告警察。这会叫他绞尽脑汁。他既知道你们偷听了那死人临终的话，也许便认定你们的缄默，是想遮掩一桩一经报警便会败露的事。果真如此，我们就要撞上了……因为可能有这一层，我们必须踮着脚尖行动，永远绕着远路走，双眼并用提防。记住这个。无论他知道不知道，我们是要挡埃利斯的路了。他也许永远不知道。可他一旦知道，那么，他怎样处置挡他路的人，你们已经见识过了——只要人家给他这个机会。」

===Original===
That was as much as he said at that time: while I saw the force of most of it, I did not agree that there was anything to fear from Ellis, for, though, as I had reason to know, he was a desperate man, I could not believe that he would challenge six men at once and I was quite certain that if and when he did he would lose his match.

Then we found pencil and paper and, taking the well-digger’s statement, made a plan of the well and did what we could to place the chamber.

We were all agreed that it would be pleasanter to reach the chamber by digging than by way of the well. The shaft had been mostly full of water for some hundred and sixty years: the chamber had been sealed for the same period: and, if you must visit places so long abandoned to Nature, it is very much more agreeable to have the daylight at your back. But, though the prospect of inspecting them by lantern was not inviting, it was, I think, the unmistakable likeness which the plan bore to a trap that made us strive so hard to find out another way.

But we could not.

===Chinese===
那时他就说到这么多为止。这些话大多有斤有两，我领教了；可要说埃利斯有什么可怕，我不认账，因为，虽说我有缘由知道他是个亡命之徒，我却不信他敢同时招惹六个人；并且我十分有数，他果真招惹的那一天，输的必定是他。

于是我们寻出铅笔和纸，就着掘井人的供述，画出那口井的图形，又尽力去安放那间石室。

我们一致认为，与其取道水井，不如一路掘进去到石室，要受用得多。那条竖井一百六十年来大半灌满了水：石室也封闭了同样的岁月：而这种久已交给造化的地方，你既然非去不可，背后有日光总要好得多。然而，虽说提着灯笼去查勘它们的前景并不诱人，我可想，图样上那副再明白不过的兽夹嘴脸，才是逼我们那样苦心别寻蹊径的缘故。

可我们终究没找出来。

===Original===
Without knowing the angle at which the shaft had been driven and the direction in which it left the well, we could make out no more than that the roof of the chamber must lie about twenty-five feet below ground and would most likely be encountered not more than twenty and not less than ten paces from the side of the well. This did not sound so formidable to Mansel and me, but, after a great deal of labour, George, who alone of us laid any claim to mathematical powers, demonstrated to our amazement that even that would leave some eight hundred square yards to be searched, and we gave up the attempt.

We studied the statement and the plan we had made until we knew them by heart, and we raked the former for inferences, until we had almost deduced the proportions of the Count: but it was not an unprofitable enterprise, for, by the time we had done, it was plain that, when we went abroad, statement and plan could both be left with some Bank, because, short of loss of reason, nothing could ever erase their particulars from our minds.

Then we discussed preparations and how soon we could start, and the getting of the servants, and what my uncle would say when told I was going to travel during the summer months. But we always came back to the treasure and the chamber and the great well.

It was two o’clock in the morning before Mansel sent us away, bidding us do nothing but get some serviceable clothes and hold our tongues.

Unless he summoned us, we were not to see him for a week, but then we were to dine with him in Cleveland Row. If all had gone well, he said, he saw no reason why we should not start a week from that day.

===Chinese===
竖井是循着什么斜度凿的、又朝井壁哪一面岔出去，我们既不知情，能推定的便只是：石室的顶必在地面之下约二十五英尺，而它多半会在离井壁至多二十步、至少十步的地方碰到。这话在曼塞尔和我听来还不算怎样吓人；可乔治——我们中间唯一敢自命有几分数学本事的——费了好大的劲，竟证明给我们看：纵然如此，也还有约莫八百平方码的地面要搜索。我们大吃一惊，便把这条路放弃了。

供述和我们画的图，我们研读得烂熟于心；又把供述里能榨出的推断都榨了出来，几乎把那位伯爵的身量都推算出来了：可这营生并不白做，因为等到弄完，事情已明明白白——我们出国的时候，供述和图尽可以寄存在哪一家银行里，因为，只要神志不清不了，任何东西也磨不掉我们心里的那些细节。

随后我们商量种种准备、多快能动身、仆人到哪里去找、还有我叔叔听说我夏天要出门旅行时会发什么话。可是话锋一转，总又回到财宝、石室和那口大井上。

直到凌晨两点，曼塞尔才放我们走，叮嘱我们什么都别做，只消置办几身合用的衣裳，并且守口如瓶。

一星期之内，除非他去召见，我们不必见他；然后到克利夫兰街同他吃饭。他说，诸事若都顺遂，从那天起一星期后动身，他不觉得有什么办不到。

===Original===
“One thing more,” he added, as we stood in his hall. “The dog or the collar may link you up with the crime. I think it unlikely. But at the first breath of trouble come straight to me.”

I needed no such instruction. Mansel had become the pillar of my state.

Indeed I had made up my mind to seek him the moment I found anywhere a report of the murder. But, though each day I searched the papers faithfully, there was no mention made of it.

Nor was there at any time, so far as I saw. I never read the French papers, but I often doubt that the murder was reported at all. The venue was lonely; the victim was foreign and had probably few friends; and, if no great search was made, the body may well have escaped notice, until there was little for an unpractised eye to find irregular.

===Chinese===
「还有一句，」我们立在他家门厅里时，他补上一句。「那只狗或是那只项圈，也许会把你们同这案子连起来。我想不会。可麻烦的风才起一丝，就径直来找我。」

这样叮嘱原是不必的。曼塞尔已经成了我安身立命的柱石。

实话实说，我早已拿定主意，不管在哪里一见登出凶案的消息，立刻便去寻他。可我虽然天天忠实地翻检报纸，那桩案子却始终无人提及。

据我所见，以后也始终没有。法国报纸我从不读，可我常常疑心，这桩凶案到底登没登过报。出事的地方僻远；死者又是外邦人，交游想必寥寥；倘若不曾大举搜寻，那尸首尽可以一直无人过问，直到外行的眼睛再也看不出什么不妥为止。

---

===Original===
The next day I visited the dog and found her in good hands. The home to which she had been taken was a famous establishment, with special quarters for dogs in quarantine, and from the condition and spirits of the many dogs I saw it was plain that everything possible was done to lighten their confinement.

The poor animal was delighted to see me, and, observing her pleasure, the kennel-man was quick to bring her some fresh food in the hope that she would now break the fast she had stubbornly maintained. To our relief she did so and soon left her plate clean, and though, when I went away, she made frantic endeavours to follow, the man insisted that she would pine no longer and that, when next I came, I should find her a different dog.

I am glad to say this came true: and, by the time I left England, she was eating regularly and seemed contented with her lot.

===Chinese===
第二天我去看那只狗，见她安置得很妥当。她被送去的地方是个有名的大犬舍，专为检疫中的狗备有特殊的居所；瞧瞧我在那里见到的许多狗的境况和精神，便明白凡能使拘禁松快些的事，他们无不尽力去做。

这可怜的畜生见了我，欢喜得不得了；狗舍管理员看在眼里，忙不迭给她端来新鲜吃食，巴望她就此结束那场死倔的绝食。叫我们宽心的是，她果然吃了，不多时便把食盆舔得精光；我起身要走时，她发了疯似的想要跟上，那人却一口咬定她不会再憔悴了，下回我再来，准见到一条换了样的狗。

我很高兴这话竟成了真：待到我离开英国，她已经按顿吃食，对自己的境遇似乎也心满意足了。

===Original===
To my surprise, the interview I had with my uncle passed off smoothly enough. He certainly gave me no blessing, but, beyond remarking that six months in the City of London were of more value than twice that time spent in knocking about Europe, he made little protest against the postponement of my apprenticeship. He then sat down and wrote me out a cheque for three hundred pounds, and, when I stammered my thanks, he said very gravely that that was a present for “singeing the King of Spain’s beard.” At first I could not think what he meant, but afterwards I realised that I owed both money and favour to my discomfiture of the communists, whose doctrines and practices he held in great abhorrence.

The week of inaction to which Mansel had committed Hanbury and myself passed very slowly, and there were moments when we felt almost mutinous. But, when we began to discuss the preparations which we should have been making, if Mansel had not told us to hold our hands, the wisdom of his order became immediately plain, for we were soon out of our depth and invariably quarrelled over the very vulnerable plans we laid, till the only matter upon which we were entirely agreed was the vanity of each other’s proposals.

On the last day but one, however, a note from Mansel came to salve my impatience. In this he said that he had found a man whom he thought I might like to be my servant, that the latter would call upon me at ten on the following day and that I was to examine him thoroughly from every point of view, for, the letter concluded, although I will answer for his past, he is particularly to serve you and you will be responsible for his engagement.

===Chinese===
出我意料，同叔叔那一席话过得相当顺遂。他自然没给我什么祝福，除了说一句在伦敦金融城里过六个月，比两倍的日子在欧洲乱跑更值钱，对我学徒期的展缓也不曾多加阻拦。随后他坐下，给我开出一张三百镑的支票；我嗫嚅道谢，他十分郑重地说，那是一份谢仪，谢我「燎了西班牙国王的胡须」。起初我想不出他什么意思，过后才明白，那笔钱和那份情面，都欠着我挫辱共产党人的那一手；共产党人的学说和行径，他是深恶痛绝的。

曼塞尔叫汉伯里和我按兵不动的那一星期，过得非常慢，有几刻我们几乎要造反了。可当我们开始商量那些本来该动手置办的准备——倘若曼塞尔不曾吩咐我们按住手——他这道命令的明哲便立刻显出来：因为我们很快便超出了力所能及，拟出来的计划又处处是破绽，回回争得面红耳赤，末了唯一叫我们完全一致的，就是彼此都看穿了对方提议的虚妄。

不过，倒数第二天，曼塞尔的一张便条来了，给我心头的焦躁敷上药。信里说，他找到一个人，想必我愿意要他做我的仆人；此人明日十点来见我；我要从各个方面把他细细考较一番；信末说，虽然他的过去由我担保，他却是专来伺候你的，雇用与否，责任在你。

===Original===
I soon found that Hanbury had received a similar note, and this real evidence of progress excited us out of all reason. At the same time we were both a little uneasy at the thought of taking a decision which, if it proved mistaken, might be the undoing of us all. Of any man who was admitted to our secret would be required a discretion and loyalty which were today uncommon and might easily be called upon to bear an extraordinary strain. To recognize these qualities in repose demanded an insight which we knew very well indeed we did not possess. To add to our concern, we did not know what Mansel had told the candidates and whether we ought to disclose that the service they were ready to enter was no ordinary one. In the end we sent him a note, asking for directions, but, though he received it, he sent no answer at all, but only, as he afterwards told us, pitched it into the fire.

We had, therefore, to use our own judgment as best we could, but that we engaged Bell and Rowley and that they turned out so well cannot be counted to our credit, for I think an idiot could have seen the stuff of which they were made.

Bell was my servant. He was a quiet, little man, very sturdily built. He was serious and well-spoken, but, though he was respectful, he had none of the manner of a servant and looked like a countryman turned clerk, which I afterwards found he was. He seemed to notice nothing, yet was exceptionally observant, and he always wore the same agreeable, but something resigned expression, as though his face were a mask. I never knew him volunteer a statement unless he thought it might be of service: he never once complained: he was most faithful, and I think he thought Mansel was a god. In this tenet he was not peculiar. Rowley and Carson, Mansel’s servant, held the same view.

===Chinese===
不久我便知道汉伯里也接到了同样的信；这一步真真切切的进展，叫我们高兴得没了道理。可同时我们两个又都有些不安：要拿的主意若是错了，也许会毁掉大家。凡要容入我们秘密的人，都得有如今少见的谨慎和忠诚，而且这两样，说不定哪天要经受一场非常的考验。要在人家安坐无事时认出这些品格，得有眼力；而我们有眼没力，自己心里明镜一般。更叫我们犯难的，是我们不知道曼塞尔对那两位候选的人说过些什么，也不知道我们该不该讲明：他们预备投身的那份差使，可不是寻常的差使。末了我们给他递了一张条子，请他示下；他倒是收到了，却一个字也不回，只——照他后来告诉我们的——把它扔进了火里。

因此我们只好各凭本事，尽力判断；至于我们雇下了贝尔和罗利，而他们后来又是那样出色，这可算不到我们的功劳簿上，因为我想，就是白痴也看得出他们是好料。

贝尔做我的仆人。他是个安静的矮小汉子，体格十分敦实。他一本正经，言辞清楚；虽然恭敬，却毫无仆人的习气，看上去像个改行做了文书的乡下人——后来我知道，他正是。他仿佛什么都看不见，眼睛却又尖得异乎寻常；脸上永远带着同一种和悦而略带认命的神气，仿佛那脸是一张面具。我从不记得他主动说过一句什么话，除非他想着那话或许有用处；他从没抱怨过一回；他最忠诚，而且我想，他心里是把曼塞尔当作一位神明的。在这个信条上他并不孤单。罗利和曼塞尔的仆人卡森，抱的是一样的见解。

===Original===
I shall ever remember our dinner with Mansel, if for no other reason, because it is the solitary occasion upon which expectations which I knew it was foolish to harbour have been so startlingly exceeded.

He gave us a short dinner, which was very well cooked and served, and we drank a pink champagne, which I believe was a very rare wine though I fear that neither Hanbury nor I was old enough to appreciate its quality, but only the fact that Mansel was doing us honour. Throughout the meal our venture was not mentioned, except that he said he was glad that we liked the men he had sent, and we talked for the most part of Oxford, “which,” he said, “is the only place in the world where a man may eat his cake and have it too, for the years he wastes there are beyond measure profitable.”

It was after the cloth had been drawn and the servants had left the room that he told us quietly that all had panned out very well, and that, if we had no objection, we would start in two days’ time. Before we had recovered from our astonishment, he began to relate exactly what he had done, wasting no words and in no way pretending to authority, but, when they were not apparent, giving his reasons for his actions and speaking as though he were a staff-officer reporting to his equal or senior the measures which he had taken in accordance with orders received. The manifest excellence of his forethought apart, how he had accomplished so much was a sheer mystery to me and ever will be, for I never in my life saw him hurry or use the telephone, and he had spent the weekend in Hampshire, as he always did when living at Cleveland Row.

===Chinese===
那一顿同曼塞尔的晚饭，我将永远记得——哪怕别无缘故，也只为这是唯一的一回：我知道存指望是糊涂的，可指望竟那样惊人地被超过了。

他给我们一顿简便的晚餐，做得好，伺候得也好；我们喝一种粉红香槟，我相信那是极稀罕的酒，只可惜汉伯里和我都还年轻，尝不出它的好处，只尝得出曼塞尔在抬举我们。整顿饭，我们的事业只字未提，只他说了句他很高兴我们中意他荐去的人；我们谈的大半是牛津，「那是，」他说，「普天之下唯一一处，人可以吃了糕又还留得着糕的地方，因为在牛津虚掷的岁月，受益无穷。」

直到撤了桌布、仆人们退出餐室，他才悄悄告诉我们：诸事都办得十分顺当，我们若不反对，两天后动身。我们还没从惊愕里醒过来，他已开始细说他做了的一切，不费一词，绝不拿架子；凡道理不显处，他便说出行事的理由，口吻俨然是一位参谋军官，遵着命令行事，向上司或同侪报告他据令采取的措施。撇开他这番远虑显而易见的高妙不谈，单说他怎样成就了这许多，在我是一个十足的哑谜，而且永远是哑谜：我一生没见他慌忙过，也没见他动过电话；而且那个周末他是在汉普郡过的——住在克利夫兰街时，他向来如此。

===Original===
Be that as it may, our preparations were complete, and we were to start on Thursday, that is, in two days’ time.

I will not set down his tale of the arrangements he had made, because they will presently appear, but will only say that the servants were to take our baggage to an hotel at Salzburg, that we were to travel to the same town by car, and that such as might desire to know our business were to be told that Mansel was a great trout-fisherman and that we were all three bound for the streams of Carinthia to see what could be done in that quarter.

And here I may say that anyone who was told this was shown one side of the truth, for Mansel was more fond of fishing than of almost anything else, and Hanbury and I learned our first lessons in the art of angling not very far from Wagensburg itself.

That his preparations had involved a certain outlay was clear, but, when we spoke of money and stammeringly asked to be allowed to contribute towards the expense, Mansel said that such matters could wait till the treasure was found: however, on our persisting, he promised to keep an account and to consider two-thirds of all that he was expending as our affair.

Then we gave him our passports, for he was to look them over and have them ordered to his liking.

===Chinese===
不管怎样，一切准备都已停当，我们定在星期四——也就是两天之后——动身。

他那套安排，我不录下来了，因为它们不久自会露面；只说一句：仆人们先把我们的行李送到萨尔茨堡（Salzburg）一家旅馆；我们三个人随后自己驱车往同一座城去；凡想打听我们营生的人，一概告诉他们：曼塞尔是位钓鳟鱼的大行家，我们三个全是奔克恩滕的溪流去的，看看在那方面能干出些什么名堂。

这里不妨说一句：凡是听了这话的人，算是见了实情的一面；因为曼塞尔爱钓鱼，几乎胜过爱世上的一切，而汉伯里和我学钓鱼的头几课，就在离瓦根斯堡不远的地方。

他的筹备显然花了一笔钱；可我们一提到钱，嗫嚅着请求分担开销，曼塞尔说，这些且等财宝找到再算：不过我们一再坚持，他便答应记一本账，把他花出去的一切，三分之二算在我们名下。

随后我们把护照交给他，因为他要检视一遍，把它们调理得合他的心意。

===Original===
After that he brought out the well-digger’s statement and the map we had made, and, when we had studied them both for as long as we pleased, he sealed them up in an envelope and asked me the name of my Bank. I told him. Then he wrote upon the envelope:

> 171016. This is the property of Richard William Chandos, and is lodged for safe custody with the Manager of the Pall Mall Branch of —﻿—﻿—’s Bank,

and gave it to me.

===Chinese===
这之后，他取出掘井人的供述和我们画的图，由我们随意研读了多久都行；然后他把两样封进一只信封，又问我的银行是哪家。我告诉了他。于是他在信封上写道：

> 171016。此件为理查德·威廉·钱多斯（Richard William Chandos）之物，存于某某银行蓓尔美尔街分行经理处保管，

写罢交给了我。

===Original===
“You must lodge that tomorrow,” he said, “and see that you get a receipt.”

This I promised to do.

Then, of course, we fell to talking of our venture, but, after telling us something of the country in the heart of which Wagensburg lay﻿—for, though he did not know the castle, he had stayed in those parts for one summer before the War﻿—Mansel began to speak of trout and trout-fishing and very soon had us engrossed in what he said, which, I think, was just what he wanted, for, if we were to set up for fishermen, it was as well that we should know something of the art. And from trout he led us to streams, and from streams to rivers, and thence, naturally, again to Oxford, and there we stayed very contentedly until it was time for us to go.

At ten on Thursday morning we were to meet again﻿—an engagement which Hanbury and I would not have missed or exchanged for one of the very bags which the Count had borne down the well, for there we were to get into Mansel’s Rolls-Royce and drive with him to Dover and so, by France and Germany, clean into Carinthia.

Yet, as it happened, we did not keep that engagement, and the plans which Mansel had laid were unfulfilled: and the whole face of our adventure was changed in the twinkling of an eye, before it was ever begun. And all this, because I stopped in the street to look into a window.

===Chinese===
「这个你明天必须去存上，」他说，「务必讨一张回执。」

这我答应照办。

然后，不用说，我们便谈起我们那桩事业来；瓦根斯堡坐落在什么样的乡野深处，曼塞尔先给我们讲了些——他虽没见过那座堡，大战前在那一带住过一个夏天——随后便说起鳟鱼和钓鳟，不久就把我们说得入了迷；我想，这正是他求之不得的，因为我们既要充作渔人，这一行的门道总该懂得几分。由鳟鱼他领我们到溪流，由溪流到江河，由此自然又回到牛津；我们在那里心满意足地待着，直到该走的时候。

星期四上午十点，我们本要再聚——这一约，汉伯里和我便是拿伯爵亲手搬下井去的那一只皮袋来换，也断乎不肯误了或是换了；因为到时我们就要坐进曼塞尔的劳斯莱斯，随他一路驶向多佛尔，再经法兰西、德意志，一径直入克恩滕。

可偏偏，我们没有赴那个约，曼塞尔布置的一切都成了空：我们那场冒险的整个面貌，在它还不曾开始之前，已在一眨眼之间改换一新。而这一切，都只因为我在街上停了停，朝一家橱窗里望了一眼。

## V We Go to Ground / V 我们遁入地穴

===Original===
By the time I awoke the next morning some order had been restored, and a table, upon which Rowley was serving breakfast, had been set in the harness-room. My head was sore and tender, but did not ache, and, though I would have liked to go out and breathe some fresh air﻿—for neither by door nor window did the harness-room give upon the outside of the castle﻿—I felt very little the worse for anything that had happened the night before.

Then Mansel and Hanbury appeared from the kitchen hall, and I learned that Carson and Bell were guarding the kitchen-quarters from the first floor of the house. To command the courtyard was simple, for this could be done from any window that looked upon it: and, as luck would have it, the southwest corner of the castle ran into a staircase-turret, from which anyone approaching the kitchen from the meadow side could be easily shot down.

There was, of course, a very great deal to be settled, but we had but to raise one issue to perceive that its consideration was depending upon our determination of another, and, by the time we had breakfasted, we had done little but agree upon two or three matters of fact.

===Chinese===
到第二天早晨我醒来的时候，秩序已大致恢复；马具房里安下了一张桌子，罗利正在桌上摆早饭。我的头又胀又软、一碰就疼，却算不得疼；虽然我很想出去吸几口新鲜空气——因为马具房无论门还是窗，都不朝着城堡的外面开——可前一夜出的那些事，在我身上竟没留下多少损耗。

后来曼塞尔和汉伯里从厨房大厅过来，我才知道，卡森和贝尔正在宅子的楼上一层看守厨房那一翼。要控住庭院本是容易的，因为随便哪扇望着院子的窗都办得到；而且说来凑巧，城堡的西南角上衔着一座楼梯角楼，凡是打草地那面摸近厨房的人，从那里都能轻易射倒。

自然，要议定的事极多；可我们每提出一件事，便看得分明，它的斟酌又系于另一件事的裁断；等到早饭吃完，我们除了把两三桩事实议定，几乎一事无成。

===Original===
*The innkeeper knew of the treasure and where it lay.* How he had learned the secret, it was idle to speculate: as like as not, he had held it for years, but, because he would not share it except with his two confederates, he had perforce been content to let the treasure lie. How nearly we had played into his hands was a disturbing thought.

*The thieves as good as knew where the treasure lay*: and, in view of what had to be done to reach the chamber, it seemed probable that they would press the innkeeper into their service: knowing what manner of men they were, the fellow would go reluctantly.

*Our party, alone of the three, knew of the iron bars.* This knowledge was of great value, for it showed that any dash for the treasure was doomed to failure, and that, even if we still had the well, unless we could work undisturbed, our chances of lifting the bags by way of the shaft would be almost negligible. Mansel had watched the water, whilst he was down in the well, and at the mouth of the shaft it rose at the pace of at least thirty inches an hour: and, since to cut through one bar would take the best part of three hours, such a load of labour, coming at the end of an exhausting day, would be more than five men could carry, unless they had each one the strength and endurance of a giant. And that would mean posting but one sentry, which was unthinkable.

===Chinese===
*店主早知道这批财宝，也知道它埋在哪里。*他是怎样得悉这个秘密的，空自揣测也是枉然：十有八九，这秘密他已守了多年；只因除了他两个同伙之外不肯分与他人，他才一直不得不听凭财宝长眠。我们差一点便亲手把事情送到他掌心里去——想起来，便叫人心神不安。

*盗贼们对财宝的下落也可谓心中有数*：而想到抵达石室须费的那些周章，他们多半会把店主强拉来听用：那家伙深知这班人是什么材料，必然去得满心不愿。

*我们这一伙，独独知道那些铁条。*这一点所知价值极大：它表明，直扑财宝的任何抢夺注定失败；还表明，纵然井仍在手，只要不能不受搅扰地做工，我们想经竖井把袋子吊上去，指望就近乎乌有。曼塞尔下井的时候曾留心过水情：竖井口上，水一小时至少涨三十英寸；而锉断一根铁条，就要占去三个钟头的大半；这样一副劳役，落在筋疲力尽的一天末尾，五个人也未必扛得起，除非人人都有巨人的气力和耐性。那便意味着只能留一个哨，那更是想都不必想。

===Original===
*Even reinforced by the landlord, the thieves would not labour as had we.* For one thing, they had neither the physique nor the condition of body which we enjoyed: for another, they were out of their element. For all that, we hoped very much that they would make an attempt to re-empty the well, for that would keep them occupied and leave us more or less free to go about our business of finding another way. For that, if we were not to abandon the enterprise, we must clearly do.

And here we stepped into a very slough of difficulty: for, without the enemy’s knowledge, to drive a new shaft to the chamber was demanding the cunning of an Odysseus, and how, in the face of such aggression as we had met the night before, we were at once to prosecute such travail, hold the stables and maintain our supplies was a question which not one of us could pretend to answer. And yet, as luck would have it, thanks to George Hanbury’s most intelligent observation, this Gordian knot was unloosed within the hour.

---

===Chinese===
*便是添上店主一臂之力，盗贼们也决计干不出我们那样的活。*一则，他们既没有我们的体魄，也没有我们练出来的筋骨；二则，他们干的不是本行。话虽如此，我们却极盼他们当真再去汲一回井：那会拴住他们的手脚，叫我们多少得些自由，去办我们的正事——另寻一条门路。这条路，我们若不打算撂下这桩事业，就显然非走不可。

而在这里，我们一脚踩进了一片困难的泥沼：因为要不叫敌人知晓，掘一条新坑道通到石室，须有奥德修斯（Odysseus）那样的狡狯；而前夜既遭了那样的侵扰，我们如何能一面即刻兴工、担起这桩苦役，一面守住马厩、接济给养，这个问题，我们谁也装不出能答的样子。然而也真凑巧，多亏乔治·汉伯里那双最慧黠的眼睛，这一个戈耳迪之结（Gordian knot），不出一个钟头便解开了。

---

===Original===
When we had breakfasted, Mansel desired Hanbury to relieve Carson, and me to take Bell’s place, so that the two could come down and get some food, and it was half an hour later, when Bell had returned to the post which commanded the courtyard, that Hanbury asked Mansel and me to come to the staircase-turret, where Carson was keeping watch.

Admission to the turret was gained from a secondary hall, which cut the servants’ quarters from the rest of the house: like the kitchen and the servants’ hall, the turret had plainly survived, when the rest of the castle was burned, for it was manifestly aged and most solidly built: I imagine that of late years it had served as a back staircase to the mansion, for there was no other, and modern oil lamps were still hanging upon its walls; but it was a dark, breakneck place, and the servants that had to use it must have complained bitterly. The stair was winding and two feet six inches in width: the rises of the steps were high, and their treads at their broadest point were none too broad: the latter were, of course, wedge-shaped and tapered to nothing. There was, however, good handhold in the shape of a fine, deep groove cut in the outer wall and running up with the stairs. Walls, steps and all were smooth and had once shown a high polish, seldom found upon stone.

All this Hanbury showed us by the light of a torch.

===Chinese===
吃罢早饭，曼塞尔请汉伯里去换卡森，叫我去顶贝尔的班，好教那两位下来吃口东西。半个钟头之后，贝尔已回到那控扼着庭院的哨位，汉伯里便请曼塞尔和我到楼梯角楼去，卡森正在那里守望。

角楼的入口开在一座次要的厅堂里，这座厅堂把仆役的下房同宅子的其余部分隔开：同厨房、仆役厅一样，城堡其余的部分焚为焦土时，角楼分明幸免于难，因为它显然年代久远、砌筑又极坚牢：我猜想近世以来，它一直充作正屋的备用楼梯，因为此外别无楼梯，墙上至今还挂着近代的油灯；可它又黑又险，是能把人脖子摔断的地方，当年不得不走它的仆役，一定怨苦连天。楼梯盘旋而上，宽二英尺六英寸：梯级的立面很高，踏面在最宽的地方也不见得宽：踏面自然是楔形的，一路渐削，削到没有。不过扶手的地方倒不缺——外壁上凿着一道又精又深的槽，随楼梯一同上去。墙、梯级乃至一切，都光滑异常，曾经有过石头上少见的极好抛光。

这一切，汉伯里就着火把的光指给我们看。

===Original===
“And now,” said he, “here is a curious thing.”

With that, he began to descend﻿—for we had gone slowly up﻿—and, when we were all but down, showed us a little landing, which we had scarcely observed, in the midst of the stair.

There were but two steps below it, between it and the hall: and the landing curved, as did the steps it served.

“But for this landing,” said George, “the stairs would not be so steep. It’s hardly a landing: it’s really the third stair up﻿—with a very broad tread: about four feet in breadth. And the height it has cost the staircase has been regained by making inconveniently high the rises of the following stairs.”

When he had pointed this out, it was quite evident.

===Chinese===
「还有呢，」他说，「这里头有一桩古怪事。」

说罢，他开始往下走——方才我们是慢慢上来的——快到楼底的时候，他指给我们楼梯半腰里一处小平台，方才我们几乎没有留意到它。

平台底下到厅堂，只剩两级：而平台同它承接着的梯级一样，是弯的。

「若不是这道平台，」乔治说，「这楼梯还不至于这么陡。它算不得平台：它其实是往上数的第三级——踏面极宽：约莫四英尺。它从楼梯里克扣下的那点高度，又靠把随后几级的立面做得高得叫人难受找了回来。」

经他这一点破，这一层便再明白不过。

===Original===
“And now,” he continued, “look at the handrail.”

Then he showed us that the handrail ceased where the landing began, and began again at once where the landing ceased.

“Finally,” said Hanbury, “look at this rise.”

With that, he stepped into the hall and lowered the torch.

At the top of the third rise, that is to say, two inches below the landing, were two little slots in the stone.

“*By Jove*,” cried Mansel. “*An oubliette.*”

===Chinese===
「还有，」他接着说，「看这扶手。」

随后他指给我们：扶手到平台起头的地方便断了，到平台完的地方又立刻续上。

「末了，」汉伯里说，「看这一级的立面。」

说罢，他跨进厅堂，把火把放低。

第三级的立面顶上——就是说，平台之下两英寸——石上凿着两道小槽。

「*老天在上*，」曼塞尔喊道，「*一口囚井（oubliette）。*」

===Original===
“An oubliette,” said Hanbury. “The innkeeper said there were no cellars, and I’ve no doubt he’s right. But, if this isn’t Axel the Red’s superfluous-guest-chamber, the next time I see him I’ll walk right up to Rose Noble and ask him the way to go home.”

Then he showed us that the broad tread or landing was composed of four stones, three of which were slabs and could probably be withdrawn, as the lid of a pencil-box: but the fourth was fixed. Each was wedge-shaped, that is to say, it had the shape of a step.

“And now imagine,” he concluded, “those three slabs withdrawn. The guest is descending in the dark. Suddenly he steps into space, and, when he clutches at the handrail, *the handrail is gone*. What sort of a fall he has *remains to be seen*.”

Without a word, Mansel turned and left us, to seek some implements, while I went down on my knees and put my two forefingers into the slots. These led into two holes, cut in the broad tread, and, so soon as I felt them, I had no longer any doubt that Hanbury’s conclusion was no fancy, but a substantial truth.

===Chinese===
「一口囚井，」汉伯里说，「店主说过这里没有地窖，我毫不怀疑他说的是实话。可这若不是红发阿克塞尔（Axel the Red）那间安置多余客人的居所，下回我再碰见他，就径直走到罗斯·诺布尔跟前，向他打听回家的路。」

随后他指给我们：那道宽踏面，或者叫平台，是四块石头拼成的；其中三块是石板，多半抽得出来，像铅笔盒的盖子一样：第四块却是钉死的。每一块都作楔形，也就是说，都成一级台阶的形状。

「现在请想一想，」他收束道，「那三块石板抽去之后。客人在黑暗里下楼。冷不防他一脚踏进虚空，伸手去抓扶手，*扶手却已无踪*。这一跤摔得怎样，*且待看了再说*。」

曼塞尔一声不响，转过身离开我们，去找几样家什；我则双膝跪地，把两个食指探进槽缝里。槽缝通到宽踏面上凿出的两个孔；指尖一触到它们，我再也不疑心汉伯里的结论是臆想——那是千真万确的实情。

===Original===
And so it proved.

Mansel returned with a sponge, and was quickly followed by Rowley, bearing some tools and cord.

So soon as the landing was cleansed, it was easy to discern the joints and even the scratches, made upon the wall of the turret when the slabs had been withdrawn.

These marks were slightly higher than we should have expected to see them: but Mansel said that that showed that, before we could draw it out, we must somehow raise the edge of the first slab, for no doubt there was a fillet below, which held it locked into place.

===Chinese===
后来的事实也正是如此。

曼塞尔拿着一块海绵回来了，罗利也很快跟上，带着些家什和绳子。

平台一经洗净，石缝便一目了然，连石板抽动时在角楼壁上蹭出的划痕都看得见。

这些痕迹比我们预想的略高一些：曼塞尔说，这正表明，要抽出石板，须先设法把第一块石板的边缘抬起来，因为底下无疑垫着一条边条，把它锁在位置上。

===Original===
In this he was right, and, but for his wit, we might have sought to withdraw the slab for forty days in vain: but, when we had raised it no more than an eighth of an inch, by means of a wedge, it yielded at once to our efforts and came directly away without any fuss.

Here let me say that the workmanship expended upon this devilish contrivance deserved a worthier theme, for the trap was most beautifully made. Each slab was recessed upon one of its edges, flanged on the other and mitred on either side, and each lay so snug against its fellow, the turret wall and the spindle round which the staircase curled, that, as Mansel said at the time, “only a brilliant observation would have seen anything at all in the landing but clumsiness of construction.”

We had now before us a hole, admitting to some dark place, so Mansel went off to recover and mend the searchlight, while Hanbury, Rowley and I withdrew the remaining slabs. When these had been displaced, the hole revealed was some three and a half feet long: and of all the unfortunates who ever stepped into that space I cannot think one was saved; for, as I have said already, there was there no handrail, the stonework around was polished, and the sides of the trap were mitred, so that even the chances of an ape that had once lost its balance there would have been small indeed.

The air that came from below smelt fairly fresh and was not dank. But the darkness was impenetrable.

===Chinese===
他这一步料得不错；若非他这份机灵，我们兴许抽它四十天也是白抽：可用楔子把它抬起不过八分之一英寸，它便立刻服了我们的努力，干干净净地脱了出来，一点麻烦也没有。

写到这里容我说一句：这件鬼蜮机关上所费的工，本该配一个更体面的题目，因为那活板做得实在精美。每块石板一边开槽、一边起缘、两侧斜削拼角；块与块之间、与角楼壁之间、与楼梯盘绕的中柱之间，都贴得那样熨帖；所以曼塞尔当时说：「除非眼光极高明，谁也看不出这平台除了砌筑的笨拙之外还有别的什么。」

眼前豁然现出一个洞口，通向某个黑暗去处，曼塞尔便离开去把探照灯找回来修好；汉伯里、罗利和我把余下的石板一一抽去。石板移开之后，露出的洞口约有三英尺半长：而凡曾踏进那方空地的苦命人，我想不出有一个保住了性命；因为前文已经说过，那里没有扶手，四周石工磨得溜光，活板的两侧又削了斜口，纵然一只猿猴在那里失过一回脚，逃得性命的机会也小得可怜。

从底下上来的空气闻着相当新鲜，也不阴湿。只是那黑暗深不可测。

===Original===
Presently Mansel returned, with the searchlight in working order, and, when we had made this fast, we lowered it into the hole.

The first thing its beam revealed was the “bed” upon which any victim of the trap must fall.

This was nothing less than a Cornish stile, that is to say, six low, thin fences of stone, built parallel and eighteen inches apart. As these were full forty feet below the trap, that anyone, falling upon them from the staircase, could fail to be broken in pieces was unthinkable. The very look of them shocked us, for it was terrible to regard preparation so nice, deliberate and permanent to send a fellow creature to meet his God.

Presently Hanbury spoke.

===Chinese===
不大一会儿，曼塞尔回来了，探照灯已经修得能用了；我们把它拴牢，吊进洞里。

光柱照出的头一样东西，是落入这活板的人必然跌上去的那张「床」。

那不折不扣是一副康沃尔梯级（Cornish stile）——就是说，六道又矮又薄的石栏，平行而立，彼此相距十八英寸。它们在活板之下足足四十英尺深；凡从楼梯上跌下来落在上面的人，还能不粉身碎骨，那是不可想象的。单是它们那副模样就叫我们寒心：眼看着布置得这样精细、这样蓄意、这样一劳永逸，专为送一个同类去见上帝，实在太可怕了。

片刻之后，汉伯里开了口。

===Original===
“Let me go down,” he said. “I’m not afraid of ghosts.”

“I’ll come with you,” said I. “It’s a two-men job.”

When Mansel had brought the searchlight, he had brought a rope also, as well as the measuring-line. But he had not brought the seat which we had used in the well; so Rowley was sent for this and for a loose cushion, which we could use as a pad between the rope and the stone.

While he was gone, Mansel kept snuffing the air and, after a little, announced that the oubliette had some opening we could not see.

===Chinese===
「让我下去，」他说，「我不怕鬼。」

「我跟你一道，」我说，「这是两个人的活。」

曼塞尔拿探照灯的时候，把绳子也带来了，测深绳也带了。只是我们下井用过的那具吊座没有带来；便打发罗利去取，另带一只松软的坐垫，好垫在绳和石头中间。

他去的时候，曼塞尔不住地嗅着空气；过了一小会儿，他宣布说，这囚井另有我们看不见的开口。

===Original===
“I can feel no draught,” he said, “but I’ll swear that this air is fresh. And that’s as it should be. It was sometimes advisable to empty an oubliette, but to use the trap for that purpose would have been inconvenient. There was, therefore, a second entrance.” He stopped there to clap Hanbury on the back. “If I am right,” he continued, “and such an entrance exists *on the northern or river side*﻿—well, if George Hanbury likes to demand two thirds of the treasure as his share, I don’t believe we can fairly dispute his claim.”

Though I had not Mansel’s foresight, I heartily agreed, for, if we had air to breathe the dungeon was plainly the place from which to drive our shaft, and, though a vent-hole would conduct the sound of our labour, provided it gave upon the river, there would be no one to hear.

Then Rowley returned with the seat and the loose cushion, and, without more ado, Hanbury was lowered into the oubliette. When he was down, I followed, full of instructions from Mansel to walk delicately and, above all things, not to give tongue, “for,” said he, “there is never any mistaking a voice which comes from underground, and, if, as I think, we have found the clue to our labyrinth, it would be a thousand pities to put the enemy wise.”

===Chinese===
「我觉不出风来，」他说，「可我敢赌咒，这空气是新鲜的。这本该如此。囚井有时也需要腾空，但若借这活板来办，未免不便。所以必定还有第二个入口。」说到这里他停了停，拍拍汉伯里的背。「若我料得不错，」他接着说，「这个入口正在*北面、临河的一面*——那么，乔治·汉伯里若要把财宝的三分之二要作他的一份，我相信我们谁也没脸驳他。」

我虽没有曼塞尔的先见，却由衷地赞成：因为既有空气可吸，这地牢（dungeon）分明就是我们掘坑道的下手之处；而且气孔固然会把我们做工的声响送出去，但只要它朝着河，便没有谁来听。

罗利带着吊座和松垫回来了；汉伯里随即不再多话，被缒下了囚井。他下去之后，我也跟着下去，怀里揣着曼塞尔一大堆嘱咐：脚步要轻，顶要紧的是不可出声，「因为，」他说，「打地底下上来的声音，从来一听便知；我们既然——照我看——找到了这座迷宫的线索，把风声漏给敌人，就太可惜了。」

===Original===
The dungeon was some thirty feet square and roughly walled with stones, laid in cement. The weight of the turret was taken by three tremendous piers, between two of which lay the stile: this hideous thing was built of clean-cut stone, with a low wall at either end to hold the fences in place. The piers and the stile stood in a corner of the dungeon and took up much of its room. The place did not seem very damp, but was chill and smelt of the earth. So far as we could see, the walls were everywhere sound.

All this I observed with difficulty, for Hanbury had found a doorway, before I was down, and would scarcely permit me to look around for impatience to see whither the postern led.

The doorway was barred by an old, iron gate, with a great clumsy lock, whose tongues, when shot, protruded into the stone jamb: but, though the gate was closed, it was not locked﻿—an old negligence, I suppose, of some varlet which the Count himself might have pardoned, since the dead cannot open gates whether they be fastened or no.

The doorway admitted to a passage some five feet wide. This began at once to descend: there were no steps, but a very steep, smooth incline, upon which, such was its angle, it would have been easy to fall: but some old, iron dogs, cemented at regular intervals into the walls, afforded handhold.

===Chinese===
地牢约莫三十英尺见方，四壁用石头粗粗砌成，灰浆勾缝。角楼的分量由三根极大的石墩承着，其中两墩之间便是那副梯级：这桩可怖的东西用凿割齐整的石头砌成，两端各有一道矮墙，好把石栏固定在原处。石墩和梯级立在地牢的一角，占去了它好大一块地方。那地方似乎不大潮湿，却阴冷，有一股泥土味。就我们所能看到的，四壁处处完好。

这些我观察得很吃力，因为我还没下到底，汉伯里已经寻见一道门口；他急着要看那道边门通向哪里，简直不容我环顾四周。

那道门口横着一扇旧铁门，配着一把又大又笨的锁，锁舌一落，便凸进石头的门框里：可门虽然关着，却并没有锁——想来是哪个无赖积年的疏忽，连伯爵本人大约也会饶恕，因为死人开不得门，锁与不锁原是一样。

门口通进一条约五英尺宽的过道。过道随即向下走：没有台阶，只是一面很陡很滑的斜坡，坡到那个角度，跌倒是极容易的：好在墙上按着一定间隔砌进些旧铁橛子，可以做扶手。

===Original===
We descended gingerly: Hanbury went first, and I came behind him, with the lamp in my hand.

We had gone, it seemed, a long way, when the passage ran suddenly into another chamber, not so big as the first and, though lofty, not nearly so high. Its longer wall﻿—for it was rectangular﻿—was before us and contained three deep embrasures, the slits of which were rudely blocked with timbers, which were barred, like shutters, into place. That these windows looked out upon the world was evident, for, so soon as we masked the searchlight, little streaks of daylight were appearing from all three. And from them, of course, came the air which had kept the great dungeon fresh.

At first it seemed that this was as far as we could go, but, after a little, we found a loose slab in a corner, close to the window wall. This was round, like a cellar-plate, and might have sealed the mouth of a cistern, for a bar had been sunk in its middle, by which a strong man could lift it out of its place.

===Chinese===
我们提心吊胆地往下走：汉伯里在前，我跟在他身后，手里擎着灯。

走了仿佛很远，过道忽然通进另一间石室，不及头一间大，虽然也高，却远没有那样高。它较长的那面墙——因为它是长方的——正对着我们，墙上开着三道很深的窗洞，窗缝里拿粗木草草堵死，木头像板窗似的横闩在原处。这几扇窗临着外面的世界，是明摆着的：因为探照灯一遮，三道缝里都漏出细细的天光。而叫大地牢一直保持着新鲜的空气，自然也是从这几道缝里来的。

起初，我们仿佛只能到此为止；可过了一小会儿，我们在靠窗那面墙的一个角落里寻见一块松动的石板。它是圆的，像地窖的盖板，也许封着一口水柜的口，因为板心嵌着一根提梁，力气大的人借它便能端开。

===Original===
We were by now quite sure that we were in a gallery which had been cut out of the cliff and that, when the shutters were down, we should see the river below us and the road on its farther side: but, though to my mind it did not matter﻿—for, by the embrasures, we could have plenty of air﻿—the slab did not look to me as though it was concealing an entrance, because, for one thing, it was round and, for another, too small to be hiding a flight of steps. And here I was at once right and wrong, for, when I had lifted it aside, the daylight showed us no entrance, but an exit as clean and simple as ever I saw. This was plainly a shoot, big enough to let a man’s body, and leading at a very steep angle directly to the river below. It was round and smooth, like a drain, and about a third of its mouth was under water.

We discovered later that the shoot in fact discharged into a deep pool: and I think there is little doubt that the bodies of those who had died upon the stile in the oubliette were afterwards disposed of in this way: for a corpse had but to be loaded, head and foot, and then shot into the pool, to sink into well-nigh impregnable oblivion.

That the passage by which we had come was little more than a ramp, down which to drag a body was child’s play, I think supports this view: but we never could make up our minds upon the purpose which the gallery or second chamber had been constructed to serve. It may have been a dungeon: it may have been merely the dreadful “robing-room” in which the dead were “attired” for their last journey: it may have been a retreat to which the Count could withdraw, if the castle fell, where he could rest for a while before he made his escape. Be that as it may, so far as we were concerned, it was a perfect withdrawing-room, airy, secluded, safe, and actually adjoining the dungeon from which we must drive our shaft.

===Chinese===
这时我们已经很有把握：自己是在一座从崖石里凿出来的画廊里；板窗一卸，就该望见脚下的河，和河对岸的大路：不过照我看，这原不要紧——借着窗洞，尽够呼吸——而那块石板在我眼里不像是藏着什么入口：一来它是圆的，二来它太小，藏不下一道台阶。这一回我是既对又错：因为我把石板端开之后，天光照出的不是入口，而是一个出口，干净利落，平生仅见。那分明是一道溜槽，大得容得下一个人的身子，以极陡的坡度直通下面的河。它圆而光滑，像一根沟管，管口约三分之一没在水里。

后来我们才弄明白，溜槽其实泻进一泓深潭：我想几乎无可怀疑，死在囚井梯级上的那些人的尸首，事后便是这样打发的：尸首只须头脚坠上重物，再顺着槽送进潭里，便沉入一个几乎打不破的湮灭里去。

我们来时走的那条过道，其实无非一道坡，把尸首拖下去同儿戏一般；我想这一层也佐证上面的看法：至于这画廊，或者说这第二间石室，当初造它是何用意，我们却始终拿不定主意。它也许是一座地牢；也许只是那间可怕的「更衣室」，死人上路之前，先在这里「穿戴」停当：也许是城堡一旦陷落时伯爵可以退守的所在，让他出逃之前先歇一口气。且不论如何，就我们而言，它是一间十全十美的退息之室：透风、隐蔽、安全，而且恰恰紧挨着我们注定要由此掘坑道的地牢。

===Original===
And that was as far as I could see.

Then we returned to the dungeon, and Hanbury ascended, and Mansel came down in his stead. I showed him all we had found. When we came to the shoot, he laughed.

“We’re doomed to get wet,” he said.

I did not understand, and said so.

===Chinese===
我的所见，也尽于此了。

随后我们回到地牢，汉伯里升上去，曼塞尔换他下来。我把我们寻见的一切指给他看。走到溜槽跟前，他笑了。

「咱们注定要湿身了，」他说。

我不懂，便照实说了。

===Original===
Mansel fingered his chin.

“William,” he said, “that bomb put the wind up us properly. So much so that this very night we leave Wagensburg﻿—bag, baggage and cars﻿—never to return. At least, that, I trust, is what Rose Noble will think. Of course, he’s no fool, Rose Noble. But, if we leave the stable doors open, he’ll have something to go on, won’t he? And I think we might call at the inn, to say ‘Goodbye’ to the landlord. That would be almost artistic.”

I could only stare.

Mansel laughed. Then he waved a hand at the chamber.

“Dormitory and parlour,” he said, “until the treasure is won. The cars stabled at Villach: supplies delivered by night, by means of the shoot. And, when we’re through, what an exit! Simple, unobtrusive and swift. All we need is a boat. Of course, George Hanbury should receive the D.S.O. And I think perhaps Axel the Red deserves a mention.”

---

===Chinese===
曼塞尔摩挲着下巴。

「威廉，」他说，「那颗炸弹着实把我们唬住了。唬到今夜我们就离开瓦根斯堡——人、行李、汽车，一样不留——从此不回来。至少，我指望罗斯·诺布尔是这么想的。当然，罗斯·诺布尔不是傻子。可只要我们敞着马厩的门走，他手里就有东西可以捉摸了，是不是？还有，我想我们该去客栈走一趟，向店主道一声『别』。那就几乎是艺术了。」

我只有瞪着他。

曼塞尔笑了。随后他朝那间石室一挥手。

「寝室兼客厅，」他说，「直到财宝到手。汽车寄在菲拉赫：给养夜间由溜槽送来。等大功告成，好一个退场！简单、不惹眼、又快。我们缺的只是一条船。自然，乔治·汉伯里理应得一枚优异服务勋章（D.S.O.）。我看红发阿克塞尔也该在战报里记上一笔。」

---

===Original===
By noon we had lowered into the oubliette everything loose that we had, except, of course, some arms and the furniture of the cars. Then Mansel and Carson descended, to arrange the electric light and install a muffled bell which should ring from the mouth of the shoot.

By lunchtime all was in order, and we had but to lay our plans.

These were easy to make.

===Chinese===
到晌午，我们手头所有零散的东西都已吊进囚井，枪械和汽车的什物自然除外。随后曼塞尔和卡森下去，布置电灯，装一口消音的铃——这铃是在溜槽口按响的。

到午饭时分，诸事齐备，只待我们定计了。

计议本不难定。

===Original===
We soon decided to leave Rowley and Bell behind in the oubliette, while the rest of us took the cars and left for Villach. To procure a good base should be easy; but we might have to go to Salzburg to find a collapsible boat. It seemed likely that we should be back by the following night. But, before we left the castle, there remained to be done two things of some importance. The first was to see, if we could, what action the enemy was taking: and the second, *to determine, once for all, the direction in which to dig our shaft*.

Now, when I perceived the gravity of this decision, the extreme difficulty of taking it and the impossibility of verifying its accuracy, I must honestly confess that my heart failed me. Had we had the estate to ourselves, to drive a tunnel near two hundred yards in length and twenty-five feet below ground, so as to hit a chamber some six feet square, would to my mind, have been a very difficult feat: but to accomplish this without being able so much as to survey, with this idea, the surface beneath which we were to burrow, without one definite measurement upon which to found our endeavour, would be, I felt, to perform a miracle.

Indeed, I said as much; to find, to my relief, that we were not so ill-equipped as I had imagined: for it appeared that, the evening before, whilst I was in the well, Mansel had pulled out a compass and taken a bearing by which he had determined the angle at which the shaft left the well. “And that,” said he, “is the kernel of the knowledge we need. The rest I will get, if not today, tomorrow: and, if not tomorrow, the next day. There’s, therefore, no cause for concern. Finally, we have yet to reconnoitre: and if we can wring a survey out of a reconnaissance, so much the better.”

===Chinese===
不多时我们便议定：罗利和贝尔留在囚井里，其余人坐汽车去菲拉赫。觅一处好的营盘该当容易；不过要寻一条折叠小船，只怕得上萨尔茨堡。看情形，次日夜里之前我们便能回来。但离开城堡之前，尚有两桩要紧事待办。头一件，是看能否窥出敌人正在做什么：第二件，*是彻底定下我们掘坑道的方向*。

这时我看清了这个决断的分量、定它的万难、以及无从验证其准确，我须得老实承认，我的心气泄了。倘或这片庄园全归我们受用，掘一条将近二百码长、深入地下二十五英尺的坑道，末了还要打中一间约六英尺见方的石室，在我心里已是一桩极难的功业：而要在连脚下的地面都不容我们抱着这个念头踏勘一眼、连一个确凿的尺寸都无从依据的情形下办成它，我觉得，那简直是显一场神迹。

我便照实说了；却发觉——这叫我宽心——我们的装备并不像我料想的那么糟糕：原来前晚我在井里的时候，曼塞尔已掏出罗盘测了一个方位，据此定下了坑道离开井口的角度。「而这，」他说，「便是我们所需要的学问的核。其余我自己去弄，今天不成便明天；明天不成便后天。所以不必悬心。末了，我们还得侦察：若能从侦察里拧出一幅勘测图来，那便更好了。」

===Original===
Such comfortable words, coming from Mansel, went far to rout my misgivings, but I could not help hoping very hard that some sort of survey would be made possible by the enemy’s absence from the well, and at once proposed that I should go up to the roof, and, taking care to keep out of sight, see if I could perceive any sign of activity. To this suggestion Mansel agreed, and within five minutes I was upon the slates, looking towards the great well.

I could not see the meadow for the bushes and trees, but work of some sort was proceeding about the well, for, though there was no sound of the windlass, I heard repeatedly the smack of stone upon stone, and once the loose clatter of stones being shot on to the ground. These sounds I could not interpret, and, since I could hear nothing else and could see nowhere any movement, I presently descended to the hall and made my report.

That the thieves were drawing no water delighted us all, for nothing could have shown us more plainly how much they were underrating the activity of the springs. Moreover, by their failure to bail, they were throwing away the precious fruit of our labour, and, of their ignorance, letting slip an invaluable advantage. When we had first taken possession, some fifty-five feet of water had covered the mouth of the shaft: at daybreak this morning it was hidden by, at the most, nineteen: but, if they did not begin bailing before dawn on the following day, the water would by then have regained almost the whole of the ground we had been at such pains to capture.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔说出的这般宽心话，很把我的疑虑驱散了些；可我仍免不住苦苦指望：敌人既离了井边，或能容我们弄出某种勘测来。我当即提议：让我上屋顶去，小心不露形迹，看看能否望见敌人活动的迹象。曼塞尔依了；不出五分钟，我已伏在石板瓦上，朝着大井望去。

灌木树木障着眼，草地看不见；可井边分明在做一种活计，因为绞盘虽无声音，石头相击的脆响却一声接一声，还有一回，是一阵石头泻到地上的乱响。这些声音我解不出来；此外什么也听不见，什么也望不见动静，我便下到厅堂里复命。

盗贼们不汲水，这叫我们个个欢喜：因为再没有比这更明白的表记，说明他们把泉水的活跃看轻到了何等地步。何况他们既不戽水，便是把我们辛苦挣来的果实白白抛掉，凭着他们的无知，放走一桩千金难买的便宜。我们初到手时，竖井口上盖着五十五英尺的水：今天拂晓，至多不过十九英尺：可他们若不在明天破晓前动手戽水，到那时，水便要把我们费尽气力夺来的地盘几乎全部收回。

===Original===
“Which shows,” said Hanbury, “that the innkeeper isn’t with them. If he were, he’d have put them wise.”

“Don’t be too sure,” said Mansel. “It’s a hundred to one they wouldn’t take his advice: but what is still more likely, is that he’ll hold his tongue. He doesn’t want to help Rose Noble to the treasure. What would be his reward? Why, as like as not, they’d leave him down in the well. He knows that perfectly. And that’s why I very much hope they have impressed him. It will keep him out of mischief, and the quality of help he’ll give them will do as much harm as good.”

It was then arranged that he and I and Carson should go out on patrol, while Rowley watched the courtyard, and Hanbury and Bell covered our advance from the other side of the house.

===Chinese===
「可见，」汉伯里说，「店主并不在他们一伙。他若在，早把底细告诉他们了。」

「也未必尽然，」曼塞尔说，「他们不听他的话，是百不有一；可更可能的，是他自己闭着嘴。他并不想帮罗斯·诺布尔得财宝。帮他得了，他领什么赏？哼，多半是他们索性把他撇在井底。这一点他明白得很。所以我才极盼他们把他强拉了来。那既免得他闲着淘气，他帮他们的那点忙，成事几何，坏事也几何。」

于是议定：他同我和卡森出去巡哨，罗利守望庭院，汉伯里和贝尔从宅子那一面掩护我们推进。

===Original===
Carson was to start from the courtyard, pass through the great gateway and move towards the meadow, with the path on his left: Mansel and I were to start from the west of the mansion: and we were to meet, if we could, in a dip which lay between the meadow and the trees which sheltered the house.

Before we set out, we closed the mouth of the dungeon. Then we took a pistol apiece: and Mansel put in his pocket a compass and a measuring-tape.

“And please remember,” he said, “we’re not out to fight: we’re going to see what’s afoot and to mark the lie of the land: a fuss, therefore, means failure: if there’s anyone in our way we must wait till he moves: he’s sure to do that before sundown, and it’s only just two. And there’s the slogan for today: ‘Take your time.’ ”

===Chinese===
卡森从庭院出发，穿过大门，朝草地去，小路在他左手：曼塞尔和我从宅邸西面出发：我们要在一处洼地里会合——那洼地夹在草地和护着房子的树木之间。

动身之前，我们把地牢的口闭上了。随后一人带一支手枪：曼塞尔把一具罗盘和一柄卷尺装进衣袋。

「另外请记着，」他说，「我们不是出去厮杀：是去看看有什么动静，把地势记下：所以一闹便是失败：挡路的不管是谁，都得等他挪窝：天黑之前他必定要走，这会儿才刚过两点。今天的口号便是：『慢慢来。』」

===Original===
Mansel and I could only emerge from a window upon the first floor, for all those below were barred: but we had a short rope-ladder, which we had not yet tried. This certainly served our purpose, but it was the most awkward appliance I ever have used, for its rungs were of rope and gave way beneath my weight in a most disconcerting manner.

When we were down, Hanbury drew up the ladder, leaving only a little grey cord to hang against the wall, some seven feet above ground. We had but to twitch this to bring down the ladder at once; so, though it was very improbable that an unfriendly eye would perceive anything suspicious, our means of ascent was continually assured.

The first thing we did was to gain the shelter of the trees and lie down in the long grass. There we lay for ten minutes as still as death. During all that time we heard not the faintest sound, except, very indistinctly, the stony chink and clatter which I had heard from the roof.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔和我只能从楼上一扇窗里出去，因为底下的窗全闩着：我们有一条短绳梯，还不曾用过。它自然顶了用，却是我平生用过的最别扭的一件家什：梯级是绳做的，我的分量一压便往下坠，难受得叫人心慌。

我们下了地，汉伯里把梯子收上去，只剩一根灰色小绳垂在墙上，离地约七英尺。只消一带这根绳，梯子应声而下；所以，纵然不怀好意的眼睛极难看出什么可疑，我们上楼的路子却时刻有靠。

我们先办的一件事，是躲进树荫，在深草里伏下。我们在那里死一般静伏了十分钟。这整段工夫里，一丝声息也没有，只有我在屋顶上听见的那种石头脆响，隐隐约约，还可闻见。

===Original===
At length Mansel gave the signal, and we began to move.

Using the greatest caution, we crawled south, keeping as close to the castle as the covert allowed. So we came to the corner where the staircase-turret stood. Here, again, we lay still for ten minutes or more. Then Mansel rose to his knees, and from his knees to his feet. For a moment he stood like a statue: then he looked slowly around. The next instant he was at the base of the turret, measuring-tape in hand.

I do not think that anything I ever saw Mansel do impressed me so much as the little survey he made at the foot of the castle wall. He was full in the open, and against the white, sunlit stone he offered a perfect mark: the work he was doing, if seen, would as good as betray our plans: his nearest cover was twenty-five paces away. Yet, though he worked for six minutes, he never once looked about him or even raised his head. Swiftly and silently he used the compass and tape, going about his business with the deliberate precision of one to whom time is no object, and entering figures in a notebook, as though he had the world to himself.

When he had quite finished, he took a long nail from his pocket and, placing its point where he had made a slight mark, pressed it well into the ground: then he hooked the end of the tape upon the head of the nail, and stepped across to where I lay in the grass.

===Chinese===
末了曼塞尔发出信号，我们开始移动。

我们以最大的小心朝南爬，紧贴着城堡，紧到树丛容许的程度。这样到了楼梯角楼立着的墙角。在这里，我们又静伏了十来分钟。随后曼塞尔由跪而立。他石像一般立了片刻：随后缓缓环顾。下一瞬间，他已到了角楼的墙根，手里拿着卷尺。

我看曼塞尔做过的事，没有一件比他在堡墙脚下那一小场勘测更叫我叹服的了。他全身在旷地里，衬着被日头照白的石头，十足一个靶子：他做的事一旦被看见，我们的计划几乎等于和盘托出：离他最近的遮蔽也有二十五步。可他做了六分钟的活，竟没有回顾一次，连头也不曾抬。他利利落落、寂然无声地使着罗盘和卷尺，办他的正事，那种从容不迫的精确，仿佛时间于他毫无干系；他往小本子上记着数字，仿佛这天地独他一人。

他完全做完之后，从衣袋里摸出一枚长钉，把钉尖按在他先前轻轻画过记号的地方，深深踩进地里：随后把卷尺的端头钩在钉头上，跨到我在草里伏着的地方来。

===Original===
“Kneel for a minute,” he said. I knelt. “You see that fir to our left, with the broken bough like a spout? I’m going to measure exactly how far it stands from the nail. Follow me, with the tape in your hand. Hold it continually taut, so that whatever happens it doesn’t slip off the nail.”

With that, he lay down in the grass and began to steal forward, paying out the tape as he went.

We had crawled and lain still and crawled again for nearly half-an-hour, and, so much were my fingers aching, I was beginning to wish the treasure at the bottom of the sea, when Mansel turned his head and signed to me to come alongside.

I did so gingerly.

===Chinese===
「跪一会儿，」他说。我跪了。「瞧见我们左边那棵枞树没有，断枝像条水槽的那棵？我要量一量它离那枚钉子究竟多远。牵着我走，手里拿着卷尺。要一直把它拉紧，无论如何别让它从钉头上滑脱。」

说罢，他伏进草里，开始潜行，一面走一面放尺。

我们爬一程、伏一阵、再爬一程，将近半个钟头；我的手指疼得厉害，已经开始巴望这批财宝沉到海底去了，这时曼塞尔回过头来，打手势叫我挨过去。

我小心翼翼地依言挨了过去。

===Original===
“I can smell tobacco,” he breathed.

We were still ten paces from the fir, to which, except for the bushes, we had had a clear run: in threading the tape through these, Mansel had shown infinite patience, for, as may be readily understood, it was a trying exercise, and to perform it noiselessly had entailed a lot of finical labour.

At first I could smell no smoke, but after a moment or two a whiff came to my nose, and I was immediately sure that it came from the south. I told Mansel this, whereupon he at once went forward, bidding me stay where I was. Half a length clear of me he stopped, and, after a long wait, signed to me to come on. When I had done so and was lying alongside, he told me very gently to part the grass.

The first thing I saw was that we were at the end of the covert through which we had crawled and that the fir stood clear, on the edge of the little dip where we were to join Carson. Beyond lay the meadow. In this, twenty paces away, stood a little, curved breastwork composed of flat, loose stones such as go to the making of piled walls, and built to shelter a sentry from every side but the south. This little sconce was occupied, for the smoke we had smelt was floating over its parapet, and the crown of a soft, felt hat was just to be seen. Whoever was there was plainly taking his ease and was sitting down on the ground, with his back to the wall.

===Chinese===
「我闻见烟丝味了，」他贴着气说。

我们离那棵枞树还有十步；若不是灌木挡着，我们本可以一路畅通地跑到它跟前：为了把卷尺从灌木中间穿过去，曼塞尔显出了无穷的耐性；因为不难想见，这是一桩磨人的差事，要做得没有一点声响，更须许多琐屑的工夫。

起初我闻不见烟；可过了一两分钟，一股烟味钻进鼻子，我立刻断定它来自南面。我把这话告诉了曼塞尔，他随即向前去，嘱我留在原地。他走到超出我半个身位的地方停下；等了好久，才打手势叫我过去。我依言过去，伏在他身旁，他便轻轻地叫我拨开草。

我头一眼看见的，是我们已到了一路爬来的树丛的尽头，枞树孤零零立在小小洼地的边上——我们要同卡森会合的地方就在那洼地。再过去便是草地。草地上，二十步开外，立着一弯小小的胸墙，用的是垒墙那种扁而松的石头，修来四面遮蔽哨兵，独独南面不设。这座小碉堡里有人：因为我们闻见的那股烟正从墙头飘过，一顶软毡帽的帽顶也隐隐可见。那里面的人分明十分自在，背靠着墙坐在地上。

===Original===
In the distance, about the well, Rose Noble was directing the building of a kind of redoubt, in the shape of a wall of flat stones piled one upon the other and packed together with earth. These were brought up from the combe, from some wall or cabin, I suppose, which we had not observed, and were borne on a sort of hurdle by a bearer at either end. The sun was hot, and I did not envy them their labour; and, indeed, it was very clear that they did not like it themselves, for they staggered under their loads, like drunken men, and finally discharged them with the slovenly recklessness of one who is past caring.

Some sort of system was observed, for the two that had brought up the stones packed those they had brought into place, while the others reluctantly descended in search of more: but, partly for want of stones and partly because of the laziness of its builders, which was quite laughable to watch, the wall rose very slowly, and I was not surprised to see Rose Noble fiercely impatient of such half-heartedness. What he said I could not hear, but the contempt and indignation of his gestures were unmistakable.

Perhaps, for us, the most engaging sight was that of the landlord of the inn, his wrist fastened by a cord to that of Punter, taking his turn with the latter of bringing up stones on the hurdle and packing them into place. A more dejected-looking workman I never have seen, and I imagine he was cursing the treasure and the well and Wagensburg from the bottom of his heart.

===Chinese===
远处，井的四周，罗斯·诺布尔正督着修一座壁垒模样的东西：一道墙，扁石一块摞一块，拿泥土挤紧。石头是从山谷里运上来的——想是取自哪面墙或哪间小屋，我们没有留意到——由一种抬架（hurdle）抬着，两端各有一名抬夫。日头正毒，我不羡慕他们这份劳役；而且，说实话，他们自己也分明不喜欢：石头压得他们东倒西歪，像醉汉一样，末了把担子卸下，漫不经心，一副全然不顾的死相。

他们多少还守着点章法：运石头的两个把运到的码放进位置，其余的便满心不愿地下谷去再搬：可是石头既短，砌墙的人又懒——那份懒相看着真教人发笑——墙便起得极慢；罗斯·诺布尔对这等三心二意急得跳脚，我一点也不奇怪。他说的话我听不见，可他手势里的轻蔑和愤懑，一目了然。

在我们眼里，最有趣的场面多半要算客栈的店主：他的手腕拿一根绳拴在潘特的手腕上，同潘特轮班，用抬架往高处运石、码放进位置。这样一副倒霉相的工人，我平生未见；我想他从心底里正把财宝、井和瓦根斯堡咒了个遍。

===Original===
No one of them was suitably attired for manual labour, but Job was wearing a pair of white flannel trousers, which might have afforded him comfort, had he not been so anxious to keep them clean. In this the labour was against him, and his manifest concern for their condition was constantly provoking Rose Noble’s wrath.

Ellis was not to be seen, which satisfied us that he was behind the breastwork, performing the duties of a sentinel according to his lights.

Now, all this encouraged me greatly, and, I fancy, Mansel as well; for it was plain as a pikestaff that, though the thieves “meant business,” when they felt the pinch of labour there was only one man among them worth his salt, and that, if they could not draw water any better than they could build walls, their chances of reaching the shaft were slight indeed, to say nothing of Ellis’ exhibition, for which he deserved to be shot. All the same, there were still six paces between us and the fir, which was our immediate goal, and these lay in view of the meadow and all its occupants.

===Chinese===
他们没有一个穿着合乎粗活的衣裳，可乔布穿着一条白色法兰绒裤子，若不是他一心护着裤子干净，本可以叫他舒服些。这活计偏偏同他为难；他对裤子成色那份看得见的挂念，不断地招惹罗斯·诺布尔的火气。

埃利斯不见踪影；我们由此断定，他在胸墙后面，就着他那点亮心眼，尽着一个哨兵的本分。

这一切叫我大大壮了胆，我猜曼塞尔也是；因为再明白不过：盗贼们虽是「来真的」，可一尝劳役的苦头，他们中间便只剩一个人配他那口饭食；他们汲水既然汲不出名堂，砌墙既然砌不出名堂，要到达竖井，指望便实在渺茫——更不必说埃利斯那一出戏了，那功劳够赏他一颗枪子。话虽如此，我们同那棵枞树——我们当前的目标——还隔着六步，而这六步全在草地和草地上下所有人的眼皮底下。

===Original===
Now this, though I did not then know it, did not matter at all, for what Mansel had wanted to do was to reach some point from which he could see the well. And this we had done. So, after a little, he brought out a second nail and pressed it into the ground. The distance between the two nails was eighty-one yards. He then took the bearing of the well from the second nail. When he had done this, for an hour he lay very still, like a dog, with his eyes fixed upon the meadow and his chin on his hands. He told me later that during that time he endured the torment of a thief who, holding already some earring of very great value, regards its fellow from without some jeweller’s shop; for that, having got so far, not to be able to measure the distance from where we lay to the well was exasperating indeed.

At last he whispered to me to move a few paces back towards the house and to wait for him in a place where the cover was thick.

I had hardly done so before I saw Carson’s head a little way to our left, and, so soon as he saw that I saw him, he nodded and disappeared.

===Chinese===
其实——我当时并不知道——这一层全然不要紧，因为曼塞尔要办的，只是找到一个望得见井的地点。这一点我们做到了。于是，过了一小会儿，他又摸出一枚钉子，按进地里。两钉之间，相距八十一码。随后他从第二枚钉子测了井的方位。这件事办完，有一个钟头，他伏着一动不动，像条狗，眼睛盯着草地，下巴搁在手上。他后来告诉我，那段工夫里，他受着一个贼的煎熬：那贼手里已经攥着一只价值连城的耳环，另一只却还在珠宝店橱窗的外头，隔窗干望；因为到了这一步，却量不出我们伏处到井的距离，实在叫人抓狂。

末了他低声嘱咐我，朝宅子那边退开几步，在一处草木稠密的地点等他。

我刚办妥，便望见卡森的脑袋在我们左面不远；他一见我望见了他，便点点头，隐没了。

===Original===
As soon as Mansel was back, he spoke in my ear.

“I think we must make a bid for the distance from the covert to the well. We may not need it; but, if we don’t take it now, I don’t think we shall be able to take it at all. Unless I’m mistaken, they’re not only building a shelter: *they’re building their future home*: and the idea of surveying even the curtilage of Rose Noble’s bower, when occupied, makes no appeal to me. Now to drive them out of the meadow would be of no use: they’d certainly run for shelter, but, as soon as they’d won it, they’d stand; and then, though they might not hurt us, they’d see us at work. And that would be fatal. The only thing to be done is to draw them into the combe. Please, therefore, go back and get Hanbury. Leave the house together and see that Bell or Rowley pulls up the ladder out of sight. Then make your way to the combe, bearing west, well away from the meadow, beyond the sentinel peak. Locate their depot of stones and try to pick up their car. Then decide two things﻿—first, what feint is most likely to draw them into the combe, and, secondly, how you and Hanbury are going to retire on the house. When you’ve taken these two decisions, await a convenient moment, and then demonstrate. Fire, start the engine of the car, shout﻿—according as you think best. If you can do it, I should think the car is a sure draw. And the moment you’ve got them going, fade right away. For heaven’s sake take no risks: it isn’t worth it. And a minute and a half is enough for Carson and me. Now, is all that clear?”

I nodded.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔一回来，便凑着我耳朵说话。

「我想，咱们必须设法把从树丛到井的距离拿到手。也许用不着；可现在不取，我怕往后就再也取不成了。若不是我料错，他们不但在修一个藏身处：*他们在修自己往后的家*：等他们住了进去，再去勘测罗斯·诺布尔安乐窝的墙根，这个念头我提不起半点兴致。现在把他们赶出草地毫无用处：他们必定逃进壁垒；可一得了庇护，他们便站住脚；到那时，纵然不伤我们，却看得见我们做工。那就是致命的了。唯一的办法，是把他们引进山谷。所以请你就此回去，把汉伯里带来。两人一同离开宅子，务必叫贝尔或者罗利把梯子收得望不见。随后往山谷去，偏西走，远远避开草地，绕过哨峰。先找见他们囤石的场子，再设法摸清他们汽车的所在。然后拿两个主意——第一，什么虚招最能把他们引进山谷；第二，你同汉伯里如何退回宅子。两个主意拿定，候一个合适的时机，然后做戏。开枪，发动汽车，喊叫——你估量怎么好便怎么办。若办得到，我想汽车是万无一失的引子。他们一起身，你们便立时隐得无影无踪。看老天面上，千万别冒险：不值得。卡森和我，一分半钟尽够了。我说这一篇，都明白了么？」

我点了点头。

===Original===
“Good,” breathed Mansel. “As you go, please release the tape and pocket the nail: and remember, *take your time*.”

“I will,” I said.

Then I turned and left him, and started to crawl back towards the house.

===Chinese===
「好，」曼塞尔贴着气说，「你走的时候，请把卷尺收起，钉子装进衣袋：还要记着，*慢慢来*。」

「好的，」我说。

随后我转过身离了他，开始朝宅子爬回去。

===Original===
It was, I suppose, some thirty minutes later that Hanbury and I rounded the southwestern shoulder of the sentinel peak to see the closed car below us on a grass-grown track, close to a ruinous byre. Of this the walls were standing, and had been rudely loop-holed: but the roof was gone. Still, the place made a good blockhouse, and the ground about it was clear, affording a field of fire. The track was wet and muddy, because of all the water which we had drawn from the well: and, further east, the ground to the north of the track had the look of a bog. Two or three blankets were lying spread out in the sun, and bottles and papers and tins made a disorderly litter about the spot. A door of the car was open, and on this hung somebody’s coat: a couple of empty glasses stood on the running-board.

We had just observed all this when Punter and the innkeeper came plodding into view, the latter dragging the hurdle, as though he were tired of life. Arrived at the byre, Punter lay down on the ground, while the other began to pluck some stones from what was left of a pen by the side of the byre. He worked slowly enough in all conscience, yet too fast, I suppose, for Punter, for, when the hurdle was laden, the latter stared upon its burden and then, as though the sight shocked him, covered his eyes and lay back upon the ground. Indeed, so ludicrous was his demeanour that Hanbury and I began to shake with laughter, and, when his unfortunate companion began to gesticulate in manifest apprehension of the trouncing this delay would provoke, we could have roared with mirth. Punter, however, took no notice at all, and at last the other sat down and put his head in his hands.

Before they moved again, our plans were laid.

===Chinese===
我想，约莫半个钟头之后，汉伯里和我绕过哨峰的西南肩，望见那辆封闭汽车在我们脚下的土路上，紧挨着一座坍败的牛栏（byre）。牛栏的四壁还立着，草草开了枪眼：只是顶没有了。话虽如此，那地方仍是一个极好的据点，四下地面开阔，正好布下射界。土路又湿又泥泞，因为我们从井里汲出的水全归了它：再往东，土路以北的地带着上去便是一片沼泽。两三条毯子摊在日头底下晒着，瓶瓶罐罐、纸张铁盒，把那一带弄得乱七八糟。汽车的一扇门敞着，门上搭着不知谁的褂子：踏板上搁着一对空杯子。

这些我们才看罢，潘特和店主便拖磨着进了眼帘，后者拖着抬架，仿佛活得不耐烦了似的。到了牛栏，潘特往地上一躺，另一位开始从牛栏旁边残存的圈栏里往外拣石头。他做得真够慢的了，可我猜在潘特眼里还是太快：抬架装满之后，后者瞪着那一驮，随后，仿佛这景象刺了他的眼，把眼睛一捂，仰面躺了下去。他这副行状实在滑稽，汉伯里和我笑得直抖；他那位倒霉的同伴一见这耽搁少不得要挨一顿好揍，惊惶之下指手画脚起来，我们简直可以放声大笑了。潘特却全然不理；末了另一位也坐下，双手抱住了头。

没等他们再动，我们的章程已定。

===Original===
From what we remembered of the combe, there would come a moment when Punter, mounting the slope, could see Rose Noble, yet have the car in his eye. At that moment we were to sally upon the car. I was to start her engine and, as though by accident, sound the electric horn, while Hanbury was to attack her petrol-tank. Directly we heard the cries sure to be raised, we were to fire at random, and I was to run up the track and Hanbury down. Once out of sight we were to take to the woods and make our way back to the castle, I by way of the shrine and Hanbury, who was fleeter of foot, by the line we had come.

The plan was simple enough, and, I think, sound: in fact, as I shall presently show, it served its purpose: but this it did at a price which we did not expect to pay, for I had just started the engine, after sounding the horn, and was watching Punter’s frenzy upon the brow of the rise, when two hands closed upon my windpipe with a grip so savage that I was unable to breathe, much less utter a sound. Be sure I fought like a madman, but the man behind me was strong and had the advantage. His thumbs were braced against the back of my neck, which might have been in a vice, and, having once got such a hold, for him to prevent me from leaving the driver’s seat was very easy. It seemed a long time before I heard cries raised, and then the noise of a shot. This sounded faint, and blurred, for the blood was pounding in my head, and I knew I was losing consciousness. Then I heard more cries, which seemed confused and distant, and I was still trying to determine what they might mean, when my senses left me.

---

===Chinese===
依我们记忆里的山谷，总有一刻：潘特爬上坡顶，望得见罗斯·诺布尔，汽车却还在他眼里。那一刻，我们便扑向汽车。我去发动发动机，装出无心似的按响电喇叭；汉伯里去毁她的油箱。喊声必然四起；一听见，我们便乱枪齐发，我沿土路往上跑，汉伯里往下跑。一出了视线，便钻进树林，各回城堡：我打小教堂那条路，汉伯里腿脚快，循我们来的那条线。

计划本极简单，我想也很稳妥：事实上——我随即就要交代——它达到了目的：但达到这目的，却付了一笔我们没有预算的代价。因为我刚按过喇叭、发动了发动机，正望着潘特在坡顶上的疯狂，两只手忽然扼住我的咽喉，掐得那样凶，我出不来气，更遑论出声。自然，我拼命挣斗，可背后那人又壮，又占着便宜。他的拇指死死抵住我的后颈，我的脖子夹在他手里，同在虎钳里没有两样；这样的把柄一旦到手，不许我离开司机座，对他易如反掌。仿佛过了很久，我才听见喊声四起，随后是一声枪响。声音发闷，又模糊，因为血在我脑袋里咚咚地擂，我知道自己正失去知觉。随后又有喊声，嘈杂而遥远；我还在捉摸它们是什么意思，知觉便离了我。

---

===Original===
“What do you know?” said Rose Noble.

I was sitting up on the ground, with my back to the near fore wheel of the closed car. The hubcap was hurting abominably, but about this I could do nothing, because I was lashed to the spokes and could not move. Rose Noble was sitting on a box a few feet away, and, immediately opposite me, Ellis was leaning against the jamb of a doorway, framed by a high stone wall, with a cigar in his mouth. For a moment or two I could not make out where I was: then I saw that the car had been moved to the farther side of the byre, which now stood between it and the combe, and wholly concealed it from anyone north of the track.

I must have lain unconscious a long time, for the sun had just gone down.

“What do you know?” said Rose Noble.

===Chinese===
「你都知道些什么？」罗斯·诺布尔说。

我坐在地上，背靠着封闭汽车的近侧前轮。毂盖硌得我疼痛难当，可这一点我毫无办法：我被捆在辐条上，动弹不得。罗斯·诺布尔坐在几英尺外的一只箱子上；埃利斯在我正对面，倚着一道门口的门框——身后是一堵高高的石墙——嘴里叼着一支雪茄。有一两分钟，我辨不出身在何处：随后我看清了，汽车已挪到牛栏那一面，牛栏如今隔在它与山谷之间，凡在土路以北的人，一概望它不见。

我想必是昏迷了很久，因为日头刚刚落下。

「你都知道些什么？」罗斯·诺布尔说。

===Original===
“I refuse to talk,” said I, “until you loosen this cord. I expect to be tied up, but this hubcap is breaking my back.”
